# sensing-selfcal research log

## 2026-11-24 · Milestone 3zh planned: which undistortion loop does a robot actually run? Six OpenCV releases (4.5.5 to 5.1.0), Kornia, VINS-Mono's camodocs and ORB-SLAM2/3's frame bounds on milestone 3zf's 179 calibrations

Written before `experiments/m3zh_versions/` was written or run. Milestone 3zg traced the COUNT-only stall to OpenCV 5.1.0's loop and advised "always pass COUNT|EPS". **Seen before this plan** (source only, nothing run): (1) in OpenCV's `modules/calib3d/src/undistort.dispatch.cpp` at tags 4.2.0, 4.5.4, 4.10.0, 4.11.0 and 4.12.0 the loop is the plain update x &larr; (x<sub>0</sub> &minus; &Delta;)/c with no &alpha;, no step rejection: the EPS flag only stops it early; (2) the damping arrived on the 4.x branch with opencv/opencv PR #27993 (A. Kretz, merged 2025-11-21, fixing issue #27916, "the fixed-point iterations do not converge"), and is in tag 4.13.0 (2025-12-30); its description does not say the error, and so the damping, exists only when the criterion has the EPS flag; milestone 3zg did not cite either and should have; (3) ORB-SLAM2 (`raulmur/ORB_SLAM2@f2e6f51`, `src/Frame.cc`) and ORB-SLAM3 (`UZ-SLAMLab/ORB_SLAM3@4452a3c`) call `cv::undistortPoints` with the default criterion, on float32 points, both for every keypoint and for the four image corners that set the frame's grid bounds `mnMinX..mnMaxY`; ROS `image_geometry` (`ros-perception/vision_opencv@a56ad9a`, rolling) calls it with the default criterion in `rectifyPoint` (C++) and `rectifyPoint` (Python); (4) Kornia's `undistort_points` (`kornia/kornia@6650da5`; pip 0.9.2) is the same undamped update for `num_iters` steps, default 5, and its docstring already warns of a two-point cycle (kornia issue #4285); (5) VINS-Mono's camodocs `PinholeCamera::liftProjective` (`HKUST-Aerial-Robotics/VINS-Mono@90dabb5`) runs a different loop, 8 steps of x &larr; x<sub>d</sub> &minus; D(x), D the additive distortion (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, p<sub>1</sub>, p<sub>2</sub>).

- **Theory for (5), to be tested.** Ignoring tangential terms, the additive map's Jacobian at the fixed point has eigenvalues 1 &minus; &rho;&prime;(r) along the ray and 1 &minus; c(r) across it (&rho; = rc). For a barrel model that does not fold (0 &lt; &rho;&prime; &le; 1, 0 &lt; c &le; 1) both lie in [0, 1): the additive loop cannot diverge where OpenCV's quotient loop (slope &minus;rc&prime;/c) can, but converges at rate about 1 &minus; &rho;&prime;, slow near a fold.
- **Run.** One probe script per OpenCV wheel (opencv-python-headless 4.5.5.64, 4.10.0.84, 4.12.0.88, 4.13.0.92, 4.14.0.94, 5.1.0.95; 4.5.4, the Ubuntu 22.04 / ROS 2 Humble version, has no wheel for this Python, and 4.2.0, ROS Noetic's, is checked by source only) writes each release's normalised output at the four corners of all 179 distinct calibrations under milestone 3zg's 13 criteria; `analyse.py` compares them with 3zg's port (`experiments/m3zg_loop/loop.py`) with and without damping.
- **H310 (no release before 4.13 damps).** For 4.5.5, 4.10.0 and 4.12.0, under every criterion, every corner output equals the port with `damping=False` within 10<sup>&minus;9</sup>.
- **H311 (4.13 and later do, with EPS only).** For 4.13.0 and 4.14.0, under every criterion, every corner output equals the damped port within 10<sup>&minus;9</sup>.
- **H312 (so 3zg's advice fails before 4.13).** Under 4.12.0 with COUNT|EPS (200, 10<sup>&minus;12</sup>), at least 50 of the 676 Duckiebot corners are off by more than 1 px (3zg: 53 corners have spectral radius &ge; 1).
- **H313 (Kornia is OpenCV's undamped loop).** Kornia 0.9.2 `undistort_points` in float64 with `num_iters` 5 and 200 equals OpenCV 5.1.0's COUNT-only output at the same count within 10<sup>&minus;9</sup> px at every corner of the 179 calibrations.
- **H314 (VINS's additive loop is stable on non-folding lenses).** At every corner of the 177 calibrations that do not fold inside their image, the 2-D spectral radius of the additive map at the converged point (OpenCV 5.1.0 COUNT|EPS 200) is below 1, and the additive loop run for 200 steps is within 0.01 px.
- **H315 (but its 8 steps are slow).** With VINS's own 8 steps, the Duckiebots' median worst-corner error is above 0.5 px and below the OpenCV default's 10.2 px (3zf).
- **H316 (ORB-SLAM's grid bounds move).** With ORB-SLAM's call (default criterion, float32, corners at (0,0), (cols,0), (0,rows), (cols,rows)) at least 20% of the 169 Duckiebots have one of the four bounds more than 5 px from the converged bounds (COUNT|EPS 200, OpenCV 5.1.0).
- **Reported, unscored.** Per release, the number of Duckiebot corners over 1 px under the default, COUNT-only 200 and COUNT|EPS 200 criteria; for ORB-SLAM, the share of a 4-px grid of each Duckiebot image whose default-undistorted point falls outside the default bounds (dropped from the frame grid, `Frame::PosInGrid`) against the converged-both case; the iterations the additive loop needs to reach 0.01 px; a figure.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Wheels, not distro builds (the loop is plain C++ with no SIMD dispatch, so a build difference is not expected, but it is not tested); 4.2.0 and 4.5.4 by source only; ORB-SLAM and VINS are read, not run, so their loops are ports; the Duckiebot lens dominates the corpus.

## 2026-11-24 · Milestone 3zg result: OpenCV's `undistortPoints` damps its iteration only when the criterion carries the EPS flag; without it the loop is plain fixed-point iteration, which cannot converge where its 2-D slope reaches 1, as it does at a corner of 35 of 169 Duckiebots; a NumPy port reproduces OpenCV 5.1.0 bit for bit; four of five predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3zg_loop/loop.py` ports `undistortPointsInternal` (OpenCV 5.1.0, `modules/geometry/src/pinhole.cpp`, five-term model); `experiments/m3zg_loop/analyse.py` runs it and OpenCV on milestone 3zf's 179 distinct calibrations, about 6 s. `tests/test_m3zg.py` (11 tests: the port against OpenCV on two lenses and four criteria, including EPS-only; the damping ablation; the radial slope against the Jacobian on a pure-radial model; the fold case of the radius solver) passed before the write-up.
- **H305 held, exactly.** At the four corners of all 179 calibrations under all 13 criteria the port's output equals OpenCV's: the largest difference is **0.0**.
- **H308 held.** The port with EPS and &epsilon; = 0 matches OpenCV's COUNT|EPS (10<sup>&minus;12</sup>) at 200 iterations within 4 &times; 10<sup>&minus;15</sup>; the port with the step rejection removed but the error still computed matches COUNT-only exactly. So the damping is the whole difference between the two criterion types, and it runs only when the EPS flag is set: OpenCV's default (COUNT, 5, 0.01) never damps. With EPS set, a Duckiebot corner needed a median 1 rejected step (at most 3) in 200 iterations.
- **H306 refuted as stated.** Of the 58 Duckiebot corners (of 676) whose COUNT-only 200-iteration round trip is over 1 px, 48 have the radial slope |&lambda;| &ge; 1 and 10 have 0.86&ndash;0.998; two corners with |&lambda;| &lt; 0.9 are not within 0.01 px (`bamboo17`, 9.8 px at |&lambda;| = 0.86; `nikola`, 0.07 px at 0.89). Tracing `bamboo17`'s corner showed why: its iterates swing between r = 1.66 and 1.80 around the fixed point at 1.70, and its tangential terms (p<sub>1</sub> = &minus;0.007, p<sub>2</sub> = 0.012 at r &asymp; 1.7) are not small, so a radial slope is the wrong quantity.
- **Post hoc, the 2-D slope (added after the first run, before the write-up).** The spectral radius &rho; of the full map's 2&times;2 Jacobian (tangential terms included) at each corner's true undistorted point (OpenCV's COUNT|EPS 200 output) separates the corners completely: **all 53 corners with &rho; &ge; 1 stall over 1 px**, the other 5 stalled corners have &rho; = 0.990&ndash;0.999 (0.99<sup>200</sup> &asymp; 0.13: too slow, not unstable), and **all 569 with &rho; &lt; 0.9 are within 0.01 px**; 54 lie in [0.9, 1). 35 of 169 Duckiebots have a corner with &rho; &ge; 1. Spearman of the largest &rho; with the default error 0.89, with the COUNT-only 200 error 0.92.
- **H307 held.** Spearman between the largest radial |&lambda;| and the default-criterion worst-corner error: **0.86** (milestone 3zf's fold slope: &minus;0.15).
- **H309 held.** On a 4-px grid of each Duckiebot image the default criterion is off by more than 1 px on a median **5.7%** of pixels (IQR 1.0&ndash;14.4%, worst 23.6%) and by more than 0.5 px on 18.7% (IQR 9.8&ndash;23.9%); the median pixel is off by 0.023 px; the smallest radius with a 1 px error is a median 279 px from the principal point (IQR 264&ndash;371 px; two robots never reach 1 px). `loop.png`, right: the error grows in a ring outward and changes sign through dark bands where an oscillating iterate happens to land near the answer.
- **Unscored.** The negative-1/c(r) branch, which returns the *distorted* point as the undistorted one, fired at 6 corners, all of the two calibrations that fold (KITTI raw camera 3 via Apollo, Duckiedrone `dd24`); no Duckiebot reached it. On a rejected step the loop overwrites its previous error with the rejected one; with at most 3 rejections per corner it made no visible difference here.
- **Reading.** The stall is not a property of the lens being "close to folding" (the fold is where the 1-D slope reaches +1; here the 2-D slope reaches &minus;1, an overshoot), which is why milestone 3zf's fold slope did not track the error. OpenCV has the cure built in, a step-halving rule, but gates it behind the EPS flag, so a caller who passes only an iteration count, or relies on the default, gets the undamped loop. Advice for practitioners: always pass `criteria=(cv2.TERM_CRITERIA_COUNT | cv2.TERM_CRITERIA_EPS, n, eps)`; a larger count alone does not help a corner with &rho; &ge; 1. The default's harm on the Duckiebot lens is confined to the outer ring (about 6% of pixels over 1 px), so for features near the centre it is negligible.
- **Caveat.** One OpenCV version (the 4.x branch carries the same loop at `modules/calib3d/src/undistort.dispatch.cpp`, not checked line by line); the 2-D criterion is post hoc and its fixed point is OpenCV's own converged output; a uniform pixel grid is a proxy for a front end's features; one lens design dominates.

## 2026-11-24 · Milestone 3zg planned: why does OpenCV's `undistortPoints` stall with a COUNT-only criterion and converge with COUNT|EPS? The loop in OpenCV 5.1.0's source, ported and tested on milestone 3zf's 179 calibrations

Written before `experiments/m3zg_loop/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3zf found that OpenCV's default criterion leaves Duckiebot image corners a median 10.2 px off, that COUNT-only iteration still leaves 40 of 169 over 1 px at 200 iterations, and that the same counts with the EPS flag set reach 0.01 px; it did not trace why. **Seen before this plan:** the source of `undistortPointsInternal` in `modules/geometry/src/pinhole.cpp` at tag 5.1.0 (commit `dd8d9c3833e6fbda076660cfbf2ae45611723481`, read through `git`). It is a damped fixed-point iteration x &larr; (1 &minus; &alpha;)x + &alpha;(x<sub>0</sub> &minus; &Delta;(x))/c(r), &alpha; = 1 at start; &alpha; is halved (and the step rejected) only when the reprojection error grew, and that error is computed only when the criterion carries the EPS flag. Without it the error stays at `DBL_MAX`, no step is ever rejected and the loop is the plain undamped iteration. A negative 1/c(r) makes the loop break and return the *distorted* coordinates. On a rejected step the previous error is overwritten by the rejected one. No calibration was run through any of this; no contraction factor, error map or count was computed.

- **Reading of the source, to be tested.** Ignoring tangential terms, the undamped map is g(r) = r<sub>d</sub>/c(r) along the ray, with c(r) = 1 + k<sub>1</sub>r<sup>2</sup> + k<sub>2</sub>r<sup>4</sup> + k<sub>3</sub>r<sup>6</sup>. At its fixed point r* (the true undistorted radius) its slope is &lambda; = &minus;r*c&prime;(r*)/c(r*). The undamped iteration converges locally iff |&lambda;| &lt; 1, the error shrinking by about |&lambda;| per step; for &lambda; &lt; &minus;1 it cannot converge. Note &lambda; is not the fold: the fold is &lambda; = +1 (d&rho;/dr = c(1 &minus; &lambda;) = 0), so a model far from folding can still have &lambda; &lt; &minus;1 where k<sub>2</sub>'s term dominates c&prime;.
- **Port.** `experiments/m3zg_loop/loop.py`: a NumPy line-for-line port of the loop for the five-term model (no tilt, rational or thin-prism terms), with the same criterion semantics.
- **H305 (the port is OpenCV's loop).** At the four corner pixels of all 179 distinct calibrations and under the 13 criteria of milestone 3zf (default; COUNT-only at 5, 10, 20, 50, 100, 200; COUNT|EPS with &epsilon; = 10<sup>&minus;12</sup> at the same counts), the port's normalised output agrees with `cv2.undistortPoints` within 10<sup>&minus;9</sup> on every coordinate.
- **H306 (the COUNT-only stall is the undamped iteration's instability).** For each Duckiebot corner compute &lambda; at its true undistorted point (bisection of the radial model, the corner's radius). Every corner whose COUNT-only 200-iteration round trip is off by more than 1 px has |&lambda;| &ge; 1; every corner with |&lambda;| &lt; 0.9 is within 0.01 px.
- **H307 (&lambda; predicts the default error).** Over the 169 Duckiebots, the Spearman correlation between the largest |&lambda;| over a calibration's four corners and its default-criterion worst-corner error is at least 0.8 (milestone 3zf: &minus;0.15 for the fold slope).
- **H308 (the damping is what fixes it).** Setting the EPS flag with &epsilon; = 0 in the port (error computed, never stopping early) gives the same outputs as COUNT|EPS with 10<sup>&minus;12</sup> in OpenCV within 10<sup>&minus;9</sup> at 200 iterations, and the port with the damping rule removed but the error still computed matches COUNT-only.
- **H309 (corners are the worst case, and most of the image is fine).** On a 4-pixel grid of every Duckiebot image, the share of pixels whose default-criterion round trip is off by more than 1 px has a fleet median below 10%.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per calibration: the largest |&lambda;| inside the image and the radius where |&lambda;| crosses 1; how many corners end in the negative-1/c break; the default error map's share over 0.5 and 1 px; the iterations an undamped loop needs, log(tol/e<sub>0</sub>)/log|&lambda;|, against the observed; `loop.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** One OpenCV version (5.1.0); &lambda; ignores tangential terms (the port and OpenCV use them); a uniform pixel grid is a proxy for a front end's features, which crowd where the texture is; the Duckietown lens dominates the corpus.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3zf result: none of 169 distinct Duckietown robot calibrations folds inside its image, but OpenCV's default `undistortPoints` misses their image corners by a median 10.2 px (164 of 169 over 1 px, worst 526 px); a 200-iteration criterion with the EPS flag closes every one to 0.01 px; KITTI's raw camera 3 (as shipped in Apollo) and a Duckiedrone, both three-term, do fold; two of six predictions held, one untestable

- **Run.** `experiments/m3zf_ros/fetch.py` read 198 files at three pinned commits (duckietown/duckiefleet, duckietown/dt-core, ApolloAuto/apollo) through `git`; `experiments/m3zf_ros/analyse.py` (OpenCV 5.1.0) as planned, seconds. `tests/test_m3zf.py` (2 tests: the three camera_info layouts, including a `roi` block whose `width: 0` must not be read as the image width; the eight-term guard and duplicate merging) passed before the first run. **Corpus:** 198 cameras, 10 with all distortion zero (nuScenes, KITTI-rectified and one Apollo long-range camera), **179 distinct**: 169 Duckiebots (640&times;480; 4 robots carry identical numbers in both Duckietown sets), 9 Apollo, 1 Duckiedrone (480&times;640).
- **H299 refuted.** **0 of 169** Duckiebot calibrations fold inside the image; none folds at all. The fleet sits in a tight band (median f 335 px, IQR 323&ndash;342; k<sub>1</sub> &minus;0.284, IQR &minus;0.300 to &minus;0.269; k<sub>2</sub> 0.060, IQR 0.050&ndash;0.072), above the line where a two-term model at the fleet's f would fold (`fleet.png`, left), with the smallest slope d&rho;/dx inside the image a median 0.39 (IQR 0.35&ndash;0.44). Five (3%) are plainly broken (f more than 20% from the median; worst f = 11,454 px, k<sub>1</sub> = &minus;5.36, k<sub>2</sub> = 44.3) and are still monotone.
- **H300 held.** All 169 have k<sub>3</sub> = 0 (ROS `camera_calibration`'s default).
- **H301 untestable.** No Duckiebot folds.
- **H302 refuted, by a lot.** Of 177 distinct calibrations that do not fold inside their image, **171 miss the 0.5 px bound** with OpenCV's default criterion; Duckiebots: median worst-corner error **10.2 px** (IQR 4.9&ndash;26.2), 164 of 169 over 1 px, worst **526 px**; the fleet's median model 5.4 px; Apollo's 1920&times;1080 cameras 0.8&ndash;1.1 px. With milestone 3ze's 100-iteration criterion (COUNT|EPS, 10<sup>&minus;12</sup>) every Duckiebot is within 0.35 px.
- **H303 held.** The fleet's component-wise median model never folds (corner 406 px).
- **H304 refuted.** **1 of 9** Apollo calibrations folds inside its image: `kitti_140/camera_03` (KITTI's raw-data colour camera, three terms, k<sub>3</sub> = &minus;0.054) at **748.2 px** against a 753.4 px corner, 2 corners beyond; its default round trip is off by 9.1 px and by 4.0 px at 200 iterations. The other three KITTI cameras fold 23&ndash;71 px outside their corners.
- **Unscored.** The Duckiedrone `dd24` (three terms, k<sub>3</sub> = &minus;0.041) folds at **273.6 px** against a 413.7 px corner, all four corners beyond; round trip off by about 146 px at 5, 100 and 200 iterations. So both folds in this corpus are three-term models, not two-term ones (milestone 3ze's H296 would have failed here).
- **Post hoc (added after the first run, before any write-up).** (1) OpenCV's default equals an explicit (COUNT, 5, 0.01) criterion on every calibration. (2) Raising the count alone does not fix it: COUNT-only at 10, 20, 50, 100, 200 iterations leaves 115, 74, 49, 46, 40 of 169 Duckiebots over 1 px, the worst stuck near 159 px (the median does converge: 3.3, 0.38, 0.0006 px). (3) The same counts with the EPS flag set (eps 10<sup>&minus;12</sup>, so only the count stops it) follow different iterates in OpenCV 5.1.0: worst 26.8, 13.8, 3.3, 0.35 and **0.0094 px**; at 200 every non-folding calibration in the corpus is within 0.01 px while the two folding ones stay at 4.0 and 146 px. One example, `bamboo17`: one corner at 139 px from 20 COUNT-only iterations on, 0.000 px with the EPS flag by 20. Why the two types differ was not traced in OpenCV's source. (4) The default error barely tracks how close a model comes to folding (Spearman &minus;0.15 against the smallest slope).
- **Reading.** User calibrations of a 160&deg; lens with ROS's two-term default do not fold: the fold is not what goes wrong in this fleet. What does go wrong is the undistortion call: `cv2.undistortPoints` with its default criterion is off by pixels to hundreds of pixels at the corners of healthy wide-lens models, silently. That corrects milestone 3ze's advice: a default-criterion corner round trip is a fold test only for mild lenses (3ze's corpus, 0.13 px); on a wide lens it fails healthy models too. The fix is one argument: `criteria=(cv2.TERM_CRITERIA_COUNT | cv2.TERM_CRITERIA_EPS, 200, 1e-12)`, after which the round trip separates the two folding models (4 and 146 px) from 177 healthy ones (at most 0.01 px). The folds that do occur here are in three-term models, including one of KITTI's published raw-data cameras, so "add k<sub>3</sub>" is not a cure either: check the fold radius whatever the term count.
- **Caveat.** Corner errors are the worst case; features nearer the centre converge faster, and how much of a real front end's feature set is affected was not measured. Duckietown is a teaching fleet with one lens design, and its repository's files are what students committed, five of them plainly broken; the KITTI numbers are Apollo's copy, not checked against KITTI's own file (cvlibs.net was not probed); one OpenCV version (5.1.0); the mechanism behind the COUNT-only stall is not established.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3zf planned: do the calibrations a real robot fleet runs with fold inside the image? 177 Duckietown robot calibrations (one 160&deg; lens, ROS's two-term fit) and 21 Baidu Apollo vehicle cameras

Written before `experiments/m3zf_ros/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3ze found none of 14 calibrations shipped with open-source SLAM/VIO systems folding inside its image and narrowed Recommendation 9 to "a guard for one's own two-term fits of strongly distorted lenses". Those were dataset calibrations made by experts. The population the narrowed advice is about is different: wide lenses calibrated by their users with ROS's `camera_calibration`, which by default fits k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, p<sub>1</sub>, p<sub>2</sub> and fixes k<sub>3</sub> = 0. Duckietown publishes exactly that: one intrinsic calibration per Duckiebot (a Raspberry Pi camera behind a 160&deg; lens, 640&times;480), 126 in `duckietown/duckiefleet` (2016&ndash;2018) and 50 in the older baseline set kept in `duckietown/dt-core`, plus one Duckiedrone. Baidu Apollo ships 21 vehicle camera intrinsics (1392&times;512 to 1920&times;1080, KITTI and nuScenes among them). `experiments/m3zf_ros/fetch.py` read all 198 files at pinned commits through `git` (the GitHub API and codeload refuse this session; git and raw.githubusercontent.com pass), keeping only the numbers with each file's URL and SHA-256. **Seen before this plan:** the file formats, and in passing the coefficients of four files: Duckiebot `a313` (f &asymp; 335 px, k<sub>1</sub> = &minus;0.298, k<sub>2</sub> = 0.073, k<sub>3</sub> = 0), Duckiedrone `dd24` (k<sub>1</sub> = &minus;0.354, k<sub>2</sub> = 0.164), Apollo's `front_6mm` and `mkz_example` front cameras (k<sub>1</sub> &asymp; &minus;0.54, k<sub>2</sub> &asymp; 0.27, k<sub>3</sub> = 0) and its `short_camera` (k<sub>3</sub> = &minus;0.185); from the first and third I judged by hand that those two models have no real fold (the derivative's discriminant is negative). All 198 files parse; every one says `plumb_bob`; 176 Duckiebot files are 640&times;480 with five coefficients; 19 Apollo files carry eight (the analysis refuses any with a non-zero sixth to eighth term). No fold radius, round trip, fleet statistic or count was computed.

- **Corpus.** Every file. Calibrations with all distortion terms zero are counted and dropped; the rest are merged into distinct calibrations when their K, D and size agree to 6 significant figures (the two Duckietown sets share robot names), and the sources listed. Denominators are distinct calibrations: Duckiebot (the 640&times;480 robots of both Duckietown sets), Apollo, and all.
- **Fold and round trip.** As milestone 3ze: `src/foldcheck.py`'s `check` (radial terms; image corners), and OpenCV's `undistortPoints` (default criteria; also 100 iterations) then `projectPoints` at the four corner pixels, the error being the largest pixel distance over the four.
- **H299 (user two-term fits of a wide lens fold).** At least 10% of the distinct Duckiebot calibrations fold inside their own image.
- **H300 (they are two-term fits).** Every distinct Duckiebot calibration has k<sub>3</sub> = 0.
- **H301 (undistortion fails silently beyond a fold; 3ze's H297 on a real fleet).** Every Duckiebot calibration that folds inside its image has a default-criteria round trip off by more than 1 px at some corner.
- **H302 (and only there; 3ze's H298 on stronger lenses).** Every distinct calibration in the corpus that does not fold inside its image has a default-criteria round trip within 0.5 px at every corner.
- **H303 (a fleet prior is safe).** The Duckiebot fleet's component-wise median model (median f<sub>x</sub>, f<sub>y</sub>, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, k<sub>3</sub>) does not fold inside the image.
- **H304 (car lenses do not fold).** No distinct non-zero Apollo calibration folds inside its image.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per calibration: source(s), f, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, k<sub>3</sub>, fold and corner radius, corners beyond the fold, both round trips, the smallest slope d&rho;/dx inside the image (3ze's diagnostic); the fleet's spread (median and IQR of f, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>); `fleet.png`: the Duckiebots' (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) against the region where a two-term model folds inside a 640&times;480 image.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Duckietown is a teaching fleet: calibrations made by students with one tool and one checkerboard procedure, many probably hurried, so the rate is not that of an engineered product; one lens design dominates the corpus; Apollo's files include sample and placeholder configurations; a fold says the model is wrong past it, not where the lens really is; tangential terms are ignored by the fold check (the round trip uses them).

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3ze result: none of the 14 distinct pinhole calibrations shipped with ORB-SLAM2/3, VINS-Mono/Fusion, OpenVINS and Kimera-VIO folds inside its own image, and OpenCV's corner round trip is within 0.13 px on all of them; on the project's folding Kinect fit the same round trip is off by up to 38 px (67 px with 100 iterations); one of four predictions held, two untestable

- **Run.** `experiments/m3ze_fleet/fetch.py` read 55 example configuration files (all reachable) from raw.githubusercontent.com, keeping only the numbers with each file's URL and SHA-256 (`calibs.json`); `experiments/m3ze_fleet/analyse.py` (OpenCV 5.1.0) ran the fold check and the round trip as planned; about 2 s. `tests/test_m3ze.py` (3 tests: the three config formats, including Kalibr's block-style lists, which the first parse crashed on before any figure was computed; duplicate merging; the round trip on a monotone and a folding model) passes.
- **Corpus.** 70 cameras: 46 radial-tangential, 14 equidistant, 6 Kannala&ndash;Brandt, 4 "Rectified" (ORB-SLAM3 stereo). Of the 46, 15 have every distortion term zero (KITTI odometry, the RealSense D435i configs, TUM fr3, uHumans2), leaving 31, which merge into **14 distinct calibrations**: TUM fr1 and fr2 (three-term, as published by TUM), EuRoC's cam0 and cam1 (ASL's calibration, in four projects), VINS-Fusion's and VINS-Mono's own EuRoC recalibrations (three), KAIST Urban's stereo pair, OpenVINS's KAIST-VIO pair, its rpng_aruco pair and its RealSense D455 (rpng_plane and rs_d455 carry the same numbers).
- **H295 refuted.** **0 of 14** fold inside their image. Two fold at all (OpenVINS's KAIST-VIO pair, k<sub>1</sub> = 0.007, k<sub>2</sub> = &minus;0.009 to &minus;0.010), at 666 and 686 px against a 412&ndash;413 px corner; the other twelve never fold.
- **H296, H297 untestable.** With no folding calibration in the corpus there is nothing to test; `analyse.py` reports them as untestable (a change to the printed verdict only, made after the first run: its first version printed "refuted" for an empty set).
- **H298 held.** OpenCV's `undistortPoints` (default criteria) then `projectPoints` at the four corner pixels: worst **0.131 px** (TUM fr1), all others 0.085 px or less; with 100 iterations every one is below 10<sup>&minus;12</sup> px.
- **Unscored, post hoc.** (1) How close each model comes to folding: the smallest slope d&rho;/dx of the radial map over the rays that land inside the image (1 is no distortion, 0 a fold) is **0.51&ndash;0.56** for the seven EuRoC-family calibrations (the 752&times;480 MT9V034 with k<sub>1</sub> &asymp; &minus;0.29), 0.95&ndash;0.99 for KAIST, KAIST-VIO and the D455, and 1.00 for TUM fr1 and fr2 (k<sub>1</sub> &gt; 0, steepest at the centre). The planned figure (fold radius against corner radius) was empty with no folds, so `fleet.png` plots this slope instead. (2) The project's own folding model, milestone 3zd's two-term board fit of the TUM Kinect (fold 380.8 px, corner 415.7 px): the same OpenCV round trip is off by **3.7, 12.8, 33.4 and 37.6 px** at the four corners with default criteria and 3.6&ndash;66.8 px with 100 iterations; more iterations do not help because no ray in the model reaches those pixels. OpenCV raises no error in either case.
- **Reading.** Folding inside the frame is not what the calibrations that open-source SLAM ships look like: every one of the 14 is monotone to its corner, and the strongly distorted EuRoC lens (k<sub>1</sub> &asymp; &minus;0.29) keeps half its slope. TUM's published model of the very Kinect whose two-term board fit folds carries a third radial term and does not fold, so the dataset's authors avoided the trap this project's refit fell into. The practical advice therefore narrows: the fold check is a guard for one's own two-term fits of strongly distorted lenses, not a common defect of published models. And it gives a second, tool-native check that needs no new code: round-trip the four corner pixels through `cv2.undistortPoints` and `cv2.projectPoints`; on these 14 calibrations it is within 0.13 px, on the folding Kinect fit it is off by tens of pixels.
- **Caveat.** A convenience sample of 14 calibrations from five projects, several of them the same EuRoC sensor; the configs are what authors chose to ship, not deployed robots' calibrations; ORB-SLAM2's files give no image size, so their (duplicate) entries took the size of the matching ORB-SLAM3 file; the fold check ignores tangential terms (the round trip uses them); one OpenCV version (5.1.0); the post-hoc Kinect round trip is a single model.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3ze planned: do the published camera calibrations that ship with open-source SLAM and VIO systems fold inside their own image, and does OpenCV's undistortion fail silently at the corner when they do?

Written before `experiments/m3ze_fleet/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3zd found the Kinect's two-term board fit folding at 381 px, inside its own 416 px corner, and the 2026-11-23 paper step shipped `src/foldcheck.py` to catch that; the paper's Recommendation 9 now says "check the fold radius before holding a distortion". Whether that matters beyond one Kinect is open: if every calibration engineers actually run with is monotone to its corner, the check is a curiosity. The TUM host still refuses this session, but the example configuration files of five widely used open-source systems are reachable (raw.githubusercontent.com): ORB-SLAM2 and ORB-SLAM3 (`Examples/*/*.yaml`), VINS-Mono and VINS-Fusion (`config/*/*.yaml`), OpenVINS (`config/*/kalibr_imucam_chain.yaml`) and Kimera-VIO (`params/*/*CameraParams.yaml`). They carry the published calibrations of TUM RGB-D, EuRoC, KITTI, KAIST Urban, UZH-FPV, TUM-VI, RealSense units and the authors' own rigs. **Seen before this plan:** the files were downloaded and some coefficients read in passing while checking their formats (for example EuRoC's k<sub>1</sub> = &minus;0.283, KAIST's k<sub>1</sub> = &minus;0.056, k<sub>2</sub> = 0.140, TUM fr1's three-term model, which milestone 3zd already found monotone); no fold radius or round trip was computed for any of them.

- **Corpus.** Every pinhole camera with a radial-polynomial distortion (OpenCV's k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, p<sub>1</sub>, p<sub>2</sub>[, k<sub>3</sub>]; Kalibr's `radtan`) in the downloaded files; fisheye, Kannala&ndash;Brandt, equidistant and MEI models are excluded (another model, another fold condition) and counted. Duplicates (the same intrinsics, distortion and size in several files) are merged and their sources listed. Calibrations with all distortion terms zero (rectified images, KITTI odometry) are counted and reported but are not in the denominators below.
- **Fold.** `src/foldcheck.py`'s `check` on each distinct calibration (radial terms only; image corners at (0, 0) and (w, h)).
- **Round trip.** At each of the four corner pixels (0, 0), (w&minus;1, 0), (0, h&minus;1), (w&minus;1, h&minus;1): OpenCV's `cv2.undistortPoints` with the full distortion vector and its default criteria, then `cv2.projectPoints` back; the error is the pixel distance to the start. Reported per calibration as the maximum over the four corners; also with a 100-iteration criterion.
- **H295 (folding inside the frame is not a one-camera curiosity).** At least two distinct calibrations in the corpus fold inside their own image.
- **H296 (the folds are two-term fits).** Every calibration that folds inside its image has k<sub>3</sub> = 0.
- **H297 (undistortion fails silently beyond a fold).** For every calibration that folds inside its image, the corner round trip with OpenCV's default criteria is off by more than 1 px at some corner.
- **H298 (and only there).** For every calibration that does not fold inside its image, the corner round trip with OpenCV's default criteria is within 0.5 px at every corner.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per calibration: source(s), size, f, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, k<sub>3</sub>, fold radius and corner radius in px, corners beyond the fold, the round-trip errors at default and 100 iterations; `fleet.png` (fold radius against corner radius).
- **Caveat set in advance.** A convenience sample: the calibrations open-source authors chose to ship, not a random draw of deployed cameras; a fold says the model is wrong past it, not where the lens really is; the fold check ignores tangential terms (the round trip uses them); a configuration file can carry a calibration rounded or copied from a dataset's own, so the source listed is where the numbers were read, not who measured them.

## 2026-11-23 · Paper step and a fold check: Section 4 and Recommendation 9 carry milestones 3zc&ndash;3zd (no distortion gate; hold a distortion only if it does not fold inside the image), with `src/foldcheck.py`, a stdlib tool that finds a calibration's fold radius exactly and tests it against the image corners

- **Why now.** The README's next step after milestones 3zc and 3zd named this paper update, and 3zd's reading proposed a practical pre-check, "compute the held model's fold radius", that no code in the project exposed: milestone 3zd found folds by a dense scan inside its analysis script.
- **Tool.** `src/foldcheck.py` (stdlib only; CLI flags or an OpenCV-style JSON with K, D and the image size). The forward map &rho;(x) = x(1 + k<sub>1</sub>x<sup>2</sup> + k<sub>2</sub>x<sup>4</sup> + k<sub>3</sub>x<sup>6</sup>) folds at the first positive root of 1 + 3k<sub>1</sub>u + 5k<sub>2</sub>u<sup>2</sup> + 7k<sub>3</sub>u<sup>3</sup> (u = x<sup>2</sup>), found by splitting [0, &infin;) at the cubic's turning points and bisecting; the fold is compared with each image corner in normalised coordinates, so f<sub>x</sub> &ne; f<sub>y</sub> is handled. Exit 1 when it folds inside the image. Rational and thin-prism terms are refused; tangential terms are ignored. `tests/test_foldcheck.py` (5 tests): two closed forms (k<sub>2</sub> only; k<sub>1</sub> &lt; 0 only), monotone models, agreement with milestone 3zd's dense scan on all six references and on 300 random (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, k<sub>3</sub>) within 10<sup>&minus;4</sup>, and the CLI's three exit codes.
- **On the project's references** (640&times;480): the Kinect's board fit folds at **380.8 px** with the image corner at 415.7 px from its principal point (all four corners beyond the fold); the target-free anchor folds at **403.2 px** against a 419.1 px corner (two of four corners beyond); TUM's three-term fr1 model and all three Xtion models never fold.
- **Paper.** `paper/draft.md`: one new Section 4 paragraph (3zc: the bias is &minus;1.37 px on the Kinect and &minus;0.71 on the Xtion, the fleet-corrected gate still fires in 24 of 31 blocks, worst regret 1.00 against 0.48, the commissioning gate in 23 of 31, the oracle-corrected statistic's median 0.47 sd, the unscored 2-sd gate 5 of 31 and 0.67 px; 3zd: never updating leaves 17.3 px at the edge with the board's fr1 model held and 14.4 with the target-free anchor's, joint gate 4.36 against f-only gate 7.62 and minimax over the pairs, the control near-tie 3.94 against 4.03, f-only minimax in 7 of 9 Xtion pairs, the windows' pools fold inside 400 px in 0 of 31 blocks, and the fold-radius formula); one abstract sentence; Recommendation 9 extended (no distortion gate; check the fold radius before holding a distortion); Section 9 (one truth at the edge; a one-camera fleet); Section 10 (done item; still needed a three-term board refit of the Kinect frames, the feature radii, a real lens change). `paper/check_numbers.py`: 22 new checks (13 against the m3zc and m3zd `summary.json` files, 9 against verbatim LOG text); **437 of 437 pass**, and a planted 4.08 in place of 4.03 was caught. `paper/ral.md`: four sentences in Section VIII and Recommendation 9; `check_short.py` passes (211 distinct numbers, all in the draft; about 6.6 pages by its rule of thumb).
- **Caveat.** A write-up of logged results plus a tool, not a new experiment. The fold check says only that a model is wrong beyond its fold, not that it is right before it (milestone 3zd's caveat); it ignores the principal point's own error and tangential terms.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3zd result: at the image edge (250&ndash;400 px) "update f, hold the distortion" survives only when the held distortion is valid out to the corner; the Kinect's two-term target calibrations fold at 381 and 403 px and sit 14&ndash;17 px from TUM's three-term model at the edge, where adopting the windows' distortion (which folds inside the image in none of 31 blocks) cuts the error to 5.5 px; on the Xtion an f-only rule is minimax in 7 of 9 pairs; two of five predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3zd_edge/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (milestone 3zb's blocks, pools, six rules and score; truth TUM's calibration), as planned below; about 8 s. `tests/test_m3zd.py` (3 tests: the fold radius against the analytic peak of f x (1 + k<sub>2</sub>x<sup>4</sup>); P(r) is zero at the edge when the held model is the truth and the joint rule's dropped k<sub>3</sub> shows; the reference fold radii the plan quotes) passed before the first run. One code change after the first run, before reading any figure: the fr3-only table crashed because it passed the folding fr1 board model to the ray inversion; it now passes the fr3 references alone. **Check:** the inner band (50&ndash;250 px) reproduces milestone 3zb's truth-TUM figures exactly (control f-only gate(0.5) 2.00, joint gate(0.5) 1.49; held board 1.57 and 1.22; held target-free 1.51 and 1.03).
- **H290 refuted, narrowly.** Control T = H = TUM, edge band, est_c, K = 5: f-only gate(0.5) worst regret **4.03 px**, joint gate(0.5) **3.94**. Per camera the f-only gate wins on both (fr1 0.47 against 1.35; fr3 4.36 against 5.51), but the pooled median over the two cameras' blocks favours the joint gate by 0.09 px: a pooling effect, not a property of either camera.
- **H291 held.** Truth TUM, held board, edge band, &delta; = 0: never's median rms P over the 17 fr1 blocks is **17.3 px** (held target-free: 14.4). The board's fr1 model puts a ray TUM images at 400 px at 36.5 px less (median signed P, all blocks pooled); it is 0.3 px off at 225 px.
- **H292 held.** Truth TUM, held board, edge band: joint gate(0.5) **4.36 px** against f-only gate(0.5) **7.62**. On the fr1 blocks alone joint always is the best rule (0.00 regret; 5.52 px rms P at &delta; = 0 against f-only gate(0.5)'s 17.34), and two gates come within 0.93 px.
- **H293 refuted.** Maximum worst regret over the three pairs, edge band: **joint gate(0.5) 4.36 px**, joint always 5.92, two gates 6.16, f-only always 7.17, f-only gate(0.5) 7.62, never 27.8.
- **H294 held.** est_c, K = 5: the block pools' (f, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) fold inside 400 px in **0 of 31** blocks (smallest fold radius 464 px), although single windows fold from 390 px.
- **Unscored.** Full band 50&ndash;400 px: the same split (joint always minimax with the board or target-free distortion held, joint gate(0.5) 2.96 against f-only 3.06 in the control). The Xtion alone (fr3, edge band, every reference monotone): f-only always or f-only gate(0.5) is minimax in **7 of 9** (T, H) pairs and joint gate(0.5) in 2 (both with the target-free anchor as the truth); adopting the windows' distortion always costs 1.7&ndash;7.4 px of regret there. In the control the windows' distortion alone, with the truth's f, would leave &minus;1.4 px at 300 px and &minus;10.7 px at 400 px (joint always, signed median), so the windows extrapolate badly to the corner too; it is just that a folded held model extrapolates worse.
- **Reading.** The advice of milestones 3za&ndash;3zc, "update f behind its half-sd gate and keep the held distortion", carries to the image corner on a camera whose held distortion is valid there (the Xtion, and the Kinect when TUM's three-term model is both held and the truth), and fails where the held distortion is a two-term fit that folds near the corner: the Kinect's board and target-free calibrations, which agree with TUM's within half a pixel inside 250 px, sit 14&ndash;17 px from it across 250&ndash;400 px. There, adopting the windows' distortion cuts the fr1 edge error to about a third (17.3 to 5.5 px at &delta; = 0). A robot cannot tell which reference is right at the corner from these data, but it can tell whether its held model folds inside the image, from the coefficients alone. That gives a cheap pre-check before "hold the distortion": compute the held model's fold radius; if it is inside the image corner, the held distortion is not a safe default at the edge, and a target recalibration with a third radial term (or the windows' distortion, as a stopgap) is. A general lesson for practitioners: a calibration validated by its reprojection error inside the region its features covered says nothing about the corner, and two-term polynomial fits of a strongly distorted lens can fold inside the frame.
- **Caveat.** One truth at the edge, TUM's published fr1 model, whose k<sub>3</sub> the windows and the other references do not fit; which fr1 model is right beyond about 300 px is not known (a folded model is certainly wrong past its fold, but not necessarily right before it); the windows' feature radii are not in the saved solves, so their distortion at the corner may be an extrapolation; the pooled-median verdicts mix two cameras and flip on 0.09 px (H290); P ignores the principal point and tangential terms; three sequences, two cameras, overlapping blocks.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3zc result: correcting the windows' distortion bias does not make a distortion gate usable; the bias is camera-specific (&minus;1.37 px on the Kinect, &minus;0.71 on the Xtion at 200 px), a half-sd bar fires on noise alone, and even a 2-sd change detector that fires in 5 of 31 blocks costs more than holding the distortion; one of five predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3zc_biasgate/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (milestone 3zb's blocks, pools, references and P(r) score), as planned below; about 25 s. `tests/test_m3zc.py` (3 tests: the correction moves the test and not the adopted estimate; the commissioning gate is a change detector and has no gate without history; the leave-out fleet betas on a hand fleet) passed before the first run. **Deviation:** after the first run, and after reading H285&ndash;H289, two unscored rules were added with a 2-sd distortion bar (two gates LOUO c2, commissioning c2) and a diagnostic of the gate's test statistic; the pre-registered nine rules, their scores and the hypotheses' code are unchanged (minimax and H287 are taken over the nine only).
- **The betas.** est_c, median window D<sub>200</sub> minus board D<sub>200</sub>: a sequence's own &minus;1.37 (fr1/desk), &minus;1.36 (fr1/xyz), &minus;0.71 (fr3/stf) px. Leave one unit out gives each camera the *other* camera's bias, &minus;0.71 for fr1 and &minus;1.37 for fr3, so it under-corrects the Kinect by 0.65 px and over-corrects the Xtion by the same; leave one sequence out gives &minus;0.87 and &minus;0.91 on fr1.
- **H285 refuted.** Control T = H = board, &delta; = 0: the LOUO-corrected distortion gate fires in **24 of 31** blocks (uncorrected 30; LOSO 23; commissioning 23).
- **H286 refuted.** Control: two gates (LOUO) worst regret **1.00 px** against f-only gate(0.5)'s 0.48 (uncorrected 1.02).
- **H287 refuted.** Maximum worst regret over the 9 pairs: f-only always and f-only gate(0.5) **2.00 px** (tied, at truth = held = TUM), two gates (LOUO) **2.20**, the uncorrected, LOSO and commissioning two gates 2.41, joint always 2.50.
- **H288 refuted.** The commissioning gate fires in **23 of 31** blocks at &delta; = 0 in every pair (it compares the block's D<sub>200</sub> with the unit's history median, so it does not depend on T or H).
- **H289 held.** Sweep &epsilon; = &minus;50% and +50%: the LOUO gate fires in **29** and **27** of 31 blocks.
- **Why it still fires (unscored diagnostic, est_c, K = 5, control).** |test statistic| / sd<sub>D</sub> per block: uncorrected median **5.4**; LOUO **2.3**; commissioning **0.80**; against the sequence's own median D<sub>200</sub> (an oracle correction no robot has) **0.47**. So even with the bias removed exactly, a half-sd bar sits at the median of the noise and fires in about half the blocks; for at most a third the bar would need 0.85 sd with the oracle correction, 1.4 with commissioning, 3.0 with LOUO and 6.4 uncorrected. Within a sequence the block pools' D<sub>200</sub> spread (sd 0.18, 0.36, 0.31 px) matches their pooled sd<sub>D</sub> (median 0.20, 0.33, 0.46 px), so the bar is about right for noise; it is the 0.5 multiplier, chosen for f in milestone 3w, that is wrong for the distortion.
- **Post hoc, unscored (2-sd distortion bar).** The commissioning change detector at 2 sd fires in **5 of 31** blocks in every pair, yet its control worst regret is **0.67 px** against f-only gate(0.5)'s 0.48, and at truth = TUM, held board or TUM, it is 2.01 and 2.00 px against the uncorrected two gates' 1.22 and 1.49; LOUO at 2 sd fires in 11&ndash;20 blocks and leaves 0.99 px in the control. Over the 9 pairs the maximum worst regret is 2.01 px (commissioning, 2 sd) and 1.96 px (LOUO, 2 sd) against f-only gate(0.5)'s 2.00: a 0.04 px edge for LOUO at 2 sd, bought with twice f-only gate(0.5)'s regret in the control (0.99 against 0.48).
- **Reading.** Milestone 3zb's suggestion, a bias-corrected D<sub>200</sub> before a distortion gate, does not rescue the gate. The windows' distortion bias is a property of the camera (or of its scene), not of the method, so a fleet correction from another camera moves it by the wrong amount; a commissioning correction removes it but leaves a half-sd test that fires on noise; and when the bar is widened until the gate fires rarely, the blocks where it does fire still cost more than they repair, because adopting the windows' k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> carries their 2 px ray error inside 250 px. The practical advice of milestones 3y&ndash;3zb gets stronger: update f behind its half-sd gate and keep the held distortion; do not gate the distortion on self-calibrated D<sub>200</sub> at all. A distortion check, if wanted, should be an alarm that triggers a target recalibration, not an update. A general lesson for gated updates: a gate's multiplier has to be set per quantity from what adopting a wrong value costs, not carried over from another parameter.
- **Caveat.** Two cameras, so the leave-one-unit-out fleet is a single other camera of a different make; the bias is measured against one board calibration per camera; the 2-sd rules were added after reading the results and are unscored; "wrong" distortions are other real calibrations or scaled ones, not a lens change; the band stops at 250 px; P ignores the principal point and tangential terms; overlapping blocks.
