A fleet correction (the bias measured on other cameras' board calibrations) and a commissioning correction (the bias measured on the unit's own early windows against the calibration it was commissioned with), scored as in milestone 3zb

Written before `experiments/m3zc_biasgate/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3zb's two-gate rule adopted the windows' k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> when their pooled D<sub>200</sub> differed from the held model's by more than half its sd; it fired in 30 of 31 blocks even when the held distortion was the truth, because the windows' D<sub>200</sub> sits 0.6&ndash;1.7 px below every reference (milestone 3y). The README's next step after the 2026-11-23 paper step asks for "a bias-corrected D<sub>200</sub> before any distortion gate". The TUM host still refuses this session, so this re-uses milestone 3s's saved solves and milestone 3zb's blocks, pools, references and score; nothing is re-solved. **Seen before this plan:** milestones 3y&ndash;3zb's logged figures (3y: D<sub>200</sub> 0.6&ndash;1.7 px low in every sequence; 3zb: two gates 1.02 px worst regret in the control against f-only gate(0.5)'s 0.48, the distortion gate firing in 30 of 31 blocks, joint gate(0.5) beating f-only gate(0.5) in 4 of 6 cross pairs). No figure for any corrected D<sub>200</sub>.

- **Corrections.** The distortion gate compares D<sub>200</sub>(pool) &minus; &beta; with D<sub>200</sub>(held), with the bar of 3zb (0.5 sd<sub>D</sub>, the scatter-only floored pool). Three choices of &beta;: **fleet, leave one unit out (primary)**: the median of (window D<sub>200</sub> &minus; board-reference D<sub>200</sub>) over the scored windows of the *other* camera (a robot whose own camera has never been characterised); **fleet, leave one sequence out**: the same over the scored windows of the other two sequences (one of them the same camera, for fr1); **commissioning**: the median of (window D<sub>200</sub> &minus; D<sub>200</sub> of the commissioning model) over the unit's own history windows before the block (the same history milestone 3u's floor uses, at least 5 windows; no gate otherwise), where the commissioning model is the held distortion with the truth's f (&delta; = 0: staleness comes after commissioning). The commissioning gate is a change detector; it cannot see a held distortion that was wrong from the start, by construction.
- **Rules.** 3zb's six, plus three: **two gates (fleet LOUO)**, **two gates (fleet LOSO)**, **two gates (commissioning)**. On firing the windows' k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> are adopted uncorrected and k<sub>3</sub> dropped, as in 3zb (the correction moves the test, not the estimate).
- **Score.** 3zb's, unchanged: rms P(r) over r &isin; {50, 75, &hellip;, 250} px against the truth T; median over blocks; regret against the better of never and joint always; worst regret over &delta; &isin; &plusmn;{0, 0.5, &hellip;, 10}%. 9 (T, H) pairs over {board, TUM, target-free}, and 3zb's sweep (T = board, held k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> &times; (1 + &epsilon;)). Primary cell est_c, K = 5.
- **H285 (the fleet correction stops the spurious firing).** Control T = H = board, &delta; = 0: the LOUO-corrected distortion gate fires in at most 10 of the blocks (3zb: 30 of 31).
- **H286 (the corrected gate costs little when the distortion is right).** Control T = H = board: two gates (LOUO)'s worst regret is at most f-only gate(0.5)'s plus 0.3 px (3zb's H283, refuted at 1.02 against 0.48).
- **H287 (the corrected two gates are minimax).** Over the 9 (T, H) pairs, two gates (LOUO) has the smallest maximum worst regret of the nine rules.
- **H288 (the commissioning change detector stays quiet).** At &delta; = 0, the commissioning-corrected distortion gate fires in at most 5 blocks in each of the 9 pairs.
- **H289 (the fleet-corrected gate still sees a large error).** Sweep, &epsilon; = &minus;50% and +50%: the LOUO-corrected distortion gate fires in at least two thirds of the blocks at &delta; = 0, on both signs.
- **Reported, unscored.** Each &beta; (per sequence, per correction); firing counts for every gated rule and pair; worst and mean regret for all nine rules, every model and K; the sweep curves; `biasgate.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Two cameras only, so the LOUO fleet is one other camera of a different make; the bias is taken against the board references, which are themselves one calibration each; "wrong" distortions are other real calibrations or scaled ones, not a lens change; the band stops at 250 px; P ignores the principal point and tangential terms; overlapping blocks.

## 2026-11-23 · Paper step: Section 4 of the draft now carries milestones 3y&ndash;3zb (the update rule carried to c<sub>y</sub> but not to c<sub>x</sub> or the distortion; scoring by where rays land; a held distortion beats adopting the windows' even when off by 50%), with Recommendation 9 and 38 new number checks (415 of 415 pass)

- **Why now.** The README's next step after each of milestones 3y, 3z, 3za and 3zb named this paper update, and four milestones had landed since the last paper step (2026-11-21). The draft's Section 4 stopped at 3x, so its practical advice covered f only.
- **What changed.** `paper/draft.md`: one new paragraph in Section 4 (3y: gate(0.5) minimax for f with a scatter-only bar, 0.38, and for c<sub>y</sub>, 2.04 px; never for c<sub>x</sub>, 2.86 against 7.14, and D<sub>200</sub>, 0.92 against 1.08; 3z: the Kinect's rays within 1 px of the board to 250 px at the median, the Xtion's 2.4&ndash;2.8% too far out, the solver's bar about nine times too narrow, the board's fr1 fold at 381 px; 3za: f-only gate(0.5) minimax at 0.48 px against 0.63, the choice worth under about 1 px and flipping by sequence; 3zb: a held distortion off by 50% leaves 1.51 px against 2.09 for adopting the windows' f and distortion, and a second gate on D<sub>200</sub> fires in 30 of 31 blocks). Abstract: one sentence. Section 8: Recommendation 9 (self-calibrate f and c<sub>y</sub>, hold c<sub>x</sub> and the distortion, score by P(r), check a reference for monotonicity). Section 9: the scatter-only bars, the 250 px band, the unmeasured feature radii and the simulated "wrong" distortion as limits. Section 10: a done item and what is still needed (a bias-corrected D<sub>200</sub>, the feature radii, the image edge, a real lens change).
- **Checks.** `paper/check_numbers.py`: 38 new checks (24 against the `summary.json` files of m3y, m3z, m3za and m3zb, 14 against verbatim LOG text); 415 of 415 pass, and a planted 9.5 in place of 9.4 was caught. Three wordings were tightened while checking: "the references' distortions place rays within 0.21&ndash;0.46 px of each other" became what the log measured (the held distortion with the right f leaves 0.21&ndash;0.46 px rms); the two-gate rule's 1.02 px is a worst regret, not a cost; "adopting the windows' f beats a held f stale by more than 1.5%" became "the windows' model ... by 1.5% or more", since H279 is the joint rule and the break-even sits on the 1.5% grid point. `paper/ral.md` gained four sentences in Section VIII and Recommendation 9; `check_short.py` passes (205 distinct numbers, all in the draft; about 6.5 pages by its rule of thumb).
- **Caveat.** A write-up of logged results, not a new result. The TUM host was not re-probed this run, since no step here needs the frames.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3zb result: a held distortion from another real calibration of the same camera moves rays by only 0.2&ndash;0.5 px inside 250 px, and even a 50% error in the held k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> costs 1.5 px, less than the 2.1 px of adopting the windows' f and distortion together; so adopting the windows' distortion still does not pay, which reference is taken as the truth decides the joint-or-f-only choice, and a second gate on the distortion fires in 30 of 31 blocks; one of five predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3zb_staledist/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (milestone 3za's blocks, rules and P(r) score), as planned below; about 13 s. `tests/test_m3zb.py` (3 tests: the two gates fire independently and drop k<sub>3</sub>; with a wrong held distortion and a perfect pool, never and f-only keep the error while joint and two gates remove it; the scaled sweep and its break-even) passed before the first run. No code change after the first run.
- **H280 held, but not for the planned reason.** est_c, K = 5: joint gate(0.5) beats f-only gate(0.5) in **4 of 6** cross pairs (truth board, held TUM or target-free: 0.49 and 0.47 against 0.57 and 0.52 px; truth TUM, held board or target-free: 1.22 and 1.03 against 1.57 and 1.51), and loses in both pairs whose truth is the target-free anchor (2.20 and 1.95 against 1.37 and 1.07). The same split holds in the controls (truth TUM: joint gate 1.49 against f-only gate 2.00; truth target-free: f-only gate 1.88 against joint gate 2.41), so which reference is the truth, not which distortion is held, decides the winner; the held source flips it only at truth = board, where the joint gate then wins by 0.05&ndash;0.08 px (the control favours f-only by 0.15).
- **H281 refuted.** Truth board, held TUM: f-only gate(0.5)'s worst regret **0.57 px** (control 0.48).
- **H282 refuted.** Maximum worst regret over the 9 pairs: f-only always and f-only gate(0.5) tie at **2.00 px** (both at truth = held = TUM, where the gate always fires), joint always 2.50, joint gate(0.5) and two gates **2.41**, never 14.53.
- **H283 refuted.** Control truth = held = board: two gates **1.02 px** against f-only gate(0.5)'s 0.48. Its distortion gate fires in **30 of 31** blocks at &delta; = 0 even when the held distortion is the truth, because the windows' D<sub>200</sub> is biased low (milestone 3y) by more than half its pooled sd.
- **H284 refuted.** Sweep (truth board, held = the board's k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> &times; (1 + &epsilon;)): joint gate(0.5) beats f-only gate(0.5) at one grid point only, &epsilon; = &minus;30% (0.55 against 0.56 px), and nowhere on the positive side up to +50%; the minimax rule is f-only gate(0.5) at 15 of 21 grid points and two gates at 5 (&epsilon; = &minus;35, +35&hellip;+50%).
- **Unscored.** At &delta; = 0 the held distortion's own error (never's median rms P) is 0.21&ndash;0.46 px across the six cross pairs, and 0.76 and 1.51 px at &epsilon; = &plusmn;25% and &plusmn;50%; adopting the windows' distortion always leaves 2.09 px (joint always, truth board), so by linear extrapolation a held distortion would have to be off by roughly 70% of its k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> before keeping it (with the right f) was worse than adopting the windows' f and distortion together. On the sweep the two-gate rule's absolute worst case (median rms P at the worst &delta;) is the steadiest, 2.41&ndash;2.52 px across &epsilon;, against 2.23&ndash;2.61 for f-only gate(0.5) and 2.45&ndash;2.76 for joint gate(0.5), because it caps the distortion error at the windows' level. Across all 72 cross cells (3 models &times; 4 K &times; 6 pairs) the minimax rule is a joint rule in 42 (58%) and two gates in 2; in the 36 control cells a joint rule in 23 (64%): a wrong held distortion does not make joint rules win more often. f-only gate's minus joint gate's worst regret over the cross cells: median +0.07 px, range &minus;0.91 to +1.07.
- **Reading.** The case 3za left open, a held distortion that is itself wrong, turns out to matter little inside 250 px, because there the distortion moves a ray by a few pixels at most and the three real calibrations of each camera agree on where rays land to within half a pixel. Adopting the windows' f and distortion together leaves about 2.1 px rms (their f scatter and milestone 3y's low distortion bias), more than the board's model with k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> off by 50% and the right f. So 3za's advice survives: update f behind the half-sd gate and keep whatever target calibration's distortion the robot has. The joint-or-f-only choice depends on which calibration is called the truth (TUM's favours joint, the target-free anchor's favours f-only), which is a statement about the references' disagreement, not about the update rule. A distortion gate is not usable as specified: the windows' bias makes it fire nearly always; it would need a bias-corrected D<sub>200</sub> or a bar that includes the bias.
- **Caveat.** "Wrong" is another real calibration or a scaled one, not a measured lens change; the band stops at 250 px, where the distortion is small, and the image edge (where it is not) is out of reach because the board's fr1 model folds at 381 px; P ignores the principal point and tangential terms; three sequences, two cameras, overlapping blocks.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3zb planned: when the held distortion is itself wrong, does adopting the windows' distortion pay? Held models built from another real calibration of the same camera, a scaled-distortion sweep, and a second gate on the distortion

Written before `experiments/m3zb_staledist/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3za scored five update rules by where rays land, P(r) over 50&ndash;250 px, with the held model being the reference itself with a stale f; its caveat and the README's next step name the case it left out: a held distortion that is itself wrong, where adopting the windows' distortion could pay. The TUM host still refuses this session, so this re-uses milestone 3s's saved solves and milestone 3za's blocks, pools and score; nothing is re-solved. **Seen before this plan:** milestone 3za's logged figures (est_c, K = 5, board: f-only gate(0.5) worst regret 0.48 px, joint gate(0.5) 0.63, never 14.24; minimax a joint rule in 23 of 36 cells); the three references' (f, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, k<sub>3</sub>) (TUM's fr3 model has no distortion at all; the board and target-free fr1 models differ by about 0.03 in k<sub>1</sub> and 0.1 in k<sub>2</sub>). No figure for any held model whose distortion differs from the truth.

- **Truth and held model.** Truth T &isin; {board, TUM, target-free} per camera (rays from T, as in 3za). Held model: f<sub>T</sub>(1 + &delta;) with the distortion (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, k<sub>3</sub>) of a source H. **Cross pairs:** H &ne; T, six (T, H) pairs, each a real second calibration of the same camera, the distortion a robot would carry if it was calibrated by another procedure. **Control:** H = T (3za's setting). **Sweep:** T = board, H = the board's own k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> scaled by (1 + &epsilon;), &epsilon; &isin; &plusmn;{0, 5, &hellip;, 50}%. &delta; &isin; &plusmn;{0, 0.5, &hellip;, 10}%, averaged over the sign as in 3za.
- **Rules.** 3za's five (never; f-only always / gate(0.5); joint always / gate(0.5), the gate on f) and a sixth, **two gates**: f by 3za's gate; the windows' k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> adopted (k<sub>3</sub> dropped) when |D<sub>200</sub>(pool) &minus; D<sub>200</sub>(held)| &gt; 0.5 sd<sub>D</sub>, where the pool's D<sub>200</sub> and sd<sub>D</sub> come from milestone 3y's scatter-only floored pool applied to the windows' own D<sub>200</sub> (history floor from the unit's earlier windows), and D<sub>200</sub>(held) uses the held f. The two gates fire independently.
- **Score.** 3za's: per block rms P(r) over r &isin; {50, 75, &hellip;, 250} px against T; median over blocks; regret at each &delta; against the better of never and joint always; worst regret over the &delta; grid. Primary cell est_c, K = 5.
- **H280 (a wrong held distortion makes the joint rule pay).** est_c, K = 5: joint gate(0.5)'s worst regret is below f-only gate(0.5)'s in at least 4 of the 6 cross pairs.
- **H281 (the cost of a wrong held distortion).** est_c, K = 5, T = board, H = TUM: f-only gate(0.5)'s worst regret exceeds 1.0 px (3za's control: 0.48).
- **H282 (two gates are minimax).** est_c, K = 5: over the 9 (T, H) pairs (6 cross and 3 control), the two-gate rule has the smallest maximum worst regret of the six rules.
- **H283 (two gates cost little when the distortion is right).** est_c, K = 5, control T = H = board: two gates' worst regret is at most f-only gate(0.5)'s plus 0.3 px.
- **H284 (break-even distortion error).** Sweep, est_c, K = 5: for each sign of &epsilon;, the smallest |&epsilon;| at which joint gate(0.5)'s worst regret is below f-only gate(0.5)'s is at most 20%, on both signs.
- **Reported, unscored.** Every rule's worst and mean regret per (T, H) pair, model and K; how often each gate fires; per-sequence medians at &delta; = 0; the sweep curves; `staledist.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** "Wrong" means another calibration of the same camera, not a measured lens change; which of the three references is right is unknown, which is why each is taken as the truth in turn; staleness is a shifted f against real windows; P ignores the principal point and tangential terms; three sequences, two cameras, overlapping blocks.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3za result: scored by where rays land, milestone 3y's "adopt f, keep the held distortion" rule survives (worst regret 0.48 px over 50&ndash;250 px, against 0.63 for adopting the windows' distortion too); which of the two wins flips by sequence and cell, and is worth under 1 px either way, while not updating at all costs up to 14 px; one of six predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3za_joint/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (milestone 3y's blocks and floored f bar), as planned below; about 7 s. **Deviation from the plan's habit:** `tests/test_m3za.py` (3 tests: what each rule adopts and when the gate fires, rms P zero on the reference and 1% of r for a 1%-long f, regret and break-even on a hand block) was written right after the first run, not before it; the plan and its hypotheses were committed before any code (commit "milestone 3za plan"). One code change after the first run and before reading results: the held model's k<sub>3</sub> (TUM's fr1 model only) now travels with the held distortion explicitly instead of through an equality test; the scores are the same.
- **H274 refuted.** est_c, K = 5, board, &delta; = 0, median rms P over 50&ndash;250 px: joint always **2.09 px** against f-only always **1.94 px**.
- **H275 refuted.** The minimax rule is **f-only gate(0.5)**, worst regret **0.48 px** (joint gate(0.5) 0.63, f-only always 1.94, joint always 2.09, never 14.24).
- **H276 refuted.** joint gate(0.5) 0.63 against f-only gate(0.5) 0.48.
- **H277 refuted.** f-only minus joint at &delta; = 0, per sequence: fr1/desk **+0.76** (joint better), fr1/xyz **&minus;0.85** (f-only better), fr3/stf &minus;0.30. The Kinect's two recordings disagree; the median cancellation of milestone 3z holds on one of them.
- **H278 refuted.** Minimax against TUM's calibration: joint gate(0.5) (1.49 px; f-only gate 2.00); against the target-free anchor: f-only gate(0.5) (1.88; joint gate 2.41).
- **H279 held.** Joint always beats never from **&delta; = 1.5%** (est_c, K = 5, board): a held f stale by more than that costs more than any of the windows' errors.
- **Unscored.** Across all 36 model &times; K &times; reference cells the minimax rule is a joint rule in **23** and an f-only rule in **13**; f-only gate(0.5)'s worst regret minus joint gate(0.5)'s is median **+0.14 px** (range &minus;0.77 to +1.07). On the 50&ndash;350 px band (board) the split is 6 and 6; at est_c, K = 5 adopting the windows' distortion costs more toward the edge (joint always 4.57 px against f-only always 2.62 at &delta; = 0; gates 1.10 against 0.00). In every cell never's worst regret is 11&ndash;19 px, at the 10% grid edge.
- **Reading.** Scoring by where rays land does not overturn milestone 3y's advice, and does not confirm the alternative that 3z suggested. The choice between adopting the windows' distortion with their f and keeping the held distortion is worth under about 1 px rms inside 250 px, flips with the sequence, the window count and the reference, and is not something these data can settle; the decision that matters is whether to update f at all (up to 14 px rms at 10% staleness) and whether to gate it (the half-sd gate removes 1.5&ndash;2 px of the adopt-always cost at &delta; = 0 in the pre-registered cell). For a practitioner the safer default stays f with gate(0.5) on top of a held target-calibrated distortion: it is minimax in the pre-registered cell, it is never more than about 1 px behind the joint rule, and at the image edge the windows' distortion is the worse of the two at est_c, K = 5. 3z's median cancellation is real on fr1/desk and absent on fr1/xyz, so it is not a property a robot can rely on.
- **Caveat.** Staleness is a shifted held f against real windows, not a real lens change, and a held distortion that is itself wrong is not modelled (that is the case where adopting the windows' distortion would pay); P ignores the principal point and tangential terms; three sequences, two cameras, overlapping blocks; the per-sequence splits rest on 8&ndash;14 blocks each.

## 2026-11-23 · Milestone 3za planned: when a robot adopts its self-calibrated focal length, should it adopt the windows' distortion with it? Five update rules scored by where rays land, P(r) over 50&ndash;250 px, on TUM's saved windows

Written before `experiments/m3za_joint/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3y's practical advice was "self-calibrate f, hold the distortion", scored by f in % and by D<sub>200</sub>; milestone 3z then found that on the Kinect the windows' long f and low distortion cancel at the median, so where a ray lands agrees with the board within 1 px out to 250 px. If so, adopting f alone onto a held distortion breaks that cancellation, and 3y's advice may be wrong when scored by what a robot uses. The README's next step after 3z asks for "an update rule scored on P(r) at the radii a robot uses". The TUM host still refuses this session, so this re-uses milestone 3s's saved solves; nothing is re-solved. **Seen before this plan:** milestones 3s&ndash;3z's logged figures (3z: est_c median signed P(r) on fr1 within about 1 px to 250 px against the board, fr3 P(r)/r +2.4&ndash;2.8%); no figure for any rule scored by P(r), no f-only-plus-held-distortion figure.

- **Pool.** For a block of K consecutive scored windows of one sequence (milestone 3y's blocks and history floor): f pool = mean, sd from 3y's scatter-only floored bar; k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> pools = block means. Models est, est_c, est_cr; K &isin; {1, 3, 5, 8}.
- **Staleness.** The held model is the reference with its focal length shifted, f<sub>R</sub>(1 + &delta;), and the reference's distortion; &delta; &isin; &plusmn;{0, 0.5, &hellip;, 10}%, averaged over the sign as in 3w. (A refocus moves f most; a held distortion that is itself stale is not modelled.)
- **Rules.** never (keep the held model); **f-only always** (pool f, held k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>); **f-only gate(0.5)** (3y's advice: adopt the pool f when |pool &minus; held| &gt; 0.5 sd, keep the held distortion); **joint always** (pool f, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>); **joint gate(0.5)** (the same f gate, but on firing adopt all three).
- **Score.** For each block the rms over r &isin; {50, 75, &hellip;, 250} px of P(r) (milestone 3z's projected-radius error of the updated model against the reference); median over blocks; regret at each &delta; against the better of never and joint always; worst regret over the grid. References: the board (scored), TUM's published calibration and milestone 4n's target-free anchor (H278). The band stops at 250 px, where 3z found the windows accurate; 50&ndash;350 px reported.
- **H274 (adopting f alone breaks the cancellation).** est_c, K = 5, board, &delta; = 0: joint always's median rms P is below f-only always's.
- **H275 (the minimax rule is joint).** est_c, K = 5, board: the minimax rule over the five is joint always or joint gate(0.5).
- **H276 (3y's advice loses).** est_c, K = 5, board: joint gate(0.5)'s worst regret is below f-only gate(0.5)'s.
- **H277 (only the Kinect cares).** est_c, K = 5, board, &delta; = 0: on fr3/stf joint always and f-only always differ by less than 0.5 px in median rms P; on both fr1 sequences joint always is lower by at least 0.5 px.
- **H278 (not the board's model).** est_c, K = 5: the minimax rule is a joint rule against TUM's and the target-free references too.
- **H279 (break-even staleness).** est_c, K = 5, board: the smallest grid &delta; at which joint always's median rms P is at most never's is &le; 3%.
- **Reported, unscored.** Every rule's curve, worst and mean regret for every model, K and reference; per-sequence medians at &delta; = 0; the 50&ndash;350 px band; `joint.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Staleness is a shifted held f against real windows, not a real lens change; the board fr1 model folds at 381 px, so the wide band stops at 350; P ignores the principal point and tangential terms; three sequences, two cameras, overlapping blocks; the pools' k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> under est_c carry a causal prior on k<sub>1</sub> from earlier windows.

## 2026-11-22 · Milestone 3z result: on the Kinect the windows put a ray within 1 px of the board out to 250 px from the centre, though their distortion term alone is 1.4 px low there; the term trades against the focal length. On the Xtion the rays land 2.4&ndash;2.8% too far out, a focal-length error, not distortion; the solver's own bar is about nine times too narrow at every radius; three of seven predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3z_radial/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored est_c windows and milestone 3b's counted windows with their 5&times;5 covariance, as planned below; under a second. `tests/test_m3z.py` (3 tests: the ray inversion, P and &Delta;D on hand cases including an f&ndash;k<sub>1</sub> trade that zeroes P while &Delta;D &lt; 0, the delta method) passed before the run. **One change before any result was computed:** the board's fr1 model (k<sub>1</sub> 0.223, k<sub>2</sub> &minus;0.568) folds back at **381 px** (its image radius peaks at x = 0.85), so it cannot place any ray at 400 px and its corner is not a calibration at all; the sweep stops at 350 px and H269 compares P(350) with P(200). The target-free fr1 anchor folds at 403 px; TUM's models and both fr3 models do not fold inside x = 1.2.
- **H267 refuted.** est_c, median |P(200)| against median |&Delta;D(200)|: fr1/desk 2.07 against 1.37 px, fr1/xyz 3.19 against 1.36, fr3/stf 5.41 against 0.74. Per window, where a ray lands is *less* accurate than the distortion term, because each window's focal-length scatter moves P directly.
- **H268 refuted.** Spearman between f error and &Delta;D(200), all 43 windows: **+0.28** (fr1/desk &minus;0.35, fr1/xyz &minus;0.17, fr3/stf +0.66). Across windows f and the distortion do not trade.
- **H269 refuted.** Median |P(r)| is smallest at the first radius, 50 px, on both cameras (fr1: P(350) 15.4 px against P(200) 2.7; fr3: 9.4 against 5.4). The fr1 edge figure is the board's fold, not the windows: against TUM's fr1 model the median signed P stays within 2.4 px out to 350 px.
- **H270 held.** Milestone 3b's windows, rms z of &Delta;D(200) against the board with the solver's covariance: **10.8**.
- **H271 held.** rms z of P(200): **9.4**, below 10.8; but rms z of P is 9.3&ndash;9.6 at every radius, so the solver's bar is about nine times too narrow for where a ray lands as well, not only for the distortion term.
- **H272 refuted.** The solver's sd(P(r))/r is smallest at 50 px (1.00%) and rises to 1.17% at 350 px: the covariance is dominated by f everywhere and pins no mid radius.
- **H273 held.** Milestone 3b (no prior, a separate run): median &Delta;D(200) against the board fr1/desk &minus;0.76, fr1/xyz &minus;1.63, fr3/stf &minus;0.19 px, all negative.
- **Unscored.** est_c median *signed* P(r) against the board, r = 50&hellip;250 px: fr1/desk +0.17 to &minus;0.50, fr1/xyz +0.11 to &minus;1.01 px, while &Delta;D(r) runs to &minus;1.37 and &minus;1.36 px. Against TUM's fr1 model, out to 300 px: within 0.56 (desk) and 0.72 px (xyz) of zero, while &Delta;D reaches &minus;1.73. fr3/stf: P(r)/r is nearly constant, +2.8% at 50 px and +2.4% at 200 px against the board (+1.5%, +1.3% against TUM's), the signature of a long focal length; &Delta;D(r) on fr3 grows to &minus;6.0 px at 350 px. est and est_cr give the same picture (board, P(200): fr1 +0.82 and &minus;0.63 px; fr3 +5.14 and +4.76 px). Milestone 3b's own medians differ on fr1/xyz (P(200) &minus;5.2 px), a run without the causal prior.
- **Reading.** Milestone 3y's "distortion biased low" is two things. On the Kinect, at the median, it is a model-term artefact: the windows' longer f and smaller distortion cancel, and where a ray lands agrees with the board within 1 px to 250 px and with TUM's calibration within 0.7 px to 300 px. That cancellation is a median property, not a per-window one (H268's fr1 correlations are weak, &minus;0.35 and &minus;0.17): any single window still scatters 2&ndash;3 px at 200 px through its f. On the Xtion the windows' rays land about 5 px too far out at 200 px, and P grows linearly from the centre, so the error is the focal length, which milestones 3v&ndash;3x already handle with an update rule, not the distortion. Two lessons carry beyond this data. First, scoring self-calibration by k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> or D(r) overstates the distortion error; the robot-relevant score is where rays land, P(r). Second, a two-term board fit with k<sub>2</sub> &lt; 0 can fold back inside the image (the fr1 board at 381 px against a 416 px corner), so a reference distortion must be checked for monotonicity before it is used at the edge. The solver's covariance is about nine times too narrow at every radius, so the history floor of milestone 3u stays necessary.
- **Caveat.** Feature radii were not available (the TUM frames are unreachable), so whether the windows' tracks reach past 250 px is still unmeasured; P ignores the principal point (3y's c<sub>x</sub> offset on fr3 adds to it off-axis) and tangential terms; the three references disagree with each other by more than P at the edge; 3b's windows overlap 3s's.

## 2026-11-22 · Milestone 3z planned: is milestone 3y's distortion bias a real error in where the windows put a ray, or the focal length and the distortion trading off? A radius sweep of the projected radius against the distortion term alone, with the solver's own 5&times;5 covariance from milestone 3b

Written before `experiments/m3z_radial/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3y found the windows' radial distortion 200 px from the centre (D<sub>200</sub>) 0.6&ndash;1.7 px below every reference in every sequence, and its next step asked where the bias comes from. The TUM host still refuses this session (`cvg.cit.tum.de`, `vision.in.tum.de`: no answer through the proxy in this run's probe), so the feature radii the windows cover cannot be measured; this step uses what is saved: milestone 3s's est_c solves (f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> per window) and milestone 3b's solves, which store the solver's full 5&times;5 intrinsics covariance (`nfba.intr_cov`). D<sub>200</sub> is a term of the model, not what a robot uses: a robot uses where a ray lands. If a window's f is high and its k<sub>1</sub> low, the two can cancel at the radii its features cover. **Seen before this plan:** milestone 3y's logged figures; milestone 3f's post hoc radial factor (window minus board, 1 + k<sub>1</sub>x<sup>2</sup> + k<sub>2</sub>x<sup>4</sup>, at x = 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.77: fr1 &minus;0.3 to &minus;0.9% inside 0.4 and +6.7 to +7.6% at the corner; fr3 within &minus;2.4%); the board references; one fr1/xyz est_c window's intrinsics (f 547.9 px, k<sub>1</sub> 0.130, k<sub>2</sub> &minus;0.257). No projected-radius figure, no per-window covariance-propagated figure, no f&ndash;D<sub>200</sub> correlation.

- **Quantities.** For a reference (f<sub>R</sub>, k<sub>1R</sub>, k<sub>2R</sub>, k<sub>3R</sub>) and an image radius r px, x<sub>r</sub> is the normalised ray radius the reference images at r: f<sub>R</sub>x(1 + k<sub>1R</sub>x<sup>2</sup> + k<sub>2R</sub>x<sup>4</sup> + k<sub>3R</sub>x<sup>6</sup>) = r (solved by bisection on [0, 1.2], where every reference here is monotone up to 400 px). The **projected-radius error** of a window is **P(r)** = f<sub>w</sub>x<sub>r</sub>(1 + k<sub>1w</sub>x<sub>r</sub><sup>2</sup> + k<sub>2w</sub>x<sub>r</sub><sup>4</sup>) &minus; r px: how far from the centre the window puts a ray the reference puts at r (the principal point is left out; it is milestone 3y's separate quantity). **&Delta;D(r)** = D<sub>w</sub>(r) &minus; D<sub>R</sub>(r), 3y's definition at any r. Radii r &isin; {50, 75, &hellip;, 400} px (400 px is the 640&times;480 image's half-diagonal).
- **Windows.** est_c: milestone 3s's scored windows that converged (as 3y; est and est_cr reported). Covariance: milestone 3b's counted windows (its own `pick` rule), P(r) and D<sub>200</sub> sd by the delta method through the 5&times;5 covariance (numerical Jacobian in f, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>; c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> do not enter either). Reference for the scored hypotheses: the board (milestone 3i's Zhang k<sub>1</sub>+k<sub>2</sub>); TUM's and the target-free references reported.
- **H267 (the two trade).** est_c, per sequence, median |P(200)| over windows is below median |&Delta;D(200)| in all three sequences: where a ray lands at 200 px is more accurate than the distortion term says.
- **H268 (coupling).** est_c, all scored windows pooled: Spearman correlation between the window's focal error (f<sub>w</sub>/f<sub>R</sub> &minus; 1) and &Delta;D(200) is below &minus;0.5.
- **H269 (pinned inside, wrong at the edge).** est_c, per camera (fr1 = both Kinect sequences, fr3): the radius minimising the median |P(r)| lies in [100, 300] px, and median |P(400)| is at least twice median |P(200)|, on both cameras.
- **H270 (the solver's own bar is too narrow for D<sub>200</sub>).** Milestone 3b's counted windows: rms z of &Delta;D(200) against the board, with the delta-method sd, exceeds 2.
- **H271 (better calibrated for where rays land).** Same windows: rms z of P(200) is below rms z of &Delta;D(200).
- **H272 (where the solver pins the curve).** Same windows: the radius minimising the median of sd(P(r))/r lies in [100, 300] px.
- **H273 (not est_c's prior).** Same windows (milestone 3b: no prior, a separate solver run): median &Delta;D(200) against the board is negative in all three sequences.
- **Reported, unscored.** Median signed P(r) and &Delta;D(r) per sequence and model at every radius against all three references; rms z per radius; the f-error and D<sub>200</sub> spearman per sequence; `radial.png` (median P(r) and &Delta;D(r) against r, per sequence).
- **Caveat set in advance.** No feature radii (the frames are not reachable), so "where the windows pin the curve" is inferred from the solver's covariance, not from point counts; P(r) ignores the principal point and tangential terms; the board itself is a 13-view calibration with its own error (about 0.5 px rms on fr1); milestone 3b's windows overlap 3s's and are not independent of them.

## 2026-11-22 · Milestone 3y result: the focal-length update rule carries over to c<sub>y</sub> but not to c<sub>x</sub> or the distortion; natural-feature windows put the radial distortion 0.6&ndash;1.7 px below every reference at 200 px from the centre, in all three sequences, so a robot should keep its held distortion; three of seven predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3y_pp_dist/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (53 counted for the history), as planned below; under a second. `tests/test_m3y.py` (3 tests: D<sub>200</sub> by hand, the scatter-only pool and its floor, pixel scoring and regret) passed before the run. No block was dropped for want of a history floor. No window was re-solved.
- **H260 held.** est_c, K = 5, f with the scatter-only pool: minimax rule **gate(0.5)**, worst regret **0.38** points (3w's solver-sd pool: 0.42). Leave-one-sequence-out picks gate(0.5) in all three folds, as in 3x. Dropping the solver sd does not change the f answer.
- **H261 held, but by "never".** est_c, K = 5, c<sub>x</sub>: the minimax rule is **never** (worst regret 2.86 px, at the grid's 10 px edge), below always's 7.14 and gate(2)'s 2.99. Nothing that adopts the pool helps: the five-window pool's median c<sub>x</sub> error is 7.1 px, more than most of the staleness grid.
- **H262 refuted.** c<sub>y</sub>: the minimax rule is **gate(0.5)** (worst regret 2.04 px; always 2.89, shrink 5.66, gate(1) 6.49), not a gate with c &ge; 1. The large c<sub>y</sub> offset milestone 3i saw on fr1/xyz is not in est_c's pools: per sequence their median c<sub>y</sub> is within 1.3 px of the board.
- **H263 held, but by "never".** D<sub>200</sub>: minimax **never** (0.92 px at the 2 px edge), always 1.08, gate(2) 1.06.
- **H264 refuted.** gate(0.5)'s gap to the minimax rule: c<sub>y</sub> 0.00 px, D<sub>200</sub> 0.16 px, but c<sub>x</sub> **4.28** px. 3w's rule is not a safe default for every intrinsic.
- **H265 refuted.** gate(2) is worse than always only for c<sub>y</sub> (7.11 against 2.89); for c<sub>x</sub> (2.99 against 7.14) and D<sub>200</sub> (1.06 against 1.08) refusing the pool is what helps.
- **H266 refuted.** D<sub>200</sub> minimax by reference: never (board), gate(3) (TUM), shrink (target-free); every rule's worst regret lies within 0.6&ndash;1.3 px of the others.
- **Unscored (offsets added after scoring).** est_c, K = 5 pools, median signed pool &minus; reference per sequence (fr1/desk, fr1/xyz, fr3/stf). **D<sub>200</sub>**: board &minus;1.46, &minus;1.20, &minus;1.02 px; TUM &minus;1.72, &minus;1.46, &minus;0.74; target-free &minus;1.09, &minus;0.83, &minus;0.65; rms z of the scatter-only bar 5.5 (board), 5.9, 3.9. **c<sub>x</sub>**: board +4.0, +1.7, **+16.8** px; TUM +1.2, &minus;1.1, +16.1; target-free +6.5, +4.2, +5.1; rms z 2.3, 2.1, 1.5. **c<sub>y</sub>**: board +0.2, &minus;1.3, &minus;0.1 px, rms z 0.57 (the bar is conservative, median pool sd 9.7 px). **f**: rms z 0.89. Leave-one-sequence-out (est_c, K = 5, board): c<sub>y</sub> chooses gate(0.5) in all three folds (held-out worst regret 1.04, 1.69, 4.17 px against always's 1.41, 4.13, 3.44); c<sub>x</sub> chooses never, never, shrink; D<sub>200</sub> gate(3), never, never. At K = 3, c<sub>y</sub>'s minimax is gate(0.5) against the board and target-free references and shrink against TUM's.
- **Reading.** The update rule found for f is a rule for parameters the windows estimate without bias. c<sub>y</sub> is one: its pools sit on the board, the scatter bar is if anything too wide, and "adopt unless within half an sd" is minimax against all three references and survives leave-one-out. The distortion is not: every sequence, on both cameras, puts the displacement at 200 px 0.6&ndash;1.7 px below every reference (about a third of fr1's 4.1 px), and the scatter bar is five times too narrow, so a pool that agrees with itself is still wrong and no update rule can tell. c<sub>x</sub> on the Xtion is off by 16 px against the board and TUM's published value and 5 px against the target-free anchor, while the references disagree among themselves by 12 px: the data cannot say what the right c<sub>x</sub> is. For a practitioner: self-calibrate f and c<sub>y</sub> with gate(0.5) at five windows; hold c<sub>x</sub> and the distortion from a target calibration and do not let natural-feature windows move them. The pre-registered "never" wins for c<sub>x</sub> and D<sub>200</sub> are bounded by the grid edge (10 px, 2 px): past roughly the pool's own error (7 px, 1 px) adopting starts to pay, and a staleness that large is a lens change a robot should catch another way.
- **Caveat.** Staleness is a shifted held value, not a real lens change; three sequences, two cameras, overlapping blocks; D<sub>200</sub> takes one radius, and the windows fit k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> under est_c's causal prior on k<sub>1</sub>, so a prior pulled by early windows could carry the distortion bias forward; it does not explain it here: without the prior (est, K = 5) always-adopt's median D<sub>200</sub> error is 1.10 px against est_c's 1.08; the scatter-only bar ignores the solver's own uncertainty, which the saved solves do not carry for c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>.

## 2026-11-22 · Milestone 3y planned: does milestone 3w's update rule for the focal length carry over to the principal point and the lens distortion? The same eight rules, scored in pixels on TUM's saved windows, with a pool whose error bar comes from scatter alone

Written before `experiments/m3y_pp_dist/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestones 3v&ndash;3x decided how a robot should update the focal length it holds; the README's next step after 3x and the paper step is "the same update decision on k<sub>1</sub> and the principal point". The TUM host still refuses this session (`cvg.cit.tum.de`, `vision.in.tum.de`: no answer through the proxy in this run's probe), so this re-uses milestone 3s's saved solves; no window is re-solved. Each saved solve carries f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> but a solver sd for f only, so the pool here takes its bar from scatter. k<sub>1</sub> alone is not scored: it trades against k<sub>2</sub> (milestones 3f&ndash;3g; the board's fr1 k<sub>1</sub> is 0.22 with k<sub>2</sub> &minus;0.57), so the distortion is scored as the curve's value at one radius instead. **Seen before this plan:** milestones 3s&ndash;3x's logged figures (all about f); milestone 3i's per-sequence median principal-point offsets for its own models (`m3i_principal/tum_summary.json`: fr1/xyz median c<sub>y</sub> offset +32.9 px, fr1/desk &minus;5.0 px); the three references' c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>; the raw intrinsics of three fr1/xyz est_c windows (printed while finding the field names). No per-block or per-rule figure for c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> or the distortion.

- **Quantities.** c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> (px), and **D<sub>200</sub>**, the radial distortion displacement 200 px from the principal point: D<sub>200</sub> = 200(k<sub>1</sub>&rho;<sup>2</sup> + k<sub>2</sub>&rho;<sup>4</sup> + k<sub>3</sub>&rho;<sup>6</sup>) px with &rho; = 200/f (about half the image half-diagonal; k<sub>3</sub> is zero except in TUM's published fr1 model; tangential terms ignored). f is scored too, as a check of the pool.
- **Pool (scatter-only, history-floored).** For a block of K consecutive scored windows of one sequence with values v<sub>i</sub>: pool = mean(v), sd = &radic;(max(s<sup>2</sup>, &tau;<sub>h</sub><sup>2</sup>)/K), where s<sup>2</sup> is the block's sample variance (none at K = 1) and &tau;<sub>h</sub> the MAD sd of the same quantity over the camera's earlier counted windows (any sequence, the model's own value else est, as in 3u), required from at least five; a block with no history floor is dropped. Same models (est, est_c, est_cr), K &isin; {1, 3, 5, 8}, 3w's eight rules (never, always, gate(c) for c &isin; {0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3}, shrink).
- **Staleness and scoring.** Held value = reference + &delta;, &delta; &isin; &plusmn;{0, 0.5, &hellip;, 10} px for c<sub>x</sub> and c<sub>y</sub>, &plusmn;{0, 0.1, &hellip;, 2.0} px for D<sub>200</sub>, &plusmn;{0, 0.5, &hellip;, 10}% for f; error |updated &minus; reference| in px (% for f), averaged over &plusmn;&delta;; regret and worst regret as in 3w. References: the board (milestone 3i's Zhang k<sub>1</sub>+k<sub>2</sub> on 13 views), TUM's published calibration (Sturm et al. 2012), milestone 4n's target-free 13-view anchor; scored against the board.
- **H260 (the scatter-only pool reproduces 3w on f).** est_c, K = 5, f: the minimax rule is gate(0.5), worst regret &le; 0.84 points (twice 3w's 0.42).
- **H261 (c<sub>x</sub>).** est_c, K = 5: the minimax rule's worst regret is below both always's and gate(2)'s.
- **H262 (c<sub>y</sub>, refusing pays).** est_c, K = 5: the minimax rule for c<sub>y</sub> is never or a gate with c &ge; 1 (milestone 3i saw large window offsets in c<sub>y</sub> on fr1/xyz, so a pool should often be refused).
- **H263 (distortion).** est_c, K = 5: the minimax rule's worst regret for D<sub>200</sub> is below both always's and gate(2)'s.
- **H264 (one rule for all four).** est_c, K = 5: gate(0.5)'s worst regret is within 0.5 px of the minimax rule's for c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> and within 0.2 px for D<sub>200</sub> (that is, 3w's rule is a safe default for every intrinsic).
- **H265 (the 2-sd gate again).** est_c, K = 5: gate(2)'s worst regret exceeds always's for at least two of c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, D<sub>200</sub>.
- **H266 (not an artefact of the board).** est_c, K = 5: the minimax rule for D<sub>200</sub> is the same against all three references.
- **Reported, unscored.** Every rule's worst and mean regret for every quantity, model and K, against all three references; blocks dropped for want of a floor; each quantity's always- and never-adopt median errors at &delta; = 0; leave-one-sequence-out choice as in 3x for est_c, K = 5; `ppdist.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** As 3v&ndash;3x: staleness is a shifted held value against real windows, not a real lens change (a refocus moves f, the principal point and the distortion together); blocks overlap; three sequences, two cameras. The references disagree with one another in c<sub>x</sub> by up to about 12 px on fr3 (board 319.5, target-free 331.2), so a principal-point result depends on its reference more than an f result did. est_c and est_cr hold a causal prior on c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> and k<sub>1</sub> from earlier windows, so their windows are not independent of the history that floors the bar.

The four cells where it does not beat always: est_c against the target-free reference at K = 5 (1.25 against 1.19) and K = 8 (1.24 against 0.68), est_cr against TUM's at K = 5 (1.20 against 0.80) and target-free at K = 8 (0.92 against 0.47). Against TUM's and the target-free references the K = 5 folds do not agree on a rule (TUM: gate(0.5), shrink, always; target-free: gate(0.5), gate(0.5), shrink). Per held-out sequence the regret is larger than pooled (est_c, K = 5: 1.32, 0.80, 0.55 against 0.42 pooled), and the best rule on a sequence beats the chosen one by up to 0.94 points (fr1/desk: 0.38 for the best rule there). Without the prior (est) at K = 5 the folds choose shrink, gate(1), shrink: out of sample 1.72 against always's 2.25.
- **Reading.** The choice of gate(0.5) at five windows is not an artefact of scoring on the data that picked it: a robot that tuned the rule on one camera's recordings would have picked the same rule and done better than always adopting on the other camera. What does not transfer is the margin per recording: on one sequence it only ties always-adopt, on another loses slightly, and on fr1/desk the 2-sd gate does better, because that sequence's pools sit near the board and refusing them costs nothing. The firm claim the data now support is the negative one, at every model, K and reference: a symmetric 2-sd change test is the wrong update rule (beaten out of sample in 36 of 36 cells). The positive claim, gate(0.5) at K &ge; 5, holds against the board and survives leave-one-out, but against the other two references the folds disagree, so for a paper it is a recommendation with a stated reference, not a universal rule.
- **Caveat.** Three folds, two sharing a camera; 8&ndash;14 overlapping blocks per held-out sequence, so per-fold numbers are weak evidence; the pooled out-of-sample number at K = 5 is identical to in-sample by construction; staleness is a shifted held value, not a real lens change; the board is about 1.6% from the other references.

- **H257 (bounded optimism).** est_c, K = 5: pooled out-of-sample worst regret &le; 0.84 points (twice 3w's in-sample 0.42), and &lt; always's pooled 1.17.
- **H258 (three windows).** est_c, K = 3: pooled out-of-sample worst regret &lt; always's (2.10 in 3w).
- **H259 (across cameras).** est_c, K = 5: the fold that holds out fr3/stf (rule chosen on the Kinect's two sequences only) has held-out worst regret &le; 1.0 point on the Xtion.
- **Reported, unscored.** The chosen rule in every fold for every model and K; held-out worst regret of every rule per sequence; pooled out-of-sample worst regret and worst 95th percentile for every model and K, set against 3w's in-sample minimax; the same against TUM's and the target-free references; `loso.png`.

Staleness is still a shifted held value, not a real lens change; the board is about 1.6% from the other references.

## 2026-11-21 · Milestone 3w result: once the robot has five pooled windows, adopt the pool unless it lies within half a standard deviation of the held focal length; that rule's worst regret is 0.42 points against always-adopt's 1.17 and the 2-sd gate's 3.33, and with one to three windows the empirical-Bayes blend is the safer rule; four of seven predictions held

- **H248 refuted.** est_c, K = 5: shrink worst regret **1.77** (at &delta; = 3%), worse than always's 1.17.
- **H249 held.** est_c, K = 5, &delta; = 0: shrink median |e| **0.0%** (always 1.17%): most blocks fall within 1 sd of a right held value and keep it exactly.

- **H253 held.** est_c, K = 5: the minimax rule is **gate(0.5)** against all three references (worst regret 0.42 board, 0.77 TUM, 0.80 target-free).
- **Unscored.** est_c, K = 5, gate(0.5): median |e| 0.0% at &delta; = 0, worst 95th percentile 4.0% (always 2.9%), mean regret over the grid 0.05 (always 0.10). est_c, K = 8: gate(0.5) 0.40, shrink 0.94, always 0.97. Across all twelve model &times; K cells against the board, the better of shrink and gate(0.5) has a smaller worst regret than both always and the 2-sd gate; shrink is minimax in seven cells (every K &le; 3 cell for est_c and est_cr, est at K = 3 and 5, est_cr at K = 8), gate(0.5) in four, gate(1) in one (est, K = 1). The symmetric 2-sd gate of milestone 3v is never close (worst regret 1.55&ndash;5.44). Shrink's mean weight is 0.12 at &delta; = 0 and 0.89 at 10% (est_c, K = 5).
- **Reading.** Milestone 3v found the honest bar too wide to *test* for staleness; this step finds it is the right width to *decide* with, if the threshold is low. Adopting the pool unless it agrees with the held value to within half its own sd keeps a right calibration exactly (the pool agrees) and replaces a stale one almost always, so its worst case is a 2% staleness it keeps half the time. The blend is better when the pool's sd is small relative to the scatter (one to three windows, before the history floor makes long pools conservative), and worse at five, where a conservative sd makes it keep a fraction of every staleness. For a practitioner: with the floored pool, "replace unless within 0.5 sd" at five or more windows, the positive-part blend below that; never a symmetric 2-sd change test.
- **Caveat.** The two winners were chosen among eight rules on the same 31 overlapping K = 5 blocks they are scored on, so their margins are optimistic; the tail cost is real (gate(0.5)'s 95th percentile 4.0% against always's 2.9%); staleness is a shifted held value, not a real lens change; the board is about 1.6% from the other references; one recording order, two cameras, three sequences.

## 2026-11-21 · Milestone 3w planned: on TUM, which rule should a robot use to update the focal length it holds, once milestone 3v showed the symmetric 2-sd gate keeps up to 4.5% error where always adopting the pool keeps 1.2%?

Written before `experiments/m3w_update/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3v scored the floored pool as a change *test*; its reading was that the decision should be asymmetric. This step scores update *rules* by what they leave the robot holding, over the same staleness grid, and asks whether one rule is close to the better of "always adopt" and "never adopt" at every staleness. The TUM host still refuses this session (`cvg.cit.tum.de`, `vision.in.tum.de`: proxy 403 in the routing probe), so this re-uses milestone 3s's saved solves through 3v's blocks; no window is re-solved. **Seen before this plan:** milestone 3v's logged figures (est_c, K = 5, history floor: 2-sd gate false alarms 0.03, worst median |e| 4.5%, always-adopt median 1.17% and 95th percentile 2.93%; est at K = 1: always-adopt 2.88% / 21.6%, reprinted when 3v was re-run to check it reproduces); no figure for any other gate threshold, any blend, or any regret.

- **Method** (`analyse.py`, analysis only). Same 43 scored windows, blocks of K &isin; {1, 3, 5, 8}, models est / est_c / est_cr, the **tf** pool (3u's history floor), held value f<sub>held</sub> = f<sub>board</sub>(1 + &delta;), &delta; &isin; &plusmn;{0, 0.5, &hellip;, 10}%, errors averaged over &plusmn;&delta; as in 3v. With d = pool &minus; f<sub>held</sub> and sd the pool's sd, the rules are: **never** (keep f<sub>held</sub>); **always** (take the pool); **gate(c)**, adopt when |d| &gt; c&middot;sd, c &isin; {0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3}; **shrink**, the positive-part empirical-Bayes blend f<sub>held</sub> + w d with w = max(0, 1 &minus; sd<sup>2</sup>/d<sup>2</sup>) (the staleness variance estimated as d<sup>2</sup> &minus; sd<sup>2</sup> from the block itself). **Regret**(&delta;) = rule's median |e| &minus; min(always's, never's) at that &delta;; a rule's **worst regret** is its maximum over the grid; the **minimax rule** is the one with the smallest worst regret. Scored against the board reference; the same against TUM's and the target-free references is reported.
- **H247 (a 1-sd gate).** est_c, K = 5: gate(1) worst regret &le; 1.0 percentage point (always's is its median at &delta; = 0, 1.17).
- **H248 (shrinkage beats always on the worst case).** est_c, K = 5: shrink worst regret &lt; always's.
- **H249 (shrinkage keeps a right calibration).** est_c, K = 5, &delta; = 0: shrink median |e| &le; 0.6% (about half of always's 1.17%).
- **H250 (no heavy tail).** est_c, K = 5: shrink's 95th-percentile |e| is &le; 4% at every &delta; on the grid.
- **H251 (a single window).** est_c, K = 1: shrink worst regret &le; 2.0 points.
- **H252 (what to deploy at three).** est_c, K = 3: the minimax rule is shrink or a gate with c &le; 1.
- **H253 (not an artefact of the board).** est_c, K = 5: the minimax rule against the board is also minimax against both TUM's and the target-free references.
- **Reported, unscored.** Worst regret of every rule for every model and K; mean regret over the grid (a uniform prior on &delta;); shrink's mean weight w at &delta; = 0 and 10%; `update.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** As 3v: staleness is a shifted held value against real windows, not a real lens change; the board is about 1.6% from the other references; blocks overlap; held sd taken as zero. The minimax rule is chosen on the same data it is scored on (six candidate rules), so its margin is optimistic.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 3v result: an honest pooled bar is too wide to catch a stale focal length below about 7%; the 2-sd gate rarely raises false alarms (3% at five windows) but misses half of 5% staleness, and keeping a stale value costs more than adopting the pool would; three of eight predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3v_stale/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (53 counted for the history), as planned below; under a second. `tests/test_m3v.py` (3 tests) passed before the run. No window was re-solved.
- **H239 held.** est_c, K = 5, history floor: false-alarm rate **0.03** (DL 0.16).
- **H240 refuted.** est_c, K = 3: power at |&delta;| = 5% is **0.46** (DL 0.61, bought with 0.19 false alarms).
- **H241 held.** est_c, K = 3: power at 1% is **0.09** (DL 0.24).
- **H242 refuted.** est_c minimum detectable staleness (power &ge; 0.8): K = 1 none within 10%, K = 3 **10%**, K = 5 **8.5%**, K = 8 **7%**. Non-increasing as predicted, but far above the 5% asked at K = 5.
- **H243 refuted.** est_c, K = 5: the worst median error after the decision is **4.5%** (at &delta; = 4.5%, where the gate keeps the stale value), against 1.17% for always adopting the pool.
- **H244 held.** est_c, K = 5, |&delta;| = 1%: the gate's 95th-percentile error **1.69%** against always-adopt's 2.93%: the gate does not adopt the outlier pools.
- **H245 refuted.** The prior-steadied-only floor (tf_p) gives est_c rms z **1.25** at K = 3 (in range) and **1.17** at K = 5, farther from 1 than the history floor's 0.87; at K = 1 it is 3.35 against 1.84. Only 21 of 31 K = 5 blocks (27 of 37 at K = 3) have five own windows of history; the rest fall back to DL.
- **H246 refuted.** est_cr, K = 5: minimum detectable staleness **6.5%** (5% at K = 8, where power at 5% is 0.91).
- **Unscored.** False alarms at K = 5: DL 0.16, q1 0.10, tf 0.03, tf_p 0.13. Without the prior (est) the history floor never reaches 0.8 power within 10% below K = 8 (8%). Against TUM's and the target-free references the est_c K = 5 MDS is 8.0% and 9.0% (board 8.5%), with the same 0.03 false alarms. The decision-error curve (`stale.png`, right) follows the never-adopt diagonal up to 4&ndash;6% staleness, then drops to always-adopt's level once the gate fires.
- **Reading.** Milestone 3u made the pooled bar honest by widening it to the scatter the camera has shown, about 3% of f per window; this step finds what that costs. A 2-sd test against a bar of that width cannot tell a 5% stale calibration from window scatter half the time, even though the pool itself is typically only 1.2% from the board: the bar is honest in rms because of a tail of bad blocks, so it is wide for the typical block. For focal length on these cameras, "keep the held value unless the pool proves it wrong" is the wrong default: adopting the five-window pool would be within 1.2% (median) and 2.9% (95th percentile) of the board whatever the staleness, while the gate keeps errors up to 4.5%. The gate's value is on the other side: when the held value is right (&delta; &le; 1%) it refuses the outlier pools that always-adopt takes. A useful rule is therefore asymmetric, not a symmetric 2-sd change test.
- **Caveat.** Staleness is a shifted held value, not a real lens change (a refocus would also move k<sub>1</sub> and the principal point); the board reference is itself about 1.6% from the other two; blocks overlap, so 31 K = 5 blocks are far fewer independent tests; held sd taken as zero; one recording order, two cameras, three sequences.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 3v planned: on TUM, can the floored pool tell a robot that the calibration it holds has gone stale, how small a staleness can it see, and does a floor from prior-steadied windows alone fix 3u's conservative K &ge; 5 bars?

Written before `experiments/m3v_stale/analyse.py` was written or run. The programme's question is whether a self-calibration knows when to replace the calibration a robot holds. Milestone 3u made the pooled bar honest; this asks what that bar buys as a decision. The TUM host still refuses this session (`cvg.cit.tum.de`, `vision.in.tum.de`: proxy 403, probed), so this re-uses milestone 3s's saved solves; no window is re-solved. **Seen before this plan:** milestone 3u's logged figures, among them est_c's share of K = 3 blocks within 2 sd of the board (95% history floor, 81% DL), which is one minus the false-alarm rate at zero staleness for K = 3, so no hypothesis below scores K = 3 false alarms; no figure at any nonzero staleness, and no figure of a prior-steadied-only floor.

- **Method** (`analyse.py`, analysis only). Same 43 scored windows, blocks of K &isin; {1, 3, 5, 8} as milestones 3t and 3u, the same pools (DL, **tf** history floor, q1). The robot holds f<sub>held</sub> = f<sub>board</sub>(1 + &delta;) for the camera, with &delta; &isin; &plusmn;{0, 0.5, 1, &hellip;, 10}% (only the held value is perturbed; the windows are the real solves, so no assumption about how the solver responds to a changed lens). The gate flags a block when |pool &minus; f<sub>held</sub>| &gt; 2 sd<sub>pool</sub> (held sd taken as 0). **Power**(&delta;) is the flagged share, averaged over +&delta; and &minus;&delta;; the **false-alarm rate** is power(0); the **minimum detectable staleness** (MDS) is the smallest |&delta;| on the grid with power &ge; 0.8. **Decision error**: the gate adopts the pool when it flags and keeps f<sub>held</sub> otherwise; its |e| against the board is compared with always adopting the pool and never adopting. **tf_p**: the history floor computed only from earlier windows that carry the model's own (prior-steadied) estimate, not `est`; at least 5 such windows, else plain DL.
- **H239 (few false alarms at five).** est_c, K = 5, tf: false-alarm rate &le; 0.10.
- **H240 (sees 5%).** est_c, K = 3, tf: power at |&delta;| = 5% &ge; 0.8.
- **H241 (blind to 1%).** est_c, K = 3, tf: power at |&delta;| = 1% &le; 0.3.
- **H242 (resolution grows with K).** est_c, tf: MDS is non-increasing over K = 1, 3, 5, 8, and &le; 5% at K = 5.
- **H243 (the gate costs little).** est_c, K = 5, tf: the largest median decision |e| over the &delta; grid is &le; 3%.
- **H244 (the gate does not adopt the outliers).** est_c, K = 5, tf, |&delta;| = 1%: the gate's 95th-percentile |e| is &le; always-adopt's.
- **H245 (a prior-only floor).** est_c, tf_p: rms z in [0.8, 1.3] at K = 3, and at K = 5 closer to 1 than tf's 0.87.
- **H246 (the recursive memory too).** est_cr, K = 5, tf: MDS &le; 5%.
- **Reported, unscored.** Power curves for every model and pool; DL's and q1's false alarms and MDS; the same against TUM's and the target-free references; tf_p at K = 1, 8 and for est_cr; `stale.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Staleness is a shifted held value against real windows, not a real lens change (a refocus also moves k<sub>1</sub> and the principal point, which this does not test); the board reference is itself about 1.6% from the other two, so &delta; is staleness against the board, not the truth; blocks overlap, so power figures rest on far fewer independent tests than blocks.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 3u result: on TUM, flooring &tau; at the scatter of the camera's own earlier windows makes three-window random-effects pools honest (rms z 2.67 &rarr; 1.09, worst block 11.0 &rarr; 3.2 sd) at no accuracy cost, beats a fixed 1%-of-f floor, and gives a single window a usable bar; all eight predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3u_taufloor/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (53 counted for the history), blocks of K &isin; {1, 3, 5, 8} as planned below; under a second. `tests/test_m3u.py` (4 tests) passed before the run. Every block had at least five history windows, so every block got a floor. No window was re-solved.
- **H231 held.** est_c, K = 3: rms z DL **2.67 &rarr; 1.09** with the history floor.
- **H232 held.** est_c, K = 3: worst block |z| **11.0 &rarr; 3.2**; 95% of blocks within 2 sd (DL 81%).
- **H233 held.** est_c rms z 1.09 at K = 3 and **0.87** at K = 5 (DL 1.58): honest, slightly conservative at five.
- **H234 held.** est_c, K = 5: median |e| **1.17%** both ways (ratio 1.00); at K = 8, 0.97% against DL's 0.93%.
- **H235 held.** est_c, K = 5: usable share 0.26 (DL 0.29).
- **H236 held.** est_c, K = 3: the fixed 1%-of-f floor gives rms z **1.33** (worst 4.7) against the history floor's 1.09.
- **H237 held.** est_cr, K = 3: rms z **2.36 &rarr; 0.99**, worst block 9.9 &rarr; 2.2.
- **H238 held.** est_c, K = 1 (one 1.2 s window): rms z **10.1 &rarr; 1.84**, 84% within 2 sd; the bar is about 3.3% of f, so no single window is "usable" by the 2% rule, and the worst window is still 8.0 sd off.
- **Unscored.** Median &tau;<sub>hist</sub> is 3.1&ndash;3.3% of f for est_c at every K against a median block &tau;<sub>DL</sub> of 2.1% (K = 3) and 5.7% (K = 5): the floor binds mostly at K &le; 3. The three est_c K = 3 blocks with &tau;<sub>DL</sub> = 0 have rms z 8.3 under DL, 3.2 under the 1% floor and 2.0 under the history floor. Without the prior (est) the floor is about 3.8% and gives K = 3 rms z 1.19 (DL 1.61); at K = 8 the history floor is conservative for every model (0.66&ndash;0.90), as is the 1% floor (0.56&ndash;0.98). Against TUM's and the target-free references est_c K = 3 gives rms z 1.00 and 1.00. Per sequence (est_c, K = 3): fr1/desk 0.96, fr1/xyz 0.68, fr3/stf 1.36.
- **Reading.** The overconfidence of small pools in milestone 3t is a small-sample &tau; problem, and the robot already holds the fix: how much its own earlier windows scattered. A floor at that scatter (robust sd of past f minus the median solver variance) costs nothing where the block's own &tau; is larger, and where a handful of windows happen to agree it keeps the bar from collapsing to the solver's far-too-small sd. For a practitioner: pool self-calibration windows by random effects with &tau;<sup>2</sup> = max(&tau;<sup>2</sup><sub>DL</sub>, &tau;<sup>2</sup><sub>history</sub>); it makes three windows (3.6 s) as trustworthy as five, and a lone window's bar honest to within a factor of two.
- **Caveat.** The history mixes `est` and prior-steadied values for est_c and est_cr, so the floor overstates their own scatter early on (part of why K &ge; 5 is conservative); blocks overlap and share history, so 37 K = 3 blocks are far fewer independent tests; one recording order per camera, two cameras, three sequences; the 1.6% disagreement among the references is not something any floor learned from the windows can see.

## 2026-11-20 · Milestone 3u planned: on TUM, does a floor on the between-window spread &tau;, learned causally from the camera's own earlier windows, make a three-window random-effects pool honest where DerSimonian&ndash;Laird's &tau; = 0 blocks were 11 sd off?

Written before `experiments/m3u_taufloor/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3t found five pooled windows honest (DL rms z 1.58 with the causal prior) but three not (2.67, max |z| 11.0): three prior-steadied windows that agree get &tau; = 0 and fall back to the solver's sd. A robot does not have to estimate &tau; from the block alone: it has seen how much its earlier windows scattered. The TUM host still refuses this session, so this re-uses milestone 3s's saved solves; no window is re-solved. **Seen before this plan:** milestone 3t's logged figures (DL, HKSJ, ivw at K = 3 and 5; the 1% quadrature floor at K = 5 only: est_c 0.93, est 1.29); no figure of any history-based floor, and no K = 1 or K = 3 figure of the 1% floor.

- **Method** (`analyse.py`, analysis only). Same 43 scored windows, sliding blocks of K &isin; {1, 3, 5, 8} consecutive windows within a sequence, as milestone 3t. For a block whose first window has camera rank r, the **history** is every counted window of the same camera with rank &lt; r (across sequences, as the causal prior uses), each window's value being the model's own estimate where it has one and `est` otherwise (before the prior starts the robot only had `est`). &tau;<sub>hist</sub><sup>2</sup> = max(0, s<sup>2</sup> &minus; median sd<sup>2</sup>) with s the robust sd (1.4826 MAD) of the history's f and sd its solver sds; with fewer than 5 history windows there is no floor. **tf** pool: DL with &tau;<sup>2</sup> = max(&tau;<sup>2</sup><sub>DL</sub>, &tau;<sup>2</sup><sub>hist</sub>), mean reweighted by 1/(sd<sup>2</sup> + &tau;<sup>2</sup>), sd = (&sum;w)<sup>&minus;1/2</sup>; at K = 1 it is the window with sd<sup>2</sup> + &tau;<sup>2</sup><sub>hist</sub>. **q1** pool: milestone 3t's DL sd with 1% of f in quadrature. Scored against milestone 4n's board calibration as in 3t (rms z, max |z|, median |e|, usable = sd &le; 2% and |e| &le; 2%).
- **H231 (the floor fixes K = 3).** est_c, K = 3: tf rms z &le; 2 (DL 2.67).
- **H232 (no far-off blocks).** est_c, K = 3: tf max |z| &le; 4 (DL 11.0).
- **H233 (not overcorrected).** est_c: tf rms z &ge; 0.7 at both K = 3 and K = 5.
- **H234 (little accuracy lost).** est_c, K = 5: tf median |e| &le; 1.1 &times; DL's.
- **H235 (still usable).** est_c, K = 5: tf usable share &ge; 0.20 (DL 0.29).
- **H236 (history beats a fixed 1%).** est_c, K = 3: |tf rms z &minus; 1| &lt; |q1 rms z &minus; 1|.
- **H237 (it helps the recursive memory too).** est_cr, K = 3: tf rms z &le; 2 (DL 2.36).
- **H238 (a one-window bar).** est_c, K = 1: tf rms z in [0.5, 2] (the solver sd alone is far from it).
- **Reported, unscored.** The same against TUM's and the target-free references; per sequence; the share of DL &tau; = 0 blocks and their rms z under each pool; median &tau;<sub>hist</sub> against median &tau;<sub>DL</sub>; est at every K; `taufloor.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The history mixes `est` and prior-steadied values for est_c and est_cr, so the floor is larger than their own scatter early on; blocks overlap and share history, so they are far from independent; the board reference itself disagrees with TUM's and the target-free ones by about 1.6%, which no floor learned from the windows can see.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 3t result: on TUM, pooling five windows that carry the causal prior halves the pooled focal error (2.20% &rarr; 1.17%) and reaches 2% three windows sooner than without it, and the random-effects bar stays about as honest (rms z 1.58 against 1.65); the dishonesty predicted for the prior shows only at K = 3; seven of eight predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3t_poolprior/analyse.py` on milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (all three models converged on all 43), blocks of K &isin; {1, 3, 5, 8} consecutive windows per sequence (K = 5: 31 blocks), as planned below; under a second on a laptop core. `tests/test_m3t.py` (3 tests) passed before the run. No window was re-solved.
- **H223 held.** K = 5, random effects, median |e| against the board: est **2.20% &rarr; 1.17%** with the causal prior (ratio 0.53); the recursive prior 0.94%.
- **H224 held.** Treating windows as independent is still badly overconfident with the prior: ivw rms z **14.7** (est 12.7, est_cr 7.2). The solver's sd<sub>f</sub> is not a bar to pool on.
- **H225 refuted at K = 5.** Random-effects rms z est 1.65, est_c **1.58**: the prior did not make the five-window bar less honest. At K = 3 it did (est 1.61, est_c 2.67, est_cr 2.36): a block of three prior-steadied windows that happen to agree gets &tau; = 0, falls back to the solver's sd and can sit 11 sd from the board (max |z| 11.0; est's worst 3.9). Five windows carry enough spread for &tau; to see the scatter that is left.
- **H226 held.** est_c random-effects rms z 1.58 &le; 2; 84% of blocks within 2 sd.
- **H227 held.** Hartung&ndash;Knapp&ndash;Sidik&ndash;Jonkman rms z 1.51 against DL's 1.58 at K = 5. Unscored and added after this result: at K = 3 HKSJ blows up on est_c (rms z 20.2, one block at |z| 122) because q falls near 0 when three windows agree; the modified form with q floored at 1 (R&ouml;ver et al. 2015) gives 2.65, no better than DL. At small K neither fixes the &tau; = 0 blocks.
- **H228 held.** Usable K = 5 blocks (sd &le; 2% and |e| &le; 2%): est 0.16 &rarr; est_c **0.29** (est_cr 0.35).
- **H229 held.** Smallest K with pooled median |e| &le; 2%: est **8** (1.49%; milestone 3d's "about eight" on an older model), est_c **5** (1.17%; at K = 3 it is 2.10%, just over). At K = 8, est_c 0.93% with rms z 0.98, est 1.49% with 1.39.
- **H230 held, narrowly.** K = 5 random-effects rms z: est_cr **1.77**, the largest of the three (est 1.65, est_c 1.58). Its median &tau; is the smallest (4.3% of f against 5.7% and 6.5%), as the near-pin predicts.
- **Unscored.** Against TUM's published reference est_c's K = 5 pooled median |e| is 1.25%, against the target-free one 1.18%. Per sequence (K = 5, est_c): fr1/desk 1.24%, fr1/xyz 0.73%, fr3/stf 1.38% (est: 2.09%, 2.71%, 2.37%). Adding 1% of f in quadrature to each pooled sd (about half the references' disagreement) brings est_c's K = 5 random-effects rms z to 0.93 (est 1.29). Median I<sup>2</sup> &ge; 0.98 at K = 5 for every model: nearly all the between-window spread is beyond the solver's sd, prior or not.
- **Reading.** For a robot that pools its self-calibrations, the causal prior and pooling are complements, not substitutes: the prior removes window-to-window scatter, pooling averages what is left, and together five windows (6 s of video) get f to about 1.2% of the board with a random-effects bar that is as honest as without the prior, where without it eight windows are needed for 1.5%. The cost the plan worried about is real but confined to very short pools: three prior-steadied windows can agree with each other more than with the truth, and DerSimonian&ndash;Laird, which estimates &tau; from those three, then trusts the solver's far-too-small sd. For a practitioner: pool at least five windows, use random effects (never inverse variance on the solver sd), and floor the pooled sd at about 1% of f, which also covers the references' own disagreement.
- **Caveat.** Sliding blocks overlap and windows that share a prior are not independent, so 31 K = 5 blocks are far fewer than 31 independent tests; three sequences, two cameras, one ordering; solver sd, not jackknife, so the comparison with milestone 3d is not like for like; the HKSJ blow-up and its modified form were looked at after the scored result; nothing here was re-solved on the images (the TUM host refused this session).

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 3t planned: on TUM, once a causal prior steadies each window, does pooling windows still give an honest error bar on f, and how many windows does it take to reach 2%?

Written before `experiments/m3t_poolprior/analyse.py` was written or run. Milestone 3d pooled windows by random effects and found about eight 1.2 s windows give f to 2% with an honest bar; milestone 3s's causal prior cuts each window's scatter by a quarter to a half but leaves the Xtion's +2.8% bias. A prior that removes scatter but not bias could make a pooled bar *less* honest: the between-window spread that random effects reads as uncertainty shrinks, the bias does not. The TUM host refused this session (CONNECT 403), so this step re-uses saved solves only; no window is re-solved. **Seen before this plan:** milestone 3s's per-window medians, robust sds and tail counts (its LOG entry) and milestone 3d's pooled figures (on milestone 3c's jackknife sds, an older model); no pooled figure of est, est_c or est_cr.

- **Method** (`analyse.py`, analysis only). Windows: milestone 3s's 43 scored windows (each has est from milestone 3p and est_c, est_cr from milestone 3s; a model's non-converged solve drops the blocks containing it). Within each sequence, windows in recording order; a block of size K is K consecutive scored windows (sliding, step 1), K &isin; {1, 3, 5, 8}, K = 5 the main figure. Each window's sd is its solver's sd<sub>f</sub> (milestone 3d used milestone 3c's jackknife sd, which these models do not have). Pools per block: **ivw** (inverse-variance), **re** (DerSimonian&ndash;Laird random effects, milestone 3d's `dl_pool`), **hk** (the DL mean with the Hartung&ndash;Knapp&ndash;Sidik&ndash;Jonkman variance q/&sum;w*, q = &sum;w*(f &minus; &mu;)<sup>2</sup>/(K &minus; 1)). Scored against milestone 4n's board calibration: z = (f<sub>pool</sub> &minus; R)/sd<sub>pool</sub>, rms z over blocks (1 = honest), median |e|, "usable" = sd &le; 2% and |e| &le; 2%.
- **H223 (pooling with the prior is more accurate).** K = 5, all blocks: median |e| of re for est_c &le; 0.85 &times; est's.
- **H224 (independence is still overconfident).** K = 5: ivw rms z for est_c &gt; 2.
- **H225 (the prior makes the random-effects bar less honest).** K = 5: re rms z for est_c &gt; est's.
- **H226 (but not dishonest).** K = 5: re rms z for est_c &le; 2.
- **H227 (Hartung&ndash;Knapp is at least as honest).** K = 5: |hk rms z &minus; 1| &le; |re rms z &minus; 1| for est_c.
- **H228 (more usable blocks).** K = 5: share of usable re blocks for est_c &ge; est's + 0.10.
- **H229 (fewer windows to 2%).** The smallest K with re median |e| &le; 2% is smaller for est_c than for est (est not reaching 2% by K = 8 counts as K = &infin;).
- **H230 (the recursive near-pin is the least honest).** K = 5: re rms z for est_cr is the largest of est, est_c, est_cr.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence; against TUM's and the target-free references; the median &tau; and I<sup>2</sup> per model; rms z with 1% of f added in quadrature to every pooled sd (about the 1.6% the three references disagree by, halved); `poolprior.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Sliding blocks overlap, so blocks are not independent; 43 windows, three sequences, two cameras, one ordering; the per-window sd is the solver's, not a jackknife, so this is not a like-for-like rerun of milestone 3d; windows that share a causal prior are not independent of each other, which no pool here models.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 3s result: on TUM, a causal prior from the camera's earlier windows alone cuts the pooled focal error from 2.88% to 2.32% (paired p = 0.0006) and keeps most of milestone 3r's leave-one-out gain; updated from its own history the prior shrinks to a near-pin on k<sub>1</sub> yet costs almost nothing; all eight predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3s_causal/run.py` on milestone 3p's 53 counted windows in recording order (fr1: 17 xyz then 13 desk windows; fr3: 23), 43 scored windows with N<sub>min</sub> = 5, two prior models each, about 15 min on 4 cores; then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `causal.png`), as planned below. `tests/test_m3s.py` (3 tests) passed before the run. Converged: est_c 43/43, est_cr 43/43.
- **H215 held.** Pooled median |e| over the 43 scored windows, est &rarr; est_c: **2.88% &rarr; 2.32%** (ratio 0.81); lower in **30 of 43**; median paired change &minus;0.56 pp; Wilcoxon **p = 0.0006**.
- **H216 held, narrowly.** est_c 2.32% against milestone 3r's leave-one-out est_p 2.07% on the same windows (ratio 1.12, bar 1.15): the causal prior keeps about two thirds of the gain that the non-causal one bought.
- **H217 held.** Robust sd of e, est &rarr; est_c: fr1/desk 2.12% &rarr; **1.44%** (0.68), fr1/xyz 5.42% &rarr; **2.66%** (0.49), fr3/stf 5.59% &rarr; **4.02%** (0.72). All three at or below 0.8.
- **H218 held.** Median f moves 0.68% (fr1/desk), 0.45% (fr1/xyz), 0.02% (fr3).
- **H219 held.** The recursive prior tightens hard: at the last window its k<sub>1</sub> width is **0.0044** against est_c's 0.039 on the Kinect (ratio 0.12) and **0.0009** against 0.041 on the Xtion (0.02). By window 10 on each camera it is effectively a pin on k<sub>1</sub> at the value the first few windows settled on (`causal.png`, right).
- **H220 held.** Pooled median |e| est_cr 2.42% against est_c 2.32% (ratio 1.04). The near-pin does not hurt the median; it cuts the tail further (below).
- **H221 held.** Median paired change est_c &minus; est: &minus;0.44 pp on the 10 windows whose prior has 5&ndash;9 earlier windows (7 lower), &minus;0.86 pp on the 33 with 10 or more (23 lower). Five earlier windows are already enough to help; more help more.
- **H222 held.** Image cost rises a median **0.07%** (max 2.8%).
- **Unscored.** Windows with |e| &gt; 10%: est 9, est_p 4, est_c 4, est_cr **2** of 43; 90th percentile of |e| 14.8% &rarr; 9.0% (est_p), 8.5% (est_c), 6.4% (est_cr). Against TUM's published reference est_c's pooled median |e| is 1.86% (est 2.82%), against the target-free one 2.15% (2.62%). fr1/desk, whose prior is mostly the earlier xyz session: 2.11% &rarr; **1.23%**, median e +1.12% &rarr; +0.43% (milestone 3r's cross-session est_px pulled it the other way, to +2.4%, because it used the *later* desk windows' centre for xyz). The Xtion's median e is +2.8% with or without the prior on its scored windows: still a fix for scatter, not for bias. Solver sd<sub>f</sub>/f 0.63&ndash;0.98% (est_c) against a robust scatter of 1.4&ndash;4.0%.
- **Reading.** Milestone 3r's gain was not an artefact of borrowing from the future.
