A robot that keeps the median and robust spread of its own earlier k<sub>1</sub> and principal-point estimates, and starts using them as a soft prior after five windows, cuts the pooled focal error by a fifth, the window-to-window scatter by a quarter to a half and the number of 10%-wrong windows by more than half, with nothing the images notice. Feeding the prior-steadied estimates back into the memory shrinks the prior to a near-pin within about ten windows; on these sequences that pin landed close to where the camera sits, so the median barely moved and the tail improved, but a pin learned from five windows is a single point of failure (milestones 3f&ndash;3j: a pin at a wrong k<sub>1</sub> hurts). For a practitioner: keep the memory from the raw window estimates (est_c), not from the prior-steadied ones, or floor the recursive width.
- **Caveat.** One recording order per camera; fr3 is one session; 43 scored windows; N<sub>min</sub> = 5 is one choice; the recursive pin's good luck depends on the first five windows, which on both cameras happened to sit near the camera's median k<sub>1</sub> &mdash; a run with a bad start is the test it has not had.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 3s planned: on TUM, does a *causal* prior, built only from the camera's earlier windows in recording order, keep milestone 3r's gain, and does updating it from its own prior-steadied history help or collapse?

Written before `experiments/m3s_causal/run.py` was run on any TUM window and before `analyse.py` was written. Milestone 3r's prior came from every other window of the camera, later ones included, which a robot does not have. **Seen before this plan:** milestone 3r's logged result and the per-camera medians and robust sds of milestone 3p's `est` intrinsics; the recording order (fr1/xyz starts at 1305031102.2 s, fr1/desk at 1305031452.8 s, so every Kinect xyz window precedes every desk window); nothing from any causal solve.

- **Method** (`run.py`). Milestone 3r's solve (milestone 3n's `est` model plus Gaussian prior residuals on c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub> in units of the window's own `est` rms), with &mu; and s the median and robust sd (1.4826 MAD) over the camera's **earlier** counted windows only, ordered by first-frame timestamp across sequences. A prior starts once N<sub>min</sub> = 5 earlier counted windows exist (a robust sd of fewer is too noisy to trust; set here, not tuned); earlier windows have no prior. **est_c**: the earlier windows' values are milestone 3p's `est`. **est_cr**: they are the earlier windows' own est_cr solutions (est before the prior starts): the robot updates its memory from its own prior-steadied history, which may tighten the prior over time. Scored windows: those with a prior (fr1: 25 of 30, fr3: 18 of 23 on milestone 3p's counts). e = (f &minus; R)/R against milestone 4n's board calibration.
- **H215 (the causal prior lowers the error, paired).** Pooled over scored windows: median |e| of est_c &le; 0.85 &times; est's, Wilcoxon signed-rank p &lt; 0.05, |e| lower in more than half.
- **H216 (causal keeps most of the leave-one-out gain).** Pooled median |e| of est_c &le; 1.15 &times; milestone 3r's est_p on the same windows.
- **H217 (scatter).** Robust sd of e for est_c &le; 0.8 &times; est's (scored windows), in at least 2 of 3 sequences.
- **H218 (the centre does not move).** Per sequence, |median f(est_c) &minus; median f(est)| / median f(est) &le; 1% on scored windows, in all 3.
- **H219 (the recursive prior tightens).** On both cameras, the k<sub>1</sub> prior width at the last scored window is smaller for est_cr than for est_c, by at least 30%.
- **H220 (and it does no harm).** Pooled median |e| of est_cr &le; 1.05 &times; est_c's.
- **H221 (a short memory is enough).** The median paired change |e<sub>est_c</sub>| &minus; |e<sub>est</sub>| is negative both for windows whose prior has 5&ndash;9 earlier windows and for those with 10 or more.
- **H222 (the images accept the prior).** Pooled median rise in image cost (prior excluded) of est_c over est &le; 1%.
- **Reported, unscored.** The same figures against TUM's and the target-free references; the k<sub>1</sub>, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> prior &mu; and s against window rank for both models; fr1/desk alone (its prior is mostly the earlier xyz session, milestone 3r's est_px made causal); the Xtion's median e; `causal.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** N<sub>min</sub> = 5 is one choice; three sequences, two cameras and one ordering per camera, so "earlier" is a single history, not a distribution over histories; on fr3 the history is one session; est_cr's tightening can lock in an early bias, which H219&ndash;H220 are there to catch.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 3r result: on TUM, a prior on k<sub>1</sub> and the principal point from the camera's other windows cuts the focal scatter by a quarter to a half and the pooled error from 3.06% to 2.43% (paired p = 0.002), without moving the centre; a prior from the other session works too; k<sub>1</sub> alone does as well; five of seven predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3r_prior/run.py` on milestone 3p's 53 counted windows, 4 prior solves each, about 30 min on 4 cores, then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `prior.png`), as planned below. `tests/test_m3r.py` (3 tests) passed before the run. **Check held:** every window's nfba start f equals milestone 3p's exactly (max relative difference 0). Converged: est_p 53/53, est_ph 52/53, est_pk1 53/53, est_px 30/30 (median 50&ndash;63 iterations).
- **H208 held.** Robust sd of e, est &rarr; est_p: fr1/desk 2.12% &rarr; **1.63%** (ratio 0.77, just over the bar), fr1/xyz 5.98% &rarr; **3.31%** (0.55), fr3/stf 5.97% &rarr; **4.08%** (0.68). Two of three at or below 0.75.
- **H209 held.** Pooled median |e| **3.06% &rarr; 2.43%** (ratio 0.79); |e| lower in **35 of 53** windows; median paired change &minus;0.34 pp; Wilcoxon signed-rank **p = 0.0018**.
- **H210 held.** Median f moves 0.39% (fr1/desk), 0.85% (fr1/xyz), 0.56% (fr3): the prior narrows the spread around the same centre.
- **H211 held.** Prior from the *other* fr1 session: median |e| fr1/desk 2.11% &rarr; **1.30%** (0.62), fr1/xyz 3.96% &rarr; **2.83%** (0.71); paired over 30 Kinect windows, lower in 20, p = 0.007. It does pull fr1/xyz's median e from &minus;0.07% to +2.39% (fr1/desk's windows sit higher), the cost of borrowing another session's centre.
- **H212 refuted.** Half the width does not help the median: pooled median |e| 2.77% (est_ph) against 2.26% (est_p, same 52 windows). It does shrink the tail further (unscored below).
- **H213 refuted.** The principal-point part adds nothing to the median: est_pk1 (k<sub>1</sub> only) 2.38% against est_p 2.43% (ratio 1.02). It does narrow fr1/desk's and fr1/xyz's c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> spread (robust sd 7.8 &rarr; 2.9 px on fr1/desk), but f follows k<sub>1</sub>.
- **H214 held.** Image cost rises a median **0.05%** (max 1.9%; lower than est in 1 window): the images barely notice the prior, the ridge is that flat.
- **Unscored.** Windows with |e| &gt; 10%: est 9, est_p 4, est_ph 3, est_pk1 4, est_px 2 of 30; 90th percentile of |e| 13.1% &rarr; 8.5% (est_p) and 6.2% (est_ph). Against the target-free reference est_p's pooled median |e| is 1.83% (est 2.62%); against TUM's, 2.68% (2.96%). The solver's median sd<sub>f</sub>/f is 0.6&ndash;1.0% with the prior against a robust scatter of 1.6&ndash;4.1%, still 2.5&ndash;4&times; overconfident (est: 3&ndash;7&times;). The Xtion's median e stays +2.0% against the board (est +2.5%): the prior does not touch the one bias left. `prior.png`: the largest gains are on the worst windows (|e| 15.1% &rarr; 0.4% on fr1/desk window 1, 25.7% &rarr; 4.6% on fr3 window 24), though not all (fr3 window 21: 23.4% &rarr; 15.3%).
- **Reading.** Milestone 3q found the leftover focal error on TUM is window-to-window scatter along a flat f&ndash;k<sub>1</sub>&ndash;principal-point ridge. A soft prior from the camera's own earlier windows, with no target and no reference value, holds each window near where the camera usually sits on that ridge: scatter falls by a quarter to a half, the pooled error by a fifth, the worst windows most, and the median does not move. The k<sub>1</sub> part does the work; the principal point is along for the ride. A prior from another session of the same camera works as well (median |e| 0.62&ndash;0.71&times;) but carries that session's centre with it. For a practitioner: keep a running median and spread of the camera's k<sub>1</sub> across self-calibrations and put it into the next window as a prior at that spread; it costs nothing the images notice and halves the number of 10%-wrong windows. Tightening it further trades median for tail. It is a fix for scatter, not for bias: the Xtion's +2% stays.
- **Caveat.** The leave-one-out prior comes from windows of the same session, scene and temperature (a best case); est_px is the honest cross-session test and exists only for the Kinect; the prior's width is the population scatter including the scatter it removes; the tail counts were added to `analyse.py` after scoring; 53 windows, three sequences, two cameras.

## 2026-11-19 · Milestone 3r planned: on TUM, does a prior on k<sub>1</sub> and the principal point drawn from the same camera's other windows narrow the window-to-window focal scatter that milestone 3q found, without moving the centre?

Written before `experiments/m3r_prior/run.py` was run on any TUM window and before `analyse.py` was written. Milestone 3q found the Kinect's leftover error is scatter (robust sd 2&ndash;6%) along an f&ndash;k<sub>1</sub>&ndash;principal-point ridge, and milestones 3f&ndash;3j found that pinning k<sub>1</sub> at the *board* value hurts. A robot with a fleet memory has a third option: a soft prior from its own earlier self-calibrations, which uses no target. **Seen before this plan:** the medians and robust sds of milestone 3p's `est` intrinsics over the 53 counted windows (Kinect c<sub>x</sub> 319.2 &plusmn; 9.8, c<sub>y</sub> 259.0 &plusmn; 7.5, k<sub>1</sub> 0.127 &plusmn; 0.040; Xtion 337.0 &plusmn; 20.9, 239.2 &plusmn; 24.3, &minus;0.018 &plusmn; 0.050), which set the priors below; nothing from any prior solve.

- **Method** (`run.py`). Milestone 3p's windows, nfba start and counting rule; milestone 3n's `est` model (mocap rates, u = 0, readout free from 0, all five intrinsics free) plus Gaussian prior residuals r<sub>j</sub> = &sigma;<sub>px</sub>(p<sub>j</sub> &minus; &mu;<sub>j</sub>)/s<sub>j</sub> on chosen intrinsics, &sigma;<sub>px</sub> the window's own `est` rms (px), so the prior is in units of the window's pixel noise. &mu; and s are the median and robust sd (1.4826 MAD) of milestone 3p's `est` values over the **other** counted windows of the same camera (leave-one-window-out; no reference calibration enters). Models: **est_p** (c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, width s), **est_ph** (same, width s/2), **est_pk1** (k<sub>1</sub> only, width s), **est_px** (Kinect only: c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, width s, &mu; and s from the *other* fr1 sequence: a prior from an earlier session). `est` itself is taken from milestone 3p; check: the nfba start's f equals milestone 3p's to 1e-6 relative. e = (f &minus; R)/R against milestone 4n's board calibration (fr1 518.28, fr3 530.71 px) unless stated.
- **H208 (the prior narrows the scatter).** Robust sd of e for est_p &le; 0.75 &times; est's, in at least 2 of 3 sequences.
- **H209 (and lowers the error, paired).** Pooled over counted windows: median |e| of est_p &le; 0.85 &times; est's, and a Wilcoxon signed-rank test of |e<sub>est_p</sub>| &minus; |e<sub>est</sub>| gives p &lt; 0.05 with |e| lower in more than half the windows.
- **H210 (the centre does not move).** Per sequence, |median f(est_p) &minus; median f(est)| / median f(est) &le; 1%, in all 3.
- **H211 (a prior from an earlier session carries).** est_px: median |e| &le; 0.9 &times; est's on both fr1/desk and fr1/xyz.
- **H212 (tighter helps).** Pooled median |e|: est_ph &le; est_p.
- **H213 (the principal point carries weight, not just k<sub>1</sub>).** Pooled median |e|: est_p &le; 0.95 &times; est_pk1.
- **H214 (the images accept the prior).** Pooled median rise in the image (robust, prior excluded) cost of est_p over est &le; 1%.
- **Reported, unscored.** All of the above against TUM's published and the target-free references; per-sequence medians and robust sds of f, k<sub>1</sub>, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> by model; solver sd<sub>f</sub> by model against the observed scatter; the Xtion's median e with the prior; `prior.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The leave-one-out prior comes from windows of the same sequences (same session, scene and temperature), so H208&ndash;H210 measure a best case; est_px is the honest cross-session test but exists only on the Kinect; the prior's width is the population scatter, which includes the scatter it is meant to remove; 53 windows, three sequences, two cameras.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3q result: on TUM, what is left after the shutter term is scatter between windows, not a bias (robust sd 2.1&ndash;6.0% against a median 0.1&ndash;2.5%); the Kinect windows' median sits inside the three references' 1.6% disagreement; k<sub>1</sub> and the principal point track a window's error, the solver's sd barely does; four of seven predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3q_residual/analyse.py` (no new solves; seconds) on milestone 3p's 53 counted windows (`summary.json`, `residual.png`), as planned below. `tests/test_m3q.py` (3 tests) passed.
- **H201 held.** Median f of est: fr1/desk **524.1** px (bootstrap 95% 518.3&ndash;531.5), fr1/xyz **517.9** (511.4&ndash;538.9), both inside the Kinect references' span 516.9 (TUM) to 525.2 (target-free); fr3/stf **544.2** (527.7&ndash;565.6), above the Xtion's span 530.7 (board) to 537.3 (TUM), though its interval covers all three.
- **H202 held.** Robust sd of e against |median e| (board): fr1/desk 2.1% against 1.1%, fr1/xyz 6.0% against 0.07%, fr3/stf 6.0% against 2.5%.
- **H203 held.** fr1 pooled Spearman of signed e with the window's k<sub>1</sub> **+0.58** (30 windows; milestone 3f without the shutter term +0.59). Within each sequence too (unscored): +0.44, +0.60, and +0.72 on fr3, whose images come undistorted.
- **H204 refuted, narrowly.** Pooled Spearman of |e| with sd<sub>f</sub>/f **+0.29** (bar +0.3); +0.21 against TUM's reference, +0.35 against the target-free one, +0.21 for est_mud.
- **H205 refuted.** Keeping the half of each sequence's windows with the smaller sd<sub>f</sub>/f: median |e| 2.11% &rarr; 1.65% (fr1/desk, ratio 0.78), 3.96% &rarr; 4.20% (fr1/xyz, 1.06), 3.63% &rarr; 2.31% (fr3, 0.64). One of three sequences reached the 25% bar.
- **H206 held.** Pooled Spearman of |e| with the window's principal-point distance from the target-free anchor **+0.42** (median distance 20.6 px); +0.38 against TUM's and target-free references, +0.31 to +0.38 for est_mud.
- **H207 refuted.** A line e = a + b(k<sub>1</sub> &minus; k<sub>1,board</sub>) fitted on fr1/desk and applied to fr1/xyz halves its median |e| (3.96% &rarr; 2.12%, ratio 0.54); fitted on fr1/xyz and applied to fr1/desk it makes it worse (2.11% &rarr; 3.51%, 1.66). The slopes differ (0.73 and 1.24 per unit k<sub>1</sub>) and so do the intercepts (7.2% and 12.1%), so the trade-off is real in each session but its line is not the camera's.
- **Unscored.** Pooled median |e| of est: 3.06% (board), 2.96% (TUM), **2.62%** (target-free); for est_mud 2.79%, 3.02%, **2.05%**. The choice of reference moves the headline figure by up to 0.7 pp, as much as any model change since milestone 3n.
- **Reading.** Milestones 3n&ndash;3p asked what bias the window's f still carries; this one finds there is little bias left to explain on the Kinect. Its windows' median sits inside the 1.6% that three honest calibrations of the same camera disagree by, and the error is the window-to-window spread (2&ndash;6% robust sd), which tracks the window's own k<sub>1</sub> (&rho; +0.44 to +0.72 in every sequence) and principal point (+0.42): within one short window, focal length, distortion and principal point trade off along a ridge, and where a window lands on it is what sets its error. The Xtion's median is 1.3&ndash;2.5% high against every reference, the one bias that remains. For a practitioner: a single window's f is good to a few percent, and its own solver sd is a weak guide to which windows to trust; pool windows (milestone 3d) and quote the reference's own uncertainty, about 1&ndash;2% on TUM, beside any claim below that. A fixed k<sub>1</sub>&ndash;f correction learned in one session does not carry to the next.
- **Caveat.** 53 windows, three sequences, two cameras; the principal-point reference is the target-free anchor because the Zhang fit's principal point is not stored; H205's gate uses each sequence's own median; the paired correction in H207 is fitted on 13 or 17 windows.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3q planned: on TUM, with the shutter term in, is the 1.9&ndash;4.0% focal error left per window a bias against the board reference or per-window scatter, and does anything the window itself reports (its k<sub>1</sub>, its solver sd, its principal point) predict it?

Written before `experiments/m3q_residual/analyse.py` was written or run. No new solves: the inputs are milestone 3n's `est` (shutter term in) and milestone 3p's `est_mud` (offset and velocity in) on milestone 3p's 53 counted windows. **Seen before this plan:** milestone 3n's and 3p's summaries as logged (per-sequence median |e| and median e, e.g. fr1/desk est median e +1.06%), and the three references below; no per-window value of k<sub>1</sub>, sd or principal point against e.

- **References** (all with the windows' k<sub>1</sub> + k<sub>2</sub> model unless noted): R<sub>board</sub> = milestone 4n's flat-board Zhang, fr1 518.28, fr3 530.71 px (the reference of every earlier milestone); R<sub>TUM</sub> = the dataset authors' published calibration, (f<sub>x</sub> + f<sub>y</sub>)/2, fr1 516.9, fr3 537.3 (Sturm et al. 2012); R<sub>tf</sub> = milestone 4n's target-free 13-view anchor, fr1 525.16, fr3 534.04. e = (f &minus; R)/R; R<sub>board</sub> unless stated.
- **H201 (the window median sits inside the references' disagreement).** For each sequence, the median f of est lies between the smallest and largest of the three references, in at least 2 of 3 sequences.
- **H202 (what remains is scatter, not bias).** For each sequence, the robust sd (1.4826 MAD) of est's e across windows exceeds |median e|, in all 3 sequences.
- **H203 (milestone 3f's k<sub>1</sub>&ndash;f trade-off survives the shutter term).** fr1 pooled (desk + xyz): Spearman of signed e (est) with the window's k<sub>1</sub> &ge; +0.4 (milestone 3f, no shutter term: +0.59).
- **H204 (the solver's own sd flags bad windows).** Pooled over 53 windows: Spearman of |e| with sd<sub>f</sub>/f (est) &ge; +0.3.
- **H205 (a gate a robot could run).** Keeping only windows with sd<sub>f</sub>/f at or below their sequence's median lowers median |e| by &ge; 25% against all of that sequence's windows, in at least 2 of 3 sequences.
- **H206 (principal point).** Pooled: Spearman of |e| with the window's distance of (c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>) from milestone 4n's target-free k<sub>1</sub>+k<sub>2</sub> anchor's (fr1 313.3, 262.5; fr3 331.2, 251.7; the Zhang fit's are not stored) &ge; +0.3.
- **H207 (a k<sub>1</sub> correction transfers between sessions of one camera).** On fr1, fit e = a + b(k<sub>1</sub> &minus; k<sub>1,board</sub>) by least squares on one sequence and apply it to the other (desk &rarr; xyz and xyz &rarr; desk); median |e| corrected &le; 0.8 &times; uncorrected on both held-out sequences.
- **Reported, unscored.** Every figure above against R<sub>TUM</sub> and R<sub>tf</sub> too, and for est_mud; per-sequence medians of f with a bootstrap 95% interval (2,000 resamples, seed 0); `residual.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** 53 windows from three sequences of two cameras; windows of one sequence share a scene and session; H205's gate uses the sequence's own median, which a robot has only after it has seen the windows; the references are themselves estimates whose spread (fr1 1.6%, fr3 1.2%) is the point of H201.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3p result: on TUM, the motion-capture velocity fits the images slightly better but does not move the focal error; the mocap&ndash;camera offset is real (a median 5.1 ms, positive in 46 of 53 windows) and does not need the velocity to find it; three of six predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3p_tumvel/run.py` on milestone 3n's 64 windows, about 60 min on 4 cores, then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `tumvel.png`), as planned below. **53 windows counted**: fr1/desk 13, fr1/xyz 17, fr3/stf 23 (milestone 3n's 54 less fr1/desk window 16, whose rate signal, padded 0.1 s past the window, runs into a mocap gap). Every counted window's est, est_mu, est_d and est_mud solve converged; mu_shuf is missing in 3 windows whose source window has a gap. `tests/test_m3p.py` (3 tests) passed before the run. Median scale 4.7 window units per metre (Kinect) and 4.9 (Xtion); median mocap spread of the keyframe centres 0.12 m and 0.07 m; median speed 0.37 and 0.21 m/s.
- **Check held.** est's f equals milestone 3n's est exactly in all 53 windows (median and max relative difference 0).
- **H195 refuted, narrowly.** est_mu's cost is below est's in **26 of 30** Kinect windows (87%) but **17 of 23** Xtion windows (74%; bar 75%). The drops are small: a median 0.8% (Kinect) and 0.2% (Xtion) of the robust cost.
- **H196 held.** est_mu's cost is below mu_shuf's in 23 of 28 Kinect windows (82%) and 17 of 22 Xtion windows (77%). The velocity's timing, not just its size, is what fits.
- **H197 held by its letter, not supported by the paired test.** Kinect pooled median |e| **2.14%** with the velocity against **2.62%** without (ratio 0.82, bar 0.9). But |e| falls in only 15 of 30 windows, the median paired change is &minus;0.09 pp (a slight rise), and a Wilcoxon signed-rank test gives p = 0.45 (all 53 windows: p = 0.44, median change &minus;0.05 pp). The medians moved because a few windows on either side of the median swapped places (fr1/xyz: 3.96% &rarr; 2.21% by median, |e| lower in 9 of 17). Read: the mocap velocity does not change a window's focal error by a detectable amount. (The paired tests were added to `analyse.py` after scoring.)
- **H198 held.** est_mud's offset: fr1/desk median **6.1 ms**, robust sd 3.5 ms; fr1/xyz **5.4 ms**, 4.9 ms; fr3/stf **4.0 ms**, 2.9 ms (bars 10 ms and 5 ms). Unscored: positive in **46 of 53** windows (sign test p = 4e-8); pooled median 5.1 ms, robust sd 4.0 ms. The solver claims a median sd of 0.7 ms (Kinect) and 0.2 ms (Xtion), so the window-to-window scatter is again 5&ndash;15&times; what it reports. With &delta;&#770; &gt; 0 the camera's motion at its stamp is what the mocap reports about 5 ms earlier: TUM's colour-image stamps run about 5 ms behind the instant the image shows, on both cameras.
- **H199 refuted.** Kinect median |&delta;&#770;<sub>est_d</sub> &minus; &delta;&#770;<sub>est_mud</sub>| **0.55 ms** (bar &ge; 2 ms; fr3 0.28 ms). Milestone 3o's synthetic 3 ms bias from leaving the velocity out does not appear on TUM. Its synthetic windows (milestone 3k's part B) put more translation into the smear than these hand-held and slow sequences do, so on TUM the rotation carries the offset's signal; the offset scatter is not from the missing velocity.
- **H200 refuted.** Freeing the offset moves f a median **0.45%** with the velocity in (Kinect; fr3 0.58%; bar 0.3%). Not consistently in one direction: est_mud's |e| is below est's in 30 of 53 windows (57%); median |e| fr1/desk 2.11% &rarr; 1.62%, fr1/xyz 3.96% &rarr; 3.45%, fr3 3.63% &rarr; 3.14%.
- **Unscored.** Readouts barely move: Kinect 27.4 ms (est) and 25.3 ms (est_mu), Xtion 25.9 and 26.2 ms; the velocity tightens the Kinect's window-to-window readout spread from 3.9 to 2.8 ms and the Xtion's from 5.2 to 3.7 ms. est_d (offset free, no velocity) also lowers the cost against est in 80% (Kinect) and 96% (Xtion) of windows. Spearman of the per-window gain in |e| from the velocity against speed: &minus;0.35 (fr1/desk), +0.13 (fr1/xyz), +0.01 (fr3).
- **Reading.** Milestone 3n left 1.9&ndash;4.0% of median focal error after the shutter term. This milestone tested two of the remaining modelled causes on the real data and neither is it: the camera's translation during readout is visible in the images (the true velocity fits better than another window's) but is too small at these speeds to move f, and a ~5 ms timing offset is there but freeing it moves f by about half a percent in either direction, not systematically toward the board. What remains is not a shutter-timing effect; the leads are the per-window geometry (milestone 3b&ndash;3j: the spread of view angles predicted the error) and the board reference itself. For a practitioner on TUM-class RGB-D data: the mocap&ndash;image offset is about +5 ms and is worth entering; the mocap velocity can be left out of a short self-calibration window; and, like the readout, the offset a single window reports is good to about 3&ndash;5 ms, not to the solver's sub-millisecond sd.
- **Caveat.** The velocity's scale comes from each window's own nfba start (a short baseline gives a noisy s); the velocity is a 40 ms difference held at the camera stamp; the offset is relative to TUM's mocap stamps after their own synchronisation, not a hardware latency; three sequences, two sensor units; H197's median test was weaker than the paired test that should have been set in advance.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3p planned: on TUM, does the motion-capture linear velocity, entered as a known input, explain more of a 1.2 s window's images and its focal error, and does a free mocap&ndash;camera offset stay put once it is in?

Written before `experiments/m3p_tumvel/run.py` was run on any TUM window and before `analyse.py` was written. Milestone 3n's free-velocity model converged in 6 of 54 windows, and milestone 3o found on synthetic windows that a free time offset with no velocity in the model is biased by about 3 ms (0.5 ms once the velocity is in). TUM's ground truth gives the optical centre's position, so the velocity can be an input rather than 36 unknowns. **Seen before this plan:** `tests/test_m3p.py` (3 tests: the velocity's sign against a moving, turning camera; the scale; a mocap gap) passed; nothing from any TUM solve of this milestone.

- **Method** (`run.py`). Milestone 3n's windows, nfba start and counting rule (converged, &ge; 100 tracks, inlier rms &lt; 1.5 px, 300 &lt; f &lt; 1000, defined sd, mocap at every keyframe and over the rate signal). From the nfba solution, all five intrinsics and the readout free from 0, five solves: **est** (milestone 3n's model, u = 0), **est_mu** (u<sub>i</sub> = &minus;s R<sub>i</sub><sup>T</sup>c&prime;(t<sub>i</sub>) from mocap, c&prime; a &plusmn;20 ms difference; s, window units per metre, the ratio of the rms spread of the nfba camera centres to that of the mocap centres), **mu_shuf** (the velocity of the window five on, same scale s: a null with the velocity's size but not its timing), **est_d** (milestone 3o's offset model on a 5 ms mocap rate signal, u = 0, &delta;&#770; free from 0), **est_mud** (both). &delta;&#770; &gt; 0 means the camera's motion at its stamp is what the mocap reports &delta;&#770; earlier. e = (f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>)/f<sub>ref</sub> against milestone 4n's board calibration, as milestone 3n.
- **Counted.** As above; a solve that does not converge (status 0) is reported and left out of that model's figures; paired comparisons use windows where both solves converged.
- **Check, not a hypothesis.** est's f equals milestone 3n's est to a median 1e-4 of f.
- **H195 (the velocity explains the images).** Robust cost of est_mu below est's in &ge; 75% of counted windows, separately for the Kinect (fr1/desk + fr1/xyz) and the Xtion (fr3).
- **H196 (its timing carries it).** est_mu's cost below mu_shuf's in &ge; 75% of windows, Kinect and Xtion separately.
- **H197 (the velocity is part of what f compensates).** Kinect pooled: median |e<sub>est_mu</sub>| &le; 0.9 &times; median |e<sub>est</sub>| (milestone 3n: 2.55%).
- **H198 (one rig, one offset).** est_mud: robust sd (1.4826 MAD) of &delta;&#770; across windows &le; 5 ms in each of the three sequences, and |median &delta;&#770;| &le; 10 ms (TUM's streams are time-stamped on one machine, Sturm et al. 2012).
- **H199 (milestone 3o's bias is on real data).** Kinect pooled: median |&delta;&#770;<sub>est_d</sub> &minus; &delta;&#770;<sub>est_mud</sub>| &ge; 2 ms.
- **H200 (with the velocity in, the offset is a nuisance for f).** Kinect pooled: median |f<sub>est_mud</sub> &minus; f<sub>est_mu</sub>|/f &le; 0.3%.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence and model: convergence, iterations, median and range of e and |e|, t&#770;<sub>r</sub>, &delta;&#770; and the solver's sd; median scale s, mocap spread and speed; cost and rms against est; Spearman of each window's change in |e| (est_mu against est) with its median speed; `tumvel.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The velocity is a central difference of mocap positions over 40 ms, and its scale comes from the window's own start, so a window with a short baseline gets a noisy s; the velocity is held at the camera stamp when &delta;&#770; is free; mocap&ndash;optical-centre lever arm and rotation taken as TUM gives them; windows of one sequence share a scene and session; the board reference is a 13-view calibration (milestone 4n).

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3o result: a 1.2 s window estimates the gyro&ndash;camera time offset and its focal shift vanishes (20 ms late: 0.81% &rarr; 0.05%), but the offset lands a median 3 ms off with a claimed sd of 0.46 ms, because it soaks up the unmodelled linear velocity; four of six predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3o_offset/run.py` (30 windows &times; 21 solves, under 10 min on 4 cores) then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `offset.png`), as planned below. **All 630 solves converged** (median 14&ndash;16 iterations). `tests/test_m3o.py` (3 tests) passed before the run. Windows' median rms rate 0.30 rad/s, rms angular acceleration 3.5 rad/s<sup>2</sup>.
- **H189 refuted.** known_d: median |&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;| **2.8&ndash;3.6 ms** at the five offsets (bar 2 ms); range &minus;8.9 to +7.1 ms; median signed error &minus;0.9 to &minus;1.9 ms.
- **H190 held.** est_d: median |&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;| 2.6&ndash;3.6 ms (bar 4 ms), median |t&#770;<sub>r</sub> &minus; 33.3 ms| 3.0&ndash;3.3% (bar 10%); median t&#770;<sub>r</sub> 33.0 ms at every offset. The solver's t<sub>r</sub>&ndash;&delta; correlation is a median &minus;0.01 to &minus;0.02: readout and offset do not trade off.
- **H191 held.** &delta; = 20 ms: median |&Delta;| **0.050%** with the offset free against **0.81%** with it fixed at 0 (ratio 0.06; milestone 3m had 0.83% for its known model). At every offset the free models stay at 0.03&ndash;0.05%, against 0.14&ndash;0.81% fixed.
- **H192 held.** &delta; = 0: median |e| 1.76% (known_d) against 1.78% (known), 0.96% (est_d) against 1.01% (est). Freeing the offset costs nothing measurable when there is none.
- **H193 refuted.** The robust sd of (&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;)/sd<sub>&delta;</sub> is **9.9** (150 known_d solves; bar [0.67, 1.5]): the solver claims 0.46 ms and is off by about 3.
- **H194 held, barely.** Spearman of |&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;| (known_d, 10 ms) against rms angular acceleration **&minus;0.32** (bar &le; &minus;0.3).
- **Unscored.** est with the offset fixed at 0 drifts its readout to 31.7 ms at 20 ms (milestone 3m: 31.8 ms). Each window's offset error is nearly the same at every true offset (the spread in `offset.png` moves rigidly with &delta;), so it is a per-window bias, not noise.
- **Post hoc diagnosis** (`diag.py`, `diag.json`; written after the scoring; known_d at 10 ms, first 12 windows, not a hypothesis test). Median |&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;|: both noises on **2.96 ms** (sd claimed 0.42); pixel noise only 3.09; gyro noise only 2.81; **no noise at all 2.97 ms**. With the generator's linear velocity set to zero: **0.00 ms** without noise, **0.25 ms** with both noises (claimed sd 0.33). With the linear velocity on and solved as a free velocity per keyframe (all 12 converged): **0.49 ms** (rms 1.0, claimed sd 0.66). So the 3 ms is not noise: known_d and est_d assume u = 0, and the offset is the parameter that bends to mimic the rolling-shutter smear of the camera's translation (milestone 3k's part B velocities). The solver's sd is honest once the model is right.
- **Reading.** A free gyro&ndash;camera offset is cheap and safe: it removes the focal shift a late gyro causes (a 20 ms offset's 0.81% falls to 0.05%), leaves the readout unbiased, and costs nothing at zero offset. But the offset estimate itself is only good to about 3 ms when the camera's linear velocity is left out of the model, and the solver reports a tenth of that. With a free velocity it is good to about half a millisecond. For TUM (the next step): the offset can go in free, but its value is only a calibration of the mocap timing if the linear velocity is modelled too, which on TUM did not converge in 300 iterations (milestone 3n); here, on synthetic windows, it converged in every case. For a practitioner: put the offset in the window as a nuisance parameter for f; do not report it as the rig's time offset unless the window also models translation.
- **Caveat.** Synthetic; milestone 3k's scenes and part B linear velocities, milestone 3m's smooth-rate model (0.5&ndash;3 Hz), one rate level; &omega; and u constant over each readout; no SIFT front end or outliers; gyro in the camera frame; the diagnosis uses 12 windows and one offset, and the free-velocity case was not run with the readout free.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3o planned: can one 1.2 s window estimate the gyro&ndash;camera time offset, with the readout known and with it free, and does that remove a late gyro's focal shift?

Written before `experiments/m3o_offset/run.py` was first run in full and before `analyse.py` was written. Milestone 3n's next step is to estimate the mocap&ndash;camera offset on TUM, but the TUM hosts refuse the proxy again this session (`curl` CONNECT 403 for cvg.cit.tum.de, webshare.cvg.cit.tum.de, vision.in.tum.de), so this milestone builds and tests the estimator on milestone 3m's synthetic part T first, which is what the TUM run would then use. **Seen before this plan:** `tests/test_m3o.py` (3 tests) passed; one timing job (window 0, 21 solves, 34 s) whose results were deleted unread.

- **Method** (`experiments/m3o_offset/tsba.py`, `run.py`). `tsba.TWindow` is milestone 3l's `rsba.Window` with the gyro as a 200 Hz signal g(s) and keyframe i's rate read by linear interpolation at t<sub>i</sub> &minus; &delta;&#770;; &delta;&#770; is one more camera-block parameter (Jacobian by central difference through the interpolation; with &delta;&#770; fixed the solve equals `rsba.Window`'s, tested). Windows: milestone 3m's part T (30 seeds, &sigma; = 0.3 rad/s per axis as a sum of three sinusoids at U(0.5, 3) Hz, 1/30 s readout held over each keyframe, 0.5 px noise). Gyro: g(s) = w(s + &delta;) + white noise 0.1&deg;/s per axis per sample (milestone 3l's level, one draw per seed shared by every &delta;), &delta; &isin; {&minus;10, 0, 5, 10, 20} ms. Models: **known** (t<sub>r</sub> = 1/30 s, &delta;&#770; = 0 fixed), **est** (t<sub>r</sub> free from 0, &delta;&#770; = 0 fixed), **known_d** (t<sub>r</sub> known, &delta;&#770; free from 0), **est_d** (both free from 0); plus **clean** (global shutter, no term). Scored: the paired shift &Delta; = (f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>)/f<sub>true</sub>, f<sub>ref</sub> the same model at &delta; = 0; e = (f &minus; f<sub>clean</sub>)/f<sub>true</sub>; and &delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;. First-order size: at the windows' rms angular acceleration (about 3 rad/s<sup>2</sup>) a 1 ms offset changes the rate by about 1% of its rms, so a 1.2 s window of several thousand observations should pin &delta; to about a millisecond if the poses do not absorb it.
- **Counted.** Every window; a solve that does not converge (status 0) is reported and left out of that model's figures.
- **H189 (the offset is identifiable when the readout is known).** known_d: median |&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;| &le; 2 ms at each of the five &delta;.
- **H190 (offset and readout are jointly identifiable).** est_d: median |&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;| &le; 4 ms at each &delta;, and median |t&#770;<sub>r</sub> &minus; 33.3 ms| &le; 10% of 33.3 ms at each &delta;.
- **H191 (freeing the offset removes a late gyro's focal shift).** &delta; = 20 ms: median |&Delta;<sub>known_d</sub>| &le; 0.3 &times; median |&Delta;<sub>known</sub>| (milestone 3m: 0.83% for known).
- **H192 (freeing it costs little when there is no offset).** &delta; = 0: median |e<sub>known_d</sub>| &le; median |e<sub>known</sub>| + 0.1 pp, and median |e<sub>est_d</sub>| &le; median |e<sub>est</sub>| + 0.1 pp.
- **H193 (the solver's sd of &delta;&#770; is honest).** known_d, pooled over &delta;: the robust sd (1.4826 MAD) of (&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;)/sd<sub>&delta;</sub> &isin; [0.67, 1.5].
- **H194 (identifiability follows the angular acceleration).** known_d at &delta; = 10 ms: Spearman of |&delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;| against the window's rms angular acceleration at the keyframes &le; &minus;0.3.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per &delta; and model: convergence and iterations; median and range of &Delta;, e, &delta;&#770; &minus; &delta;, t&#770;<sub>r</sub>; the median t<sub>r</sub>&ndash;&delta; correlation from the solver's covariance (est_d); est's readout drift with &delta; fixed at 0 (milestone 3m: 31.8 ms at 20 ms); `offset.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Synthetic; milestone 3k's scenes, milestone 3m's smooth-rate model with its chosen band, one rate level; &omega; constant over each readout; no SIFT front end or outliers; the gyro is in the camera frame (no rotation fault); linear interpolation of a 200 Hz signal; the offset is constant over the window.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3n result: on real TUM video, motion-capture rates in the window solve find a rolling shutter on both cameras (Kinect readout 27.6 ms, Xtion 25.9 ms, positive in all 54 windows, about 0 with shuffled rates) and cut the median focal error from 2.87% to 1.88% (fr1/desk), 5.00% to 3.96% (fr1/xyz) and 7.56% to 3.63% (fr3); four of five predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3n_tumrs/run.py` on 64 of milestone 3b's windows (the 65th had no solution there), about 2 h on 4 cores (a first attempt was stopped by the 30 min background limit before writing anything; `run.py` now checkpoints each window and the rerun reproduced every readout it had printed), then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `tumrs.png`). **54 windows counted**: fr1/desk 14, fr1/xyz 17, fr3/stf 23. Not counted: 3 with a mocap gap at a keyframe (fr1/desk 12, fr1/xyz 5, fr3 0), 6 whose nfba start did not converge, 1 with an undefined sd. Every counted window's none and est solve converged; shuf is missing in 3 windows whose source window had a mocap gap; **est_u converged in only 6 of 54** (300-iteration cap), so its figures are not read. `tests/test_m3n.py` (4 tests, including the sign of the rate) passed before the run.
- **Check held.** none's f equals milestone 3b's to a median 6e-6 of f; the nfba start equals it exactly in every window. In 8 of 54 windows this solver's tighter tolerance moves f by more than 1e-4, and in fr3 window 12 (an f already 37% off) by 10.7%.
- **H184 held.** fr1/desk, est: median t&#770;<sub>r</sub> **27.3 ms**, positive in **14 of 14** windows (all 54 counted windows are positive, range 13.4&ndash;41.4 ms). Positive means rows exposed top to bottom, the usual CMOS readout.
- **H185 held.** fr1/desk robust sd of t&#770;<sub>r</sub> 3.0 ms, 11% of the median (bar 40%). The solver's own median sd is 0.67 ms, so window-to-window scatter is again about 4.5&times; what the solver claims.
- **H186 held.** With another window's rates, fr1/desk's median |t&#770;<sub>r</sub>| is 2.8 ms, 0.11&times; est's (bar 0.3), and est's cost is below shuf's in **13 of 13** fr1/desk windows that have a shuf solve (51 of 51 over all sequences). The fit uses the timing of the rates, not just one extra parameter.
- **H187 held.** fr1/desk median |e| **1.88%** with the term against **2.87%** without (0.66&times;, bar 0.7); |e| falls in 12 of 14 windows.
- **H188 refuted.** The slow Xtion sequence (median rms rate 0.18 rad/s) gains most: median |e| **7.56% &rarr; 3.63%** (0.48&times;; predicted &ge; 0.8&times;), lower in 14 of 23 windows. Milestone 3k's bound used the window's mean rate over 1.1 s (0.08 rad/s); the per-keyframe rates the mocap gives are about twice that, and the window solve amplifies the shutter's distortion well past the first-order stretch (milestone 3k, H168), so that bound was too low.
- **Unscored.** fr1/xyz: 5.00% &rarr; 3.96% (lower in 12 of 17), readout 27.8 ms, robust sd 4.3 ms, shuf 0.9 ms. Pooled Kinect (31 windows): **27.6 ms**, robust sd 4.0 ms; median |e| 4.12% &rarr; 2.55%. Xtion: **25.9 ms**, robust sd 5.2 ms, shuf &minus;0.5 ms. Sequence medians of f with the term: fr1/desk 523.7 px (+1.05% from the board), fr1/xyz 517.9 (&minus;0.07%; without the term 511.3, &minus;1.35%), fr3 544.2 (+2.54%; without 541.2, +1.97%). Cost falls a median 12% (fr1/desk), 10% (fr1/xyz), 14% (fr3) against none. The gain does not follow the window's rms rate (Spearman +0.11, &minus;0.20, &minus;0.04). **Sensitivity to the rate's differencing span** (d = 10 / 20 / 40 ms): Kinect readout 24.6 / 27.6 / 29.8 ms and |e| 2.92 / 2.55 / 2.36%; Xtion 19.3 / 25.9 / 36.4 ms and |e| 4.56 / 3.63 / 5.01%. The longer span smooths the rate and the readout grows to keep their product, so the Xtion's readout is known only to about &plusmn;30% from this data, and its f gain is largest at the 20 ms span used for scoring; the Kinect is stable to about &plusmn;10%.
- **Reading.** This is the programme's first real-data evidence for a cause of the natural-feature focal error that milestones 3b&ndash;3j could not explain. Both TUM cameras are rolling-shutter, with a readout of about 26&ndash;28 ms of their 33 ms frame period, and modelling it with the camera's own measured rotation removes a third (Kinect, hand-held desk) to a half (Xtion) of the median focal error of a 1.2 s window. The null control is clean: rates from the wrong time give a readout near zero and never fit better. What is left (1.9&ndash;4.0% median) is still well above what the solver's sd claims, so the shutter is part of the story, not all of it. For a practitioner: a robot with an IMU should put a rolling-shutter term in any short self-calibration window and estimate the readout there, as milestones 3l and 3m found on synthetic data; a target-free 1.2 s window measures it to about 3&ndash;5 ms per window on these sensors.
- **Caveat.** The readouts are estimates from motion capture, not a datasheet (no sourced figure for either sensor was found; Kerl et al. 2015 model both cameras' rolling shutter on TUM-style data but the text read here gives no readout); the Xtion figure depends on the rate's differencing span; mocap&ndash;camera timing and the optical-centre rotation are taken as TUM gives them; the free-velocity model did not converge in 300 iterations and was not tried longer; windows of one sequence share a scene; H188 was set from a bound that used mean rather than per-keyframe rates.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3n planned: the TUM rolling-shutter test. With motion-capture angular rates in the window solve and the readout free, does TUM's Kinect show a rolling shutter, and is it what a 1.2 s window's focal length compensates?

Written before `experiments/m3n_tumrs/run.py` was first run on any window and before `analyse.py` was written. Milestones 3k&ndash;3m built and stress-tested the fix on synthetic windows and specified this test (per-keyframe mocap rates, readout free); it waited on the TUM host, which answers the proxy this session (`curl` 200 for cvg.cit.tum.de; the three sequences fetched by `../m3b_natural/fetch.sh`). Neither the Kinect's nor the Xtion's colour readout time is sourced here, which is why it is estimated, not given. **Seen before this plan:** nothing from this milestone's solves; milestone 3m's synthetic result says a free readout absorbs a rate-scale error exactly and tolerates a 10 ms mocap&ndash;camera offset (0.35%) and a 10&deg; rotation (0.42%).

- **Method** (`experiments/m3n_tumrs/run.py`). Milestone 3b's windows (12 keyframes, every 3rd frame) of fr1/desk, fr1/xyz (Kinect) and fr3/structure_texture_far (Xtion). Per window: milestone 3b's SIFT tracks and `nfba` solve from the start that won there; from that solution, milestone 3l's `rsba.Window` (all five intrinsics free, soft-L1 LM) under **none** (t<sub>r</sub> = 0: milestone 3b's model in this solver), **est** (per-keyframe rates, t<sub>r</sub> free from 0), **est_u** (plus a free linear velocity per keyframe) and **shuf** (est with the rates of the window five on in the same sequence, circular: a null with the rates' size but not their timing). Rates: TUM's ground truth is the colour camera's optical-centre pose at 100 Hz (Sturm et al. 2012); the camera-frame body rate at a keyframe's timestamp is log(R(t&minus;d)<sup>T</sup>R(t+d))/2d with R slerped, d = 20 ms, entered as w = &minus;&omega;<sub>b</sub> (`tests/test_m3n.py` checks the sign against a camera turning at a known rate), so **t&#770;<sub>r</sub> &gt; 0 means rows exposed top to bottom**. Sensitivity, unscored: d = 10 and 40 ms. e = (f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>)/f<sub>ref</sub> against milestone 4n's board calibration (fr1 518.28, fr3 530.71 px).
- **Counted.** Milestone 3b's counting rule (converged, &ge; 100 tracks, inlier rms &lt; 1.5 px, 300 &lt; f &lt; 1000, defined sd) on the none solve, plus mocap rates at every keyframe; a model's solve that does not converge is reported and left out of that model's figures.
- **Check, not a hypothesis.** none's f equals milestone 3b's (median |&Delta;f|/f &le; 1e-4); reported either way.
- **H184 (the Kinect has a rolling shutter a window can see).** fr1/desk (median mocap rate 0.38 rad/s, the fastest), est: median t&#770;<sub>r</sub> &isin; [10, 45] ms and t&#770;<sub>r</sub> &gt; 0 in &ge; 75% of counted windows.
- **H185 (one sensor, one readout).** fr1/desk, est: robust sd (1.4826 MAD) of t&#770;<sub>r</sub> across windows &le; 40% of its median.
- **H186 (the timing carries it, not the extra parameter).** fr1/desk: median |t&#770;<sub>r</sub>| under shuf &le; 0.3 &times; that under est, and est's robust cost is below shuf's in &ge; 75% of windows.
- **H187 (the shutter is part of what the Kinect's f compensates).** fr1/desk: median |e<sub>est</sub>| &le; 0.7 &times; median |e<sub>none</sub>|.
- **H188 (it is not what the slow Xtion's f compensates).** fr3/stf (median rate 0.08 rad/s): median |e<sub>est</sub>| &ge; 0.8 &times; median |e<sub>none</sub>| (milestone 3k's bound: its rates imply about a tenth of its error).
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence and model: counted windows, convergence, median and range of e and |e|, median t&#770;<sub>r</sub>, its sd and robust spread, cost and rms change against none; fr1/xyz under every hypothesis's statistic; est_u; d = 10 and 40 ms; the pooled per-unit t&#770;<sub>r</sub>; Spearman of each window's change in |e| against its rms rate; `tumrs.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The mocap&ndash;camera time offset and the optical-centre rotation in TUM's ground truth are taken as given (milestone 3m: 10 ms and 10&deg; cost under 0.5%); the rate is a central difference over 40 ms, so vibration faster than that is not in it; rates are constant over a readout; the windows of one sequence share a scene and session; the board reference is itself a 13-view calibration (milestone 4n), 0.3% from TUM's published one.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3m result: the gyro fix survives a 10&deg; camera&ndash;IMU rotation (0.42% median focal shift) and a 10 ms time offset (0.35%), but a gyro scale or readout 5% off with the readout held fixed costs 1.23%, always the same sign; a free readout absorbs it exactly; four of six predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3m_gyrofault/run.py` (30 windows &times; 26 solves, about 6 min on 4 cores) then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `gyrofault.png`), as planned below. All 780 solves converged except part T's plain solve in 1 of 30. `tests/test_m3m.py` (5 tests: the fault generators, the scale&ndash;readout identity, the scoring) passed before the scored analysis. **Check passed:** the exact-gyro clean, none, known and est solves equal milestone 3l's to 0 (the run is deterministic).
- **H178 held.** 1&deg; rotation, readout known: median |&Delta;| **0.044%** (range &minus;0.25% to +0.18%).
- **H179 held.** 10&deg;: median |&Delta;| **0.42%** (range &minus;3.2% to +1.2%); 3&deg; 0.15%; ratio 10&deg;/1&deg; 9.4.
- **H180 held.** With the readout free a scale error is absorbed to solver precision: |&Delta;| &le; 4e-10 at s = 1.02 and 1.05, s&middot;t&#770;<sub>r</sub>(s)/t&#770;<sub>r</sub>(1) = 1 to 1e-10 (t&#770;<sub>r</sub> 32.8 and 31.8 ms).
- **H181 refuted.** s = 1.05, readout known: median |&Delta;| **1.23%** against the [0.05, 0.4]% predicted, and **negative in all 30 windows** (&minus;0.26% to &minus;3.4%); s = 1.02: 0.50%, also negative in all 30. The model sees &omega; only through &omega;t<sub>r</sub> (tested), so this is also the cost of a readout taken 2% or 5% too long. The first-order guess (relative error &times; milestone 3k's 4.17%) is 5&times; too small, and unlike milestone 3k's shift its sign does not depend on the window; why was not examined, and a readout too short (s &lt; 1) was not run.
- **H182 held.** Time offset, readout known: median |&Delta;| **0.35%** at 10 ms, **0.83%** at 20 ms (2 ms 0.05%, 5 ms 0.14%). The offset's rate error is 2.4%, 6.1%, 12% and 24% of the rms rate; Spearman of |&Delta;| against each window's relative rate error only +0.21 to +0.35, so the window's geometry again sets most of the size.
- **H183 refuted.** A free readout does reduce the misalignment's shift (10&deg;: 0.29% against 0.42%; 3&deg;: 0.07% against 0.15%) and slightly the 20 ms offset's (0.71% against 0.83%; at 2&ndash;10 ms est is 0.05&ndash;0.2 pp worse). Its readout stays at 33.0 ms (10&deg;) and drifts to 31.8 ms at 20 ms.
- **Unscored.** Part T's own baselines (smooth sum-of-sines rates, exact gyro): none 3.31% (29 of 30 converged), known 1.75%, est **1.00%**: on these smoother paths the free readout beats the known one even with a perfect gyro, which part B did not show (1.13% against 1.14%); not examined. Against the clean f, the worst fault tested (20 ms, known) leaves |e| 1.91%, still under part T's none 3.31%.
- **Reading.** Of the three ways a real camera&ndash;IMU rig is wrong, the one that matters is the product of the gyro's scale and the readout time. Held at a spec-sheet value 5% off, it biases f by about a quarter of the error, always the same way, which pooling windows (milestone 3d) would not average out. Left free, the window absorbs it exactly. Misalignment and timing are forgiving: a 3&deg; rotation or a 5 ms offset costs under 0.15%, below the 1.1% the window's own noise leaves, and even 10&deg; or 20 ms stays under 1%. For a practitioner: estimate the readout in the window rather than importing it, align the gyro to a few degrees and sync it to within 10 ms. For the TUM test: motion-capture rates with a few degrees of body-to-camera rotation error and under 10 ms of timing error are good enough, provided the readout is free (TUM's actual mocap&ndash;camera timing error is not sourced here).
- **Caveat.** Synthetic, milestone 3k's scene and path models, no SIFT front end or outliers; &omega; constant over each readout; part T's sinusoid band (0.5&ndash;3 Hz) is chosen, not measured; one rate level; fault sizes swept, none from a datasheet; the faults were not estimated, only tolerated; the one-sign scale result is unexplained and only s &gt; 1 was run.

## 2026-11-18 · Milestone 3m planned: does milestone 3l's gyro fix survive a wrong gyro (a camera&ndash;IMU rotation off by 1&ndash;10&deg;, a 2&ndash;5% scale error, a 2&ndash;20 ms time offset)?

Written before `experiments/m3m_gyrofault/run.py` was first run in full and before `analyse.py` was written. Milestone 3l's caveat: its estimator was given the generator's own gyro model, so it showed the rolling shutter is identifiable, not that the fix survives the faults a real camera&ndash;IMU rig has. The TUM test (per-keyframe mocap rates, readout free) is still blocked: `curl` CONNECT 403 today for cvg.cit.tum.de, webshare.cvg.cit.tum.de and vision.in.tum.de. Mocap rates on TUM would themselves carry a time offset and a body-to-camera rotation error (the mocap markers are on the Kinect's body, not its optical centre), so this milestone also says how good those rates must be. **Seen before this plan:** one timing job (the first part B window, all 26 solves converged in 47 s); no focal length was printed.

- **Method** (`experiments/m3m_gyrofault/run.py`). Milestone 3l's estimator (`rsba.Window`, all five intrinsics free, its start), milestone 3k's part B windows at &sigma; = 0.3 rad/s (30 seeds, 1/30 s readout, 0.5 px noise) and milestone 3l's gyro noise draw. Each window is solved with the readout known (**known**) and free from 0 (**est**) under a gyro that is **R**otated, &omega;<sub>meas</sub> = R(&theta;)&omega;, &theta; &isin; {1, 3, 10}&deg; about a random axis per window; **S**caled, &omega;<sub>meas</sub> = s&omega;, s &isin; {1.02, 1.05}; or **T**ime-offset, &omega;<sub>meas</sub>(t<sub>k</sub>) = &omega;(t<sub>k</sub> + &delta;), &delta; &isin; {2, 5, 10, 20} ms. Part T needs &omega; as a function of time, so its windows keep milestone 3k's scenes and linear velocities with &omega;(t) per axis a sum of three sinusoids at U(0.5, 3) Hz, random phases, sd 0.3 rad/s per axis (part B's &sigma;), held constant over each keyframe's readout as milestone 3k's generator does. None of these fault sizes is from a datasheet or a calibration report; they are swept. Scored quantity: the paired shift **&Delta;** = (f<sub>fault</sub> &minus; f<sub>exact</sub>)/f<sub>true</sub> against the same window, pixel noise and model with the exact gyro, which removes the 0.3&ndash;1.4% estimation noise milestone 3l's post hoc found. First-order expectation: &Delta; &asymp; (relative rate error) &times; milestone 3l's none shift (4.17%).
- **Check, not a hypothesis.** The exact-gyro clean, none, known and est solves of parts R and S equal milestone 3l's (median |&Delta;f|/f &le; 1e-6).
- **H178 (calibration-grade alignment is harmless).** &theta; = 1&deg;, known: median |&Delta;| &le; 0.1%.
- **H179 (misalignment grows about linearly and stays below the noise).** &theta; = 10&deg;, known: median |&Delta;| &le; 1.0%, and median |&Delta;|(10&deg;) / median |&Delta;|(1&deg;) &isin; [5, 20].
- **H180 (a free readout absorbs a scale error exactly).** S, est: median |&Delta;| &le; 0.01% at both scales, and median s&middot;t&#770;<sub>r</sub>(s)/t&#770;<sub>r</sub>(1) within 0.5% of 1. (The model sees &omega; only as the product &omega;t<sub>r</sub>; a failure would be a solver artefact.)
- **H181 (with the readout known, a scale error costs about its own size times the shutter's shift).** S = 1.05, known: median |&Delta;| &isin; [0.05, 0.4]%.
- **H182 (a time offset of 10 ms is tolerable).** T, known: median |&Delta;| &le; 0.6% at &delta; = 10 ms and &le; 1.2% at 20 ms.
- **H183 (a free readout does not rescue a rotated or late gyro).** median |&Delta;<sub>est</sub>| &ge; 0.8 &times; median |&Delta;<sub>known</sub>| at &theta; = 10&deg; and at &delta; = 20 ms (both must hold).
- **Reported, unscored.** Per fault and model: median and range of &Delta; and of e against the clean f; median t&#770;<sub>r</sub>; the rms rate error per &delta; and |&Delta;| against relative rate error (Spearman); part T's none and known |e| (its own baseline); convergence; `gyrofault.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Synthetic, milestone 3k's scene and path models, no SIFT front end or outliers; &omega; constant over each readout (no vibration within a frame); the sinusoid bandwidth of part T is chosen, not measured; misalignment and offset are fixed per window and unmodelled by the estimator (estimating them is the next step if they matter); one rate level (&sigma; = 0.3 rad/s).

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3l result: with a gyro, a rolling-shutter term in the window solve cuts milestone 3k's hand-held focal shift from 4.17% to 1.14% (0.71% with a free velocity), and one window estimates the readout to 3.6% with no false shutter on global-shutter data; five of six predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3l_gyro/run.py` (76 windows, 9 models on part B &sigma; = 0.3, 5 elsewhere; under 20 min on 4 cores) then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `gyro.png`), as planned below. Every solve converged except milestone 3k's plain model at &sigma; = 1.0 (22 of 30). **Check passed:** this solver's clean and none f equal milestone 3k's nfba solves to a median 1.6e-6 and 4.2e-5 of f (8 of 76 none solves differ by more than 1%, the diverging &sigma; = 1.0 ones).
- **H172 refuted, narrowly.** Part B &sigma; = 0.3: median |e| **1.14%** with the gyro and known readout against **4.17%** without (ratio 0.27; bar 0.25). Range &minus;6.2% to +5.2% (none: &minus;18% to +14%).
- **H173 held.** Part A, pitch and yaw at 1 rad/s: 0.29% (none: 5.9%); pitch 0.47%, yaw 0.21%.
- **H174 held.** A free linear velocity per keyframe: 0.71% against 1.14%; median signed e 0.03% (known: 0.14%).
- **H175 held.** Readout free from 0: median t&#770;<sub>r</sub> 33.4 ms (range 30.6&ndash;37.8; true 33.3), median relative error 3.6%, median sd 0.23 ms; median |e| 1.13% (bar 2.28%). With u free too: 33.3 ms, 1.2%, |e| 0.80%.
- **H176 held.** Global-shutter rendering solved with a free readout: |t&#770;<sub>r</sub>| &le; 3.3 ms in 30 of 30 windows (range &minus;0.33 to +0.39 ms); f moves a median 0.01%.
- **H177 held.** 1 &deg;/s gyro bias: 1.15% against 1.14% (0.01 pp); 5 &deg;/s: 1.10%.
- **Unscored.** &sigma; = 1.0: none 18.9% (9 of 30 over 50%), known 1.0%, est 0.69% (readout 33.2 ms, 1.0% error), all converged. Part A with a free readout: yaw 32.6 ms (2.1% error) but **pitch 24.2 ms (27% error, range 16&ndash;33)** and f 0.99%; the null solve at constant pitch finds &minus;6.7 ms on global-shutter data. A steady pitch rate makes the rolling shutter a vertical stretch that f, c<sub>y</sub> and k can partly absorb, so the readout is weakly identified; random hand-held motion separates them. The remaining shift is 2&ndash;3 solver sd (known_u 2.2, est 3.0), so the solver's own sd still understates it.
- **Post hoc** (`explore.py`, `explore.json`; written after the verdicts above were read). Six part B &sigma; = 0.3 windows with the full model (gyro, known readout, free u): with the pixel noise off, f is recovered to under 1.4e-7 of f in all six, so the model and solver are exact and the 0.3&ndash;1.4% left with 0.5 px noise is estimation noise on a 36-parameter-larger model, not a bias. The two renderings share 99.2&ndash;99.7% of their observations.
- **Reading.** Given a gyro, a rolling shutter is a solvable nuisance in a 1.2 s target-free window, not a hard floor: the shift falls by about 4&times; with the readout known and stays down when the window estimates the readout itself, which it does to a few percent from ordinary hand-held motion while finding no shutter on a global-shutter camera. A 5 &deg;/s gyro bias did not matter here: its rotation over one readout (at most 2.9 mrad per axis) is small beside that of the 0.3 rad/s rates (about 10 mrad), and why it moves f even less than that ratio suggests was not examined. For a practitioner: put the gyro in the window solve, leave the readout free, and avoid steady single-axis calibration motions. For the programme: the TUM test is now specified (per-keyframe mocap rates, readout free); a readout near zero rules the shutter out as what TUM's f compensates, a consistent one rules it in and measures the Kinect's readout.
- **Caveat.** Synthetic, milestone 3k's one scene and path model, no SIFT front end or outliers; the estimator's shutter model equals the generator's (constant &omega; and u over one readout), so this shows identifiability and the size of the fix, not robustness to a wrong model (vibration within a frame, a gyro time offset, a wrong camera&ndash;IMU rotation); the gyro noise and bias are not from a datasheet.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3l planned: with a gyro, does a rolling-shutter term in the 1.2 s window solve remove milestone 3k's focal shift, and can one window estimate the readout time itself?

Written before `experiments/m3l_gyro/run.py` was first run in full and before `analyse.py` was written. Milestone 3k found that a 1/30 s rolling shutter moves a short window's f by a median 4.2% under 0.3 rad/s hand-held rates, with a sign set by the window's geometry, so no mean-rate correction can undo it. The fix a robot would use is to model the shutter in the solve with the angular velocity its IMU already measures (Karpenko et al. 2011; Hedborg et al. 2012; Oth et al. 2013 estimate the readout with a target). This milestone asks whether that fix works inside the project's own window, and whether the readout time is identifiable from one target-free 1.2 s window, which is what the TUM test (per-frame mocap rates, readout unknown) would need. The TUM host still refuses the proxy (connection failed for cvg.cit.tum.de, webshare.cvg.cit.tum.de, vision.in.tum.de). **Seen before this plan:** one timing job (part B, &sigma; = 0.3, the first seed): every model below converged in 11&ndash;42 iterations, about 20 s; its focal lengths were not printed.

- **Method** (`experiments/m3l_gyro/`). `rsba.py`: milestone 3b's window model plus a rolling-shutter term, a point seen at row v exposed at &tau; = t<sub>r</sub>(v &minus; H/2)/H, x<sub>cam</sub> = exp([&omega;<sub>i</sub>&tau;]<sub>&times;</sub>)(R<sub>i</sub>X + t<sub>i</sub>) + u<sub>i</sub>&tau;, &omega;<sub>i</sub> the keyframe's gyro reading, solved by the same robust Schur-complement Levenberg&ndash;Marquardt as `nfba.bundle` (soft-L1 at 1 px, all five intrinsics free, milestone 3k's start). Data: milestone 3k's own windows, motion and noise draws (part B, both &sigma;; part A pitch and yaw at 1 rad/s), rendered at t<sub>r</sub> = 1/30 s. Models: **clean** (global-shutter rendering, no term; the reference f), **none** (rolling-shutter rendering, no term: milestone 3k's solve in this solver), **known** (gyro &omega;, t<sub>r</sub> = 1/30 s given, u = 0), **known_u** (plus a free u per keyframe), **est** (t<sub>r</sub> free from 0, u = 0), **est_u** (t<sub>r</sub> and u free), **bias1** and **bias5** (known, with a gyro bias of 1 and 5 &deg;/s per axis, random sign, fixed over the window), **null** (global-shutter rendering solved with est). Gyro white noise 0.1 &deg;/s per axis per keyframe. The bias and noise are not from a datasheet (the Bosch and TDK product pages were unreachable from this container), so the bias is swept. e = (f &minus; f<sub>clean</sub>)/f<sub>true</sub>; known_u, est_u, bias1, bias5 run on part B &sigma; = 0.3 only. `tests/test_m3l.py` (4 tests: zero &tau; equals `nfba.project`; at the generator's own row times the model equals milestone 3k's camera to 1e-6 px; known shutter recovers f within 0.3% on a small noiseless-ish window; a free t<sub>r</sub> is recovered within 10% there) passed before this plan.
- **Check, not a hypothesis.** This solver's clean and none f should match milestone 3k's nfba solves of the same windows (median |&Delta;f|/f &le; 0.1%); reported either way.
- **H172 (a gyro and a known readout remove the shift).** Part B &sigma; = 0.3: median |e<sub>known</sub>| &le; 0.25 &times; median |e<sub>none</sub>|.
- **H173 (rotation only: near exact).** Part A pitch and yaw at 1 rad/s pooled: median |e<sub>known</sub>| &le; 0.5%.
- **H174 (translation is the residue).** Part B &sigma; = 0.3: median |e<sub>known_u</sub>| &lt; median |e<sub>known</sub>|.
- **H175 (the readout is identifiable from one window).** Part B &sigma; = 0.3, est: median |t&#770;<sub>r</sub> &minus; t<sub>r</sub>|/t<sub>r</sub> &le; 0.2 and median |e<sub>est</sub>| &le; 2 &times; median |e<sub>known</sub>|.
- **H176 (no shutter where there is none).** null, part B &sigma; = 0.3: |t&#770;<sub>r</sub>| &le; 0.1 &times; (1/30 s) in &ge; 90% of windows.
- **H177 (a 1 &deg;/s gyro bias does not matter).** Part B &sigma; = 0.3: |median |e<sub>bias1</sub>| &minus; median |e<sub>known</sub>|| &le; 0.3 percentage points.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per model and part: median and range of e, convergence, sd<sub>f</sub>, the shift in sd<sub>f</sub> units; t&#770;<sub>r</sub> and its sd for est, est_u and null; bias5; &sigma; = 1.0 (where milestone 3k's solves diverged in 9 of 30) for none, known and est; `gyro.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Synthetic, milestone 3k's scene and path model, no SIFT front end or outliers; the estimator's shutter model is the generator's (constant &omega; and u over one readout), so a pass shows identifiability and the size of the fix, not robustness to a wrong shutter model; the gyro's extrinsic rotation and time offset are taken as known; constant &omega; per readout ignores vibration within a frame.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3k result: a rolling shutter can put a TUM-sized error into a 1.2 s window's focal length (random 0.3 rad/s hand-held rates, 1/30 s readout: median shift 4.2%), but its sign and size depend on the window's geometry, so the window's mean rotation rate does not bound it (R<sup>2</sup> 0.03); two of five predictions held

- **Run.** `experiments/m3k_shutter/run.py` (156 jobs, each a global- and a rolling-shutter solve of the same scene and noise; about 6 min on 4 cores) then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `shutter.png`), as planned below. Every part A solve and every &sigma; = 0.3 part B solve converged; at &sigma; = 1.0, 24 of 30 converged and 9 of 30 shifted f by more than 50% (&minus;80% to +5000%: diverged). The global-shutter solves land a median 0.31% from the true f. `tests/test_m3k.py` (4 tests) passed before the run.
- **H167 held.** Pitch: median shift &minus;1.1%, &minus;2.5%, &minus;4.5%, &minus;7.3% at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2 rad/s; ratios 2:1 = 1.64, 0.5:1 = 0.56. Roughly linear up to 1 rad/s, flattening at 2.
- **H168 held.** Pitch at 1 rad/s: median |shift| **6.4%** against 1.3% from the isotropic first-order stretch (4.8&times;). The window's solve amplifies a vertical stretch it cannot represent, and the sign is not the stretch's: per seed &minus;8.7% to **+10.9%** (2 of 8 positive).
- **H169 refuted.** Yaw moves f as much as pitch (median |shift| at 1 rad/s 5.6%, 0.88&times; pitch's; median sign positive, per seed &minus;10.7% to +13.9%); only roll is small (1.2%, 0.19&times;). Yaw's shear is not absorbed by c<sub>x</sub> alone in a short window.
- **H170 refuted.** Hand-held random rates: the shift does not follow the window's mean pitch rate (OLS slope &minus;96, R<sup>2</sup> 0.03, both &sigma; pooled, the diverged solves included as planned). Post hoc, converged solves only, a regression on all three mean rates explains R<sup>2</sup> 0.18 (&sigma; = 0.3) and 0.13 (&sigma; = 1.0).
- **H171 refuted.** At each TUM window's mocap mean rate (fr1/desk 0.38, fr1/xyz 0.14, fr3/stf 0.08 rad/s median) with all of it in pitch, the implied shift reaches half the observed error in 10 of 57 windows (18%; bar 50%); median implied 0.53% against observed 5.25%. Explaining each window's error that way would take 1.8&times; (fr1/desk), 8.4&times; (fr1/xyz) and 20&times; (fr3/stf) its mocap mean rate.
- **Unscored.** Part B, &sigma; = 0.3 rad/s per axis with 0.3 m/s translation rates: median |shift| **4.2%**, sd 7.2%, range &minus;18% to +14%; &sigma; = 1.0: median 19%. The shift is 4&ndash;39 global-shutter sd<sub>f</sub> (pitch and yaw, 0.25&ndash;2 rad/s), so a window's own covariance never flags it. k<sub>1</sub> moves with f under pitch (&minus;0.08 at 1 rad/s).
- **Post hoc** (`explore.py`, `explore.json`; written after the verdicts above were read, not pre-registered). Rotation alone, &sigma; = 0.1 rad/s: median |shift| 0.98% (13% of windows over 2%); &sigma; = 0.3: 2.7% (67% over 2%); translation alone at 0.3 m/s: 1.0% (20% over 2%). The rotation term dominates; translation adds about a percent.
- **Reading.** The bound this milestone set out to compute does not exist in the form planned: a rolling shutter's effect on a short window's f is not a small, sign-stable stretch scaled by the mean rate. Pitch and yaw rates both move f, by about 1.3&times; the rotation during readout (in radians), with a sign set by the window's geometry, and with hand-held random rates the effect is as large as TUM's observed error (4.2% at 0.3 rad/s per axis and the longest readout; 1% at 0.1 rad/s). So the rolling shutter is **not ruled out** as what the natural-feature f compensates, and the earlier finding that rotation does not predict error (milestone 3e, Spearman +0.04, again here) is no evidence against it, because the mean rate is the wrong predictor. Against it: fr3/stf, the slowest sequence (0.08 rad/s mean), has the largest error (7.2%), which random rates near its mean would not produce (0.1 rad/s gives about 1%) unless its high-frequency jitter or readout differ. The test that settles it needs the per-frame angular velocity (TUM's motion capture runs at 100 Hz) and a rolling-shutter term in the window model, with the readout time from a source; both wait for the TUM host.
- **Caveat.** Synthetic: one scene shape and path model, one camera, no SIFT front end or outliers; the readout is the 1/30 s upper bound, not a sourced Kinect figure; part A's 8 seeds per cell give rough medians; the TUM side bounds with the window's largest rotation, not its per-frame rates.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3k planned: can a rolling shutter put a 5% error into a 1.2 s window's focal length? A synthetic sweep through the project's own solver, set against the TUM windows' motion-capture rates

Written before `experiments/m3k_shutter/run.py` was first run in full and before `analyse.py` was run. Milestones 3f&ndash;3j ruled out the static intrinsics (distortion, principal point) as what a natural-feature window's f compensates; the README's next candidates are a rolling shutter, track bias and longer windows. The TUM host refuses the proxy again in this session (`curl` CONNECT 403 for cvg.cit.tum.de; webshare and vision.in.tum.de time out), so the track-bias re-track and longer windows wait, and this milestone asks how large the rolling-shutter effect can be from first principles and the motion already stored. Which camera TUM's Kinect units use and their readout time are not sourced here, so the readout is set to the whole 30 Hz frame period, 1/30 s, an upper bound for any 30 Hz rolling shutter; the effect is linear in rate &times; readout to first order, so results scale down for a shorter readout. **Seen before this plan:** one pilot job (part A, pitch at 1 rad/s, seed 0): the rolling-shutter solve moved f by **+10.9%** while the isotropic first-order stretch model below predicts &minus;1.3%; the hypotheses are set knowing that one number.

- **Method** (`experiments/m3k_shutter/`). `rs.py`: a rolling-shutter camera, row v exposed at &tau; = t<sub>r</sub>(v &minus; c<sub>y</sub>)/H, camera moving with angular velocity &omega; and linear velocity u (camera frame) during readout, solved as a fixed point (10 iterations, under 1e-6 px). Synthetic windows shaped like milestone 3b's: 12 keyframes over 1.1 s on a smooth hand-held path (15&deg; rotation, 0.25 m translation, small jitter), 400 points 1.5&ndash;5 m away, 0.5 px noise, fr1 board intrinsics (f 518.28, c (315.7, 260.1), k<sub>1</sub> 0.25, k<sub>2</sub> &minus;0.75). Each window is solved twice with milestone 3b's `nfba.bundle` (all five intrinsics free, start f &times; 1.08, centred c, k = 0, the start milestone 3h's tests use): once rendered global-shutter, once rolling-shutter, same scene and noise; e = (f<sub>rs</sub> &minus; f<sub>gs</sub>)/f<sub>true</sub> is the shutter's own shift. **Part A:** constant &omega; about one camera axis (pitch x, yaw y, roll z) at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2 rad/s, 8 seeds each. **Part B:** hand-held, each keyframe's &omega; drawn N(0, &sigma;<sup>2</sup>) per axis, &sigma; &isin; {0.3, 1.0} rad/s, u N(0, 0.3<sup>2</sup>) m/s per axis, 30 seeds each. The **isotropic first-order** model (`rs.first_order_bias`): a pitch rate stretches rows by s = &omega;t<sub>r</sub>f/H, and an isotropic f fitted by least squares takes the vertical share of it, e<sub>iso</sub> = &minus;s &Sigma;dy<sup>2</sup>/&Sigma;(dx<sup>2</sup> + dy<sup>2</sup>). The **gain** g = median e/(&omega;t<sub>r</sub>) for pitch at 1 rad/s. `tests/test_m3k.py` (4 tests: zero readout equals `nfba.project`; the fixed point converges; each point's row time matches its row; a pitch rate is a vertical stretch of the predicted size) passed before this plan.
- **H167 (the shift is linear in rate).** Pitch, median e at 2 rad/s over median e at 1 rad/s in [1.6, 2.4], and at 0.5 over 1 in [0.4, 0.6].
- **H168 (the window amplifies the stretch).** Pitch at 1 rad/s: median |e| &ge; 2&times; |median e<sub>iso</sub>|. (The pilot suggests far more, and of the opposite sign.)
- **H169 (pitch is the axis that matters).** At 1 rad/s, median |e| for yaw and for roll each &le; 0.5&times; pitch's.
- **H170 (hand-held: the mean pitch rate predicts the shift).** Part B, both &sigma; pooled: ordinary least squares of e on g &middot; t<sub>r</sub> &middot; (mean over keyframes of &omega;<sub>x</sub>) has slope in [0.5, 1.5] and R<sup>2</sup> &ge; 0.5.
- **H171 (a rolling shutter is a plausible cause on TUM).** For milestone 3c's 57 scored windows, take each window's mean rate &omega;&#772; = milestone 3e's motion-capture rotation (largest between any two keyframes) / 1.1 s, put all of it in pitch at t<sub>r</sub> = 1/30 s: the implied |e| = |g| &omega;&#772; t<sub>r</sub> is &ge; half the observed |e| in &ge; 50% of windows. This is generous to the rolling shutter (all rotation in pitch, constant sign, the longest readout); a refutation says it cannot be the main cause even then.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per axis and rate: median e, g, the shift in units of the global-shutter sd<sub>f</sub>, the k<sub>1</sub> shift; part B: sd and median |e| per &sigma;; per TUM sequence: median observed |e|, mocap rate, implied |e|, and the rate that would be needed to explain |e| (and its ratio to the mocap rate); Spearman of rate against |e| (milestone 3e found +0.04 with the rotation itself); `shutter.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Synthetic: one scene shape, one path model, one camera; the global-shutter solve itself is not the TUM solve (no SIFT front end, no outliers). The TUM side has no per-keyframe pitch rate (only the window's largest rotation is stored; the video and motion capture are refused today), so H171 can only bound, not attribute; the sign of TUM's error cannot be tested against the sign of the pitch here. Hand-held jitter faster than the 0.1 s keyframe spacing is not in the mocap rate and could make instantaneous rates larger than &omega;&#772;.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3j result: re-solved on the TUM video, the board's distortion is worse than first order said (median focal error 5.25% to 10.5%, f above the board's in 52 of 53 windows), the principal point mildly worse (5.87%), all four 10.7%; first order gets the size right on median but the sign wrong in a quarter of the distortion re-solves

- **Run.** `experiments/m3j_tumpin/run.py` (about 12 min on 4 cores after a 1.3 GB download) then `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `tumpin.png`). All 57 windows rebuilt; 4 of 57 k-pinned re-solves hit the iteration cap and are excluded (fr1/desk 16, fr1/xyz 2, 13, 16); every c and ck re-solve converged with its pins held. `tests/test_m3j.py` (4 tests, the scoring on known cases) passed before the scored analysis.
- **Deviation from the plan.** The plan said the free f would be asserted equal to milestone 3c's. 51 of 57 rebuilt windows match it to 1e-6, 55 to 0.1%; fr3/stf 0 differs by 0.18% and fr3/stf 12 by 3.35% (this session's OpenCV SIFT build). Instead of failing the run, `analyse.py` scores every hypothesis on all 57 (the free solve of this run as the baseline for each pin) and again on the 55 reproduced windows; every verdict is the same on both (`summary.json`, `reproduced_only`).
- **H161 held.** k pinned: median |e<sub>p</sub>| **10.46%** against 5.25% free (first order from the fresh covariances 8.37%; milestone 3g's 8.72%; 3h's shrunk transfer 7.12%). The re-solve is worse than every earlier estimate. f ends above the board's f in 52 of 53 windows. Per sequence: fr1/desk 11.3%, fr1/xyz **18.1%**, fr3/stf 6.1% (free 2.80%, 5.18%, 7.24%).
- **H162 held, narrowly.** c pinned: median |e<sub>p</sub>| **5.87%** against 5.25% (first order 6.75%). The sign differs by sequence: fr1/desk 2.80% to 3.40%, fr1/xyz 5.18% to 7.10%, **fr3/stf 7.24% to 5.88%**.
- **H163 held.** All four pinned: **10.74%** (first order 7.22%).
- **H164 refuted.** First order and re-solve moved f the same way in 74% (k), 95% (c) and 70% (ck) of windows; bar 90%. The k disagreements are not only tiny steps: their median first-order step is 3.5% of f (agreements 9.2%), and among steps of 2% or more 78% agree.
- **H165 held.** Median re-solved/first-order step ratio 0.91 (k), 1.01 (c), 0.87 (ck), inside [0.67, 1.5]; fr1/xyz's k ratio 0.67 sits on the edge and its ck ratio is 0.43.
- **H166 held.** k pinned: median relative gap between the re-solved and first-order sd ratios 0.078 (bar 0.2).
- **Unscored.** Median robust-cost rise when pinned: k 3.2% (fr1/desk 5.1%, fr1/xyz 6.6%, fr3/stf 0.4%), c 1.2%, ck 5.3% (fr1/xyz 11.8%). The step-binned ratio is noisy below a 2% step (single windows with ratios from &minus;256 to +158 where the first-order step is near zero) and 0.9&ndash;1.0 for k at 2&ndash;10%. One window (fr3/stf 19) has a first-order step of &plusmn;6&times; f, a degenerate covariance, against a re-solved +27%; it is off the figure's scale.
- **Reading.** Re-solving confirms the first-order verdicts of milestones 3g and 3i and makes the distortion one sharper. The fr1 Kinect's natural-feature windows do not accept the board's distortion: pinning it raises the robust cost 5&ndash;7% and drives f up by 10&ndash;18%, above the board's own f in nearly every window. That is not a linearisation artefact; on TUM it is larger than first order predicted. fr3, whose images TUM ships already undistorted, barely resists the pin (0.4% cost) and its error barely moves. So the windows' free k and f are a matched pair: the board's k is the wrong partner for any f the window supports. The principal point is a weaker, sequence-dependent lever: it helps the far, textured fr3/stf scene and hurts both fr1 ones. For a robot, the practical rule from 3f&ndash;3i now holds on the re-solve: in a 1.2 s natural-feature window, leave the intrinsics free and pool windows (milestone 3d); do not import the board's distortion. The question of what the free f compensates stays open (rolling shutter, motion, track bias); the TUM video is now on disk, so those are testable.
