- **Caveat.** 57 windows, three sequences, two Kinect units; the TUM reference is the board calibration the error is scored against; each pinned solve starts at the free solution and may stop in a nearby local minimum; 2 windows did not reproduce 3c's free f exactly (verdicts unchanged without them).

Written before `experiments/m3j_tumpin/run.py` was first run and before any pinned TUM solve was computed. From that solution, milestone 3h's `bundle_pinned` re-solves with **k** = (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>), **c** = (c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>) and **ck** = all four pinned at the board's Zhang k<sub>1</sub> + k<sub>2</sub> values (milestone 3i's `board_ref`, asserted equal to 3i's). First order f<sub>c</sub> from the fresh covariance with milestone 3g's formula. Error e = (f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>)/f<sub>ref</sub> against milestone 3c's per-window reference, as in 3c&ndash;3i.

- **H163 (pinning all four does not rescue it).** Median |e<sub>p,ck</sub>| &ge; the free median |e| (3i first order 7.17%).

- **H166 (the narrowed bar is real on TUM).** For k, median |(sd<sub>p</sub>/sd<sub>free</sub>) &minus; r<sub>c</sub>|/r<sub>c</sub> &le; 0.2 (r<sub>c</sub> the first-order sd ratio), as 3h's H152 on board windows.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence: median |e| free and pinned, median step and ratio; the robust cost rise when pinned (how hard the data resist each pin); the ratio by first-order step bin (3h's bins); `tumpin.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** 57 windows, three sequences, two Kinect units; the TUM reference is the board calibration the error is scored against; a pinned solve is started at the free solution and can stop in a nearby local minimum; the rebuild relies on OpenCV reproducing 3c's SIFT tracks (asserted, not assumed).

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3i result: the principal point is not what a natural-feature window's focal length compensates: pinning the board's (c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>) raises the TUM median error from 5.25% to 6.76% (first order), while on clean board windows the same pin, re-solved, lowers it from 0.55% to 0.37%

- **Run.** `tum.py` (seconds; analysis only) and `run.py` (about 2 min on 4 cores) then `analyse.py`, exactly as planned below. Part A: 57 windows; the script reproduced milestone 3g's f<sub>c2</sub> for every one and the board reference reproduced milestone 4n's f and d. Part B: 149 of 150 windows converged (the same left 4-view draw as milestone 3h hit the iteration cap); every free solution equalled 3h's (asserted); all 447 principal-point re-solves and all 149 four-term re-solves converged with their pins held. `tests/test_m3i.py` (4 tests) passed before the run.
- **H154 refuted.** Median |e<sub>c</sub>| **6.76%** against 5.25% as solved (ratio 1.29; bar 0.8). Per sequence: fr1/desk 2.80% to 4.58%, fr1/xyz 5.18% to 7.72%, fr3/stf 7.24% to 7.26%.
- **H155 refuted.** Pinning (c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) together: median |e<sub>ck</sub>| **7.17%**, worse than none (5.25%), better than k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> alone (milestone 3g, 8.72%). fr1/xyz 12.0% (3g 16.5%).
- **H156 refuted.** Spearman of the signed principal-point step against the signed as-solved error **&minus;0.22** (bar &le; &minus;0.4): the step barely knows which way the window is wrong.
- **H157 held.** Median |step| 2.8% of f (four-term 6.0%), inside the range 3h checked.
- **H158 held.** On the board windows the first-order principal-point step matches the re-solve: median relative gap 0.089, sign agreed in 96% of windows; re-solved/first-order ratio 1.01, 1.02, 1.04 at t = 0.5, 1, 2 (steps a median 0.1&ndash;0.4% of f).
- **H159 held.** On clean board corners the board's principal point helps f: median |f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>|/f<sub>ref</sub> 0.55% free, **0.37%** pinned (ratio 0.66; better or equal in five of six camera-size cells; right camera 6 views 0.52% to 0.53%).
- **H160 refuted.** All four of c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> pinned: 0.47% (ratio 0.85; bar 0.6): adding the board's distortion to its principal point gives back part of the gain, as 3h's k-only pin (0.65%) suggested it would.
- **Unscored.** TUM windows sit far from the board's principal point: median c offset (window &minus; board) fr1/desk (+4.1, &minus;5.0) px, fr1/xyz (+4.9, **+32.9**) px, fr3/stf (**+23.4**, &minus;10.5) px; on the board windows median |offsets| 1.4&ndash;7.0 px. Yet f and c are only weakly coupled in each window's covariance: median corr(f, c<sub>x</sub>) about 0.0, corr(f, c<sub>y</sub>) &minus;0.13 to &minus;0.28; conditional-to-marginal sd ratio 0.95&ndash;0.97. TUM's published principal point instead of the board's: median |e| 4.24%, 7.89%, 7.18% per sequence, about the same as the board's. Random-effects K = 5 pooling of the c-conditioned f: median error 2.06% (raw 2.45%) but rms z 1.27 (raw 1.10), fr1/xyz rms z 1.78.
- **Reading.** Two findings that look opposed and are not. On a planar board seen in 4&ndash;8 views, the window's principal point is the weak link and the board's value is a good prior (one third off the focal error), and the linearisation is accurate there. In 1.2 s of natural-feature video the windows' principal points wander tens of pixels from the board's, yet f barely leans on them (correlation &minus;0.1 to &minus;0.3), so pinning c moves f by a few percent in a direction unrelated to its error and adds scatter. Together with milestones 3f&ndash;3h this rules out the static intrinsics as what f compensates in short natural-feature windows: neither the distortion nor the principal point, nor both, pinned at the board's values, brings the median error under the as-solved 5.25%. What is left is outside the pinhole-plus-radial model: the Kinect's rolling shutter, the window's motion (milestone 3e found the axis swing does not predict it), or feature-track bias. For a robot the practical rule from 3f&ndash;3h stands and extends: in short natural-feature windows leave every intrinsic free and pool windows (milestone 3d); a board prior on c or k is only worth pinning when the data are board-like.
- **Caveat.** As planned: Part A is first order (Part B shows the principal-point linearisation is accurate at 0.1&ndash;0.4% steps on board windows; TUM's steps are about 2.8%, untested by re-solve); the TUM reference is the board calibration the error is scored against; 57 windows, three sequences; Part B is two cameras of one rig and a planar target. The TUM re-solve with c, k or both pinned is milestone 3j and still needs the video.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3h result: the first-order conditioning tracks the pinned re-solve (re-solve/first-order step 0.97 at the board's distortion, sign agreed in 99% of windows) but overshoots as the step grows (0.85 at 2&ndash;5% of f, 0.74 at 5&ndash;10%); the narrowed bar is real; shrunk by that overshoot, milestone 3g's verdict stands (median error 7.1% against 5.25% unpinned)

- **H149 held.** At t = 1, median |f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f<sub>c</sub>| / |f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f| **0.13**; sign agreed in **98.7%** of windows. But the steps at t = 1 are small here (median 0.21% of f): on these clean board windows the solved k is already near Zhang's.
- **H150 held.** Spearman of the gap |f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f<sub>c</sub>|/f against the first-order step **+0.69** over 1043 pairs.
- **H151 held, on the minimum count.** Exactly 20 pairs had a first-order step of 5&ndash;15%; median (f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f)/(f<sub>c</sub> &minus; f) **0.77** (bar 0.67&ndash;1.5). All 20 are steps that lower f; milestone 3g's steps raise it.
- **H152 held.** At t = 1, the re-solved sd of f with k pinned over the free sd (median 0.976) matches the first-order ratio (0.962) to a median 1.7%.
- **H153 refuted.** Pinning the board's (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) does not help even on clean corners: median |f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>|/f<sub>ref</sub> 0.55% free, **0.65%** pinned (ratio 1.17; bar 0.6). Per camera and window size the pinned error is higher in five of six cells (left, 6 views: 0.47% to 0.45%).
- **Unscored.** Median re-solve/first-order ratio by first-order step: &lt;0.25%: 0.96; 0.25&ndash;0.5%: 0.96; 0.5&ndash;1%: 0.94; 1&ndash;2%: 0.92; 2&ndash;5%: 0.85 (n 73); 5&ndash;10%: 0.74 (n 18); 10&ndash;15%: 1.03 (n 2); the overshoot is systematic, the re-solved step shorter than the predicted one. By t: 1.00, 0.99, 0.97, 0.95, 0.91, 0.88, 0.84 at t = 0.25 to 4.
- **Post hoc** (`post_hoc_3g` in `analyse.py`, added after H149&ndash;H153 were scored): scaling each of milestone 3g's 57 two-term steps by this milestone's median ratio for its step bin gives median |error| **7.12%** (3g first order 8.72%, unpinned 5.25%); fr1/desk 5.72%, fr1/xyz 11.39% (signed +10.4%), fr3/stf 6.19% (unpinned 7.24%). A transfer from board windows to TUM windows, indicative only.
- **Reading.** On this solver and real images, milestone 3g's first-order formula is a fair guide to the re-solve: right in direction almost always, right in size to within a few percent below a 1% step, and about a quarter too long at 5&ndash;10%. Its narrowed error bar is the re-solve's bar too, so 3g's warning about a confidently wrong estimate is not an artefact of the linearisation. Shrinking 3g's steps by the overshoot measured here does not rescue the board prior on the Kinect (fr1/xyz still +10%), so the open question shifts from "does the linearisation mislead?" to "what does f compensate for that the window model lacks?". And even on clean board windows, where solved and board distortion nearly agree, pinning the board's distortion slightly worsens f: the free solve's own k is the better partner for its f.
- **Caveat.** As planned: board corners on a planar target, not natural features; two cameras of one rig; the 5&ndash;15% band holds only 20 pairs, all of one sign; the reference shares images with the windows. The TUM re-solve is now milestone 3i.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3h planned: does the first-order conditioning of milestones 3f and 3g predict the re-solve? Checked on real board images, where the re-solve can be run

Written before `experiments/m3h_linear/run.py` was first run and before any pinned re-solve or conditioned f was computed. Milestone 3g's caveat was that its two-term step leaves the linearisation's range on fr1/xyz (a 13.6% step), and its re-solve needs the TUM video, which this session's proxy still refuses (CONNECT 403 on cvg.cit.tum.de, vision.in.tum.de, cdn2.vision.in.tum.de and huggingface.co, probed today). The question underneath 3f and 3g can be asked where the re-solve is possible: does Gaussian conditioning on (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) under the solver's covariance land where the pinned bundle adjustment lands, and how does the gap grow with the step? Seen before this plan: one smoke solve of the left camera's first six views (k free, f 537.8 against Zhang's 536.3), to time the solver; no conditioning or pinned solve.

- **Method** (`experiments/m3h_linear/`). OpenCV's stereo set (`data/`, two cameras, 13 views each). Windows: 25 seeded random subsets of 4, 6 and 8 views per camera (150 windows). Correspondences are the detected 9&times;6 corners (`selfcal.detect`, subpix11), one track per corner, board geometry withheld. Free solve: milestone 3b's `nfba.bundle` unchanged (soft-L1, 1 px; f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>), started from `selfcal.init_fronto` at f<sub>0</sub> = 0.8, 1.0 and 1.25&times; the image width, lowest cost kept; covariance `nfba.intr_cov`. Reference k<sub>ref</sub>, f<sub>ref</sub>: Zhang k<sub>1</sub> + k<sub>2</sub> on all 13 views of that camera. Targets k<sub>t</sub> = k + t(k<sub>ref</sub> &minus; k), t &isin; {0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4} (t = 1 is the board's distortion; larger t reaches larger steps). Per target: f<sub>c</sub>, milestone 3g's conditioned f; f<sub>p</sub>, the re-solve with k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> pinned at k<sub>t</sub> by the same LM with those columns dropped (`pinned.bundle_pinned`, started at the free solution), and its sd of f. `tests/test_m3h.py` (4 tests: no pins reproduces `nfba.bundle`; pinned values stay and the true k recovers f on a synthetic scene; a tiny step matches the conditioning to 5% and the pinned sd matches the conditional sd ratio; the one-term formula) passed before this plan.
- **H149 (first order predicts the re-solve at the board's distortion).** At t = 1: median |f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f<sub>c</sub>| / |f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f| &le; 0.25 and sign(f<sub>c</sub> &minus; f) = sign(f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f) in &ge; 90% of windows. Refuted if either fails.
- **H150 (the gap grows with the step).** Spearman of |f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f<sub>c</sub>|/f against the first-order step |f<sub>c</sub> &minus; f|/f over all (window, t) pairs &ge; +0.5.
- **H151 (in milestone 3g's range).** Over pairs whose first-order step is 5&ndash;15% of f, the median of (f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f)/(f<sub>c</sub> &minus; f) lies in [0.67, 1.5]. Unscored (reported as such) if fewer than 20 pairs fall in that range.
- **H152 (the narrowed bar is real).** At t = 1, median |(sd<sub>p</sub>/sd<sub>free</sub>) &minus; r<sub>c</sub>| / r<sub>c</sub> &le; 0.2, where r<sub>c</sub> is the first-order conditional-to-marginal sd ratio.
- **H153 (on clean corners the board's distortion helps).** At t = 1, median |f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>|/f<sub>ref</sub> &le; 0.6&times; the free solve's median |f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>|/f<sub>ref</sub>.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per camera and window size: medians of the step, the ratio (f<sub>p</sub> &minus; f)/(f<sub>c</sub> &minus; f) by t, the error before and after pinning; the binned ratio against step; `linear.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Board corners on a planar target are not natural features in a 3-D scene: the windows are cleaner, the points coplanar and the covariance shape different from TUM's, so this tests the linearisation of this solver on real images, not the TUM windows themselves; the re-solve on TUM (now milestone 3i) remains the direct test. The reference shares images with the windows (Zhang uses the board geometry the windows do not get). Two cameras of one stereo rig.

## 2026-11-17 · Milestone 3g result: pinning both k<sub>1</sub> and k<sub>2</sub> at the board's values makes the focal error worse, not better (median 5.25% to 8.72%; fr1/xyz +12.9%), and narrows the bar by about 40% while doing it; at first order a board distortion prior does not rescue a 1.2 s window

- **Run.** `experiments/m3g_condk12/analyse.py` (seconds; analysis only), exactly as planned below; `tests/test_m3g.py` (4 tests: two-term conditioning against Monte Carlo draws of a 5-D Gaussian, the one-term case equal to milestone 3f's bivariate formula, the covariance scale cancelling, uncorrelated terms leaving f) passed before the run. The script asserts that milestone 3b's lowest-cost start equals milestone 3c's solve for every window (intrinsics and sd) and that its k<sub>1</sub>-only conditioning reproduces milestone 3f's f<sub>c</sub> to 1e-6; all 57 passed. Output `summary.json`, `condk12.png`.
- **H144 refuted.** Median |e<sub>c2</sub>| **8.72%** against 5.25% as solved (ratio 1.66; bar 0.6).
- **H145 refuted.** Median signed e<sub>c2</sub> on fr1/xyz **+12.9%** (as solved &minus;1.4%, k<sub>1</sub> alone +12.4%).
- **H146 refuted.** Median |e<sub>c2</sub>| against milestone 3f's k<sub>1</sub>-only |e<sub>c</sub>|: fr1/desk 8.27% against 5.75%, fr1/xyz 16.45% against 12.39%. Two terms are worse than one on both Kinect sequences.
- **H147 refuted, narrowly.** fr3/stf median |e<sub>c2</sub>| 7.75% against 7.24% as solved (k<sub>1</sub> alone had helped: 5.26%).
- **H148 held.** Median step |f<sub>c2</sub> &minus; f|/f 6.65% over all windows (fr1/desk 5.6%, **fr1/xyz 13.6%**, fr3/stf 5.9%); on fr1/xyz alone the step is past the 10% bar, so that sequence's numbers are an extrapolation by this measure.
- **Unscored.** Median signed e<sub>c2</sub>: fr1/desk +3.3%, fr3/stf +7.6%: the two-term step pushes f up on every sequence. corr(k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) in the solver's covariance, median &minus;0.93, &minus;0.91, &minus;0.74 (fr1/desk, fr1/xyz, fr3/stf). Conditional-to-marginal sd of f: 0.66, 0.59, 0.57 with both terms pinned (0.69, 0.64, 0.78 with k<sub>1</sub> alone). Conditioning on k<sub>2</sub> alone: median |e| 13.7% (fr1/xyz 31%). Random-effects K = 5 blocks on f<sub>c2</sub> with sd<sub>JK</sub> times the sd ratio (assumed): all blocks rms z **3.07**, median |error| 7.00% (as solved 1.10 and 2.45%); fr1/xyz 17.2%, rms z 4.05.
- **Reading.** Milestone 3f's rule, "pin the whole distortion model, never one coefficient", does not survive at first order: pinning the whole model is worse than pinning k<sub>1</sub>, which was already worse than pinning nothing. Within each window f, k<sub>1</sub> and k<sub>2</sub> lie along one ridge (k<sub>1</sub>&ndash;k<sub>2</sub> correlation near &minus;0.9 on the Kinect), the board's (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) sits well off it, and the solver's covariance says getting there costs a 6&ndash;14% rise in f. Either the window's covariance shape is a poor guide that far from its solution (fr1/xyz's step is past the pre-set range), or the natural-feature windows and the board calibration describe the lens differently in a way that f compensates, for example through the principal point or the rolling shutter, which neither model nor this analysis separates. For a robot the practical reading is a warning: plugging the last board calibration's distortion into a short-window self-calibration as a prior would move f the wrong way and shrink its error bar by about 40% at once, a confidently wrong estimate (pooled rms z 3.07). Until the re-solve (milestone 3h) says otherwise, leave the distortion free in short windows and pool, as milestone 3d does.
- **Caveat.** As planned: first order, under the solver's covariance shape (its scale cancels, but milestone 3b found it 9&times; too small, and the shape may be off too); the conditional sd is assumed; 57 windows, three sequences. The two-term step is larger than 3f's one-term one, so this milestone leans harder on the linearisation; the re-solve with both terms pinned remains the test and is now milestone 3h.

Written before `experiments/m3g_condk12/analyse.py` was first run and before any window's two-term conditioned f was computed, after milestone 3f's results were read. - **Method** (`experiments/m3g_condk12/`). Scored set, errors e, sd<sub>JK</sub> and the board reference (milestone 4n's k<sub>1</sub> + k<sub>2</sub> fit per unit) exactly as milestone 3f. **Two-term conditioned estimate** (Gaussian conditioning on k = (k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) = k<sub>ref</sub> under the solver's covariance &Sigma;, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> free): f<sub>c2</sub> = f + &Sigma;<sub>f,k</sub> &Sigma;<sub>k,k</sub><sup>&minus;1</sup> (k<sub>ref</sub> &minus; k). The scale of &Sigma; cancels. Check: the same formula with k<sub>1</sub> alone must reproduce milestone 3f's f<sub>c</sub> for every window (to 1e-6 relative), or the script stops.
- **H144 (both terms shrink the error).** Median |e<sub>c2</sub>| over the 57 windows &le; 0.6&times; median |e| (milestone 3e: 5.25%, so &le; 3.15%). Refuted above.
- **H145 (fr1/xyz's offset goes).** Median signed e<sub>c2</sub> on fr1/xyz within &plusmn;1.5%. Refuted outside.
- **H146 (two terms beat one on the Kinect).** Median |e<sub>c2</sub>| below milestone 3f's median |e<sub>c</sub>| on each of fr1/desk (5.75%) and fr1/xyz (12.39%). Refuted if either is not.
- **H147 (no harm on the near-undistorted unit).** Median |e<sub>c2</sub>| on fr3/stf &le; its as-solved 7.24%. Refuted above.
- **H148 (the linearisation is in range).** Median |f<sub>c2</sub> &minus; f|/f &le; 10%; if refuted, H144&ndash;H147 are read as indicative only.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence: medians of e<sub>c2</sub>, |e<sub>c2</sub>|, the step, corr(k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) and the conditional-to-marginal sd ratio of f, &radic;(&Sigma;<sub>ff</sub> &minus; &Sigma;<sub>f,k</sub>&Sigma;<sub>k,k</sub><sup>&minus;1</sup>&Sigma;<sub>k,f</sub>)/&radic;&Sigma;<sub>ff</sub>; conditioning on k<sub>2</sub> alone (for comparison); milestone 3d's DerSimonian&ndash;Laird K = 5 blocks on f<sub>c2</sub> with sd<sub>JK</sub> times that ratio (an assumed conditional sd, flagged as such); `condk12.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** First order, under the solver's covariance shape (milestone 3b found its scale 9&times; too small; only the shape enters f<sub>c2</sub>), and a 1.2 s window's k can sit far from the board's, which is why H148 is scored and the re-solve (3h) stays the test. The reference is the same board calibration the error is scored against. 57 windows, three sequences, a shared scene and session within each.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3f result: a window's k<sub>1</sub> does track its focal error (Spearman +0.59), but pinning k<sub>1</sub> alone at the board's value makes fr1 worse (fr1/xyz +12.4%) and helps only the nearly undistorted fr3 (7.2% to 5.3%); the windows agree with the board on the distortion curve, not on how k<sub>1</sub> and k<sub>2</sub> share it

- **Run.** `experiments/m3f_condk1/analyse.py` (seconds; analysis only), exactly as planned below; `tests/test_m3f.py` (4 tests: the conditioning formula against Monte Carlo draws of a bivariate Gaussian, the covariance scale cancelling, zero correlation leaving f unchanged, the least-squares helper) passed before the run. Every window's f matched milestone 3c's and 3e's (the script asserts it). Output `summary.json`, `condk1.png`.
- **H140 held.** Spearman of &Delta;k<sub>1</sub> against signed error over 57 windows **+0.59** (per sequence 0.59, 0.52, 0.81).
- **H141 refuted.** Median |error| 5.25% as solved, **7.01%** conditioned (1.34&times;; bar 0.6&times;). Per sequence: fr1/desk 2.80% to 5.75%, fr1/xyz 5.18% to 12.39%, fr3/stf 7.24% to 5.26%.
- **H142 refuted.** Median signed conditioned error on fr1/xyz **+12.4%** (as solved &minus;1.4%).
- **H143 held.** The conditioning step is a median 4.4% of f (fr1/xyz 9.7%, just inside the bar), so the refutations are not an extrapolation artefact by this measure.
- **Unscored.** &Delta;k<sub>2</sub> against error: Spearman &minus;0.28; &Delta;k<sub>1</sub> against &Delta;k<sub>2</sub>: &minus;0.82. Median window k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>: fr1/desk 0.136, &minus;0.224; fr1/xyz 0.102, &minus;0.155 (board 0.223, &minus;0.568); fr3/stf 0.002, 0.008 (board &minus;0.001, 0.075). Least squares of error on &Delta;k<sub>1</sub> per unit: fr1 slope 0.82, intercept **+8.9%**, R<sup>2</sup> 0.55; fr3 slope 1.02, intercept +2.0%, R<sup>2</sup> 0.66 (with &Delta;k<sub>2</sub> 0.55 and 0.88). Random-effects K = 5 blocks (milestone 3d's pool, conditional sd sd<sub>JK</sub>&radic;(1 &minus; &rho;<sup>2</sup>), an assumption): all blocks rms z 3.04, median |error| 4.10% (as solved 1.10, 2.45%); fr3/stf **1.43%** with rms z 0.67 (as solved 2.36%, 0.84); fr1/xyz 10.25%, rms z 4.95.
- **Post hoc** (added to `analyse.py` after the hypotheses above were read): the radial factor 1 + k<sub>1</sub>r<sup>2</sup> + k<sub>2</sub>r<sup>4</sup> of the windows minus the board's, median per sequence, at normalised radius r = 0.2, 0.4, 0.6, 0.77 (0.77 is the image corner): fr1/desk &minus;0.3%, &minus;0.5%, +1.5%, +6.7%; fr1/xyz &minus;0.4%, &minus;0.9%, +1.1%, +7.6%; fr3/stf 0.0%, &minus;0.1%, &minus;0.8%, &minus;2.4%.
- **Reading.** Distortion and focal length are coupled within a window, as milestone 3e's covariance said, but the board's k<sub>1</sub> is not the value the window is missing. On the Kinect of fr1 the windows and the board draw nearly the same distortion curve over the inner image (within 1% out to r = 0.4) and split it differently between the two terms (window k<sub>1</sub> about half the board's, k<sub>2</sub> about a third), so moving k<sub>1</sub> alone to the board's value with k<sub>2</sub> left where the window put it bends the curve the wrong way and drags f up 10&ndash;12%; the fr1 regression puts a window with the board's k<sub>1</sub> at +8.9%. On fr3, where both fits find almost no distortion, conditioning helps (pooled K = 5 error 2.36% to 1.43%). For a robot the rule is: if a prior calibration is used to steady self-calibration, pin the whole distortion model (or the curve), never one coefficient of it. Whether pinning both k<sub>1</sub> and k<sub>2</sub> removes fr1/xyz's offset is still open and is the re-solve's question (milestone 3g, needs the TUM video).
- **Caveat.** As planned: first order, under the solver's covariance shape; corr(k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) was not stored, so the two-term conditioning could not be done here; the conditional sd is assumed; the radial comparison is post hoc. 57 windows, three sequences.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3f planned: if a robot conditions each window's focal length on the k<sub>1</sub> it already knows, does the error go? A first-order answer from the stored solves

Written before `experiments/m3f_condk1/analyse.py` was first run and before any window's k<sub>1</sub> or k<sub>2</sub> was tabulated against its error, after milestone 3e's results were read. Milestone 3e's reading pointed at distortion: |corr(f, k<sub>1</sub>)| in the solver's covariance is a median 0.63&ndash;0.77, and fr1/xyz keeps a shared offset no motion measure explains. The direct test (re-solve every window with k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> held at the board calibration) needs the TUM video, and the TUM host refused this session's proxy (curl: CONNECT 403, probed three times). So this milestone takes the first-order version from what is stored: milestone 3c's per-window solves (`m3c_jackknife/results.json`: f, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> and their solver sd) and milestone 3e's corr(f, k<sub>1</sub>) from the same solves. One value was seen before this plan: fr1/desk window 0 (k<sub>1</sub> 0.052 against the board's 0.223, f 7.7% low), printed while finding the file layout; nothing else.

- **Method** (`experiments/m3f_condk1/`). Scored set: milestone 3c's 57 windows, its errors e = (f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>)/f<sub>ref</sub> against milestone 4n's k<sub>1</sub> + k<sub>2</sub> board calibration per unit, and its sd<sub>JK</sub>. Reference distortion: the same board fit (fr1 k<sub>1</sub> 0.2226, k<sub>2</sub> &minus;0.568; fr3 k<sub>1</sub> &minus;0.0008, k<sub>2</sub> 0.0746). **Conditioned estimate** (Gaussian conditioning on k<sub>1</sub> = k<sub>1,ref</sub> under the solver's covariance, k<sub>2</sub> left free): f<sub>c</sub> = f + &rho;<sub>f,k1</sub> (sd<sub>f</sub>/sd<sub>k1</sub>)(k<sub>1,ref</sub> &minus; k<sub>1</sub>). The covariance's scale cancels in the coefficient, so milestone 3b's 9&times; under-reported sd does not enter it; only its shape does. This is a linearisation of the re-solve, not the re-solve.
- **H140 (k<sub>1</sub> carries the error).** Spearman of &Delta;k<sub>1</sub> = k<sub>1</sub> &minus; k<sub>1,ref</sub> against signed e over the 57 windows &ge; +0.5. Refuted below.
- **H141 (conditioning shrinks the error).** Median |e<sub>c</sub>| &le; 0.6&times; median |e| (milestone 3e: 5.25%). Refuted above.
- **H142 (fr1/xyz's offset goes).** Median signed e<sub>c</sub> on fr1/xyz within &plusmn;1.5%. Refuted outside.
- **H143 (the linearisation is in range).** Median |&rho;<sub>f,k1</sub> (sd<sub>f</sub>/sd<sub>k1</sub>)(k<sub>1,ref</sub> &minus; k<sub>1</sub>)|/f &le; 10%, i.e. the correction is a modest step from the solve, not an extrapolation; refuted above, in which case H141&ndash;H142 are read as indicative only.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence: medians of k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>, &Delta;k<sub>1</sub>, e, e<sub>c</sub>; Spearman of &Delta;k<sub>2</sub> against e; an ordinary least-squares fit of e on &Delta;k<sub>1</sub>, &Delta;k<sub>2</sub> per unit (descriptive, fitted on the scored data); milestone 3d's DerSimonian&ndash;Laird K = 5 blocks on f<sub>c</sub> with sd<sub>JK</sub>&radic;(1 &minus; &rho;<sup>2</sup>) (an assumed conditional sd, flagged as such); `condk1.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** First order only: k<sub>1</sub> of a 1.2 s window can sit far from the board's, and the linear step may not be where the re-solve lands, which is why H143 is scored and the re-solve stays the test (milestone 3g, when the TUM host answers). corr(k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>) was not stored, so conditioning on both terms is not possible here. The reference k<sub>1</sub> is from the same board calibration the error is scored against, which a fielded robot would have from its last calibration. 57 windows, three sequences, shared scene and session within each.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3e result: the optical axis swings least on fr3/stf (4.7&deg;), not on the near-critical fr1/xyz (8.3&deg;), and axis spread does not predict a window's focal error (Spearman +0.08); the solve reads the swing as well as motion capture does (&rho; 0.98), so the signal is real but it is not the one

- **Run.** `experiments/m3e_axis/run.py` (about 10 min on 4 cores; one re-solve per window, no jackknife) and `analyse.py` (`summary.json`, `axis.png`), exactly as planned below. Every one of the 57 scored windows reproduced milestone 3c's f (the script asserts agreement within 0.1%; all matched to the printed 0.01 px). `tests/test_m3e.py` (5 tests: roll gives zero spread, a pan gives its angle, the solver and motion-capture conventions agree, total rotation counts roll, Spearman with ties) passed before the run.
- **H135 refuted.** Median solver axis spread D: fr1/desk 20.5&deg;, **fr1/xyz 8.3&deg;**, fr3/stf 4.7&deg;. fr1/xyz is below half of fr1/desk but well above fr3/stf.
- **H136 refuted.** Spearman of D against |f error| over 57 windows **+0.08** (bar &le; &minus;0.3). Motion-capture D gives +0.05 and total rotation +0.04.
- **H137 refuted.** The lower-tercile gate (D &lt; 5.1&deg;) drops 3 of 18 fr1/xyz windows (17%; bar &ge; 50%) and 16 of 39 fr1/desk and fr3/stf windows (41%; bar &le; 25%), nearly all from fr3/stf.
- **H138 refuted.** Median |error| of kept windows 5.30% against 5.25% for all (ratio 1.01; bar 0.75).
- **H139 held.** Spearman of solver D against motion-capture D 0.98; medians agree to 0.4&deg; in every sequence.
- **Unscored.** The axis swing is 84&ndash;94% of the total rotation (median per sequence), so roll about the optical axis is not what hid fr1/xyz in milestone 3d. D against sd<sub>JK</sub>/f: Spearman &minus;0.27 (more swing, a somewhat tighter honest bar). |corr(f, k1)| in the solver's covariance has median 0.72, 0.77, 0.63 and |corr(f, k2)| 0.60, 0.64, 0.23 (fr1/desk, fr1/xyz, fr3/stf); neither predicts |error| (Spearman +0.07, +0.11). Milestone 3d's random-effects K = 5 blocks: all windows rms z 1.10, median-of-five error 2.29%; after the tercile gate rms z 1.37 and 2.68% (fr1/xyz 2.98%, fr3/stf 11.5% on its 5 remaining blocks). At the median gate (7.9&deg;) fr3/stf keeps 3 of 24 windows.
- **Reading.** On this video, how far the camera's view direction swings in 1.2 s says nothing about how far off its self-calibrated focal length is, either within a sequence or across them: fr3/stf barely swings yet pools to within 1.2% at eight windows (milestone 3d), and fr1/xyz swings more yet stays biased. Sturm's criticality is about the motion *class*, and none of these hand-held windows is close to pure translation (fr1/xyz's minimum is 1.4&deg;, median 8.3&deg;), so the per-window geometry is not the limiting factor at 1.2 s. The error that is left looks scene- or camera-session-specific (fr1/xyz's shared offset, the near-unit f&ndash;k1 coupling on every sequence), which points at distortion, not motion: the next test fixes k1 and k2 at the camera's previous calibration, which a fielded robot does have, and asks whether fr1/xyz's offset goes. For a robot the practical rule stands from milestone 3d: pool by random effects until the sd reaches the target; a motion gate on axis swing would cost windows and buy nothing.
- **Caveat.** As planned: 57 windows, three sequences, windows within a sequence share a scene and session; the tercile threshold is from this data. The reading about distortion is a hypothesis from unscored correlations, not a finding.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3e planned: does the spread of the camera's optical axes, read from the solve's own poses, mark the near-critical windows that total rotation missed?

Written before `experiments/m3e_axis/run.py` was first run and before any axis-spread value was computed, after milestone 3d's results were read. Milestone 3d found that total motion-capture rotation does not pick out fr1/xyz, whose windows land a shared &minus;3% off with a jackknife sd that does not grow. Sturm (1997) lists pure translation and rotation about the optical axis among the critical motions for focal length; total rotation counts roll about the optical axis, which carries no information on f. So the candidate signal is how far the *optical axis* swings within the window, and a robot can read it from its own solved poses.

- **Method** (`experiments/m3e_axis/`). Each of milestone 3c's windows is re-solved exactly as 3c did (best start from milestone 3b, OpenCV RNG seeded per window, `nfba.solve_window` unchanged); a window's f must reproduce 3c's within 0.1% or the script stops. Scored set: 3c's 57 scored windows, with 3c's f, error against milestone 4n's board reference and sd<sub>JK</sub>. **Axis spread D** = the largest angle between the optical axes of any two of the window's 12 keyframes (solver poses: x<sub>cam</sub> = R X + t, axis = third row of R), in degrees. The same D from TUM's motion capture (axis = third column of the camera-to-world rotation) is the check. Roll is excluded by construction. Also from the solve: the correlations of f with k1 and k2 in its intrinsics covariance.
- **H135 (D marks fr1/xyz).** Median solver D on fr1/xyz &le; 0.5&times; the median on each of fr1/desk and fr3/stf. Refuted otherwise.
- **H136 (D predicts error).** Spearman of solver D against |f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>|/f<sub>ref</sub> over the 57 windows &le; &minus;0.3. Refuted above.
- **H137 (a gate on D picks the right windows).** Dropping windows below the lower tercile of solver D (over all 57) removes &ge; 50% of fr1/xyz's scored windows and &le; 25% of fr1/desk's and fr3/stf's together. Refuted if either fails.
- **H138 (the gate helps).** Median |error| of the kept windows &le; 0.75&times; that of all 57. Refuted above.
- **H139 (the solver sees what motion capture sees).** Spearman of solver D against motion-capture D &ge; 0.8. Refuted below.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence: medians of D (solver and motion capture), total rotation and the roll share of it; Spearman of D against sd<sub>JK</sub>/f and of |corr(f, k1)|, |corr(f, k2)| against |error|; milestone 3d's random-effects K = 5 blocks on the gated windows (rms z, median |error|); gate sensitivity at the lower quartile and the median; `axis.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** 57 windows in three sequences of one scene type, which share a scene and session within a sequence; the gate threshold is a tercile of this same data, not a fixed figure; D from 12 keyframes over 1.2 s is the swing of a hand-held camera, not designed motion.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3d result: pool five 1.2 s windows by random effects and the sd stays honest (rms z 1.10; independence gives 2.79), but f lands a median 2.2&ndash;2.5% off; about eight windows (10 s) reach 2%, and a rotation gate does not find the near-critical case

- **Run.** `experiments/m3d_pool/pool.py` (seconds; analysis only, on milestone 3c's `summary.json`), exactly as planned below; `tests/test_m3d.py` (5 tests: the pooling helpers on known cases, including about-unit rms z for DerSimonian&ndash;Laird under simulated random effects) passed before the run. Output `summary.json`, `pool.png`.
- **H130 held.** Inverse-variance pooling, which treats windows as independent, gives rms z 2.79 over 45 K = 5 blocks, and it worsens with K (2.17, 2.55, 2.79, 2.90 at K = 2, 3, 5, 8).
- **H131 held.** DerSimonian&ndash;Laird rms z 1.10 at K = 5 (2-sd coverage 96%), and 0.89 at K = 8; per sequence 1.09, 1.39, 0.84 at K = 5.
- **H132 refuted, on fr3/stf.** Median |error| of the median of five windows: fr1/desk 1.48%, **fr3/stf 2.29%** (bar 2%), fr1/xyz 2.98% (the predicted &gt; 2% held there).
- **H133 refuted.** Share of K = 5 blocks with random-effects sd &le; 2% and |error| &le; 2%: fr1/desk 45%, **fr3/stf 0%** (median sd 3.48%), fr1/xyz 0%.
- **H134 refuted.** The 5&deg; gate keeps 15 of 18 fr1/xyz windows (it was to drop at least half) and drops 13 of 24 fr3/stf windows, whose gated K = 5 error rises to 6.38%. Gated rms z stays honest (1.24). At 10&deg; it keeps 6 of 18 fr1/xyz windows and none of fr3/stf.
- **Unscored.** Median random-effects sd and |error| by K over all blocks: K = 1 6.45% and 5.25%; 2: 4.91%, 4.67%; 3: 4.14%, 3.76%; 5: 3.31%, 2.45%; 8: 2.53%, 1.42%. At K = 8 the median of windows is 0.25%, 2.83% and 1.18% off on fr1/desk, fr1/xyz, fr3/stf; fr1/xyz is the one sequence still above 2%.
- **Reading.** The between-window spread is real error, not noise to average away: a robot that treats its windows as independent will claim 2&ndash;3&times; too much precision, and the claim grows with every window it adds. A random-effects pool keeps the bar honest at every K tried, so the useful rule is "pool by random effects, accept when its sd reaches the target", and on this data that takes about eight windows (10 s of hand-held video) for a 2.5% sd, not one. Rotation from motion capture is not the near-criticality signal: fr1/xyz rotates as much as the others by this measure, and a rotation threshold mostly removes fr3/stf's slow, good windows. What makes fr1/xyz near-critical has to be read from the solve itself (e.g. the spread of viewing directions, which milestone 1 found predicted error on the board).
- **Caveat.** Sliding blocks overlap and share a scene, so the 45 blocks are far from 45 independent tests; fr1/xyz's shared offset (about &minus;3%) is visible only against the board reference, which a robot does not have.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3d planned: pool natural-feature windows by the keyframe-jackknife sd; how many 1.2 s windows pin f to 2%, and which pooled sd stays honest?

Written before `experiments/m3d_pool/pool.py` was first run on milestone 3c's data, after 3c's results were read (its summary: a single window's sd<sub>JK</sub> is honest overall but a median 6.4% of f; an inverse-variance mean over a whole sequence was &minus;4.5% off on fr1/xyz). I had not printed the per-window rotation values before setting the gate below. Analysis only: no solve is re-run.

- **Method.** Inputs: 3c's 57 scored windows (`m3c_jackknife/summary.json`: f, sd<sub>JK</sub>, motion-capture rotation). Within a sequence the scored windows are put in time order; a block of size K is K consecutive scored windows, sliding by one (K = 1, 2, 3, 5, 8; headline K = 5, about 6 s of video). Each block gives (i) the inverse-variance mean under sd<sub>JK</sub> with sd 1/&radic;&Sigma;w (windows treated as independent), (ii) the DerSimonian&ndash;Laird random-effects mean with sd 1/&radic;&Sigma;w* (adds the between-window spread &tau;<sup>2</sup>), (iii) the plain median (milestone 3b's pooling; point only). Reference and z as 3c (milestone 4n's board f per unit). **Gate:** windows with motion-capture rotation below 5&deg; are dropped before blocking, as a stand-in for a gyro reading (3&deg; and 10&deg; reported unscored).
- **H130 (independence overclaims).** Inverse-variance rms z over all K = 5 blocks &gt; 2. Refuted at or below 2.
- **H131 (random effects is honest).** DerSimonian&ndash;Laird rms z over all K = 5 blocks &le; 2. Refuted above.
- **H132 (pooling helps where motion is rich, not on near-critical motion).** The median of five windows has median |error| &le; 2% on fr1/desk and fr3/stf, and &gt; 2% on fr1/xyz. Refuted if any of the three fails.
- **H133 (a usable answer in about 6 s).** On fr1/desk and fr3/stf, at least half of K = 5 blocks have a random-effects sd &le; 2% of f *and* |error| &le; 2%. Refuted below half in either.
- **H134 (a rotation gate removes the near-critical case).** The 5&deg; gate drops at least half of fr1/xyz's scored windows, and random-effects rms z over the gated K = 5 blocks is &le; 2. Refuted if either fails.
- **Reported, unscored.** Every K: rms z, 2-sd coverage, median sd and |error| per method and sequence; gate sensitivity at 3&deg; and 10&deg;; `pool.png`.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Sliding blocks overlap, so blocks are not independent (fr1/desk has 15 scored windows, fr1/xyz 18, fr3/stf 24, giving 11, 14 and 20 K = 5 blocks); windows of one sequence share a scene and a session; a DerSimonian&ndash;Laird &tau;<sup>2</sup> from five windows is itself noisy; the gate uses motion capture, which a robot would replace with an IMU.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3c result: a delete-one-keyframe jackknife makes a natural-feature window's sd of f honest overall (rms z 1.76, from the solver's 9.3; 2-sd coverage 89%), but the honest sd is a median 6.4% of f, so one 1.2 s window rarely pins f to 2%; per-track correlation is not the cause (track sandwich rms z 6.6)

- **Runs.** `experiments/m3c_jackknife/run.py` (about 50 min on 4 cores, after a first attempt was stopped by the 30-minute background limit before it wrote anything; the rerun saves each window as it finishes and skips the jackknife on windows that already fail milestone 3b's counting rules, which are not scored) re-solved all 65 windows from milestone 3b's best start with a seeded RNG, then 12 warm-started delete-one-keyframe solves and the two sandwiches per counted window; `analyse.py` scores (`summary.json`, `jackknife.png`). One window (fr1/desk 3) was run once beforehand as a timing and plumbing pilot; only its solve statuses and iteration counts were read. `tests/test_m3c.py` checked first, on synthetic scenes, that re-anchoring keeps every projection, that deleting a keyframe re-indexes cleanly, that under i.i.d. noise the track sandwich matches the solver's sd within 1.5&times; and the jackknife within 2&times;, and that the lag-1 statistic reads 0.8 on injected per-track offsets and 0 on independent noise.
- **Reproduced.** All 64 windows milestone 3b solved re-solve to within 0.1% of its f (median difference 0); the same 57 windows count. 8 of the 684 delete-one solves hit the iteration cap; no window lost its jackknife.
- **H124 held.** rms z<sub>JK</sub> over 57 windows 1.76 (solver 9.31, track sandwich 6.63, keyframe sandwich 3.03). Coverage at 1 and 2 sd: 60% and 89% (solver 7% and 18%).
- **H125 refuted, on fr1/xyz.** rms z<sub>JK</sub>: fr1/desk 1.51, **fr1/xyz 2.58**, fr3/stf 0.98.
- **H126 held.** Across-window spread over median sd<sub>JK</sub>: 0.89, 1.60, 1.19 (solver 4.2, 8.4, 11.7).
- **H127 refuted.** Median sd<sub>JK</sub>/f on fr1/xyz 5.74% against fr1/desk 3.73% (1.54&times;; bar 2&times;; solver 1.28&times;).
- **H128 refuted.** Track-clustered sandwich rms z 6.63 (per sequence 4.8, 8.9, 5.5). Lag-1 residual correlation along tracks, median per sequence (x, y): fr1/desk &minus;0.12, 0.14; fr1/xyz 0.06, 0.04; fr3 &minus;0.15, 0.06, so tracks carry little correlated error once points are refitted (the synthetic test shows a constant per-track offset is largely absorbed by the point).
- **H129 refuted.** Only 5 of 57 windows (9%) have sd<sub>JK</sub>/f &le; 2%, and their median error is 2.8%.
- **Unscored.** Median sd<sub>JK</sub>/f: 3.7%, 5.7%, 9.1% (solver 0.85&ndash;1.09%). Keyframe-clustered sandwich: rms z 2.64, 3.98, 2.36, so per-keyframe clustering gets most of the way and the jackknife's nonlinearity the rest. Spearman of sd<sub>JK</sub> against rotation: 0.22, 0.15, 0.35 (none tested). Against TUM's calibration, rms z<sub>JK</sub> 1.74 overall. Inverse-variance mean under sd<sub>JK</sub>: fr1/desk 513.1 px (&minus;0.99%), **fr1/xyz 494.7 px (&minus;4.5%)**, fr3 531.6 px (+0.16%); the fr1/xyz mean is pulled to the low side by the windows the jackknife calls tightest, so weighting by sd<sub>JK</sub> does not rescue near-critical motion, where milestone 3b's plain median (&minus;1.4%) was better. **Post hoc, chosen after scoring:** the single most influential keyframe carries a median 38&ndash;46% of a window's jackknife variance (over half in 28&ndash;46% of windows), so a window's f hinges on one or two keyframes.
- **Reading.** H5's failure in milestone 3b was a covariance problem, and the cure is the one milestone 3 found on board corners: resample keyframes. The solver's sd assumes independent pixel noise around a linear model; the error that matters here enters per keyframe (one frame's pose, blur or rolling shutter moves the whole solve) and through nonlinearity, which only a re-solve sees. Track correlation, the first candidate, is ruled out. The honest answer is sobering for a robot: a 1.2 s hand-held window gives f to about &plusmn;4&ndash;9% (1 sd), and only 9% of windows reach 2%. So the useful output of one window is the honest interval, which tells the robot to pool windows, and the near-critical case still escapes: on fr1/xyz's translation-only motion the jackknife stays too small (rms z 2.6) and does not grow as the motion weakens (1.54&times;). A motion check (rotation from the IMU or the poses) remains needed for that case.
- **Caveat.** Same 65 windows and references as milestone 3b; windows of a sequence share a scene and are not independent. A 12-keyframe jackknife has about 11 degrees of freedom, so single-window sd<sub>JK</sub> is noisy; the hypotheses pool over windows for that reason. H124 held at 1.76 against a bar of 2, driven by fr3's 0.98.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3c planned: on natural-feature video, does a jackknife over keyframes give each window an honest sd for f, where the solver's sd was 4&ndash;12&times; too small?

Written before any milestone-3c quantity was computed, after milestone 3b's results were read. Milestone 3b found that one 12-keyframe window of TUM RGB-D video gives f to within 3&ndash;7% (median) but its bundle-adjustment sd understates the error 4&ndash;12&times; (rms z 9.3) and does not grow on the near-critical fr1/xyz motion. On board corners, milestone 3 found the delete-one-view jackknife honest where the solver's sd was 2&times; small. Milestone 3b named three candidate causes: SIFT errors correlated along a track, per-frame effects (rolling shutter, blur), and weak motion the linearised sd misses. This milestone asks whether a covariance that does not trust the residual model fixes it, and which clustering does: **per keyframe** (delete-one-keyframe jackknife; a keyframe-clustered sandwich, unscored) or **per track** (a track-clustered sandwich).

- **Method** (`experiments/m3c_jackknife/`). Every one of milestone 3b's 65 windows is re-solved by its `nfba.solve_window`, unchanged, from the start that had the lowest cost in milestone 3b, with OpenCV's RNG seeded per window (milestone 3b did not seed it, so RANSAC can pick slightly different tracks; agreement with milestone 3b's f is reported). A window is **scored** if the re-solve passes milestone 3b's counting rules. Then, warm-started from the full solution: 12 delete-one-keyframe solves (tracks keep &ge; 2 remaining views; deleting keyframe 0 re-anchors the gauge on keyframe 1), with sd<sub>JK</sub> = &radic;((n&minus;1)/n &Sigma;(f<sub>i</sub> &minus; f&#772;)<sup>2</sup>) over the delete-one solves that converge with 300 &lt; f &lt; 1000; with fewer than 10 such, sd<sub>JK</sub> is undefined and the window is left out of H124&ndash;H129 (reported). Sandwiches: S<sup>&minus;1</sup>BS<sup>&minus;1</sup> on the camera block after eliminating points, B summed from the points-adjusted robust scores per cluster with a G/(G&minus;1) factor. Reference as milestone 3b: milestone 4n's board calibration (fr1 518.28, fr3 530.71 px); z = (f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>)/sd.
- **H124 (the jackknife is honest overall).** rms z<sub>JK</sub> over scored windows &le; 2 (solver: 9.3). Refuted above.
- **H125 (honest in every sequence).** rms z<sub>JK</sub> &le; 2 in each of fr1/desk, fr1/xyz, fr3/stf. Refuted above in any.
- **H126 (it follows the real scatter).** In each sequence, the robust spread of f across windows (1.4826 MAD) over the median sd<sub>JK</sub> lies in [0.5, 2] (solver: 4.2&ndash;11.7). Refuted outside in any.
- **H127 (it sees weak motion).** Median sd<sub>JK</sub>/f on fr1/xyz &ge; 2&times; that on fr1/desk (solver 1.28&times;). Refuted below.
- **H128 (per-track correlation is the cause).** rms z under the track-clustered sandwich &le; 2 overall. Refuted above.
- **H129 (a usable gate).** Windows with sd<sub>JK</sub>/f &le; 2% are at least 25% of scored windows, and their median |f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>|/f<sub>ref</sub> is at most 2%. Refuted if either fails.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence: rms z, 1- and 2-sd coverage under the solver, sd<sub>JK</sub>, the track-clustered and the keyframe-clustered sandwich; z against TUM's published calibration; Spearman of each sd against the window's motion-capture rotation; lag-1 correlation of residuals along a track between consecutive keyframes (inliers); an inverse-variance mean of each sequence's windows under sd<sub>JK</sub>; failures of delete-one solves by cause.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Windows of a sequence share a scene and session, so they are not independent draws; the two references differ by 0.3% (fr1) and 1.2% (fr3). A jackknife over 12 keyframes is itself noisy (about 11 degrees of freedom), so single-window sd<sub>JK</sub> varies; H124&ndash;H126 pool over windows for that reason. A keyframe-clustered sandwich with 12 clusters and per-keyframe pose parameters is biased low (each keyframe's own pose absorbs part of its cluster score), which is why it is unscored.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4u result: centring the 5% withhold window on the unit's own 13-view calibration keeps every one of 18 cells under 1.1% false alarms (pooled 0.26%) and makes withholding symmetric (&plusmn;4%: 19&ndash;22% on both units, from 11&ndash;71%), lifting &plusmn;4% detection 1.25&times; over rule W; &plusmn;2% power stays at 0.73&times; ungated

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4u_owncal/analyse.py` (about 6 min on 4 cores, no new solve) imports milestone 4t's code unchanged, rebuilds the 18 cells for both twins, and applies rule U, rule W, the out-of-sample anchor and no withholding to the same fired masks (`summary.json`, `owncal.png`). It reproduced milestone 4t first (W pooled S0 0.30%, ungated 1.61%, W &plusmn;2% detection 3.89%), which the script asserts. `tests/test_m4u.py` checked first the two anchors, U's edges and its symmetry under s, and that U at f<sub>P</sub> is 4t's W.
- **Anchors.** f<sub>U</sub> (13-view f of the old pool): fr1 A 525.16, B 526.04, C 521.09 px; fr3 A 534.04, B 537.14, C 534.46 px.
- **H118 held.** S0 under U, per cell: fr1 AA 0.29%, BB 0.12%, CC 0.26%, AB 0.24%, BA 0.18%, AC 1.05%, CA 0.31%, BC 0.43%, CB 0.19%; fr3 AA 0.06%, BB 0.38%, CC 0.13%, AB 0.25%, BA 0.30%, AC 0.13%, CA 0.12%, BC 0.00%, CB 0.26%. All under 2.5%.
- **H119 held.** Pooled 0.26% (W 0.30%, ungated 1.61%).
- **H120 held.** U withholds 5.35% of usable splits at s = 1 (fr1 4.0%, fr3 6.7%; W 5.8%).
- **H121 held.** Share withheld at +4% / &minus;4%: fr1 19.1% / 21.6% (ratio 1.13; W 11.1% / 38.1%), fr3 19.5% / 22.2% (1.14; W 71.4% / 10.9%).
- **H122 refuted.** &plusmn;2% detection 3.91% against 5.34% ungated (0.73&times;, the same as W's 3.89%).
- **H123 held, at the bar.** &plusmn;4% detection 14.20% against W's 11.36% (1.250&times;; bar 1.25&times;; ungated 19.06%). By side: fr3 +4% 11.7% (W 1.4%), fr1 &minus;4% 16.8% (W 11.1%); the near-side gains are partly offset on the far sides (fr1 +4% 15.6% against W 18.0%, fr3 &minus;4% 12.7% against 14.9%).
- **Unscored.** The out-of-sample anchor (the pool in neither role) gives the same picture: pooled S0 0.26%, worst cell 1.05%, withheld 5.39%, &plusmn;2% 3.92%, &plusmn;4% 13.9%, so the in-sample overlap of f<sub>U</sub> with the old set does not drive U's figures. U agrees with W on 94&ndash;100% and with milestone 4q's oracle flag on 98&ndash;100% of s = 1 splits per cell (W: 94&ndash;100%). Window sweep under U (pooled S0, worst cell, withheld at s = 1, &plusmn;2%, &plusmn;4%): 3% 0.23%, 1.06%, 11.6%, 2.79%, 0.41%; 4% 0.24%, 1.06%, 7.3%, 3.67%, 5.7%; 5% as above; 6% 0.30%, 1.11%, 4.5%, 3.98%, 16.9%; 8% 0.32%, 1.11%, 3.7%, 4.01%, 17.7%. So on these pools a window of 6&ndash;8% stays as safe as 5% and keeps 89&ndash;93% of the ungated &plusmn;4% firing; that was read after scoring and is a candidate to pre-register, not a result. Resid-only twin under U: pooled S0 1.00%, worst cell 4.68% (fr3 BB again), so the jitter is still needed.
- **Reading.** Centring on the unit's own calibration fixes what milestone 4t's reading blamed on the fleet prior: withholding is now even on both sides (ratios 1.13 and 1.14), and the Xtion catches a +4% change in 11.7% of splits instead of 1.4%. It did not move &plusmn;2% power, which confirms milestone 4t's post hoc reading that the 2% loss is the far solves' spurious firing (counted as detection when every split is decided), not the window's placement; at 2% a 5% window rarely binds on a real change. The remaining &plusmn;4% loss is the window itself: a 4% change applied to a 6-view solve already 1% high leaves a 5% window, which is why wider windows recover it on this data. For a robot, U needs nothing it lacks (its own calibration and its two small-set solves) and is the better default than the spec-sheet window.
- **Caveat.** Same 18 cells as milestones 4p&ndash;4t, so the far-solve mechanism, rule W and now U were all named and scored on these six pools; a new session or unit remains the test. H123 held exactly at its bar (1.2504&times;). Splits share views; no interval.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4u planned: if the withhold window is centred on the unit's own current calibration instead of the fleet prior, is the update test still safe in every pairing, and does it get back the power rule W lost on the side where each unit's f sits?

Written before any milestone-4u quantity was computed, after milestone 4t's results were read. Milestone 4t's rule W withheld the decision when the old 7-view f or the scaled new 6-view f lay more than 5% from the fleet prior f<sub>P</sub> = 527.1 px, and it made every cell safe (at most 1.11%) but caught &plusmn;2% changes at only 0.73&times; the ungated rate, because f<sub>P</sub> sits 1&ndash;2% from each unit's own f and so a 4% change toward the window's near edge was withheld most of the time (fr3 +4%: 71%, &minus;4%: 11%). A robot also holds its own current calibration, so this milestone tests **rule U: withhold when the old 7-view f or s &middot; f<sub>new</sub> lies more than 5% from f<sub>U</sub>, the 13-view f of the old pool** (the full calibration the unit was commissioned with on the views the old set is drawn from). Everything else is milestone 4t's code unchanged: the same pools, solves, own-&tau; jittered twin ("jit", scored), resid-only twin (unscored), 7 + 6 design, 1,716 split pairs, per-split 99th-percentile threshold, 18 cells. The run must first reproduce milestone 4t's figures (rule W pooled S0 0.30%, ungated 1.61%, W &plusmn;2% detection 3.89%). Rates are defined as in 4t (false alarms over decided splits; detection over every usable split, a withheld split counted as not detected).

- **H118 (U is safe in every cell).** S0 at most 2.5% in each of the 18 cells. Refuted above in any.
- **H119 (near nominal).** S0 pooled over the 18 cells at most 1%. Refuted above.
- **H120 (withholding is rare).** At s = 1, U withholds at most 10% of usable splits pooled. Refuted above.
- **H121 (U withholds symmetrically).** On each unit, pooled over its nine cells, the share withheld at s = 1.04 and at s = 0.96 differ by at most a factor of 2 (larger over smaller; W: fr1 3.5, fr3 6.5). Refuted above 2 on either unit.
- **H122 (U keeps the power).** &plusmn;2% detection pooled under U at least 0.8&times; the ungated jit arm's. Refuted below.
- **H123 (U beats W at &plusmn;4%).** &plusmn;4% detection pooled under U at least 1.25&times; W's (4t: 11.4%). Refuted below.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per cell: S0, share withheld at each s and detection at &plusmn;1, 2, 4% under U, W and ungated; agreement of U with W and with milestone 4q's oracle flag; the same under an out-of-sample anchor (the 13-view f of the unit's pool that is in neither the old nor the new role, the mean of the other two for the same-pool cells) and for windows of 3, 4, 6 and 8%; the resid-only twin under U.
- **Caveat set in advance.** f<sub>U</sub> is fitted on 13 views that include the old set's 7, so the old solve's distance from it is partly in-sample, which is why the out-of-sample anchor is reported. No new views: the 18 cells are milestones 4j, 4p and 4s's pools, already used to name the far-solve mechanism and to score W. The same 5% window means a 4% change still lands 1% from the edge, so some withholding of real changes remains by construction. Splits share views; no interval.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4t result: withholding the decision when either small-set solve is more than 5% from the fleet prior brings every one of 18 cells under 1.2% false alarms (pooled 0.30%, from 1.61%) at 5.8% of splits withheld, but costs a quarter of the &plusmn;2% detections and over half the &plusmn;4% ones, because a fixed anchor withholds real changes on the side where the unit's f already sits

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4t_withhold/analyse.py` (about 6 min on 4 cores, no new solve) rebuilds milestone 4s's pools, solves, twins and per-split thresholds for all nine pairings of A, B and C on each unit, arms "jit" (scored) and "resid" (unscored), and applies rule W to the decision only (`summary.json`, `withhold.png`). It reproduced milestone 4s first (jit fr1 CC S0 7.47%, jit pooled S0 over the ten C cells 2.60%), which the script asserts. `tests/test_m4t.py` checked first that f<sub>P</sub> is milestone 4k's 527.1 px and W's edges, including that the robot's scaled new f (s &middot; f<sub>new</sub>) is what is compared.
- **H113 held.** S0 under W, per cell: fr1 AA 0.29%, BB 0.12%, CC 0.32%, AB 0.36%, BA 0.24%, AC 1.11%, CA 0.31%, BC 0.56%, CB 0.31%; fr3 AA 0.06%, BB 0.46%, CC 0.13%, AB 0.25%, BA 0.31%, AC 0.13%, CA 0.12%, BC 0.00%, CB 0.26%. All under 2.5% (ungated, the same twin: up to 7.47%).
- **H114 held.** Pooled over the 18 cells: 0.30% (ungated 1.61%).
- **H115 refuted, by one cell.** fr1's C cells: CC 0.32%, CA 0.31%, BC 0.56%, CB 0.31% and **AC 1.11%** (bar 1%; ungated 5.73%).
- **H116 held.** W withholds 5.80% of usable splits at s = 1 (fr1 3.8%, fr3 7.8%).
- **H117 refuted.** &plusmn;2% detection, withheld counted as missed: 3.89% against 5.34% ungated (0.73&times;; bar 0.8&times;).
- **Unscored.** W and milestone 4q's oracle flag (far from the pool's median of that size) agree on 94.4&ndash;100% of splits per cell; pooled S0 under the oracle 0.27% (W 0.30%). Share withheld by scale, pooled: 24.5% at &minus;4%, 6.2% at &minus;2%, 5.8% at 0, 10.1% at +2%, 41.3% at +4%; by unit, fr1 withholds 38% at &minus;4% (11% at +4%) and fr3 71% at +4% (11% at &minus;4%): the side where each unit's f already sits from f<sub>P</sub> (fr1 &minus;0.2% to &minus;1.1%, fr3 +1.3% to +1.9%). Detection under W against ungated: &plusmn;1% 1.03% (2.39%), &plusmn;4% 11.4% (19.1%); at +4% on fr3 1.4% (13.7%). Resid-only twin under W: pooled S0 1.03% (ungated 2.34%), worst cell 4.84% (fr3 BB, the flat Xtion pool of milestones 4q and 4r), so W does not replace the jitter. **Post hoc, computed after scoring:** firing above the false-alarm floor, &plusmn;2% detection minus S0, is 3.61 points under W against 3.73 ungated (0.97&times;), so most of H117's loss is the far-solve firing that ungated &plusmn;2% counts as detection.
- **Reading.** The two failure modes milestones 4r and 4s found need two fixes, and together they work: the per-view focal jitter makes the threshold honest where the twin's spread was too narrow, and withholding the decision on a far small-set solve removes the near-degenerate view sets the linear twin cannot represent. With both, every pairing of three pools on two cameras sits at or under 1.11% false alarms, and W needs nothing a robot lacks (its two solves and the spec sheet's f), where milestone 4s's diagnostic needed every subset of a pool. Its cost is where the window is centred: f<sub>P</sub> is a fleet figure 1&ndash;2% from each unit's own f, so a 4% change toward the window's near edge is withheld most of the time (fr3 +4%: 71%). A real update of that size would be asked for more views rather than missed silently, which is the right failure for a robot, but it is a failure of power; a window centred on the unit's own current calibration should keep the symmetry, and is the next thing to pre-register.
- **Caveat.** No new views: the 18 cells are the pools of milestones 4j, 4p and 4s, the far-solve mechanism was named on the same data, and the oracle's rates on the C cells were read before the plan. The one cell over 1% (fr1 AC) is within 0.11 points of the bar; with splits sharing views no interval is given. The TUM host refused the proxy (HTTP 403), so a new unit or session was not tried.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4t planned: if the update test withholds its decision whenever either small-set solve lands more than 5% from the fleet's nominal focal length, is it safe in every pairing of the three pools, and what does withholding cost in power?

Written before any milestone-4t quantity was computed, after milestone 4s's results were read. Milestone 4s found the Kinect's third pool over-alarming at up to 7.5% whatever per-view jitter its twin carried, and a post hoc diagnostic put 83&ndash;97% of those false alarms on the splits whose old 7-view or new 6-view solve is a "far" solve (more than 5% from the median solve of its size in its own pool, milestone 4q's flag). That flag is an oracle: it needs the median over every subset of a pool a robot only partly has at decision time, and its rates on the C cells were read before this plan. Milestone 4k showed the far solves are true minima of near-degenerate view sets (a warm start lands on every one of them), so they cannot be solved away, and that clipping the *delete-one* solves to a window around the fleet prior does harm (it narrows R). This milestone tests the deployable version on the decision only: **rule W withholds the decision (no update, no alarm, ask for more views) when the old 7-view f or the scaled new 6-view f (s &middot; f<sub>new</sub>, what the robot sees) lies more than 5% from f<sub>P</sub> = 527.1 px**, milestone 4k's fleet prior (TUM's mean published f over the two units); R, the twin and the threshold are untouched. Everything else is milestone 4s's code imported unchanged: the own-&tau; jittered twin ("jit", milestone 4r's `pool_twin`), the 7 + 6 design's 1,716 split pairs, 4i's per-split 99th-percentile threshold on z<sub>R</sub><sup>2</sup>, 4p's `decide`, ungated. All 18 cells are scored: AA, BB, CC, AB, BA, AC, CA, BC, CB on each unit. The run must first reproduce milestone 4s's figures (jit fr1 CC S0 7.47%, jit pooled S0 over the ten C cells 2.60%).

- **Rates.** A false-alarm rate is the share of *decided* (not withheld) splits at s = 1 that fire. A detection rate counts every usable split and scores a withheld one as not detected, since the robot did not update.
- **H113 (W is safe in every cell).** S0 at most 2.5% in each of the 18 cells. Refuted above in any.
- **H114 (near nominal).** S0 pooled over the 18 cells at most 1%. Refuted above.
- **H115 (W fixes the Kinect's third pool).** S0 at most 1% in each of fr1's five C cells (jit, ungated: 2.9&ndash;7.5%). Refuted above in any. Partly foreseen: the oracle flag's no-far rate over those cells was 0.49%; W's flag differs (a fixed anchor, not the pool median) and is computed here for the first time.
- **H116 (withholding is rare).** At s = 1, W withholds at most 10% of usable splits pooled over the 18 cells. Refuted above.
- **H117 (withholding keeps the power).** Detection at &plusmn;2% pooled over the 18 cells, under W, is at least 0.8&times; the ungated jit arm's (every split decided). Refuted below 0.8&times;. Some loss is expected by construction, since ungated firing on far splits is counted as detection.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per cell: S0 under W and under the oracle flag, share withheld at each s, detection at &plusmn;1%, &plusmn;2%, &plusmn;4%; how often W and the oracle flag agree; the same for the resid-only twin; harmful adoptions (s = 1 splits where the test fires on a far solve) under W against ungated.
- **Caveat set in advance.** No new views: the three pools of each unit are those of milestones 4j, 4p and 4s, so the fourteen A/B cells were already the basis of milestones 4p&ndash;4r, and the far-solve mechanism was named on the same data this rule is scored on. f<sub>P</sub> sits &minus;1.2% to +1.4% from the units' 13-view f, so W's window is asymmetric per unit (about &minus;3.8% to +6.4% of fr1's f, &minus;6.4% to +3.6% of fr3's); a 4% change applied to the new views can itself be withheld, which H117's detection counts. Splits share views; no interval. The TUM host refused the proxy this session (HTTP 403), so a new sequence or unit was not available.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4s result: on a third pool of views the per-view focal jitter keeps the Xtion safe (at most 0.53% false alarms) but not the Kinect (up to 7.5%), whose third pool fits a small jitter and still over-alarms; borrowing τ does not help, and the three 13-view calibrations agree within the twin's sd

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4s_third/prepare.py` reproduced pools A (milestone 4j) and B (milestone 4p) exactly on both units and picked C (gap 0.5 s, see the amended plan); `run.py` solved 9,440 k1 + k2 subsets of C (about 1 h 55 min on 4 cores; all 13-view solves converged, 5-view 1,283 and 1,282 of 1,287); `analyse.py` (about 10 min on 3 cores) runs milestone 4r's `pool_twin`, 4p's `decide` and 4i's per-split threshold unchanged on all three pools (`summary.json`, `third.png`). Its A/B cells reproduce milestone 4r first (jittered fr3 BB S0 0.58%, resid-only pooled &plusmn;2% 9.08%), which the script asserts. The hypotheses were planned as H104&ndash;H108 and renumbered H108&ndash;H112 at merge, unchanged in content, because milestone 3b, planned and landed in parallel, had already used H104&ndash;H107. `tests/test_m4s.py` checked first that C is disjoint from A and B and keeps the 0.5 s gap, the pick rule's two exclusions, that A and B load as before, and the borrowed-&tau; conversion.
- **Pool C.** Median tilt fr1 0.244 (A 0.268, B 0.147), fr3 0.083 (A 0.101, B 0.063), so C sits between A and B on both units. Fitted &tau; (share of f): fr1 0.77% (A 0.75%, B 1.88%), fr3 0 (A 0.22%, B 1.80%): fr3 C's twin already spreads its 6-view f as widely as the real solves (1.11% against 1.10%), fr1 C's needs a small jitter (twin 0.46% against real 0.63%).
- **H108 refuted, on the Kinect only.** Own-&tau; jittered twin, S0 per C cell: fr1 CC 7.47%, AC 5.73%, CA 2.93%, BC 5.14%, CB 2.97% (all five above 2.5%); fr3 CC 0.47%, AC 0.29%, CA 0.41%, BC 0.12%, CB 0.53%.
- **H109 refuted.** Pooled over the ten cells: 2.60% (bar 1%); fr3's five alone are all under 0.6%.
- **H110 refuted.** C jittered with A's and B's mean &tau; (fr1 1.31%, fr3 1.01% of f): fr1 CC 7.18%, AC 5.44%, CA 2.93%, BC 5.02%, CB 3.09%; fr3 0.18&ndash;0.53%. Pooled 2.55%. A larger &tau; on the Kinect's C moves its false alarms by less than 0.3 points.
- **H111 held.** Resid-only twin: fr1 CC 7.71%, AC 6.44%, CA 3.46%, BC 5.84%, CB 3.96%; fr3 CC 0.47%, AC 0.23%, CA 0.23%, BC 1.34%, CB 1.11%. Pooled 3.08%. So the jitter is not what keeps the Xtion's C cells safe (they already were, apart from the cells holding B) and does not rescue the Kinect's.
- **H112 held.** 13-view f, in the jittered twins' sd: fr1 C against A &minus;1.38, against B &minus;0.84; fr3 +0.11 and &minus;0.42. In share of f: fr1 C 521.09 is &minus;0.77% from A and &minus;0.94% from B (resid-only twin's sd: &minus;2.10 and &minus;1.59), fr3 C 534.46 is +0.08% and &minus;0.50%. Against TUM's published f: fr1 C +0.81% (A +1.60%, B +1.77%), fr3 C &minus;0.53% (A &minus;0.61%, B &minus;0.03%).
- **Unscored.** Pooled &plusmn;2% over the C cells: resid-only 8.84%, own &tau; 6.36% (0.72&times;), borrowed 5.15% (0.58&times;), f + principal point 7.71% (0.87&times;); &plusmn;4%: 26.9%, 20.1%, 16.0%, 24.0%. Milestone 4q's gate (1% cut) on the jittered set sd: 17.3% of C splits scored, S0 4.36% pooled, worst cell 6.55%, &plusmn;2% 14.7% (resid-only gate: 35.7% scored, S0 5.69%). Far solves (more than 5% from the median of their size) at 5, 6 and 7 views: fr1 A 29, 6, 1; B 72, 34, 8; **C 56, 96, 62**; fr3 A 46, 19, 1; B 202, 112, 38; C 197, 118, 46. **Splits holding a far solve** (milestone 4q's flag, old or new set; a diagnostic added after the scored figures were read, which it leaves unchanged): fr1's C cells hold 65&ndash;157 of about 1,710 each, and with the own-&tau; twin 48&ndash;85% of them fire against 0.26&ndash;1.05% of the rest (CC 79.0% against 0.26%; AC 85.3% against 1.05%); they carry 83&ndash;97% of every fr1 C cell's false alarms. Without them the jittered S0 over fr1's five C cells is 0.49% (resid-only 1.01%), fr3's 0.13% (0.52%). On fr3 the far splits are as many (65&ndash;162 per C cell) but fire in only 1.3&ndash;7.7% of cases, because its twin's spread is already as wide as the real one.
- **Reading.** The test the programme has waited on since milestone 4m came out split by camera. On the Xtion, the third pool behaves as milestone 4r's reading predicts: a flat pool whose twin already carries its spread is safe with or without the jitter, and every cell, including C against the flat pool B, stays under 0.6% once the pools are jittered. On the Kinect the per-view focal jitter does not transfer: C is a well-tilted pool whose robust 6-view spread is close to A's, so the fitted &tau; is small (0.77%), yet its 7-view solves hold 62 far solutions where A's hold one, and the update test fires on 3% to 7.5% of unchanged splits whatever &tau; the twin is given (borrowing a larger one changes it by less than 0.3 points). A robust spread that ignores the far solves cannot see this error, so the jitter, fitted to that spread, cannot fix it; the diagnostic shows it directly, since the splits holding a far solve carry nearly all of fr1 C's false alarms and the rest sit at 0.5%: the Kinect's over-alarm is the far-solve mechanism milestone 4p first named, which milestone 4q found absent on the Xtion's flat pool, back on a different pool and a different camera. For a robot, milestone 4r's one-number fix is not enough on its own: a pool's far-solve count is a second thing it has before testing, and on this evidence the threshold must account for it (or the far sets be withheld) as well as for the per-view jitter. The three 13-view calibrations of each camera agree within the jittered twin's sd (at most 1.4), so the full calibrations are consistent; the trouble is the small-set solves the test compares.
- **Caveat.** C comes from the same hand-held sweeps as A and B, and its frames are as close as 0.5 s to theirs; the gap was halved from 4p's 1.0 s before any C frame was picked, because 1.0 s left no candidate in 12 of 13 blocks. Splits share views; no interval. The far-solve diagnostic was added after the five hypotheses were scored, so the reading it supports is a description, not a tested hypothesis; it is the hypothesis the next milestone should pre-register.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4s planned: does the per-view focal jitter keep the update test safe on a third pool of views that played no part in suggesting it?

Written before any milestone-4s pool was picked or solved, after milestone 4r's results were read. Milestone 4r's fitted per-view focal jitter brought every cell's false alarms under 0.6%, but on the two pools (A, B) whose over-alarm motivated it, so its caveat asked for a third disjoint pool; milestones 4m and 4q could not get one because the TUM host refused the proxy, and this session's probe got through. Pool C: a third 13 views per unit from the same two sequences (`experiments/m4s_third/prepare.py`), milestone 4p's detection, 13 time blocks and pick rule imported unchanged, which must reproduce A and B exactly; in each block the frames A and B kept and every frame within 1.0 s of either are removed before the pick. *Amended before any C frame was picked or solved:* the 13 time blocks span only 2.1 s (fr1) and 2.3 s (fr3) apart from one long block each, so 1.0 s either side of two frames left no candidate in 12 of 13 blocks on each unit (counts per block printed by a dry run of the pick, nothing else); the gap is 0.5 s, which leaves 11 to 38 candidates in every block. C's views are therefore closer in time to A's and B's (at least 0.5 s) than B's are to A's (at least 1.0 s). C is solved with k1 + k2 exactly as milestone 4p solved B (sizes 5, 6, 7 and 13, cold start 1.3&times; C's own k1 Zhang f). C's twin is milestone 4r's (`pool_twin`, imported unchanged; resid stream index unit + 4, jitter stream tag 7 as in 4r). The scored cells are the ten that hold C: CC, AC, CA, BC, CB on each unit, 7 + 6 design, milestone 4g's 1,716 split pairs, milestone 4i's per-split 99th-percentile threshold, ungated. The A and B arms must reproduce milestone 4r's figures (jittered fr3 BB S0 0.58%, resid-only pooled &plusmn;2% 9.08%) before anything is scored.

- **H108 (the jitter keeps fresh views safe).** Twin with each pool's own fitted &tau; (4r's rule, C's &tau; from C's own 6-view solves): S0 false alarms at most 2.5% in each of the ten C cells. Refuted above in any.
- **H109 (near nominal).** The same, pooled over the ten cells: at most 1%. Refuted above.
- **H110 (a borrowed &tau; is enough).** C's twin jittered with the mean of A's and B's fitted &tau; (as % of f) instead of its own: S0 at most 2.5% in each of the ten cells. Refuted above in any.
- **H111 (the jitter is needed).** Resid-only twin (&tau; = 0 everywhere): S0 above 2.5% in at least one of the ten cells. Refuted if every cell is at or below 2.5%.
- **H112 (calibrations agree within the jittered twin's sd).** 13-view f of C against A and against B, in the jittered twins' sd (4r's std over draws, combined in quadrature): at most 2 sd for all four gaps (two units &times; two pools). Refuted above 2 on any.
- **Reported, unscored.** C's fitted &tau; and the real and twin 6-view spread; per-cell S0 and &plusmn;2%, own &tau; against resid-only (power ratio) and borrowed &tau;; &plusmn;1% and &plusmn;4%; milestone 4q's gate (1% cut) on the jittered set sd; 4r's f + principal-point variant; C's median tilt against A and B; anchors against TUM's published f.
- **Caveat set in advance.** C comes from the same hand-held sequence as A and B, interleaved in time, so this is fresh views of one session, not a new session or mount. Splits share views within a pool; no interval. A pool with fewer usable frames per block after two exclusions may be weaker than A and B for that reason alone; its tilt is reported, not adjusted for.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3b result: from natural-feature tracks, one 1.2-second window of video gives f to within 3&ndash;7% (median), and the solver's sd is 4&ndash;12&times; too small (rms z 9.3, H5 refuted); the median over many windows lands within 1.5% of the board calibration

- **Runs.** `experiments/m3b_natural/run.py` on TUM RGB-D fr1/desk, fr1/xyz (Kinect) and fr3/structure_texture_far (Xtion), fetched by `fetch.sh` (the TUM host answered this session): 65 disjoint 12-keyframe windows, each solved from 3 starting f (195 solves, about 45 min on 4 cores; `results.json`); `analyse.py` scores them (`summary.json`, `natural.png`). `tests/test_m3b.py` checked first that the projection matches `cv2.projectPoints`, that the undistortion inverts it, that union-find tracks drop components holding two features of one view, and that on a synthetic 8-view scene with 0.5 px noise the solver recovers &sigma; (0.5 &plusmn; 0.1 px) and f within 4 of its own sd from an 8% start.
- **Counted.** 57 of 65 windows (fr1/desk 15 of 17, fr1/xyz 18 of 22, fr3 24 of 26). Failures: 6 not converged in 400 iterations, 1 could not initialise (fr1/desk, motion blur), 1 with an undefined covariance (negative variance from a near-singular reduced system; this cause was not in the plan, and the rule `sd_undefined` was added to `analyse.py` after the run). Median 1,034, 1,800 and 2,122 tracks per window, inlier rms 0.55, 0.44 and 0.35 px; 562,769 observations over the counted windows.
- **H104 refuted.** On fr1/desk the median |f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>| / f<sub>ref</sub> is 2.80% (bar 2%). fr1/xyz 5.18%, fr3 7.24%. Single windows range from &minus;13.7% to +36.5%.
- **H105 (= H5) refuted.** rms z over the 57 counted windows 9.31 (bar 2): fr1/desk 6.85, fr1/xyz 9.81, fr3 10.22 (against TUM's calibration 6.82, 9.69, 10.59). 2-sd coverage 27%, 11%, 17%.
- **H106 refuted.** Robust spread of f across windows over the median sd<sub>BA</sub>: 4.20 (fr1/desk), 8.38 (fr1/xyz), 11.74 (fr3); bar 2. The scatter is 3.3%, 9.2% and 11.2% of f; the solver's median sd 0.85%, 1.09% and 0.91%. So the overconfidence is not the reference's fault.
- **H107 refuted.** Median sd<sub>BA</sub>/f on fr1/xyz is 1.28&times; that on fr1/desk (bar 2&times;), while the real scatter is 2.8&times; larger. The median rotation per window is 23&deg; on fr1/desk, 7.9&deg; on fr1/xyz and 4.8&deg; on fr3 (motion capture); the solver's sd does not follow it (Spearman sd against rotation 0.04, 0.18, 0.18, none significant), while on fr3 the error does (|rel| against rotation +0.58, p = 0.003: the windows that rotate more are further off, not closer).
- **Unscored, chosen after scoring.** Local minima are common (the three starts end more than 1% apart in 5, 10 and 16 windows) but do not explain the overconfidence: windows whose starts agree have rms z 7.37, 11.36 and 7.19. The median of a sequence's windows is close: fr1/desk 522.0 px (+0.72% against Zhang's 518.28, +0.99% against TUM), fr1/xyz 511.0 (&minus;1.41%, &minus;1.14%), fr3 538.4 (+1.45%, +0.20%); with an sd taken from the across-window spread (1.2533 &times; MAD / &radic;n) their z's are 0.67, &minus;0.52 and 0.50 (TUM: 0.91, &minus;0.42, 0.07). Median k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub>: fr1/desk 0.136, &minus;0.224 (Zhang 0.223, &minus;0.568); fr3 0.002, 0.008 (TUM undistorts fr3, so near 0 is right).
- **Reading.** H5, the proposal's question for natural features, fails clearly: on hand-held video a 12-keyframe window's covariance understates its focal error 4&ndash;12&times;, more than the 2&times; milestone 3 found with board corners, and the solver does not notice when the motion is weak (fr1/xyz is nearly pure translation, the critical motion for f, yet its sd is barely larger). What survives is the across-window spread: the median of 15&ndash;24 windows lands within 1.5% of both references and the spread gives it an honest-looking interval. For a robot this says not to trust a single short window's sd at all, and to calibrate from the scatter of many windows instead, which is milestone 4r's lesson (a per-view error the model does not carry) in a stronger form. Candidate causes, none tested yet: SIFT localisation errors correlated along a track over consecutive 30 Hz frames, rolling shutter on both sensors during hand-held motion, and the near-critical motion of a 1.2 s window.
