- **Caveat.** As set in advance: windows of one sequence share a scene and a session, and consecutive windows are about a second apart, so the across-window figures are not independent draws; the references differ by 0.3% (fr1) and 1.2% (fr3). The median-of-windows result is post hoc and descriptive.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 3b planned: with natural-feature tracks instead of board corners, is target-free self-calibration still accurate, and is its covariance still honest (H5)?

Written before any scored milestone-3b quantity was computed. Every milestone so far fed the solver a checkerboard's corners (board geometry withheld). The proposal's H5 asks the question a field robot faces: with SIFT tracks of an ordinary scene (mismatches, looser localisation, points seen in only some views), does the z-score of f against the target calibration keep rms &le; 2 after a robust loss? The TUM host answered this session's proxy, so the data are three TUM RGB-D sequences (CC BY 4.0) shot by the two cameras whose board views milestones 4j&ndash;4r used: **fr1/desk** (Kinect, hand-held sweep over a desk, rotation and translation), **fr1/xyz** (Kinect, translation along the three axes, little rotation: near the critical motion for focal length) and **fr3/structure_texture_far** (Xtion, a sideways pass over a textured zig-zag structure; TUM ships fr3 images already undistorted).

- **Method** (`experiments/m3b_natural/nfba.py`, `run.py`). Disjoint windows of 12 keyframes, every 3rd frame (36 frames, ~1.2 s), from frame 0: 17, 22 and 26 windows. SIFT (OpenCV, 4,000 features, contrast 0.02); matches between keyframes 1, 2 and 3 apart (Lowe ratio 0.75, then a RANSAC fundamental matrix at 2 px); tracks by union-find, one feature per view, &ge; 3 views. Start: essential matrix of the first keyframe and its widest-parallax partner at the starting f, then PnP. Bundle adjustment over f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k<sub>1</sub>, k<sub>2</sub> (milestone 4n's model), every pose and point, soft-L1 loss with a 1 px knee, Levenberg&ndash;Marquardt with IRLS weights and a Schur complement; gauge: first keyframe fixed, scale by one translation component of the farthest keyframe. Covariance of the intrinsics: the IRLS-weighted Gauss&ndash;Newton matrix at the solution, points eliminated, &sigma;<sup>2</sup> = weighted SSR / dof. Each window is solved from three starting f (450, 554.3 = a generic 60&deg; field of view, 680 px) and the lowest robust cost is kept. A window **counts** if it has &ge; 100 tracks, initialises, converges (relative cost change &lt; 10<sup>&minus;7</sup> within 400 iterations), has inlier rms &lt; 1.5 px and 300 &lt; f &lt; 1000; the rest are reported as failures, by cause.
- **Reference.** Primary: each unit's target calibration from milestone 4n, Zhang with k<sub>1</sub> + k<sub>2</sub> on its 13 board views (fr1 518.28 px, fr3 530.71 px). Secondary, reported: TUM's published calibration (mean of f<sub>x</sub>, f<sub>y</sub>: fr1 516.9, fr3 537.3). z = (f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>) / sd<sub>BA</sub>.
- **H104 (accuracy on general motion).** On fr1/desk, the median over counted windows of |f &minus; f<sub>ref</sub>| / f<sub>ref</sub> is at most 2%. Refuted above.
- **H105 (= H5, honest covariance after a robust loss).** Over the counted windows of all three sequences, rms z &le; 2. Refuted above.
- **H106 (honest without the reference).** In each sequence, the robust spread of f across its counted windows (1.4826 MAD) is at most 2&times; the median sd<sub>BA</sub>. Refuted in any sequence above 2. (Separates an overconfident sd from a bias of the reference or of the corner localiser, which H105 cannot.)
- **H107 (the solver sees weak motion).** The median sd<sub>BA</sub>/f on fr1/xyz is at least 2&times; that on fr1/desk. Refuted below 2.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per sequence: counted share and failure causes; median f, relative error to both references, rms z and 1&sigma;/2&sigma; coverage; how often the three starts end more than 1% apart in f and which had the lowest cost; tracks, observations, inlier rms; each window's rotation and translation from TUM's motion-capture ground truth (never shown to the solver) against its sd<sub>BA</sub>.
- **Pilot, disclosed.** Before this entry, three single-window pilots (one per sequence, windows at frames 0 and 300) were run to make the pipeline converge: a 6-frame step left fr1/desk too few tracks; SciPy's TRF stalled, so the solver became the LM above; holding a component of keyframe 1's translation for scale converged slowly, so the farthest keyframe holds it. One fr3 pilot window showed that two gauges ended in different minima (different cost), which is why three starts are used, and printed sd/f of 1.6% and 0.3% for those two minima; no pilot f was compared with a reference.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Windows of one sequence share a scene and a capture session, so they are not independent draws of the camera's error; consecutive windows are about a second apart. The references disagree with each other by 0.3% (fr1) and 1.2% (fr3), which bounds how small a z can be read meaningfully, hence H106.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4r result: one per-view focal jitter, fitted from each pool's own 6-view solves, brings every cell's false alarms under 0.6% (the flat Xtion pool from 4.44%), but halves the test's power; and milestone 4p's "2.7-sd gap" between the Xtion's two calibrations was mostly an sd computed the wrong way

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4r_jitter/analyse.py` (about 2 min on 4 cores, no new solve) rebuilds milestone 4p's twins draw for draw, adds the per-view focal jitter at the fitted &tau;, and reapplies milestone 4i's per-split threshold, 4p's `decide` and 4q's gate unchanged (`summary.json`, `jitter.png`). Its &tau; = 0 arm reproduces milestone 4q's ungated figures exactly (fr3 BB S0 4.44%, pooled &plusmn;2% 9.08%), which the script asserts. `tests/test_m4r.py` checked first that a common focal shift moves every subset's twin f by exactly that shift (g<sub>S</sub> &middot; &part;u/&part;f = 1 at 5, 6 and 13 views), that the jitter is one draw per view, and the fit of &tau; on a known case.
- **Fitted &tau;** (sd of each view's own f, % of f): fr1 A 0.75%, B 1.88%; fr3 A 0.22%, B 1.80%. The twin's 6-view robust spread without it: 0.45%, 0.80%, 0.46%, 0.63% against real 0.59%, 1.23%, 0.50%, 1.23%, so the flat pools (B) need the most.
- **H100 held.** Within B on fr3, S0 false alarms 0.58% (from 4.44%).
- **H101 held.** Every cell at or below 0.58%: fr1 AA 0.29%, BB 0.35%, AB 0.58%, BA 0.29%; fr3 AA 0.12%, BB 0.58%, AB 0.35%, BA 0.41%. Pooled 0.37% (resid-only 1.42%), now below the 1% nominal level: the jittered threshold is conservative.
- **H102 refuted.** Pooled &plusmn;2%: 4.07% against 9.08% (0.45&times;; bar 0.8&times;). Per cell fr1 AA 14.0% (19.3%), BB 0.8% (2.1%), AB 2.0% (4.9%), BA 2.1% (5.4%); fr3 AA 7.6% (10.3%), BB 1.9% (9.2%), AB 2.1% (9.2%), BA 2.1% (12.3%). Median per-split threshold on z<sub>R</sub><sup>2</sup> rises from 10&ndash;14 to 12&ndash;35 (fr3 BB 11.8 to 35.2).
- **H103 held, but not because of the jitter.** A-against-B gap in 13-view f, in the jittered twin's sd: fr1 0.15, fr3 0.54. With the resid-only twin's sd taken the same way (std over its own draws) the gap is already 0.29 and 1.59. Milestone 4p's 0.98 and 2.71 used &Vert;g<sub>13</sub>&Vert; &times; rms, the iid-noise sd, which on these units is 1.6&ndash;3.5&times; smaller than the resid model's own (fr3 A 0.74 against 1.37 px; fr1 B 0.77 against 2.73 px), because the resid model's within-view correlation does not average away over 13 views. So the "two calibrations of the Xtion disagree by 2.7 sd" reading of milestones 4p and 4q is withdrawn: under the twin's own noise model they agree within 1.6 sd.
- **Unscored.** With milestone 4q's gate on the jittered set sd (1% cut): 15.5% of splits scored (resid-only gate 44%), S0 0.28% pooled, worst cell 0.54%, &plusmn;2% 15.9% of scored splits (4q's gate 15.9% at 44% scored), &plusmn;4% 61.3%. &plusmn;1% ungated 1.2% (resid-only 3.4%), &plusmn;4% 17.8% (31.0%). Jittering cx and cy as well as f at a jointly fitted &tau; (0.28%, 0.65%, 0.10%, 0.60% of f) is between the two: pooled S0 0.68%, worst cell fr3 BB 1.81%, &plusmn;2% 5.9% (0.65&times;). The predicted-power figure listed in the plan was not computed (see the run record).
- **Reading.** A single number per pool, read off the scatter of the robot's own small-set solves, is enough to make the update test safe on every pool and pairing here, including the flat Xtion pool that defeated milestones 4p and 4q, so the over-alarm was the twin understating how much each view's solve moves, as milestone 4q concluded. The price is steep: the threshold widens so much that the test catches a 2% focal change half as often (4.1% of splits), and with false alarms at 0.37% against a 1% target it is now too cautious, because a per-view f offset is one of several shapes the missing error could take and the one that costs the most power (the f + principal-point variant keeps 0.65&times; of the power at 1.8% worst-cell false alarms). Combined with the gate, the robot tests rarely (15% of splits) but then catches 2% changes as often as before (15.9%) at 0.3% false alarms. The 13-view check H103 corrects an earlier reading rather than confirming this one: the Xtion's two calibrations were never as far apart as milestone 4p said.
- **Caveat.** As set in advance: the pools are 4p's and 4q's, whose results motivated the term, so these passes are consistency checks, not confirmation on unseen data; &tau; summarises whatever per-view error there is and does not identify it. Splits share views, so no interval.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4r planned: does a per-view focal jitter, fitted from the pool's own solves, make the update test's threshold safe?

Written before any milestone-4r quantity was computed, after milestone 4q's results were read. Milestone 4q found that the flat Xtion pool's over-alarm (fr3 B, S0 4.44% ungated, 5.85% gated) is not the far solves but splits the twin rates as precise and are not, and read that as an error shared within a view that the twin's noise (each view's 13-view residual pattern, resampled) does not carry. Two figures already on file point the same way: the real robust spread of 6-view f is 1.23% on fr3 B against a median twin sd of 0.65% (milestones 4p, 4q), and the two 13-view calibrations of the Xtion differ by 2.71 of the twin's sd (4p). This milestone adds the simplest such error to the twin: each view sees its own effective focal length, f + &delta;f<sub>v</sub>, &delta;f<sub>v</sub> ~ N(0, &tau;<sup>2</sup>) independent across views (the physical stand-ins: rolling shutter and motion during a hand-held sweep, frame timing, board flex), entered as the corner shift &part;u/&part;f &middot; &delta;f<sub>v</sub> at the twin. &tau; is fitted **per pool from that pool's own solves only** (what a robot has): the value at which the twin's robust spread of 6-view f across sets (1.4826 MAD over the 1,716 sets, median over draws) equals the real one (milestone 4n / 4p's `spread`, n = 6); &tau; = 0 if the twin already matches. Fitted on the first 2,000 of each pool's 10,000 draws; the resid draws are milestone 4p's (same seeds and streams), the jitter a separate stream (seed 20261114, tag 7). Everything else is milestone 4p / 4q's code imported unchanged: twin sets, milestone 4i's per-split 99th-percentile threshold on z<sub>R</sub><sup>2</sup>, the 1,716 split pairs of the 7 + 6 design, the four pairings AA, BB, AB, BA. The resid-only arm must reproduce milestone 4q's ungated figures (fr3 BB S0 4.44%, pooled &plusmn;2% over all four pairings 9.1%) before anything is scored.

- **H100 (the fitted jitter makes the flat Xtion pool safe).** Within B on fr3, ungated S0 false alarms at most 2.5% (resid-only 4.44%). Refuted above.
- **H101 (and every cell).** Ungated S0 at most 2.5% in each of the eight unit &times; pairing cells. Refuted above in any.
- **H102 (at a small cost in power).** Pooled &plusmn;2% (over sign, unit and the four pairings) at least 0.8&times; the resid-only pooled rate. Refuted below.
- **H103 (the fit predicts what it was not fitted to).** With the jittered twin's sd of each pool's 13-view f (std over its draws), the A-against-B gap in 13-view f is at most 2 sd on both units (resid-only: 0.98 on fr1, 2.71 on fr3). The 13-view f's were not used in the fit. Refuted above 2 on either.
- **Reported, unscored.** Fitted &tau; per pool (as % of f); per-cell S0 and &plusmn;2%; &plusmn;1% and &plusmn;4%; milestone 4q's gate (1% cut) recomputed with the jittered twin's set sd; a variant that also jitters the principal point per view (&delta;c<sub>x</sub>, &delta;c<sub>y</sub> at the same fitted &tau;); the jittered twin's mean predicted power.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The pools are milestones 4p and 4q's, whose results motivated the term, so a pass is a consistency check on the data that suggested it, not a confirmation (a third disjoint pool is still the test; the TUM host refused this session's proxy in milestone 4q). &tau; is a one-number summary of whatever per-view error there is; the experiment does not identify its physical cause. Splits share views, so no interval.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4q result: abstaining when the twin says the views cannot resolve 2% raises the update test's power 1.75× and removes every far solve, but does not make the flat Xtion pool safe (5.9% false alarms): the over-alarm is not the far solves

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4q_gate/analyse.py` (about 6 min on one core, no new solve) rebuilds milestone 4p's twins and per-split thresholds with its code imported unchanged, adds each set's twin sd of f, and scores the gate (`summary.json`, `gate.png`). It reproduces milestone 4p first (within-B fr3 S0 4.44%, ±2% across pools 7.96%), which the script asserts. `tests/test_m4q.py` checked first the set sd, the far flags and that gated rates count only scored splits.
- **H96 refuted.** Within B on fr3, gated S0 false alarms 5.85% (564 of 1,711 splits scored), up from 4.44%.
- **H97 refuted.** Gated S0 per cell: fr1 AA 0.79%, AB 0.22%, BA 0.17%, BB none scored; fr3 AA 0.08%, AB 2.27%, BA 1.93%, **BB 5.85%**.
- **H98 held.** Gated ±2%, pooled over sign, unit and the four pairings: 15.9% against 9.1% ungated (×1.75). Per cell the gated rate is above the ungated one in every scored cell (fr1 AA 25.2% against 19.3%, AB 7.9% against 4.9%, BA 9.2% against 5.4%; fr3 AA 13.1% against 10.3%, BB 12.3% against 9.2%, AB 14.7% against 9.2%, BA 18.3% against 12.3%).
- **H99 refuted.** Scored share: fr1 AA 74%, AB 27%, BA 35%, **BB 0%**; fr3 AA 74%, BB 33%, AB 52%, BA 57%.
- **Unscored.** Median twin sd of a 6-view set's f: fr1 A 0.49%, B 1.09%; fr3 A 0.48%, B 0.65% of f, so the gate reads milestone 4p's "B is weaker" from the twin alone. Splits holding a far solve (> 5% from its size's median): 4, 42, 34, 12 (fr1 AA, BB, AB, BA) and 20, 149, 112, 57 (fr3); the gate scores none of them. Cut-off sweep, pooled (scored share, S0, ±2%, worst cell's S0): 0.75% 22%, 1.24%, 23.2%, 3.1%; 1% 44%, 1.41%, 15.9%, 5.9%; 1.5% 74%, 1.51%, 11.7%, 6.1%; 2% 89%, 1.46%, 10.1%, 5.3%. ±1% at the 1% cut 5.0% (ungated 3.4%), ±4% 54.4% (31.0%). A tilt-only gate (new set at least as tilted as the old): 53% scored, S0 1.50%, ±2% 10.1%, worst cell fr3 BB 5.3%.
- **Reading.** One prediction held and it is the one a robot would use first: a gate that needs nothing but the twin it already has removes every split with a far solve and buys three quarters more power on the splits it keeps, and on the Kinect it turns the flat pool's weak test (2.1% power) into an abstention rather than a guess. What it does not do is make the Xtion's flat pool safe, and that overturns milestone 4p's reading: the 4.4% over-alarm is not the far solves the linear twin cannot represent (none survive the gate, and the rate rises), but splits the twin rates as precise and are not. That fits two other numbers: the two 13-view calibrations of the Xtion differ by 2.7 of the twin's own sd (milestone 4p), and on the Xtion the twin overstates the test's power 1.96× (milestone 4o). Both say the twin's per-set sd is too small on this unit by an error shared within a view, which neither the corner noise nor the residual pattern carries. The safe reading for a robot today: gate on the twin sd to choose when to test, and size the threshold for a per-view error the twin does not yet model.
- **Caveat.** As set in advance: the pools are milestone 4p's and its over-alarm motivated the gate, so this checks the gate on the data that suggested it; a third disjoint pool (the TUM host refused this session's proxy) is still the test. Splits share views, so no interval; the sweep is descriptive.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4q planned: can the robot tell from the views alone when not to run the update test?

Written before any milestone-4q quantity was computed, and after milestone 4p's results were read (so this is not a test on unseen data; see the caveat). Milestone 4p found that a flatter pool of views (B) makes the 7 + 6 update test both weaker and, on the Xtion, unsafe within the pool (S0 false alarms 4.44%), and its next step asked for a reference or gate built from the new set's own geometry, checked on a third disjoint pool. The TUM host refused this session's proxy (403, probed 2026-11-16), so no third pool can be extracted; this milestone scores a gate on milestones 4n and 4p's stored solves of pools A and B instead. The gate uses only what a robot has before it compares anything: the k1 + k2 twin of each pool (milestone 4p's `twin_pool`, imported unchanged, same draws and seeds) gives every view set's first-order sd of f, sd<sub>S</sub> = (sd of the twin's f of S over its 10,000 resid draws) &times; the pool's 13-view fit rms. A split (old set o from one pool, new set n from the same or the other) is **scored** only if &radic;(sd<sub>o</sub><sup>2</sup> + sd<sub>n</sub><sup>2</sup>) &le; 1% of the old pool's 13-view f, i.e. if the design target (a 2% focal change) sits at least two of the twin's own sd's from zero; otherwise the test abstains. The 1% is fixed here from that target, not fitted; other cut-offs (0.5% to 2%) are reported unscored. Rates below are over scored splits, on milestone 4g's 1,716 split index pairs, with milestone 4p's per-split twin threshold, in all four pool pairings (AA, BB, AB, BA). The ungated figures must reproduce milestone 4p's (within-B fr3 S0 4.44%, pooled ±2% across pools 7.96%) before anything is scored.

- **H96 (the gate makes the flat Xtion pool safe).** Within B on fr3, gated S0 false alarms at most 2.5% (ungated 4.44%). Refuted above.
- **H97 (and keeps every pairing safe).** Gated S0 false alarms at most 2.5% in each of the eight unit &times; pairing cells. Refuted above in any.
- **H98 (abstaining buys power).** Gated ±2% rate, pooled over sign, unit and the four pairings, at least 1.2&times; the ungated pooled rate. Refuted below.
- **H99 (the gate still lets the robot test).** At least 20% of usable splits scored in each of the eight cells. Refuted below in any.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per-cell scored shares, S0 and ±2% rates; ±1% and ±4%; the cut-off sweep; how many splits holding a far solve (more than 5% from the median of its size) the gate drops; the median sd<sub>S</sub> of 6-view sets per pool; a tilt-only gate (scored only if the new set's median tilt measure is at least the old set's) for comparison.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The data are milestone 4p's and its within-B over-alarm motivated the gate, so a pass here is a consistency check on the pools that suggested it, not a confirmation; a third disjoint pool (or another sequence) is still the test. Splits share views, so no interval.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4p result: on 13 views it has never seen, the update test stays under the 2.5% false-alarm bar (0.9% and 1.9%) but not at nominal (1.4% pooled), and catches a 2% focal change in 8.0% of splits against 14.8% in-sample; the fresh views are less tilted, and the drop follows the weaker pool, not freshness alone

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4p_fresh/prepare.py` (detection on all 1,507 + 1,327 frames, a few minutes) reproduced milestone 4j's 13 frames per unit and picked a disjoint 13; `run.py` solved 9,440 k1 + k2 subsets of the fresh pools (about 40 min on 4 cores); `analyse.py` (about 5 min) builds a twin per pool and scores the 7 + 6 test within and across pools (`summary.json`, `fresh.png`). Pool A reproduces milestone 4n's k1 + k2 figures exactly (S0 0.588% / 0.117%, ±2% pooled 14.76%), which the script asserts before scoring. `tests/test_m4p.py` checked first that the pools are disjoint and at least 1 s apart, the pick rule's exclusion, and that the cross-pool `decide` equals milestone 4g's `decisions` within one pool.
- **H92 held.** S0 across pools, both directions: 0.91% (fr1; A old 1.17%, B old 0.65%) and 1.90% (fr3; 1.87% and 1.93%), against 0.59% and 0.12% within A.
- **H93 refuted.** Pooled: 1.40% (bar 1%).
- **H94 refuted.** ±2% across pools, pooled over sign, unit and direction: 7.96%, 0.54× the in-sample 14.76% (bar 0.7×). fr1 4.9% / 5.4% (A old / B old) against 19.3% within A; fr3 9.2% / 12.3% against 10.3%.
- **H95 refuted, narrowly, on fr3.** 13-view k1 + k2 f of B against A: fr1 526.04 against 525.16 (+0.17%), fr3 537.14 against 534.04 (+0.58%; bar 0.5%). In the pools' own twin sd of the 13-view f (resid model at each fit's rms) the gaps are 0.98 (fr1) and 2.71 (fr3).
- **Unscored: pool B is a weaker set of views.** Median tilt of the kept frames (prepare's foreshortening measure) 0.147 against 0.268 (fr1) and 0.063 against 0.101 (fr3); spacing the same (37 against 37 px, 34 against 35). Robust spread of f over 6-view sets 1.23% against 0.59% (fr1) and 1.23% against 0.50% (fr3); far 6-view solves (> 5% from the median) 34 against 6 and 112 against 19. Within B: S0 false alarms 0.58% (fr1) and **4.44% (fr3)**, above the programme's 2.5% bar; ±2% 2.1% (fr1) and 9.2% (fr3). Pooled ±2%: A 14.8%, B 5.7%, across 8.0%, so the across-pool rate sits between the two pools' own rates rather than below both. Median per-split threshold: fr1 10.3 (A), 14.2 (B), 13.2 / 13.1 across; fr3 10.7, 11.7, 11.4 / 12.0. The plain jackknife J across pools at S0: fr1 0.82% / 0.65%, fr3 1.75% / 1.28%; χ²<sub>1</sub>: 2.7% / 2.7%, 5.0% / 5.3%. ±4% across pools, pooled over sign and direction: fr1 23.5%, fr3 33.5% (within A 53.6% and 46.9%). Anchors against TUM's published f: target-free fr1b +1.77% (fr1 +1.60%), fr3b −0.03% (fr3 −0.61%); flat-board Zhang k1 + k2 on fr1b 523.42 (+1.2%) and on fr3b 545.01 (+1.43%, against fr3's 530.71, −1.2%), so on fr3b's flatter views the target-free solve lands nearer TUM than Zhang does.
- **Reading.** Three of four predictions failed, and the way they failed is the result. On views the test never saw, the twin reference still keeps false alarms below the 2.5% bar (1.4% pooled), but the in-sample 0.1–0.6% was optimistic by a factor of 1.5 (Kinect) to 16 (Xtion); the power halves. Most of that is not freshness but geometry: the rule that picked the fresh frames was the same, yet it found flatter boards, and on a flatter pool the solves scatter twice as much and the test, within that pool alone, both loses power (2.1% on the Kinect) and, on the Xtion, over-alarms (4.4%) because far solves the linear twin cannot represent sit in its tail. The two 13-view calibrations of the Xtion differ by 2.7 of the twin's own sd, so the twin also understates the between-pool variation of the full calibration there. For a robot this says the test's safety depends on the views as much as on the threshold: a new set that is flatter than the one the threshold was built on should be held back or scored with a reference built on its own views, and an in-sample false-alarm rate should be read as a lower bound.
- **Caveat.** As set in advance: one hand-held sequence per unit, B interleaved in time with A, so this is fresh views of one session, not a new session or mount; splits share views within a pool, the two directions are not independent, no interval. The tilt difference between the pools was seen before any solve (the cold-start Zhang f was printed) and the hypotheses were left as written; it is a description of the pools, not a fitted explanation, and no experiment here separates the effect of tilt from that of freshness.

## 2026-11-16 · Milestone 4p planned: does the update test keep its false-alarm rate and its power on views it has never seen?

Written before any milestone-4p solve or statistic was computed. Every update-test figure since milestone 4g scores splits of the same 13 views per unit, so the old and new sets, the twin and its reference all come from one pool (`paper/draft.md`, Section 9: scoring every split "says nothing about a fresh set of views"). Here a second, disjoint set of 13 views is picked from each TUM sequence (`experiments/m4p_fresh/prepare.py`): the same detection, spacing floor, 13 time blocks and pick rule as milestone 4j's `prepare.py`, imported unchanged, except that in each block the frame 4j kept and every frame within 1.0 s of it are removed first. The fresh set B is solved with k1 + k2 (milestone 4n's solver, start 1.3× B's own k1 Zhang f, sizes 5, 6, 7 and 13: 4,720 solves per unit), its own k1 + k2 twin is fitted, and the 7 + 6 test is scored with the old set from one pool and the new set from the other, in both directions (A old, B new; B old, A new), on milestone 4g's 1,716 split index pairs, against the per-split twin threshold built from the two pools' twins (milestone 4i's `references`, as in the swap scenario S3; independent draws per pool, seed 20261114, A's draws the ones milestone 4n used). The in-sample comparison is milestone 4n's k1 + k2 7 + 6 figures, which A must reproduce first (S0 0.59% / 0.12%, ±2% pooled 14.8%).

- **H92 (fresh views keep the false alarms safe).** S0 across pools, k1 + k2, 7 + 6, both directions pooled: R above its per-split twin threshold on at most 2.5% of splits on each unit. Refuted above on either.
- **H93 (and near nominal).** The same, pooled over units and directions: at most 1%. Refuted above.
- **H94 (the power carries over).** ±2% applied to the new set, pooled over sign, unit and direction: at least 0.7× the in-sample k1 + k2 rate (milestone 4n: 14.8%), so at least 10.4%. Refuted below.
- **H95 (two disjoint calibrations of one camera agree).** 13-view k1 + k2 f of B against A: within 0.5% on each unit. Refuted on either.
- **Reported, unscored.** Per direction and unit rates; ±1% and ±4%; the in-sample S0 and ±2% rates within B (B's own 1,716 splits, the same code as milestone 4n); the plain jackknife J and χ²<sub>1</sub>; B's anchors against TUM's published f and flat-board Zhang on B; the median per-split threshold across pools against within a pool; converged counts and far solves.
- **Seen after the plan, before any target-free solve.** `prepare.py` reproduced 4j's 13 frames on both units and picked B's 13 at 1.0–24.3 s (fr1b) and 1.2–15.6 s (fr3b) from 4j's frames. The cold starts print the k1 flat-board Zhang f of each pool: fr1 520.79, fr1b 524.47 (+0.71%); fr3 530.59, fr3b 544.85 (+2.69%). fr3b's views are less tilted than fr3's (tilt measure 0.04–0.23, nine of 13 below 0.07), so H95 may fail on fr3 for a reason the plan did not name; the hypotheses are left as written.
- **Caveat set in advance.** B comes from the same hand-held sequence as A, interleaved in time (one view per block each), so it is fresh views of the same session, not a new session, temperature or mount; a drift across sessions is not tested. Within a pool the splits still share views, so no interval is given; the two directions are not independent of each other.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4o result: with k1 + k2 on the Kinect the twin's predicted power matches the real rate (0.995×, from 1.34×), and the gains compound: fitting k2 and then waiting for the twin's best new set catches a 2% focal change in 17.9% of old sets against 6.2% blind with k1 (×2.86)

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4o_k2wait/analyse.py` (about 3 min on 4 cores) builds a k1 twin on milestone 4j's solves and a k1 + k2 twin on milestone 4n's, from the same 10,000 resid draws per unit, and runs milestone 4l's `predict` on 7 + 6 and 6 + 6; `score.py` (under a minute) applies milestone 4l's `unit_stats` and 4m's `design_stats` and `pooled`, imported unchanged (`summary.json`, `k2wait.png`). The k1 arm reproduces milestones 4l and 4m's TUM figures exactly (fr1 AUC 0.749, mean π / real 1.34, median threshold 9.52; 6 + 6 pooled chosen 9.73% against 7.33% blind), checked before the k1 + k2 arm was read. `tests/test_m4o.py` checked first σ̂'s parameter count with the extra lens term (p 219 to 220), that the twin sets restricted to sizes 5–7 give the same R as the full ones for 6- and 7-view sets, and the pooled top-quarter rate and the H90 ratio on known cases.
- **H86 held.** AUC of π<sub>s</sub> for the real ±2% detection at 7 + 6, k1 + k2: fr1 0.775 (k1 0.749), fr3 0.749 (0.748).
- **H87 held.** Mean π over the real ±2% rate on fr1: 0.995 (0.192 against 0.193), against k1's 1.34. fr3 (unscored): 1.96 against 1.89.
- **H88 held.** Top quarter by π, TUM pooled: 30.7% against 14.8% over all splits (×2.08; k1 21.5% against 10.0%). fr1's top quarter 38.9% (k1 20.3%), fr3's 22.6% (unchanged).
- **H89 held.** 6 + 6, TUM pooled, old set fixed: the twin's pick catches ±2% in 15.2% of old sets against 11.3% blind (×1.35; k1 ×1.33).
- **H90 held.** The k1 + k2 chosen rate is 1.56× the k1 chosen rate (15.2% against 9.73%).
- **H91 held.** S0 false alarms, k1 + k2: top quarter at 7 + 6 1.65% (fr1; k1 2.35%) and 0.23% (fr3); chosen splits at 6 + 6 0.59% and 0.35%.
- **Unscored.** 6 + 6 under k1 + k2, which milestone 4n did not score: blind ±2% on fr1 14.0% (k1 6.2%), fr3 8.6% (8.4%); S0 false alarms of a blind pick 0.37% and 0.34% (k1 0.63% and 0.13%). Per unit, k1 blind to k1 + k2 chosen: fr1 6.2% to 17.9% (×2.86), fr3 8.4% to 12.5% (×1.49); pooled 7.33% to 15.2% (×2.07). Ceiling (any of the 7 candidates fires) on fr1 32.7% (k1 19.2%), so the twin's pick reaches 55% of it (k1 42%); fr3 26.1% (unchanged), 48%. Within-old-set AUC at ±2%: 0.710 and 0.702 (k1 0.640 and 0.681). Predicted against real gain at 6 + 6: fr1 1.31 against 1.28, fr3 1.25 against 1.46. The sd rule: 15.6% pooled (π's 15.2%), the same pick as π in 42% and 41% of old sets. ±4% at 6 + 6: fr1 chosen 52.3% (k1 31.6%); ±1%: 5.1% (1.8%). At 7 + 6 every split holding a far solve sits in the bottom quarter by π (fr1 4 of 4, fr3 20 of 20; k1 8 and 36), and the sd ranks as well as π (AUC 0.775 and 0.746).
- **Reading.** The two gains are independent and multiply. Fitting the lens order the Kinect needs doubles the update test's power at 6 + 6 as it did at 7 + 6 (6.2% to 14.0% blind), and the twin's choice among new sets adds the same third on top (×1.28 on fr1, ×1.35 pooled), so a robot that does both catches a 2% focal change nearly three times as often as one that does neither, with false alarms at or below 0.6%. The twin's level, 1.34× too high on the Kinect with k1, is right with k1 + k2: part of the "real f varies more than the twin says" gap of milestone 4l was distortion the k1 model left to be absorbed differently by each view set, which the linear twin cannot represent. It is not all of it: on the Xtion, whose lens k1 already follows, the twin still overstates its power 1.96×, so the remaining overstatement is something else (per-view effects the residual-pattern noise does not carry). The twin ranks a little better on the Kinect too (AUC 0.749 to 0.775 across splits, 0.640 to 0.710 within an old set).
- **Caveat.** As set in advance: 13 views per unit, splits and candidates sharing views, no interval; 5 + 6 (28 candidates) not run, its 4-view delete-one solves were never computed with k1 + k2. The k1 + k2 solves are milestone 4n's, untuned.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4o planned: with the lens the Kinect needs (k1 + k2), does the twin still rank updates, and does waiting for the best new set add to the doubling?

Written before any milestone-4o quantity was computed. Milestone 4n found that fitting k1 + k2 on the Kinect (fr1) doubles the 7 + 6 update test's power for a ±2% focal change, and milestone 4m that, with the old calibration held fixed, picking the candidate new set the twin rates highest gains ×1.33 on the TUM units (k1). The paper's open question (`paper/draft.md`, conclusion) is whether those two gains compound. No new solve: milestone 4n's k1 + k2 solves (5, 6, 7 and 13 views per unit) cover 7 + 6 and 6 + 6, so milestone 4l's predicted power (`rig_modules`, `sigma_hat`, `predict`, imported unchanged; σ̂ counts the extra lens parameter) and milestone 4m's choice rule (`groups`, `choose`, `design_stats`, imported unchanged) are run with a k1 + k2 twin beside a k1 arm on 4j's stored solves (same 10,000 resid draws per unit, seed 20261114). The k1 arm must reproduce milestones 4l and 4m's TUM figures (fr1 7 + 6 AUC 0.749, mean π / real 1.34; 6 + 6 chosen 9.73% against 7.33% blind) before anything is scored.

- **H86 (the twin still ranks on the Kinect).** k1 + k2, 7 + 6: AUC of π<sub>s</sub> for the real ±2% detection at least 0.70 on each TUM unit (k1: 0.749, 0.748). Refuted below on either.
- **H87 (the twin's level is closer on the Kinect).** k1 + k2, fr1, 7 + 6: mean π over the real ±2% rate nearer 1 than k1's 1.34 (|log ratio| smaller). Refuted if not.
- **H88 (testing the top quarter still pays).** k1 + k2, 7 + 6, TUM pooled: the real ±2% rate in the top quarter of each unit's splits by π is at least 1.5× the rate over all splits. Refuted below.
- **H89 (waiting still pays).** k1 + k2, 6 + 6, TUM pooled: the chosen rate is at least 1.2× the blind rate (k1: ×1.33). Refuted below.
- **H90 (and the gains compound).** k1 + k2, 6 + 6, TUM pooled: the chosen rate is at least 1.3× the k1 chosen rate (9.73%). Refuted below.
- **H91 (without false alarms).** k1 + k2: S0 false alarms at most 2.5% on each unit for the top quarter at 7 + 6 and for the chosen splits at 6 + 6. Refuted above on either.
- **Reported, unscored.** The 6 + 6 blind rates and S0 false alarms under k1 + k2 (milestone 4n scored 7 + 6 only); ceilings; within-old-set AUC; the sd rule; ±1% and ±4%; the twin's predicted gain against the real one.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The same 13 views per unit; splits and candidates share views, so no interval. 5 + 6 (28 candidates) cannot be run: its 5-view sets need 4-view delete-one solves that milestone 4n did not compute.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4n result: fitting the Kinect's lens with k1 + k2 doubles the update test's power for a 2% focal change (9.8% to 19.3% of 7 + 6 splits) at unchanged false alarms; on the undistorted Xtion it changes nothing

- **Runs.** 9,440 k1 + k2 solves (`experiments/m4n_k2/run.py`, 5-, 6-, 7- and 13-view sets of both units, about 2 h on 4 cores, most of it fr1); `analyse.py` (about 4 min) builds a k1 and a k1 + k2 twin per unit from the same 10,000 resid draws and scores every 7 + 6 split under both. The k1 arm reproduces milestone 4j's 7 + 6 figures exactly (S0 0.763% / 0.058%, ±2% 10.03%, median per-split threshold 9.52 / 10.84, 8 far 6-view solves), which was checked before the k1 + k2 solves finished. `tests/test_m4n.py` checked first that the k1 + k2 twin reproduces the k1 + k2 solver's 13-view solve and a small nonlinear perturbation, that each private module keeps its own lens model, and that the restricted `twin_sets` matches milestone 4i's.
- **H82 refuted.** Robust spread of f over 6-view sets, k1 + k2 over k1: 0.81 (fr1, 0.73% to 0.59% of f) and 0.97 (fr3, 0.51% to 0.50%). The extra term did not cost precision; on the Kinect it bought some.
- **H83 refuted.** fr1 6-view solves more than 5% from the median: 6 (k1: 8). fr3: 19 (k1: 30).
- **H84 held.** R against its per-split k1 + k2 twin threshold, S0 at 7 + 6: 0.59% (fr1) and 0.12% (fr3); k1 0.76% and 0.06%.
- **H85 refuted.** ±2% at 7 + 6, pooled: 14.8% against k1's 10.0%. All of the gain is on the Kinect: fr1 21.9% / 16.6% (−2% / +2%) against 11.0% / 8.6%; fr3 9.6% / 11.0% against 10.1% / 10.4%.
- **Unscored.** Paired, on the 1,696 fr1 splits usable under both models: at −2% the k1 + k2 test alone fires on 241 and the k1 test alone on 53 (agreement 82.7%); at +2% 191 and 53; under S0 8 and 10 (agreement 98.9%). fr3 agrees on 95–96% at ±2% and 99.8% under S0. ±4%: 41.5% to 50.2%; ±1%: 2.0% to 4.1%. S3 (the other unit's old views): 6.3% / 7.9% to 16.3% / 17.0%, with the units' 13-view f now 1.69% apart (k1: 1.40%; TUM: 3.5%). The plain jackknife at ±2%: fr1 13.6% / 9.7% to 21.3% / 18.5%. Median per-split threshold 10.31 (fr1) and 10.68 (fr3). Anchors: target-free 13-view f on fr1 525.16 px with k1 + k2 (+1.60% against TUM's 516.9; rms 0.152 to 0.129 px), fr3 534.04 (−0.61% against 537.3); flat-board Zhang on the same 13 views with k1, k1 + k2 and k1 + k2 + k3: fr1 520.79, 518.28, 517.39 (+0.75%, +0.27%, +0.09%), fr3 530.59, 530.71, 530.67. The one far 5-view k1 + k2 solve on fr1 sits about 175× the median (a 5-view set's own f, used only as a delete-one value).
- **Reading.** The pre-registered picture was wrong in the useful direction. On a lens that k1 cannot follow, the unmodelled distortion does not sit quietly in the residuals: each view set absorbs a different share of it into f, so the k1 solves of 6-view sets scatter more than the views' noise says, and a 2% focal change hides in that scatter. Adding k2 takes most of it out (spread −19%, far solves 8 to 6) and the update test catches twice as many ±2% changes on the Kinect and more than twice as many real unit swaps (6.3% / 7.9% to 16.3% / 17.0%, the swap involving the Kinect either way), with false alarms unchanged (0.59%). On the Xtion, whose images arrive undistorted, k2 changes nothing measurable: no precision cost, no gain. A fleet should fit the lens order its lens needs, and the cost of one term too many is not visible at 6 views here. The remaining +1.6% bias of the target-free f on the Kinect is not the lens order: flat-board Zhang on the same corners moves from +0.75% to +0.09% of TUM's figure as k2 and k3 are added, while the target-free solve stays at +1.6%, so the gap is the freedom the free board points leave (the board's metric shape traded against f and distortion), which milestone 5 showed is the only difference between the two solvers.
- **Caveat.** Only the 7 + 6 design; one hand-held sequence of 13 views per unit, splits sharing views, no interval. The k1 + k2 solves and threshold of fr1 were not tuned: the plan's solver, start and seed were used as written. k3 was not tried in the target-free solver.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4n planned: does a second radial term (k1 + k2) change the update test on the TUM Kinect, whose lens k1 alone cannot follow?

Written before any milestone-4n subset statistic was computed. Milestone 4j's caveat: fr1's lens (a Kinect; TUM's d0 0.26, d1 −0.95, d4 1.16) is not described by k1 alone, its 13-view f sits +1.85% above TUM's, and its residuals carry the unmodelled distortion into the twin. Every milestone since (4k–4m) kept k1 only. - **Seen before this plan.** A timing probe printed the 13-view solves with k1 and k1 + k2 (fr1 526.47 to 525.16 px, rms 0.152 to 0.129; fr3 533.87 to 534.04), flat-board Zhang with k1 and k1 + k2 on the same 13 views (fr1 520.79 and 518.28; fr3 530.59 and 530.71), and six k1 + k2 solves at each of 5, 6 and 7 views per unit.
