- **H84 (the twin reference stays safe under the richer model).** R against its per-split k1 + k2 twin threshold, S0 at 7 + 6: false alarms at most 2.5% on each unit (k1: 0.76% and 0.06%). Refuted above on either.
- **H85 (k2 lowers the power).** ±2% at 7 + 6, pooled over sign and unit: below milestone 4j's 10.0% (k1, same analysis). Refuted at or above.
- **Reported, unscored.** The 13-view anchors against TUM, including Zhang with k1 + k2 + k3 (TUM's own model order); the paired comparison on splits usable under both models (how often the ±2% verdict agrees); ±1% and ±4%; S3 (each unit's new views against the other unit's old ones; TUM puts the real f difference at 3.5%) with k1 + k2; the plain jackknife; χ²<sub>1</sub>; converged counts.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Thirteen views per unit from one hand-held sequence each; the splits share views, so no interval is given. Only the 7 + 6 design (the paper's recommended one) is re-run. k1 + k2 is still not TUM's five-term model, so a residual lens error remains on fr1.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4m result: with the old calibration fixed, the new set the twin rates highest catches a 2% focal change 1.33× as often as a blind pick on the TUM cameras (7.3% to 9.7%), 1.63× with 28 candidates; the twin gets the gain right but leaves most of the ceiling unused

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4m_wait/analyse.py --rig opencv` and `--rig tum` (about 15 min each, run side by side on 4 cores): milestone 4l's `rig_modules`, `sigma_hat` and `predict` imported unchanged, on 6 + 6, 5 + 6, 7 + 5 and 7 + 4 (12,012, 36,036, 10,296 and 25,740 splits per OpenCV camera; the TUM units have slightly fewer usable). `score.py` groups the splits by old set and scores H77–H81 (`summary.json`, `wait.png`). `tests/test_m4m.py` checked first the grouping (old sets with one candidate dropped), the tie rule, the blind rate as the group mean, the within-old-set AUC and the pooling on known cases. Old sets counted: 1,716 (6 + 6) and 1,287 (5 + 6) per OpenCV camera; 1,708 / 1,715 and 1,267 / 1,280 on fr1 / fr3.
- **H77 refuted.** TUM, 6 + 6, pooled: the twin's pick catches ±2% in 9.73% of old sets against 7.33% blind, ×1.33 (bar 1.5). fr1 6.2% to 8.0%, fr3 8.4% to 11.4%.
- **H78 refuted, narrowly.** OpenCV, 6 + 6, pooled: 31.6% against 27.0%, ×1.17 (bar 1.2). Left 28.5% to 32.8%, right 25.5% to 30.4%.
- **H79 held.** S0 false alarms of the chosen splits: left 1.40% / 1.32%, right 0.06% / 0.23%, fr1 0.35% / 0.71%, fr3 0.23% / 0.94% (6 + 6 / 5 + 6); the blind rates are 0.12–1.40%.
- **H80 held.** TUM pooled: ×1.63 at 5 + 6 (4.9% to 8.0%) against ×1.33 at 6 + 6.
- **H81 held.** The smallest twin sd of f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub> picks a split that catches ±2% in 10.4% of TUM old sets at 6 + 6, 6.5% above π's 9.7%; it picks the same candidate as π in 39–50% of old sets at 6 + 6 and 17–18% at 5 + 6.
- **Unscored.** Ceiling (any candidate fires) at 6 + 6: 55.1%, 54.9%, 19.2%, 26.1% (left, right, fr1, fr3); the twin's pick reaches 60%, 55%, 42% and 44% of it. Within-old-set AUC at ±2%: 0.638–0.711 at 6 + 6 and 5 + 6. The twin's predicted gain (its mean π on the chosen over the blind mean) against the real one, 6 + 6 / 5 + 6: left 1.15 / 1.23 against 1.15 / 1.34, right 1.20 / 1.30 against 1.19 / 1.34, fr1 1.31 / 1.46 against 1.29 / 1.40, fr3 1.25 / 1.40 against 1.36 / 1.75. At 7 + 5: ×1.40 TUM, ×1.25 OpenCV. At 7 + 4 the choice fails on the Kinect: 4.9% blind to 2.5% chosen, where the blind rate is inflated by 3.4% false alarms (milestone 4j's over-alarm at 4 new views); OpenCV gains ×1.84 there. ±4% at 6 + 6: fr1 26.4% to 31.6%, fr3 38.2% to 49.0%.
- **Reading.** Milestone 4l's doubling on the TUM cameras came mostly from choosing the old set, which a robot cannot do. Holding the old calibration fixed and choosing among 7 candidate new sets gains a third (×1.33), and among 28 about two thirds (×1.63); more candidates help, so a robot that waits through more views should gain more, which 13 views per unit cannot show. The twin predicts the gain itself well (within 0.11 on 7 of 8 unit-designs at 5–6 new views) even though it overstates the level 1.2–1.9×: it is a sound way to compare candidates, not to state a detection rate. It still leaves most of the ceiling unused: some candidate catches the change in 19–55% of old sets, the twin's pick in 8–33%, so most of what separates a lucky new set from an unlucky one is noise in the corners the twin cannot foresee. The cheaper sd rule does as well, so no threshold draws are needed to choose. Choosing never raised false alarms past 1.4%, except where the test itself over-alarms (4 new views on the TUM cameras), and there choosing should not be used.
- **Caveat.** As set in advance: 13 views per unit, candidates that share views, old sets of 5–6 views (smaller than 4i–4l's 7), no interval, "waiting" modelled as best-of-all-candidates. A bigger view pool (the TUM sequences hold 820 and 916 qualifying frames) would test a real stopping rule; the TUM host refused this session's proxy (403), so that run waits for a session that can fetch it.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4m planned: with the old calibration fixed, does waiting for the new view set the twin rates highest catch a 2% focal change more often?

Written before any milestone-4m quantity was computed. Milestone 4l's caveat: at 7 + 6 each old set leaves exactly one new set among a unit's 13 views, so choosing a split by the twin's predicted power π chose the old set as well. A robot holds one old calibration and can only choose which new views to test with. Here every old set is held fixed and its candidate new sets are the disjoint subsets of the views it leaves, so the choice is the robot's: same old set, pick the new set the twin rates highest. No new solve and no new data (the TUM host refused the proxy this session, so a larger view pool was not an option): milestone 4i's (OpenCV) and 4j's (TUM) cold-start solves and milestone 4l's predicted power, computed the same way (resid twin, 10,000 draws per unit, seed 20261114, σ̂ with the degrees-of-freedom correction) for more designs.

- **Designs.** 6 + 6: each of the 1,716 six-view old sets has 7 candidate six-view new sets (the 7 views it leaves, minus one). 5 + 6: each of the 1,287 five-view old sets has 28 candidates. 7 + 5 (6 candidates) and 7 + 4 (15) reported. An old set counts if at least 2 of its candidates are usable.
- **Rules.** *Choose*: the usable candidate with the highest π = (π<sub>0.98</sub> + π<sub>1.02</sub>)/2 (ties to the first in mask order). *Random*: the mean over the old set's usable candidates, the rate of picking one blind. The real detection is milestone 4i's per-split twin threshold on R, S1 at s, as scored in 4i, 4j and 4l. Rates are pooled over old sets and over s ∈ {0.98, 1.02}.
- **H77 (waiting pays on the TUM cameras).** 6 + 6, fr1 and fr3 pooled: the chosen rate is at least 1.5× the random rate. Refuted below.
- **H78 (and on OpenCV's).** 6 + 6, left and right pooled: at least 1.2×. Refuted below.
- **H79 (without raising false alarms).** S0 false alarms of the chosen splits at most 2.5% on each of the four units, at 6 + 6 and at 5 + 6. Refuted above on any.
- **H80 (more candidates, more gain).** On the TUM units pooled, the chosen / random ratio at 5 + 6 (28 candidates) exceeds the ratio at 6 + 6 (7). Refuted if not.
- **H81 (no threshold draws needed).** Choosing instead the candidate with the smallest twin sd of f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub> (no reference quantile) gives a chosen rate, TUM pooled at 6 + 6, within 10% (relative) of π's. Refuted outside.
- **Reported, unscored.** The ceiling (the share of old sets where any candidate fires); the share of old sets where the chosen candidate fires and the blind mean is below one half; within-old-set AUC (pairs of candidates of the same old set); ±1% and ±4%; 7 + 5 and 7 + 4; π's predicted gain against the real one.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Thirteen views per unit, so the candidate pools are small (7 or 28) and share views with each other; the old sets at 6 + 6 and 5 + 6 are smaller than milestones 4i–4l's 7, so their own R is wider. Old sets share views, so no interval is given; two rigs, four units. "Waiting" is modelled as seeing every candidate and taking the best, not as a stopping rule over views that arrive in order.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4l result: the twin ranks which updates can see a 2% focal change (AUC 0.66–0.75) and testing only its top quarter doubles the TUM cameras' power, 10.0% to 21.5%, at 0–2.3% false alarms; it overstates the level 1.2–1.9×

- **Runs.** `experiments/m4l_predict/analyse.py --rig opencv` and `--rig tum` (about 5 min each on 4 cores): milestone 4i's twin draws (resid model, 10,000 per split, seed 20261114) rebuilt per unit; every per-split threshold reproduces the scored ones of milestones 4i and 4j (medians at 7 + 6: 10.93, 12.49, 9.52, 10.84; at 7 + 4 on fr3: 0.43, as 4j stored), and the real ±2% rates reproduce theirs (left 32.8%, right 33.4%, fr1 9.8%, fr3 10.3%). `score.py` scores H73–H76 and writes `summary.json` and `power.png`. `tests/test_m4l.py` checked first that the predicted power equals the normal-theory power on a Gaussian case, the AUC on known orderings, the quartile gate and σ̂'s degrees of freedom (N 1,404, p 237 on the 54-corner board). Before the scored run, a 500-draw check of the OpenCV rig (not kept) printed the per-design means, so the level on those two units was seen before scoring; nothing was changed after it.
- **H73 refuted, on OpenCV's cameras.** AUC of π<sub>s</sub> for the real detection at ±2%, 7 + 6: left 0.659, right 0.693, fr1 0.749, fr3 0.748 (bar 0.70 on each).
- **H74 held.** Mean π over the real ±2% rate: left 1.88 (0.615 against 0.328), right 1.19 (0.399 against 0.334), fr1 1.34 (0.131 against 0.098), fr3 1.89 (0.194 against 0.103). The twin overstates power on all four units.
- **H75 held.** Top quarter of each TUM unit's splits by π: real ±2% rate 21.5% pooled (fr1 20.3%, fr3 22.6%), against 10.0% over all splits; the other three quarters: 6.3% and 6.2%.
- **H76 held.** S0 false alarms in the top quarter: left 0.70%, right 0.00%, fr1 2.35% (10 of 426; all splits 0.76%), fr3 0.00%.
- **Unscored.** OpenCV's top quarter: 48.3% (left) and 50.0% (right) against 32.8% and 33.4%. Absolute gates: π ≥ 0.3 passes 92.9% / 69.6% / 0% / 28.0% of splits (left / right / fr1 / fr3), with real ±2% rates 34.7%, 42.3%, none, 21.2%; π ≥ 0.5 passes 74.3% and 35.3% on OpenCV's (38.1%, 49.6%) and no TUM split. AUC at ±4%: 0.84, 0.88, 0.76, 0.86; at ±1%: 0.58, 0.59, 0.75, 0.71. Ranking by the twin's predicted sd of f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub> alone: AUC 0.660, 0.690, 0.752, 0.743 (Spearman with π 0.98 on each unit), so the cheaper gate does as well. σ̂ without the degrees-of-freedom correction: level ratios 2.09, 1.40, 1.63, 2.26. Every split that holds one of milestone 4j's far solves (8 on fr1, 36 on fr3) sits in the bottom quarter by π. At 7 + 4 the prediction fails on the TUM units: AUC 0.42 (fr1) and 0.64 (fr3), level ratio 0.75 and 0.41, and fr3's top quarter raises 5.0% false alarms; at 7 + 5 AUC 0.64–0.73 and ratios 1.48–1.82.
- **Reading.** A unit's own twin can say before a test runs which updates are worth testing. On the hand-held TUM captures, testing only the quarter of updates the twin rates highest doubles the chance of catching a 2% focal change, and every split that carries a far, near-degenerate solve is in the quarter it rates lowest; the sd of the twin's f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub> carries the same ranking, so a robot needs no threshold draws to make the choice. The ranking is moderate (AUC 0.66–0.75), weakest on OpenCV's cameras where most splits are already informative, and the twin's absolute power is 1.2–1.9× too high: the real f's vary more than the linear twin fed with the unit's residual patterns says, so an absolute gate ("test when π ≥ 0.5") is not safe to read as a power figure. Use the twin to rank or to wait for a better view set, not to promise a detection rate. At 4 new views the twin's delete-one sets are 3-view and the prediction breaks down, a third reason to collect at least 6.
- **Caveat.** At 7 + 6 each old set leaves exactly one new set among the 13 views, so choosing a split by π chooses the partition, old set included; a robot holds one old set and can only wait for better new views, and that gain is not measured here (it needs more than 13 views per unit). The quarters share views, so no interval is given; two rigs, four units. fr1's top-quarter false alarms (2.35%) sit three times its all-split rate and just inside the bar.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4l planned: can the unit's twin tell, before the test runs, which updates are able to catch a 2% focal change?

Written before any milestone-4l quantity was computed. Milestones 4j and 4k found that power is a property of the unit's views: a 2% focal change at 7 + 6 views is caught in 33% of splits on OpenCV's cameras and 10% on the TUM ones, and the far, near-degenerate subset solves are real minima, not a solver artefact. A fleet cannot change how informative a camera's views are, but it can decide which updates to run the test on, or wait for more views. The twin of milestone 4i already holds what that decision needs: for each (old, new) split, its draws give the reference threshold, and the same draws with a shift added give the twin's own predicted power. Here that prediction is scored against the real detections, split by split, on all four units.

- **Predicted power.** For each 7 + 6 split and s in {0.98, 1.02}, π<sub>s</sub> = the share of the split's 10,000 twin draws (resid model, milestone 4i's seed and chunks, so the thresholds are the scored ones of 4i and 4j) with z<sub>R</sub><sup>2</sup> = (σ̂·Δ + (s − 1)·f<sub>13</sub>)<sup>2</sup> / (σ̂<sup>2</sup>(R<sub>old</sub> + s<sup>2</sup>R<sub>new</sub>)) above the split's own twin threshold, where Δ and R are the twin's unit-scale draws and σ̂ = rms·√(N/(N − p)) is the unit's 13-view residual scale with the degrees-of-freedom correction (N residuals, p free parameters). π = (π<sub>0.98</sub> + π<sub>1.02</sub>)/2. Nothing is fitted to the real detections. Code: `experiments/m4l_predict/analyse.py`, which imports milestone 4i's (left, right) and 4j's (fr1, fr3) analysis unchanged; real statistics are their cold-start solves.
- **H73 (the twin ranks the splits).** AUC of π<sub>s</sub> for the real detection (R against its per-split twin threshold, S1 at s), pooled over the two signs: ≥ 0.70 on each of the four units. Refuted below on any.
- **H74 (and gets the level).** Mean π over the splits within a factor of 2 of the real ±2% rate on each unit (0.5 ≤ ratio ≤ 2). Refuted outside on any.
- **H75 (choosing the update doubles the power on the TUM units).** In the top quartile of each TUM unit's splits by π, the real ±2% rate pooled over the two units and signs is ≥ 20% (all splits: 10.0%). Refuted below.
- **H76 (without raising false alarms).** S0 false alarms in the same top quartile ≤ 2.5% on each of the four units. Refuted above on any.
- **Reported, unscored.** The OpenCV units' top quartile; absolute gates (π ≥ 0.3, 0.5) with the share of splits that pass; σ̂ without the correction; 7 + 4 and 7 + 5; ±1% and ±4%; the ranking by the twin's predicted sd of f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub> alone (no threshold), as a cheaper gate; whether the real far solves of milestone 4j sit in the bottom quartile.
- **Caveat set in advance.** Selecting splits by π selects old sets as well as new ones; a robot has one old set, so the gate's practical form is "wait for a new set the twin says can see the change". The splits reuse views, so quartiles share views and no interval is given; two rigs, four units.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4k result: the far solves on the TUM cameras are real minima of near-degenerate view sets, not a cold-start artefact; a warm start reproduces every one of them, and a basin check that drops them raises the false alarms at 4 new views on the Xtion from 4.7% to 20%

- **Runs.** 11,442 warm-started solves (`experiments/m4k_warm/run.py`, f started at f<sub>P</sub> = 527.1 px, about 85 min on 4 cores); milestone 4j's analysis run unchanged on arms W, WB and CB (`analyse.py`, about 10 min); `compare.py` sets each subset's warm and cold solves side by side (post hoc). `tests/test_m4k.py` checked first that f<sub>P</sub> is TUM's mean, that the basin check drops only solves outside ±10% and leaves its input untouched, and that a warm and a cold start land on the same f on a well-conditioned 6-view set.
- **H68 refuted.** Arm W, 6-view solves more than 5% from the median: 76 (fr1) and 30 (fr3), against 8 and 30 cold. On fr3 the warm start changes nothing: every 5-, 6- and 7-view subset that converged from both starts lands on the same f (1,285 / 1,285, 1,716 / 1,716, 1,716 / 1,716). On fr1 it is worse: the 13-view solve from f<sub>P</sub> stops at f = 508.88 px with rms 0.483 px, against the cold start's 526.47 px at 0.152 px (cost 145.5 against 14.5), and the cold start reaches a lower cost on 89 of the 6-view and 98 of the 7-view sets, the warm start on none.
- **H69 refuted.** Arm W, 3-view sets converged: 96.5% (fr1, 276 / 286) and 80.8% (fr3, 231 / 286), against 95.8% and 82.9% cold.
- **H70 refuted.** Arm WB, S0 at k = 4: 4.36% (fr1) and 20.09% (fr3), against 3.27% and 4.73% cold.
- **H71 refuted, on fr1 at k = 6.** Arm WB: k = 5 1.28% / 0.88%, k = 6 2.13% / 0.18% (fr1 / fr3).
- **H72 refuted.** Arm WB, ±2% at k = 6 pooled: 10.6% (cold 10.0%).
- **Unscored.** Arm CB (cold solves with the basin check, which leaves the fr1 13-view solve and twin as in milestone 4j): S0 8.09% / 19.09% at k = 4, 1.02% / 0.88% at 5, 0.71% / 0.18% at 6; ±2% at k = 6 10.5%, ±4% 43.1%, ±1% 2.1%. The basin check leaves 275 of fr1's 715 and 89 of fr3's 715 four-view sets usable (cold: 686 and 587), because most 3-view solves lie more than 10% from f<sub>P</sub> (fr3: 86 of 286 survive). Arm W: S0 2.12% / 4.66% at k = 4, 3.35% / 0.69% at 5, 5.28% / 0.06% at 6; ±2% 11.0%. S3 at k = 6 (WB): 5.4% / 8.3%. Harmful adoptions (R, per-split twin): cold 0.29%, W 0.46%, CB 0.73%, WB 1.12%.
- **Post hoc, computed after the scored run.** All 38 of milestone 4j's far 6-view cold solves (8 on fr1, including f = 9,289 px, and 30 on fr3) come back from the warm start at the same f to 10<sup>−4</sup>. fr3's 30 fit their corners as well as the 13-view solve does (median rms 0.071 px against 0.075 px): they are minima of the data, in view sets whose geometry leaves f nearly free, and the cost surface has no lower point nearer f<sub>P</sub>.
- **Reading.** Milestone 4j's caveat that "the far solves could be removed by a warm start or a basin check" was wrong on both counts. A warm start cannot remove a far solve that is the subset's true minimum, and on these two cameras every one of them is. Starting f at the spec does not start the rest of the problem well either: the poses and board come from a fronto-parallel guess at that f with the principal point at the image centre and k1 = 0, and on the strongly distorted Kinect that start falls into a worse minimum on the full 13 views and on 5–6% of 6–7-view sets (why the 1.3× start avoids it was not studied). A basin check that drops solves far from the spec does harm: it removes exactly the delete-one values that widen the robust jackknife when a view set is near-degenerate, so the surviving sets' R is too narrow for their twin threshold, and false alarms at 4 new views rise to 8–20%. The power gap on the TUM cameras (10% against 33% on OpenCV's) is not the solver's doing: it is how little these hand-held views pin f down. For a fleet: keep the lowest-cost solve over several starts (here the cold start is never beaten on a set of 6 or more views), never clip f to a window, and treat a wide delete-one spread as information the test needs.
- **Caveat.** Two units, one sequence each; the splits reuse views, so no interval is given. One warm start was tried (f only); a start that also sets the principal point and k1 from the spec, or poses from a previous solve, was not.

## 2026-11-15 · Milestone 4k planned: warm-start every subset solve from the fleet's nominal focal length, with a basin check, and re-score the TUM units

Written before any milestone-4k solve was run. Milestone 4j found that on the two TUM RGB-D cameras the cold-start solver (start f at 1.3× the k1 Zhang f: 677.0 and 689.8 px, 28–29% above the units' own 13-view f) lands in far minima on some subsets (6-view solves more than 5% from the median: 8 of 1,709 on fr1, up to f = 9,289 px, and 30 of 1,716 on fr3; none on OpenCV's cameras) and fails to converge on 4% and 17% of 3-view sets. A robot in a fleet never starts cold: it knows the spec of its camera model. Here every subset is re-solved from that spec, and a basin check fixed now drops any solve the spec says is implausible.

- **Start.** f<sub>P</sub> = the mean over the two units of TUM's published (f<sub>x</sub> + f<sub>y</sub>)/2 = (516.9 + 537.3)/2 = 527.1 px, one figure for both units, as a fleet with one spec sheet would hold; principal point at the image centre and k1 = 0 as before. Nothing else in milestone 4f's solver changes (`experiments/m4k_warm/run.py`): every subset of 3–7 views and all 13, both units, 11,442 solves. The twin is refitted from the same start and must reproduce the new 13-view f.
- **Arms.** W: warm start alone. WB (scored): warm start, and a solve with |f/f<sub>P</sub> − 1| > 10% counts as not converged (a far solve then cannot enter as a set or a delete-one value; 10% is wider than any change the test is meant to catch, and the units' published f are within 2% of f<sub>P</sub>). Milestone 4j's splits, scenarios, statistics and per-split twin references (resid model, 10,000 draws, seed 20261114) are run unchanged on each arm by importing `m4j_tum/analyse.py`.
- **H68 (the warm start stays in the basin).** Arm W: 6-view solves more than 5% from the unit's median 6-view f number at most 2 on each unit. Refuted above on either.
- **H69 (and converges on small sets).** Arm W: ≥ 97% of 3-view sets converge on each unit (cold: 95.8% and 82.9%). Refuted below on either.
- **H70 (false alarms at 4 new views come down).** Arm WB, R against its per-split twin threshold, S0 at k = 4: ≤ 2.5% on each unit (cold: 3.27% and 4.73%). Refuted above on either.
- **H71 (and stay low at 5–6).** Arm WB, the same at k = 5 and at k = 6: ≤ 1.5% on each unit and each k (cold: 0.06–0.80%). Refuted above on any.
- **H72 (power recovers part of the way).** Arm WB, ±2% at k = 6 pooled over sign and unit: ≥ 15% (cold: 10.0%; OpenCV's cameras: 33.1%). Refuted below.
- **Reported, unscored.** Arm W's rates; the cold solves with the same basin check (CB; milestone 4j's solves were already looked at, so this arm cannot be scored); the 13-view f and its change from cold; S3 (the unit swap); ±1% and ±4%; harmful adoptions; far solves at 4, 5 and 7 views; solver evaluations per solve.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The scaled-focal scenarios scale the new set's solved f after the solve, as in milestones 4g–4j, so the warm start sits at the unscaled spec; a real 2% change would start 2% from f<sub>P</sub>, well inside the 10% check. Two units, one sequence each; the splits reuse views, so no interval is given.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 4j result: on two more cameras (TUM RGB-D's Kinect and Xtion) the twin reference stays quiet at 5–6 new views (0.1–0.8% false alarms) but over-alarms at 4 (3.3% and 4.7%), and a 2% focal change is caught in 10% of 6-view updates, not 33%

- **Runs.** 13 views per unit by `prepare.py`'s rule (fr1: 820 of 1,507 frames qualified; fr3: 916 of 1,327); 11,442 solves (`run.py`, 2 h on 4 cores, most of it fr1, whose 3-view sets often run to the 2,000-evaluation cap). Converged: fr1 274 / 286 three-view sets, 698 / 715 four-view, ≥ 99.5% at 5–7; fr3 237 / 286, 708 / 715, ≥ 99.8%. Usable 4-view sets (≥ 3 of their 4 delete-one solves converged): 686 and 587 of 715. `analyse.py` (milestone 4g's splits and 4i's twin, imported unchanged, about 5 min); `check.py` 1,280 cold-start solves (8 min). `tests/test_m4j.py` checked first that the corners load, that the 8×6 board is wired through the solver, and that the twin on 48 corners matches the solver.
- **H63 held.** fr3's 13-view f is 533.87 px against TUM's (fx + fy)/2 = 537.3: −0.64%. fr1 (unscored): 526.47 against 516.9, +1.85%, the bias expected from fitting k1 alone to a lens TUM describes with d1 = −0.95. Rms 0.152 px (fr1) and 0.075 px (fr3).
- **H64 refuted, on fr1.** fr3: median sd(nonlinear Δf)/sd(g<sub>S</sub>·ε) 1.000–1.004 for n = 4–7, correlation ≥ 0.993. fr1: 1.002 at n = 5 and 6, but at n = 4 one of the four random sets never converged from the cold start (40 of 40 draws; the other three read 1.01–1.08), so the median is undefined; and at n = 7 two of four sets jumped to far minima (24 of 160 draws more than 5 sd from the linear prediction; sd ratio up to 9.6, median 2.61). The linear prediction holds where the solver stays in its basin; the misses are the cold-start solver's.
- **H65 refuted, at 4 new views.** R against its per-split twin threshold, S0: fr1 3.27% at k = 4, 0.80% at 5, 0.76% at 6; fr3 4.73%, 0.62%, 0.06% (k = 4–6 pooled 2.47% and 3.48%; bar 0.5–2.5%). The 4-view sets' delete-one solves are 3-view, where the twin is least trustworthy (milestone 4i) and the real solves fail most often.
- **H66 refuted.** ±2% at k = 6 pooled over sign and unit: 10.0% (fr1 11.0 / 8.6%, fr3 10.1 / 10.4%; milestone 4i: 33.1%). ±4%: 33.7–48.0%; ±1%: 1.5–2.8%.
- **H67 refuted.** The units' own 13-view f differ by 1.40%, not TUM's 3.5%: fr1's k1-only bias (+1.85%) closes most of the gap. S3 at k = 6: R fires on 6.3% (fr1's new views against fr3's old) and 7.9% (the reverse). That sits between the same test's ±1% (2.0%) and ±2% (10.0%) rates on scaled focal lengths, so the real change is caught about as often as a scaled one of its size.
- **Unscored.** Per-split twin thresholds at 7 + 6, 10th / 50th / 90th percentile: 6.88 / 9.52 / 17.33 (fr1) and 8.62 / 10.84 / 16.60 (fr3), near milestone 4i's 11–13. At k = 6 the plain jackknife against its twin reference catches ±2% in 12.1% (false alarms 0.82% / 0.12%), one threshold per design 8.0%, R against χ²<sub>1</sub> 16.7% at 1.23% / 0.52%, R against milestone 4h's 18.45 3.2%, J against χ²<sub>1</sub> 1.8%. The iid noise model: ±2% 9.3%, S0 over k = 4–6 2.92% / 5.67%. Harmful adoptions: 0.29% of decisions (R, per-split twin), 0.18% (R, χ²<sub>1</sub>).
- **Post hoc, computed after the scored run.** Why the power fell: the robust spread of f over all 6-view sets is 0.73% of f (fr1) and 0.51% (fr3), against 0.51% and 0.30% on OpenCV's cameras (milestone 4f's solves), and the cold-start solver lands far away on some subsets that OpenCV's views never produced: 6-view solves more than 5% from the median, 8 of 1,709 (fr1, up to f = 9,289 px) and 30 of 1,716 (fr3), against 0 of 1,716 on each OpenCV camera. A far solve that enters as a delete-one value inflates J (J against χ²<sub>1</sub> barely fires) and, as a new set, fires the test whatever the truth. The real-spread-to-predicted ratio milestone 4g used is dominated by those solves here (R at 7 + 6: 10.4 on fr1) and is not reported as a finding.
- **Reading.** The twin reference transfers to two different cameras, lenses and boards in the one way that matters most for a fleet: at 5 and 6 new views it stays at or below its nominal 1% (0.06–0.80%) with nothing tuned on these units. It does not transfer at 4 new views, where its delete-one sets are 3-view and both the twin and the real solver leave their linear regime; the paper's recommendation to collect at least 6 new views before testing now has a second reason. The 33% power does not transfer: power is a property of the unit, set by how precisely 6 views fix its focal length and by how often the cold-start solver falls out of its basin, and these hand-held 640×480 captures give 10%. A real 1.4% difference between two units is caught as often as a scaled change of that size, which is the first check of the test on a change no script applied.
- **Caveat.** Two units, one sequence each (about 50 s, one person holding the board), 13 views; the splits reuse views, so no interval is given. fr1's lens is not described by k1 alone. The far solves could be removed by a warm start or a basin check, which would change the power figures; that is a separate, pre-registered step.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 4j planned: the twin reference and the update test on two more units (TUM RGB-D's Kinect and Xtion)

Written before any milestone-4j focal length was looked at. Every result of milestones 1–4i comes from one stereo rig (OpenCV's two cameras, one board, 13 views), and the paper's Section 10 asks first for the twin reference and the robust test "pre-registered on a second unit's views". The TUM RGB-D benchmark (Sturm et al., IROS 2012; CC BY 4.0) publishes checkerboard calibration sequences for each of its cameras, with the authors' own calibration: `freiburg1_rgb_calibration` (a Microsoft Kinect, fx 517.3, fy 516.5, strong distortion d0 0.2624, d1 −0.9531) and `freiburg3_calibration_rgb_depth` (an Asus Xtion whose images come undistorted, fx 535.4, fy 539.2). Two new units, a different board (8×6 inner corners), a different sensor and lens, and a real focal difference between them (3.5% in TUM's figures), which gives the swap scenario a true change instead of a scaled one.

- **Data.** 13 views per unit picked by a rule written into `experiments/m4j_tum/prepare.py` before any solve: full-board detections with 5×5 corners, the even board's 180° ambiguity resolved by the first row pointing right, corner spacing ≥ 15 px, then 13 equal-count blocks in time and the most foreshortened of each block's sharper half. A first version of the rule (sharpest frame per time block) picked boards 7–8 px between corners and 11 of 13 fronto-parallel views; it was replaced after looking only at corner spacing and a contact sheet, no solve. Only corners and frame names are stored (`corners_fr1.json`, `corners_fr3.json`); a person holds the board in every frame, so no image is committed (`fetch.sh` gets the sequences). fr1: 820 of 1,507 frames qualify; fr3: 916 of 1,327.
- **Solves.** Milestone 4f's solver unchanged (k1 only, start 1.3× the k1 Zhang f, references 0 and n // 2): every subset of 3–7 views and all 13, both units, 11,442 solves (`run.py`). Splits, scenarios, J and R exactly as milestone 4g, with the two TUM units in place of left and right: S0, S1 (s = 0.96–1.04) and S3 (the old set from the other unit). The twin and its references exactly as milestone 4i (resid model scored, iid reported, 10,000 draws per split, seed 20261114).
- **H63 (anchor).** fr3's 13-view f lies within 1% of TUM's (fx + fy)/2 = 537.3 px. Refuted outside. (fr1's is reported against 516.9 only: the k1 model cannot follow its d1 = −0.95, so a bias is expected there.)
- **H64 (the linearisation holds on these units).** As H59 with 4 random sets of each size 4–7 per unit, 40 draws each, iid noise at the unit's 13-view rms: the median over sets of sd(nonlinear Δf)/sd(g<sub>S</sub>·ε) lies in 0.8–1.25 for each n on both units. Refuted outside.
- **H65 (the twin reference is near nominal on new units).** R against its per-split twin threshold, S0 false alarms over k = 4–6 in 0.5–2.5% on each unit (milestone 4i: 1.44% and 0.37%). Refuted outside on either.
- **H66 (power carries over).** ±2% at k = 6, pooled over sign and unit, detected in ≥ 25% of splits (milestone 4i: 33.1%). Refuted below.
- **H67 (a real unit swap is caught).** S3 at k = 6 (old 7 views from the other unit, new 6 from this one; the old set's reference drawn from the other unit's twin, as in milestone 4i): R against its per-split twin threshold fires on ≥ 50% of splits on each unit. Refuted below on either.
- **Reported, unscored.** fr1's f against TUM's; both units' 13-view rms; J against its twin reference; one twin threshold per design; R against χ²<sub>1</sub> and against milestone 4h's independent-offset reference (18.45 at 7 + 6; unit-free); the iid model; ±1% and ±4%; k = 4 and 5; harmful adoptions; the twin's and the real scale ratios (milestone 4i's post hoc check).
- **Caveat set in advance.** fr1's lens is not described by k1 alone, so its residuals carry the unmodelled distortion; the resid noise model passes that pattern into the twin, which is the point of a per-unit twin, but a large model error could also bend H64. The 13 views of each unit come from one hand-held sequence of about 50 s, so they are not independent sessions; no interval is given.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 4i result: a reference from the unit's own linearised twin catches a 2% focal change from 6 views in 33% of splits, not 19%, at 0.4–1.4% false alarms; once the reference knows the geometry, the plain jackknife does as well as the robust one

- **Runs.** The twin is each camera's 13-view 5×5 solve, re-run to keep its poses and points (f 534.336 and 535.753 px, equal to milestone 4f's stored solves to 10<sup>−8</sup>; rms 0.114 and 0.153 px). Gains g<sub>S</sub> for every subset of 3–7 views (5,720 per camera, about 15 s), 10,000 noise draws per split, every split of milestone 4g at 7 + 4, 7 + 5 and 7 + 6 (`experiments/m4i_geomref/analyse.py`, about 4 min). `tests/test_m4i.py` checked first that the twin reproduces the stored solve, that a gain matches a small nonlinear perturbation, that it does not depend on which view fixes the gauge, and that a per-split reference fires on 0.6–1.4% of independent twin draws. `check.py` ran 9,600 cold-start nonlinear solves on twin corners (25 min on 4 cores).
- **H59 held.** sd(nonlinear Δf)/sd(g<sub>S</sub>·ε), median over sets, n = 4–7: 1.0001–1.0004 (left) and 0.9990–1.0017 (right); median correlation ≥ 0.9995. At 3× the real rms: 0.999–1.018. The resid model: 0.9995–1.005. **Not at 3 views:** some 3-view sets leave the linear regime (left, iid: 40 of 320 draws more than 5 sd from the linear prediction; resid: 52 left, 24 right; one right set's nonlinear sd is 23× the linear one). 3-view sets enter only as the delete-one sets of 4-view new sets, so the twin is least trustworthy at 7 + 4.
- **References.** resid model, 7 + 6: the per-split 99th percentile of z<sub>R</sub>² has median 10.93 (left) and 12.49 (right), 10th–90th percentile 8.78–14.69 and 9.81–16.15; one percentile pooled over the design: 11.64 and 12.90. Milestone 4h's independent-offset model gave 18.45, χ²<sub>1</sub> 6.63. Medians at 7 + 4: 8.73 and 7.38; at 7 + 5: 11.85 and 12.57. For the plain jackknife J: median 3.83 and 4.13 at 7 + 6.
- **H60 refuted, on the right camera.** R against its per-split twin threshold, S0 over k = 4–6: 1.44% (left, inside 0.5–2.5%) and 0.37% (right, below).
- **H61 held.** ±2% at k = 6 pooled over sign and camera: 33.1% (per camera and sign 31.5–35.3%), against 18.6% for milestone 4h's model reference.
- **H62 held.** 90th over 10th percentile of the per-split threshold at 7 + 6: 1.67 (left) and 1.65 (right).
- **Unscored.** ±4% at k = 6: 81.2–84.7% (model reference: 66.4–75.1%); ±1%: 4.1–10.5%; ±2% at k = 5: 18.2%, at k = 4: 8.9%. The plain jackknife J against its own per-split twin threshold: ±2% at k = 6 in 34.7%, false alarms 1.59% / 0.35%. One twin threshold per design instead of per split: R 30.8% at 1.60% / 0.66%. The iid noise model instead of the unit's residual patterns: R 30.7% at 1.74% / 0.42%. The swap stays invisible to f alone (0.5% at k = 6). Harmful adoptions: 0.24% of decisions (per-split), 0.36% (per-design). Each split's threshold correlates only weakly with its real z<sub>R</sub>² under S0 (Spearman 0.24 left, 0.10 right).
- **Post hoc, computed after the scored run.** Under the twin (resid), sd(f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub>) over the root mean predicted variance at 7 + 6 is 0.75 / 0.76 for R and 0.52 / 0.54 for J; on the real views milestone 4g measured 0.88 / 0.48 and 0.57 / 0.31. At 7 + 4 the twin gives R 0.31 / 0.29 against the real 0.31 / 0.25. So the twin reproduces most of the jackknife's small-set overstatement on the left camera from the views' geometry alone, and the right camera's jackknife overstates its real spread about 1.6× more than its twin says. That is why the right camera sits below the bar: the same extra the independent-offset model missed in milestone 4f (its views do not add independent offsets).
- **Reading.** The jackknife's overstatement at 4–7 views is mostly geometry: a linear twin built from the unit's own solve predicts it, and a reference drawn from that twin is 11–13 at 7 + 6 views rather than the independent-offset model's 18.45. Against it the robust test fires on 1.4% and 0.4% of unchanged updates and catches a 2% focal change from 6 views in a third of splits, 1.8× milestone 4h's honest figure, with nothing borrowed and from solves the robot already ran. It does not reach the 43% of the borrowed threshold or the 54% of χ²<sub>1</sub>, whose 2–3% false alarms rested on cancelling errors. Two side findings change the paper's recommendation. First, with a geometry-aware reference the MAD buys nothing: the plain jackknife catches 34.7%. Second, one threshold per design does almost as well as one per split (30.8%); the per-split spread is real (H62) but barely tracks the real statistics, so most of the gain is the twin getting the design's tail right.
- **Caveat.** One rig, one board, 13 shared views; no interval is given. The twin is linear and is fed the unit's own 13 residual patterns, so error sources the residuals do not show (the right camera's extra spread) leave the test conservative there rather than lenient. That is the safe direction for a fleet, but it means the 0.37% is a property of this unit, not a guarantee.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 4i planned: a reference for each split from the unit's own linearised bundle adjustment

Written before any milestone-4i statistic was computed on the real splits. Milestone 4h gave the robust jackknife test (R) a reference with nothing borrowed, from a model in which each view adds an independent offset to f. On this rig it was conservative (0.2–0.5% false alarms) and weak (±2% at 6 new views caught in 19% of splits), because the model ignores the views' geometry: a small set near the critical configuration has delete-one solves that swing far (milestone 4f), which widens R in a way independent offsets do not. A reference that knows the geometry should fire near its nominal 1% and recover part of the power χ²<sub>1</sub> seemed to have (54%). Here the robot's own 13-view solve is the twin: for any view subset S its first-order change in f under a pixel perturbation ε is g<sub>S</sub>·ε, with g<sub>S</sub> = J<sub>S</sub>(J<sub>S</sub><sup>T</sup>J<sub>S</sub>)<sup>−1</sup>e<sub>f</sub> from the subset problem's Jacobian at the twin (`experiments/m4i_geomref/twin.py`). One matrix product then draws every set's f and its delete-one f's, and so z<sub>R</sub><sup>2</sup>, for each (old, new) split, and the split's own 99th percentile is its threshold. A unit can compute it from solves it already ran; nothing is borrowed from another unit.

- **Data.** Milestone 4g's splits, scenarios and real statistics, imported unchanged; the twin is the 13-view 5×5 solve of milestone 4f (re-run to keep its poses and points; it must reproduce the stored f to 10<sup>−8</sup>). No new real solve.
- **Noise models** (the statistic is scale-free, so only the pattern matters). *resid* (scored): each view gets the 13-view fit's residual pattern of a view drawn at random from the unit's own 13, with a random sign, so the within-view correlation milestone 6 found (columns, the bent board) is carried to every view. *iid*: independent N(0, 1) per pixel coordinate (reported). 10,000 draws per split, seed 20261114; designs 7 + 4, 7 + 5, 7 + 6; S3 (swap) uses the other camera's twin for the old set.
- **H59 (the linearisation holds for this solver).** Synthetic corners from each camera's twin plus iid noise at the real 13-view rms, solved by the same cold-start solver as the real runs (8 random sets of each size 3–7, 40 draws each, `check.py`): the median over sets of sd(nonlinear Δf)/sd(g<sub>S</sub>·ε) lies in 0.8–1.25 for each of n = 4, 5, 6, 7 on both cameras. Refuted if any falls outside. (3× the rms and the resid model reported.)
- **H60 (the per-split twin reference is near nominal on real views).** R against its per-split threshold (resid model), S0 false alarms over k = 4–6 in 0.5–2.5% on each camera. Refuted outside on either.
- **H61 (and recovers power).** ±2% at k = 6, pooled over sign and camera, detected in ≥ 25% of splits (milestone 4h's model reference: 18.6%). Refuted below.
- **H62 (geometry matters split by split).** Over the 1,716 splits at 7 + 6, the ratio of the 90th to the 10th percentile of the per-split threshold is ≥ 1.5 on both cameras. Refuted below on either (then one threshold per design would do).
- **Reported, unscored.** The iid model; a per-design twin reference (one 99th percentile pooled over the design's splits); J against its own twin references; ±1% and ±4%; k = 4 and 5; the swap; harmful adoptions; the Spearman correlation of each split's threshold with its real z<sub>R</sub><sup>2</sup> under S0.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The twin is linear and its noise comes from 13 residual patterns of one rig, so a unit whose errors are dominated by something the residuals do not show (a shared offset across views, for instance) would not be described by it. The 4f solves showed that the real spread of f is 2–6× the solver's σ, which a scale-free statistic absorbs only if the extra spread has the same pattern over views as the pixel noise. One rig, one board, 13 shared views: no interval is given.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 4h result: a reference derived from the independent-offset model keeps the robust jackknife test at 0.2–0.5% false alarms, but it catches a 2% focal change from 6 views in 19% of splits, not 54%; χ²<sub>1</sub> looked right only because two errors cancel

- **Runs.** No new solve: milestone 4g's splits, scenarios and statistics, imported unchanged. Model references by Monte Carlo, 10<sup>6</sup> unchanged updates per design and offset law (seed 20261114), 400,000 per set size for n<sub>old</sub> = k = 4..40 (`experiments/m4h_modelref/analyse.py`, 30 s). `tests/test_m4h.py` checked first that the vectorised variances equal milestone 4g's, that the plain jackknife's reference is a Welch-t one (99th percentile of z² 9.0–11.0 at 7 + 6), that a reference from one seed fires on 0.8–1.2% of another seed's draws, and that the statistic is scale-free.
- **References.** Gaussian offsets, 99th percentile of z<sub>R</sub>²: 25.84, 22.90 and 18.45 at 7 + 4, 7 + 5 and 7 + 6 views (9 + 4: 23.28), against χ²<sub>1</sub>'s 6.63; of z<sub>J</sub>²: 11.93, 10.44, 9.74. Student-t offsets make R's tail heavier still (7 + 6: 21.56 with t<sub>5</sub>, 31.71 with t<sub>3</sub>) and J's lighter (8.97, 8.39). Under the model, R against χ²<sub>1</sub> fires on 8.5%, 7.2% and 6.1% of unchanged updates at k = 4, 5, 6.
- **H56 held.** R against its Gaussian model reference, S0 over k = 4–6: 0.45% (left) and 0.20% (right) false alarms.
- **H57 held.** R against the model reference, ±2% at k = 6 pooled over sign and camera: 18.6% (per camera and sign 17.7–20.0%), against 53.8% for R against χ²<sub>1</sub>.
- **H58 held.** Under the Gaussian model with n<sub>old</sub> = k = n, P(z<sub>R</sub>² > 6.63) is 10.7% at n = 4, 3.8% at 10, 2.7% at 15 and 2.2% at 20; it reaches 1.6% only at 40. The model's 99th percentile is 12.0 at n = 10 and 8.84 at 20.
- **The cancellation.** On the real views, R against χ²<sub>1</sub> fired on 3.0%, 4.6% and 3.1% (left) and 1.1%, 1.4% and 0.2% (right) of unchanged updates at k = 4, 5, 6, below the model's 8.5%, 7.2% and 6.1%. The robust jackknife still overstates the real spread at these set sizes (milestone 4g, H53: real/predicted 0.88 and 0.48), and that overstatement offsets χ²<sub>1</sub>'s leniency. Milestone 4f found the overstatement fades as sets grow; the model's leniency fades more slowly (still 2.2% at 20 + 20), so on a unit with more views R against χ²<sub>1</sub> should drift towards the model's rates, not stay at 2–3%.
- **Unscored.** At k = 6 the model reference catches ±4% in 66.4–75.1% of splits (χ²<sub>1</sub>: 92.2–94.3%) and ±1% in 2.2–4.2%; at k = 5, ±2% in 6.0–10.8%. With the same calibrated basis the plain jackknife against its own model reference catches ±2% at k = 6 in only 8.6% pooled, so the robust scale does buy power (18.6% against 8.6%) once both tests have a derived reference. The t<sub>5</sub> and t<sub>3</sub> references give 14.8% and 7.8% at ±2%, k = 6. The unit swap is invisible to f alone under every reference (0.3% at k = 6). Harmful adoptions over all designs and scenarios: 0.63% (R, χ²<sub>1</sub>), 0.14% (R, model reference).
- **Reading.** Milestone 4g's 54% is not the power of a 1% test. It is the power of a test whose reference is about 6× too lenient in theory and happens to land near 2–3% on this rig because the jackknife is still too wide at 6 and 7 views. A reference derived from the independent-offset model is the only one in this programme that borrows nothing and rests on a stated assumption, and with it the f-alone test is quiet (0.2–0.5%) but catches a 2% change in only 19% of 6-view updates. Between that and the borrowed threshold's 43% (milestone 4g) lies the price of borrowing nothing at 6 new views. For a fleet the practical choice is (a) the model reference, conservative on this rig, which a robot can compute for any set sizes in about a second, or (b) χ²<sub>1</sub> as an approximate 2–5% test, with its real rate checked on each unit. The paper's recommendation 2 has to change accordingly.
- **Caveat.** One rig, one board, 13 shared views; no interval is given. The model ignores the near-critical behaviour of small sets that milestone 4f found, which is why it is conservative here; a unit whose views add independent offsets would sit at the nominal 1%.

## 2026-11-14 · Milestone 4h planned: a reference for the robust jackknife that comes from a model, not from χ²<sub>1</sub> or a sibling

Written before any milestone-4h statistic was computed on the real solves. Milestone 4g's best test (the robust, MAD-based jackknife R against χ²<sub>1</sub> at 1%) caught a 2% focal change in 54% of 6-view updates at 2.3% false alarms. But χ²<sub>1</sub> was never derived for R: a MAD of 6 or 7 delete-one values is itself noisy, so z<sub>R</sub>² should have a heavier tail than χ²<sub>1</sub>. A first Monte Carlo of the ideal case (each view adds an independent Gaussian offset to f, R and z<sub>R</sub>² formed exactly as `m4g_allsplits/analyse.py` does; 400,000 draws, seed 0; no real solve read) gives a 99th percentile of z<sub>R</sub>² of 18.4 at 7 + 6 views and 22.6 / 25.8 at 7 + 5 / 7 + 4, and says R against χ²<sub>1</sub> would fire on 6.0–8.5% of unchanged updates. If so, milestone 4g's 2.3% on the real views is two errors cancelling: a lenient reference against a robust jackknife that still overstates the real spread (milestone 4g, H53: real/predicted 0.88 and 0.48). Nothing in that cancellation would survive a unit with more views, where the overstatement fades (milestone 4f). Here the test gets a reference from the model itself, which a robot can compute for any set sizes with nothing borrowed, and the model's own verdict on when χ²<sub>1</sub> becomes safe.

- **Data.** Milestone 4f's stored 5×5 solves and milestone 4g's splits, scenarios and statistics, unchanged (`experiments/m4h_modelref/analyse.py` imports them). No new solve.
- **Model references.** For each design (n<sub>old</sub>, k), q<sub>M</sub> = the 99th percentile of z<sub>R</sub>² (and of z<sub>J</sub>² for J) when every view's offset is drawn iid from N(0, 1), by Monte Carlo (10<sup>6</sup> draws, fixed seed; the statistic is scale-free, so τ never enters). Sensitivity: the same with Student-t<sub>5</sub> and t<sub>3</sub> offsets.
- **H56 (the model reference is quiet on real views).** R against q<sub>M</sub> (Gaussian): S0 false alarms over k = 4–6 ≤ 1.0% on each camera. Refuted above on either.
- **H57 (and keeps some power).** R against q<sub>M</sub> (Gaussian): ±2% at k = 6, pooled over sign and camera, detected in 15–35% of splits (R against χ²<sub>1</sub>: 53.8%; J against χ²<sub>1</sub>: 17.7%). Refuted outside.
- **H58 (χ²<sub>1</sub> is not safe for R until large sets).** Under the Gaussian model, P(z<sub>R</sub>² > 6.63) with n<sub>old</sub> = k = n is above 2% for every n ≤ 20. Refuted if it falls to 2% or below at some n ≤ 20.
- **Reported, unscored.** J against its own q<sub>M</sub>; k = 4 and 5; ±1% and ±4%; the swap; the t<sub>5</sub> and t<sub>3</sub> references; the model's P(z<sub>R</sub>² > 6.63) and q<sub>M</sub> for n<sub>old</sub> = k from 4 to 40; the real false-alarm rate of R against χ²<sub>1</sub> per k next to the model's.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The model assumes independent per-view offsets, which milestone 4f found only roughly true (the right camera's A13 / DP ratio 1.2–1.7). The splits share 13 views, so no interval is given. H56 and H57 test one rig.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 4g result: on every disjoint split the borrowed threshold catches a 2% focal change in 43%, not 61%; a robust (MAD) jackknife against plain χ²<sub>1</sub> catches it in 54% with nothing borrowed and the same false-alarm rate

- **Runs.** No new solve: milestone 4f's 8,100 stored 5×5 solves per camera; every 4- to 9-view set had all its delete-one solves converge, so every split is usable. 37,752 splits per camera with an old set of 7 views (25,740 / 10,296 / 1,716 at k = 4 / 5 / 6) and 715 with an old set of 9 and a new set of 4, each scored under 10 scenarios (`experiments/m4g_allsplits/analyse.py`, under 5 s). `tests/test_m4g.py` checked the formulae and that the plain jackknife is quiet (< 5%) with a ratio near 1 on synthetic independent-offset data before the real run.
- **H52 refuted.** At k = 6, pooled over sign and camera, J against χ²<sub>1</sub> detects ±2% in 17.7% (inside 10–28%; 60 splits: 17.5%). But J against the borrowed threshold E<sub>1</sub> detects it in 43.3% (outside 51–71%; 60 splits: 61.3%), and E<sub>1</sub>'s left-camera false alarms over k = 4–6 are 4.4% (not ≥ 5%; 60 splits: 11.1%). Re-estimated on every split, the two cameras' S0 99th percentiles of z² under J are 4.62 (left) and 2.02 (right), against 3.56 and 1.03 from milestone 4d's 180 statistics each.
- **Post hoc, computed after H52 failed.** This script reproduces milestone 4d's figure exactly on milestone 4b's 60 splits with 4d's stored thresholds (61.25% of 240 decisions). With 4d's thresholds on every split, ±2% is caught in 57.8% and the left camera's false alarms are 10.4% (right 0.5%). So the splits themselves were representative: the drop from 61% to 43% comes from the threshold. A 99th percentile from 180 dependent statistics was too low, and power and false alarms fell together once it was estimated on every split. The borrowed test is also lopsided: ±2% is caught in 56–61% of splits on the left camera and 26–30% on the right.
- **H53 refuted.** S0, k = 6, real sd of f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub> over the root mean predicted variance: R 0.88 (left) and 0.48 (right); J 0.57 and 0.31. The MAD brings the predicted spread from 1.7× to 1.1× the real one on the left camera and from 3.3× to 2.1× on the right, so the right camera's is still twice too wide. At k = 4 and 5 both are much too wide (R 0.25–0.31 at k = 4), because the new set's own delete-one solves sit near the critical set (milestone 4f).
- **H54 refuted, narrowly.** R against χ²<sub>1</sub>, S0 over k = 4–6: 3.4% (left) and 1.2% (right) false alarms; at k = 6 alone, 3.1% and 0.2%. Pooled over both cameras and k = 4–6 it is 2.3%, the same as the borrowed threshold's 2.4% and well above J's 0.3%.
- **H55 held.** R against χ²<sub>1</sub>, ±2% at k = 6 pooled: 53.8% (left 50.0–50.1%, right 54.3–61.0%), 10 points above the borrowed threshold and 3× the plain jackknife, with nothing borrowed from another unit.
- **Unscored.** At k = 6, R catches ±1% in 15.4–16.4% and ±4% in 92.7–94.1% (J: 1.5–2.5% and 69.4–75.4%; E<sub>1</sub>: 13.3–13.4% and 90.7–92.5%). At k = 5 R catches ±2% in 32.0% pooled (E<sub>1</sub> 27.7%, J 8.9%); at k = 4, 11.9–12.0%. An old set of 9 and a new set of 4 is no better than 7 + 4 (R ±2%: 13.8–15.0%): the new set's size limits the test. f alone cannot see the unit swap (0.67% in f): R fires on 1.6–2.6% of swap splits, as on unchanged ones. Harmful adoptions under milestone 4's rule: R 0.28–0.89% of decisions per design, E<sub>1</sub> 0.27–0.75%, J 0.06–0.20%.
- **Sensitivity.** Every 7 + 6 split uses all 13 views, so leaving a view out leaves no k = 6 split; the pre-registered leave-one-view-out range is undefined there (the gap was found on the first run). At k = 5 the ranges over the 13 left-out views are: R ±2% 23.6–37.3%, E<sub>1</sub> 18.8–35.3%, J 5.1–11.4%. False alarms (k = 4–6): R 2.6–4.0% (left), 0.9–1.7% (right); E<sub>1</sub> left 1.2–7.4%.
- **Reading.** Two corrections and one gain. First, the paper's headline power for the borrowed-threshold test (61%) was flattered by a noisy threshold from 180 statistics; on every split it is 43%, and the paper has to say so. Second, a robust jackknife is not an honest error bar (the right camera's is still 2× wide), so H53 fails. But against a plain χ²<sub>1</sub> it gives the best test this programme has found: a 2% focal change is caught in 54% of 6-view updates, at the same false-alarm rate as the borrowed threshold, evenly on both cameras, with nothing taken from another unit. For a robot, that removes the need for a sibling unit or a commissioning-time tail estimate at 6 new views. It does not remove the dependence on set size: at 4 new views every variant is weak (13% at most).
- **Caveat.** One rig, one board, 13 views; the splits are every combination of the same views, so they are far from independent and no interval is given. R's advantage was predicted (H55), but its 3.4% left-camera false alarms are just above the pre-registered bar, and a second unit could put it on either side.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 4g planned: every disjoint split, and a robust jackknife that needs nothing borrowed

Written before any milestone-4g statistic was computed. Milestone 4f solved every subset of 3 to 12 of the 13 views on 5×5 corners and found that the plain view jackknife overstates f's spread mostly because a few delete-one solves of a small set land near the critical set; the median of the per-set jackknife variances was 2–3× closer to the real spread than their mean at 4 views. Two things follow that need no new solve. First, the update test of milestones 4c–4d was scored on 60 splits per k; with every subset stored, every disjoint (old, new) split can be scored, which is the "pre-registered rerun on fresh splits" the paper draft's Section 10 asks for. Second, a per-set *robust* jackknife (the MAD of the n delete-one focal lengths in place of their standard deviation) may give the f-alone test a usable scale from the unit's own views, where milestone 4d had to borrow a threshold from the other camera and milestone 4e's split-halves were deaf.

- **Data.** Milestone 4f's stored 5×5 solves only (`experiments/m4f_setsize/results.json.gz`, 8,100 per camera); no new solve. `experiments/m4g_allsplits/analyse.py`.
- **Splits.** Every old set of 7 views and every disjoint new set of k = 4, 5, 6 views from the 13: 25,740, 10,296 and 1,716 splits per camera. Scenarios as milestone 4c: S0 unchanged; S1 f of the new set scaled by s ∈ {0.96, 0.98, 0.99, 0.995, 1.005, 1.01, 1.02, 1.04} (its variance by s²); S3 swap (the old set from the other camera). A set is usable if it and at least n − 1 of its n delete-one solves converged.
- **Variances of f per set** (from its delete-one focal lengths f<sub>i</sub>, m usable): J = (m − 1)/m Σ(f<sub>i</sub> − f̄)² (milestones 4b–4f); R = (m − 1)²/m · (1.4826 · median|f<sub>i</sub> − median f<sub>i</sub>|)², the same with the MAD in place of the standard deviation. Statistic z² = (f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub>)² / (v<sub>old</sub> + s²v<sub>new</sub>), against χ²<sub>1</sub> at 1% (6.63). A zero denominator counts as fired. E<sub>1</sub>: milestone 4c's borrowed threshold (the other camera's S0 99th percentile of z² under J, k ≥ 4), recomputed on all splits.
- **H52 (the 60-split figures survive every split).** Pooled over cameras at k = 6: J against χ²<sub>1</sub> detects ±2% in 10–28% of splits (60 splits: 17.5%); J against E<sub>1</sub> detects ±2% in 51–71% (61.3%); and E<sub>1</sub>'s S0 false alarms on the left camera (k ≥ 4) are ≥ 5% (11.1%). Refuted if any fails.
- **H53 (R has the right scale).** S0, k = 6: the real sd of (f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub>) about each camera's mean, over √(mean(v<sub>old</sub> + v<sub>new</sub>)) with R, lies in 0.67–1.5 on both cameras. Refuted outside on either. (Under J the same ratio is predicted near 0.4–0.7.)
- **H54 (R is quiet).** R against χ²<sub>1</sub>: S0 false alarms over k = 4–6 ≤ 3% on each camera. Refuted above on either.
- **H55 (R has power with nothing borrowed).** R against χ²<sub>1</sub>: ±2% at k = 6, pooled over sign and camera, detected in ≥ 40% of splits. Refuted below.
- **Reported, unscored.** k = 4 and 5; ±0.5, ±1 and ±4%; the swap; an old set of 9 views with a new set of 4 (715 splits); harmful adoptions under milestone 4's rule; the rates with every split that uses view v left out, for each of the 13 v (min–max).
- **Caveat set in advance.** The splits are every combination of the same 13 views, so they are far from independent: rates are reported without intervals, and the leave-one-view-out range is the only spread given. A MAD of 6 or 7 numbers is itself noisy, so R's z² may have a heavier tail than χ²<sub>1</sub> even if its scale is right (H53 can hold while H54 fails).

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 4f result: the jackknife's overstatement of f's spread falls from 7–8× at 4 views to 1.1–1.4× at 10, and the solver's σ is 2–6× small at every size

- **Runs.** 10,854 new solves on 5×5 corners, merged with milestones 4d and 4e's 5,346: every subset of 3 to 12 of the 13 views on both cameras (16,172 view sets, plus the two all-view solves). All converged except 2 of the right camera's 286 three-view sets. `analyse.py` was checked on synthetic independent-offset data first (`tests/test_m4f.py`: every ratio reads 0.85–1.2 there), then run once on the real solves.
- **H48 held.** Jackknife over real (disjoint pairs): 7.8 at 4 views and 1.8 at 6 (left), 8.2 and 3.1 (right). Against A13 the ratio falls at every step from 4 to 10 views on both cameras (Spearman −1.0): left 6.7, 3.0, 1.6, 1.4, 1.3, 1.2, 1.1; right 6.7, 4.6, 1.9, 1.8, 1.7, 1.6, 1.4.
- **H49 refuted.** Left: 1.28, 1.17, 1.10 at 8, 9 and 10 views (inside 0.67–1.5). Right: 1.68, 1.57, 1.43 (outside at 8 and 9). The right camera needs about 10 views before the jackknife comes within 1.5×.
- **H50 refuted.** A13 over disjoint pairs: 1.17, 1.16, 1.17 at 4, 5, 6 views (left, inside 0.8–1.25); 1.22, 1.40, 1.66 (right, outside at 5 and 6). On robust scales the gap shrinks (0.94–1.01 left, 1.13–1.29 right), so heavy tails explain part of the right camera's gap but not all of it. On the right camera the views do not add independent offsets, and A13 overstates the real spread there. The jackknife's real overstatement on that camera above 6 views is therefore probably larger than the A13 ratios say.
- **H51 held.** The solver's own σ over real (A13), n = 4..10: 0.17–0.24 (left), 0.19–0.59 (right). On unbiased corners it is 4–6× too small on the left camera, worse than the 2× milestone 1 found on 11×11 corners. That is consistent with milestone 6 (better corners make σ less honest).
- **Unscored.** At 11 and 12 views the jackknife ratio is 1.04 / 1.01 (left) and 1.28 / 1.16 (right), but A13 divides by 1 − n/13 ≤ 0.15 there. Robust jackknife ratios (median v<sub>J</sub>) are much closer to 1 at small n: 2.4 (left) and 4.1 (right) at 4 views against 6.7 for the mean, so a few delete-one solves landing near the critical set make most of the small-set overstatement, as milestone 4e guessed. Leave-one-view-out ranges of the jackknife ratio (A13): 4.9–9.5 / 4.5–12.8 at 4 views; 0.86–1.39 / 1.46–1.92 at 8; 0.42–1.32 / 0.99–1.71 at 10 (left / right). Real per-set sd (A13) falls from 11–14% of f at 3 views to 2.1–2.7% at 4, 0.6–0.7% at 6 and 0.24–0.40% at 10.
- **Reading.** Milestone 4e's guess holds: the jackknife's overstatement is mostly a small-set effect from near-critical delete-one solves, and it shrinks steadily as the set grows. It is within 1.5× from about 7 views on the left camera and 10 on the right. For a deployed robot this means two things. Below 6 views neither σ is usable: the solver's is 4–6× too small and the jackknife's 2–8× too large. With 10 or more logged views, the plain jackknife is a usable, mildly conservative error bar without a borrowed threshold. Milestone 4e's split-half scale would also become usable then (5 + 5 halves), and the two can be checked against each other on one unit. The second camera shows that "independent per-view offsets" is only an approximation, so a submission should report the disjoint-pair reference wherever 2n ≤ the views available.
- **Caveat.** One stereo rig, one board, 13 views shared by every set; the per-n figures are far from independent and the leave-one-view-out ranges are the only uncertainty given. 5×5 corners only.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 4f planned: does the jackknife stop overstating f's spread once the view set leaves the critical region?

Written while the milestone-4f solves ran, before any of them was analysed. Milestone 4e found, post hoc, that f's spread per view falls 2–5× between 3 and 5 views and then roughly follows 1/n. It guessed that the same effect is why the leave-one-view-out jackknife overstated the real set-to-set spread of 7-view sets (1.8× and 2.3× on 5×5 corners): deleting one view from a small set lands near the critical set, so a few delete-one solves swing far and inflate the variance. If the guess is right, the overstatement should shrink as the old set grows, and a robot that logs about 10 views could use the jackknife as it is. Here every subset of 3 to 12 of the 13 views is solved on each camera (8,086 per camera, `experiments/m4f_setsize/run.py`; milestones 4d and 4e's 5×5 solves reused), on 5×5 corners only (milestone 6b's unbiased localiser), with milestone 4b's solver, reference views and start.

- **Quantities** (per camera, relative to the all-view f<sub>13</sub>, converged solves only):
  - real sd, disjoint pairs (DP), n = 3..6: √(mean d²/2) over every unordered pair of disjoint n-view sets, d = (f<sub>A</sub> − f<sub>B</sub>)/f<sub>13</sub>. Model-free; needs 2n ≤ 13.
  - real sd, around all views (A13), any n: √(mean (f<sub>S</sub> − f<sub>13</sub>)² / (1 − n/13)) over every n-view set S. Exact if each view adds an independent offset (then var(f<sub>S</sub> − f<sub>13</sub>) = var(f<sub>S</sub>)(1 − n/13)); the only real reference above 6 views.
  - jackknife J(n) = √(mean v<sub>J</sub>) over n-view sets with all n delete-one solves converged, v<sub>J</sub> = (n−1)/n Σ(f<sub>i</sub> − f̄)²/f<sub>13</sub>² (milestone 4b's `jackknife_cov`, f entry).
  - reported R(n) = √(mean σ<sub>f</sub>²)/f<sub>13</sub> (the solver's own covariance).
  - ratio ρ<sub>J</sub>(n) = J(n)/real(n); reference DP for n ≤ 6 where stated, A13 otherwise.
- **H48 (overstatement falls with set size).** On each camera ρ<sub>J</sub>(4) > ρ<sub>J</sub>(6) against DP, and the Spearman correlation of ρ<sub>J</sub>(n) (A13) with n over n = 4..10 is ≤ −0.8. Refuted if either fails on either camera.
- **H49 (honest from about 8 views).** ρ<sub>J</sub>(n) (A13) lies in 0.67–1.5 for each of n = 8, 9, 10 on both cameras. Refuted if any falls outside.
- **H50 (the independent-offset model holds where it can be checked).** At n = 4, 5, 6, A13 over DP lies in 0.8–1.25 on both cameras. Refuted otherwise (and then H48's and H49's A13 reference is suspect above 6 views).
- **H51 (the solver's σ stays small at every size).** R(n)/real(n) (A13) ≤ 0.67 for every n = 4..10 on both cameras (milestone 1: about 2× small on 13 views). Refuted if any exceeds.
- **Reported, unscored.** n = 3, 11, 12; robust versions (1.4826 MAD for the real spread, median v<sub>J</sub> for the jackknife); convergence counts per n; a leave-one-view-out sensitivity (each ratio recomputed with every set holding view v dropped, for each of the 13 v; min–max reported).
- **Caveat set in advance.** All sets are drawn from the same 13 views and overlap heavily, so the per-n figures are far from independent and carry no honest interval beyond the leave-one-view-out range. The A13 reference at n ≥ 11 divides by 1 − n/13 ≤ 0.15 and is too noisy to score. 5×5 corners only: the other localisers carry a bias (milestone 6b) that adds a shared offset to every set.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 4e result: a threshold from the old set's own 4 + 3 split-halves is quiet but deaf, because f's spread does not shrink as 1/n below 5 views

- **Runs.** 3,168 new solves (every 3- and 4-view subset of the 13 views not already stored, × 3 localisers × 2 cameras), all with the milestone-4b solver; merged with milestones 4b and 4d, every one of the 171 old sets per camera and localiser had all 35 partitions usable. 14,040 decisions scored with milestone 4c's `score()`, unchanged, none left undecided.
- **H43 held.** z<sub>H</sub>² against F<sub>1,6</sub>, 5×5 corners, S0, k ≥ 4: 0.6% (left, 95% 0.1–3.1%) and 0.0% (right) false alarms.
- **H44 refuted.** ±2% at k = 6 pooled: 0.8% (0.2–3.0%), against 61% for the borrowed threshold. Against χ²<sub>1</sub> it is still only 4.2%; ±4% is caught in 7–9% (F) and 27–31% (χ²<sub>1</sub>).
- **H45 refuted.** Real sd of (f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub>)/f<sub>old</sub> at k = 6 over predicted: 0.67% / 1.94% = 0.34 (left), 0.50% / 2.62% = 0.19 (right). The split-half scale overstates the spread 2.9× and 5.2×, more than the jackknife did (1.8× / 2.3×).
- **H46 refuted.** S0 99th percentiles of z<sub>H</sub>²: 5.34 (left) against 1.24 (right), a factor 4.3.
- **H47 held.** Harmful adoptions 0.02% of 4,680 decisions, because the test almost never fires.
- **Unscored.** Median τ̂ per view (5×5): 2.98% left, 3.96% right (11×11 3.23 / 6.48%, SB 4.30 / 5.11%). On 11×11 and SB corners the picture is the same: scale ratio 0.46 / 0.22 and 0.33 / 0.25, ±2% detected in 0–2%. The unit swap is invisible to f alone (0.0%); the union with W against χ²<sub>3</sub> is W alone (79.4% swap, 1.9% false alarms).
- **Why, post hoc (computed after H45 failed).** The robust sd (1.4826 MAD) of each subset's f around the all-view f, divided by √(1/n − 1/13) (the part of the gap a subset's own views make if each view adds an independent offset), should be the same at every n. On 5×5 corners it is 3.29% / 5.21% per view at 3 views, 2.15% / 2.17% at 4, 1.68% / 1.35% at 5, 1.67% / 1.04% at 6 and 1.55% / 0.95% at 7 (left / right); the update spread at k = 6 implies 1.20% / 0.91%. Below 5 views the halves sit near the critical set, where milestone 2 found the error grows fastest, so a 3-view half carries 2–5× the per-view spread a 6- or 7-view set does. 1/n holds roughly from 5 views up.
- **Reading.** Two of five predictions held, and both only because the test is silent. A per-unit scale from the old calibration's own views is the right idea for the threshold not to travel between units, but it needs halves in the regime where 1/n holds: at least 5 views each, so about 10 views in the old set, or halves of an old set enlarged by views a robot logs during normal operation. With 7 views, the borrowed or pooled threshold remains the only usable f-alone test. It may also be part of why milestone 3 found the jackknife very conservative at 4 views (deleting one view from a small set lands near the critical set); that was not tested here.
- **Caveat.** The 35 partitions of 7 views are dependent, and the 13 views are shared by every split, so the intervals are too narrow; the post hoc per-view figures use subsets that overlap one another heavily.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 4e planned: a threshold from the old calibration's own views, with no sibling unit

Written before any milestone-4e solve was run. Milestone 4d's f-alone update test caught a 2% focal change in 61% of splits on unbiased 5×5 corners, but its threshold was borrowed from the other camera, and it no longer travelled: 11.1% false alarms on the left camera, because the jackknife overstates the real set-to-set spread by different amounts on the two cameras (1.5× and 2.8×). The paper draft names a per-unit tail estimate as what a submission needs. Here the scale comes from the old set itself, which a robot always has: each old set of 7 views is split into 4 + 3 views in all 35 ways, and the spread of the two halves' focal lengths, scaled by 1/n, predicts the spread between the old set and a new set of k views. Nothing is borrowed from another unit and the new views are never used to set the threshold.

- **Solves.** Every 3- and 4-view subset of the 13 views (1,001 per camera) that milestones 4b and 4d did not already solve, on all three localisers (`experiments/m4e_selfscale/run.py`: the same solver, k<sub>1</sub> model, reference views and start).
- **Statistic.** For an old set O and each partition (A, B) with both halves converged, r = (f<sub>A</sub> − f<sub>B</sub>)/f<sub>O</sub>. The per-view scale is τ̂² = median(r²) / 0.45494 / (1/4 + 1/3) (the median of χ²<sub>1</sub> makes it robust to a near-critical half). Predicted variance of the relative update v = τ̂²(1/7 + 1/k), and z<sub>H</sub>² = ((f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub>)/f<sub>old</sub>)² / v. A decision needs at least 20 of the 35 partitions converged. Splits, disturbances (S1 focal change, S0 unchanged, S2 principal-point shift, S3 unit swap, where the old set and so its scale come from the other camera), k ≥ 4 scoring and the adoption rule are milestone 4c's.
- **Reference.** Primary: F<sub>1,6</sub> at 1% (13.75), since τ̂ rests on 7 views. Secondary, also scored: χ²<sub>1</sub> (6.63).
- **H43 (quiet with nothing borrowed).** On 5×5 corners, z<sub>H</sub>² against F<sub>1,6</sub>: S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4) ≤ 3% on each camera. Refuted if above on either (the borrowed threshold: 11.1% left).
- **H44 (power kept).** On 5×5 corners, F<sub>1,6</sub>: a ±2% focal change at k = 6, pooled over sign and camera, detected in ≥ 40% of splits (borrowed threshold 61%). Refuted below.
- **H45 (the scale is right).** On 5×5 corners, S0, k = 6: the real standard deviation of (f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub>)/f<sub>old</sub> about each camera's mean, divided by the root mean predicted v, lies within 0.67–1.5 on both cameras (the jackknife: 0.67/1.23 = 0.54 and 0.51/1.17 = 0.44). Refuted outside on either.
- **H46 (the tail travels).** On 5×5 corners, the two cameras' S0 99th percentiles of z<sub>H</sub>² (k ≥ 4) are within a factor of 2 of each other (jackknife z<sub>f</sub>²: 3.56 against 1.03, 3.5×). Refuted otherwise.
- **H47 (no harm).** On 5×5 corners, F<sub>1,6</sub>: harmful adoptions over all scenarios (k ≥ 4) ≤ 1.5% (borrowed threshold 1.3%). Refuted above.
- **Reported, unscored.** The same on 11×11 and SB corners; χ²<sub>1</sub>; ±1% and ±4%; the principal-point shift and the unit swap; a union with milestone 4b's W against χ²<sub>3</sub> for the principal point; comparisons with the borrowed (E<sub>1</sub>) and oracle thresholds.
- **Caveat set in advance.** The 1/n scaling assumes each view adds an independent offset; 3- and 4-view halves sit nearer the critical set than 7-view sets, which would inflate τ̂ and make the test conservative. The 35 partitions of 7 views are not independent, and the splits reuse 13 views, so the intervals are too narrow.
