## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 4d result: unbiased 5×5 corners nearly double the f-alone test's power for a 2% focal change (61% against 34–35%), but the sibling's threshold stops travelling (11% false alarms on the left camera)

- **Runs.** 4,290 solves on 5×5-window corners (milestone 4b's 2,145 view sets per camera), 1 not converged (a 3-view set); re-solving the all-view set on 11×11 and SB corners reproduced milestone 4b's stored f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> and k1 exactly (difference 0.0 on all four fits, OpenCV 5.0.0). Scored with milestone 4c's `score()` (factored out of its `main`, output byte-identical), 4,680 decisions on 5×5 corners, k ≥ 4.
- **H38 held.** W against χ²<sub>3</sub> with J: 1.7% false alarms pooled (95% 0.8–3.6%), 2.2% left, 1.1% right.
- **H39 held.** Standard deviation of (f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub>)/f on unchanged splits at k = 6: 0.59% on 5×5 corners, against 1.02% (11×11) and 0.86% (SB). Per camera 0.67% / 0.51% (11×11 1.03 / 1.01%, SB 0.91 / 0.81%).
- **H40 held.** z<sub>f</sub>² against the other camera's 99th percentile detects ±2% at k = 6 in 61.3% of splits (55–67%; −2% 64%, +2% 58%), against 34.2% (11×11) and 35.4% (SB).
- **H41 refuted.** The borrowed threshold over-alarms on the left camera: 11.1% (7.3–16.5%), right 0.0%. The left camera's own 99th percentile of z<sub>f</sub>² is 3.56, the right's 1.03: the jackknife σ shrank less than the real spread, and by different amounts (the jackknife σ of the difference is 1.23% / 1.17% of f against a real spread of 0.67% / 0.51%, rms z 0.65 / 0.36).
- **H42 held.** The real unit swap, W against χ²<sub>3</sub> with J, k ≥ 4: 79.4% (75–83%), against 30.3% (11×11) and 74.7% (SB).
- **Unscored.** At k = 6 on 5×5 corners: f alone against the borrowed threshold detects ±1% in 28% / 18% (11×11: 4 / 10%; SB: 12 / 7%) and ±4% in 98% / 97%; the oracle threshold gets ±2% in 66% / 62%, so the borrowed one loses little power, only calibration. W against χ²<sub>3</sub> detects ±2% in 20% / 25% (11×11 8 / 9%, SB 12 / 12%) and a 10 px principal-point shift in 27% (6% / 12%). Harmful adoptions over all scenarios: 1.3% (f alone, borrowed threshold), 1.5% (W, χ²), 2.3% (the exploratory union, whose left-camera false alarms are 12.8%). The all-view target-free f on 5×5 corners is 534.3 / 535.8 px, +0.27% / −0.08% from Zhang on the same corners (532.9 / 536.2).
- **Reading.** Four of five predictions held. The half-width end squares' localiser bias was a large part of the set-to-set offset that made the test deaf: with corners that bias does not reach, the focal-length spread between disjoint view sets falls by a third to two fifths, and the f-alone test catches a 2% focal change three times in five and a 1% change about one time in four, where before it caught 2% one time in three and 1% hardly at all. The cost is calibration of the threshold: the jackknife now overstates the spread 1.5× on one camera and 2.8× on the other, so one sibling's tail is no longer the other's. A deployable version needs a threshold pooled over several sibling units or a per-unit tail estimate, not one borrowed unit.
- **Caveat.** The 99th percentiles still rest on about two of 180 statistics from splits that reuse 13 views, so the left camera's 11% might shrink with more splits; the two cameras share every board pose. The H41 explanation (unequal jackknife shrinkage) was computed after the hypothesis failed.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 4d planned: does the unbiased 5×5 window make the update test sharper?

Written before any milestone-4d solve was run. Milestone 6b found that this board's half-width end squares pull the 11×11 `cornerSubPix` window's outer-ring corners by up to 8 px and bias Zhang's f by +0.6–0.9%, and SB's by −0.1 to −0.4%, while a 5×5 window is unbiased (f within 0.04% on renders). Milestones 4b–4c ran the update test only on 11×11 and SB corners. The bias depends on each view's pose, so it should differ from one view set to the next and add to the set-to-set offset that made the test deaf to a 2% focal change. Here milestone 4b's solves are repeated on 5×5 corners (`experiments/m4d_window/run.py`: the same 2,145 view sets per camera, solver, reference views and start) and scored with milestone 4c's statistics and references, unchanged.

- **H38 (the jackknife stays honest).** W against χ²<sub>3</sub> with the jackknife covariance J: S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4) ≤ 3% pooled and ≤ 5% on each camera. Refuted otherwise.
- **H39 (less set-to-set offset).** The standard deviation of (f<sub>new</sub> − f<sub>old</sub>)/f on unchanged splits at k = 6, pooled over cameras after removing each camera's mean, is smaller on 5×5 corners than on both 11×11 and SB corners. Refuted if it is not smaller than both.
- **H40 (more power for focal change).** z<sub>f</sub>² against the other camera's S0 99th percentile (E<sub>1</sub>) detects a ±2% focal change at k = 6, pooled over sign and camera, in ≥ 40% of splits (11×11: 34%, SB: 35%). Refuted below 40%.
- **H41 (the borrowed threshold travels).** E<sub>1</sub> S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4) ≤ 5% on each camera. Refuted if above on either (SB's left camera was at 5.0%).
- **H42 (the swap).** W against χ²<sub>3</sub> with J detects the real unit swap S3 (k ≥ 4, both directions) in ≥ 50% of splits (11×11: 30%, SB: 75%). Refuted below.
- **Reported, unscored.** The full power table and harmful adoptions for every reference; thresholds; the all-view target-free f on 5×5 corners against 5×5 Zhang (532.9 / 536.2 px, milestone 6b's best estimate); a check that this environment reproduces milestone 4b's stored all-view solutions on 11×11 and SB corners (OpenCV here is 5.0.0).
- **Caveat carried over.** The splits reuse 13 views, so the Wilson intervals are too narrow, and a 99th percentile from 180 statistics rests on about two of them.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 6b result: the printed board's end squares are half width, which biases the 11×11 window's f by +0.6–0.9% and SB's by −0.1 to −0.4%; a 5×5 window is right

- **Runs.** 26 renders at the real poses (truth: each camera's all-view 11×11 Zhang fit with k1 + k2), 5 settings × 20 seeds × 2 cameras, 3 localisers, 600 flat-board Zhang and 600 target-free fits; all detections and fits succeeded except one SB render. Matched setting, calibrated so the renders' own statistics equal the real images': render blur σ 0.80 / 0.81 px and pre-JPEG noise 1.31 / 1.50 grey levels (left / right) give edge σ 0.949 / 0.960 px (real 0.948 / 0.957) and noise 0.54 / 0.63 (real 0.52 / 0.61). Tests check the renderer puts corners at the projected points (< 0.1 px) and the statistics recover known blur and noise.
- **H33 refuted.** On the full-square renders no outer-ring corner differs by more than 1 px between 11×11 and SB (max 0.19 / 0.36 px), against 22 / 30 on the real images (max 7.7 / 5.6 px).
- **H34 refuted.** All three localisers are within 0.035–0.060 px rms of the truth on the ideal pattern; 11×11 is lowest on the ring of both cameras. The error is almost all systematic (the per-corner mean over 20 seeds has the same rms), not noise.
- **H35 refuted, second clause.** SB's f is within 0.025% of the truth (the 0.2% clause held), but so is every localiser's (11×11: −0.004% / +0.030%): on an ideal pattern none of them moves f, so the real 0.8–1.3% shift is not localiser behaviour on a clean board.
- **H36 refuted.** Target-free BA on SB corners: rms z of f against the truth 1.55 / 1.80. The reported σ is 2–2.5× *larger* than the seed-to-seed spread (spread/σ 0.46 / 0.39), but a fixed bias of −0.02% / +0.04% of f, the localiser's systematic error, is 1.5–2σ. A sandwich clustered by view absorbs it (rms z 0.75 / 0.98).
- **H37 held.** Adjacent along-column residual correlation −0.09 to +0.08 for every localiser on the renders (real 0.32–0.56). Unscored: SB's along-row correlation on the right camera is 0.26–0.35 on renders too.
- **Unscored, the main finding (written after H33 was refuted).** Magnified crops of the six worst real corners show the 11×11 corner 4–8 px off the visible corner, towards the board's edge, with SB and 5×5 on it. Every real corner with an 11×11–SB gap over 1 px is in grid column 0 or 8. Measured outward along the grid lines (`outer.py`), the real board's end squares in those two columns reach only 0.49–0.53 of the corner spacing past the last corner (10–90%: 0.35–0.59), against 0.94–0.97 on the other two sides; on the renders the same measurement reads 0.95–1.01. Re-rendered with half-width end squares (`ablate.py`, 5 seeds), the 11×11 window misplaces 12–18 ring corners per 13-view set by more than 1 px (max 3.5–9.6 px) and biases Zhang's f by +0.43% to +1.47% depending on the white margin and the surround (real 11×11 minus 5×5: +0.64% / +0.91%). SB is biased the other way, −0.32% to −0.43% (real SB minus 5×5: −0.12% / −0.39%), and fails to detect the board when the surround is narrow and dark. The 5×5 window is untouched by any surround (f within 0.04% of the truth, ring rms 0.05–0.06 px). Full-width end squares with any margin or surround leave all three localisers unbiased, so the half-width squares are the cause.
- **Reading.** One of five predictions held. The question milestone 6 left, which localiser is right, has an answer for this board: the 5×5 window. Zhang's f on 5×5 corners, 532.9 px (left) and 536.2 px (right), is the best estimate; the 11×11 values used in milestones 1–5 (536.3 / 541.1) are about 0.6–0.9% high, and SB's (532.2 / 534.1), used in milestones 4b–4c, 0.1–0.4% low. The general lesson for calibration practice: a corner whose sub-pixel window reaches past a cut-short end square is pulled towards the board's edge, so either print full end squares and a margin of at least one window, or size the window below the shortest end square.
- **Caveat.** The renders leave out the bend, the clipboard and background clutter, vignetting and the camera's own noise; the half-width ablation fixes the reach at 0.5 for every row, where the real reach varies (0.35–0.59). The ablation and the reach measurement were added after H33 failed and were not pre-registered.

## 2026-11-13 · Milestone 6b planned: which corner localiser is right? Renders with a known camera at the real poses

Written before any image was rendered. Milestone 6 found that the corner localiser alone moves flat-board Zhang's f by 0.9–1.3% (4.8 / 7.2 px) and target-free f by 1.8 / 4.2 px, that `cornerSubPix` 11×11 and `findChessboardCornersSB` disagree by more than 1 px on 52 corners, all on the grid's outer ring, and that no ground truth said which is right. Here the truth is known: each camera's 13 views are re-rendered from a known camera and known poses, and every localiser is scored against the exact projections.

- **Renderer.** The true camera of each side is its all-view flat-board Zhang fit on 11×11 corners with k1 and k2 (OpenCV model, no tangential terms), and the 13 board poses are that fit's poses, so each render puts the board where it was in the real image. Each 640×480 pixel is the mean of a 4×4 grid of sub-pixel rays, each undistorted (fixed-point inversion of the radial model), intersected with the board plane and shaded: a flat 10×7-square checkerboard (9×6 inner corners), a white margin of 0.5 square around it (the real board's white border, read off the images), a uniform mid-grey background beyond. Then a Gaussian blur, additive Gaussian noise, 8-bit rounding and JPEG encoding.
- **Matched setting, fixed before any localiser is scored on a render.** Black and white levels are the medians of the real images' square interiors near detected corners; blur σ is fitted to the real images' edge profiles (an erf fit across the board's inner edges); noise σ is the rms of a 3×3 Laplacian in flat square interiors, rescaled to a pixel σ; JPEG quality 90. Also swept: blur 0.5× and 2× the matched value, and noise 0 and 2×. 20 noise seeds per setting.
- **Localisers.** The three of milestone 6: `cornerSubPix` 11×11 and 5×5 half-windows, and SB with `CALIB_CB_ACCURACY`. Error = detected minus true projection, matched by corner index (truth ordering).
- **H33 (the render reproduces the ring).** In the matched setting, at least one outer-ring corner per camera differs by more than 1 px between 11×11 and SB, as on the real images. Refuted if none does on either camera: then the real ring disagreement comes from something the render leaves out (the clipboard edge, clutter, lighting), not from the ideal pattern.
- **H34 (SB is closest to the truth).** Matched setting, pooled over seeds and views: SB's rms corner error is below 11×11's, on the outer ring and in the interior, on both cameras. Refuted if 11×11 or 5×5 is lower on either subset on either camera.
- **H35 (the localiser picks the focal length).** Flat-board Zhang (k1 + k2, as the truth) on SB corners recovers f within 0.2% of the truth on both cameras (median over seeds), and 11×11's |f error| is larger than SB's on both. Refuted if SB is off by more than 0.2% on either camera, or 11×11 is no worse on either.
- **H36 (σ is honest when the world is ideal).** Target-free all-view BA (k1 + k2) on SB corners, z = (f − f<sub>true</sub>)/σ<sub>f</sub> over the 20 seeds: rms z ≤ 1.5 on both cameras. Refuted if above 1.5 on either. On the real images the nested rms z was 3.12 with SB corners (milestone 6); if renders are honest the 2–3× comes from the world, not the localiser on an ideal pattern.
- **H37 (no column structure on an ideal pattern).** In the matched setting, the target-free all-view residuals' adjacent along-column correlation is below 0.2 for every localiser on both cameras (real: 0.44–0.56 with 11×11, 0.32 / 0.56 with SB). Refuted if 0.2 or more for any localiser on either camera.
- **Reported, unscored.** All five predictions in the blur and noise sweep; per-ring error maps; target-free f error per localiser; whether the real-image f shift between localisers (milestone 6) is reproduced.
- **Caveat set in advance.** A render is a model of the image, not the image: it leaves out the clipboard's rounded edge, background clutter, vignetting, defocus that varies with depth, the bend milestones 5–6 found, and the sensor's real noise and demosaicing. A localiser that wins on renders is right *for the ideal pattern*; a real-image gap the renders do not reproduce points at what they leave out.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 4c result: the 3-parameter test is information-limited, but testing f alone against a borrowed tail threshold catches a 2% focal change 3–4× as often

- **Runs.** No new solve: milestone 4b's 8,580 stored solves, its splits and disturbances, k ≥ 4, 9,360 decisions (360 unchanged splits per detector). Satterthwaite ν on unchanged splits: median 6.2 (11×11) / 5.9 (SB), 10–90% 3.0–10.1, so the Hotelling threshold has median 68 / 79 against χ²<sub>3</sub>'s 11.3. Empirical 99th percentiles of W on unchanged splits: 17.5 / 14.7 (11×11, left / right), 9.8 / 15.2 (SB); of z<sub>f</sub>²: 2.03 / 1.69 and 4.22 / 1.95, against χ²<sub>1</sub>'s 6.63.
- **H28 held.** F reference: 0 false alarms in 360 on both detectors (95% 0–1.1%).
- **H29 held.** F keeps 19% (11×11) and 22% (SB) of χ²'s ±4% detections at k = 6 (8% against 43%; 13% against 56%).
- **H30 held.** The other camera's W threshold: 2.2% / 0.6% false alarms (11×11, left / right), 0.6% / 2.8% (SB).
- **H31 held.** The oracle threshold on W detects ±2% at k = 6 in 4.6% (11×11) and 10.0% (SB) of splits: no threshold on the 3-parameter statistic rescues it.
- **H32 refuted.** z<sub>f</sub>² against χ²<sub>1</sub> detects ±2% in 3.3% (11×11) and 7.9% (SB), fewer than W against χ²<sub>3</sub> (8.3% / 11.7%); its false alarms are 0.0% / 0.3%. Against χ²<sub>1</sub> the f-only test is too quiet, because the jackknife overstates f's variance by about 3× (99th percentile 1.7–4.2 where χ²<sub>1</sub> says 6.6).
- **Unscored, and the main finding.** z<sub>f</sub>² against the *other camera's* 99th percentile (E<sub>1</sub>) detects ±2% at k = 6 in 41% / 28% (11×11, −2% / +2%) and 40% / 31% (SB), and ±4% in 78–85%, with false alarms 2.2% / 0.6% (11×11) and 5.0% / 0.0% (SB, left / right). The same camera's own threshold (oracle) gives 43% / 28% and 38% / 33%: the borrowed threshold loses almost nothing. A 1% change stays invisible (4–12%). The f-only test cannot see the unit swap (1–4%), which lives in c<sub>x</sub>; an exploratory union (W or z<sub>f</sub>², both thresholds borrowed, not pre-registered) keeps the swap (21% / 73% at k ≥ 4) and the ±2% power (29–43% at k = 6), at 2.8% / 4.2% false alarms (left camera 4.4% / 5.6%).
- **Harmful adoptions** (milestone 4's rule, all scenarios, k ≥ 4): f-only with E<sub>1</sub> 0.47% (11×11) and 0.96% (SB); the union 0.68% / 2.29%.
- **Reading.** Four of five predictions held. The jackknife test's deafness had two sources: for the 3-parameter statistic it is information (the oracle buys nothing), but for f the χ² reference is badly mis-scaled, and a tail threshold borrowed from a sibling unit recovers most of the oracle's power. The practical test is two statistics, f against a borrowed threshold for focal change and W for principal-point moves.
- **Caveat.** The thresholds are 99th percentiles of 180 statistics from splits that reuse 13 views; the E<sub>1</sub> false-alarm rate on the SB left camera (5.0%) shows the borrowed tail does not always match. The union and the E<sub>1</sub> power were not pre-registered.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 4c planned: is the jackknife test deaf because of its χ² reference or because 4–6 views carry too little information?

Written before any milestone-4c number was computed. Milestone 4b's jackknife test (J) was quiet (1.7–2.5% false alarms at a nominal 1%) but caught a ±2% focal change in only 7–12% of splits with 6 new views. Two explanations predict different fixes: the χ²<sub>3</sub> reference is wrong for a covariance estimated from 4–7 delete-one solves (then a better reference or a narrower test buys power back), or the views simply do not carry the information (then no threshold helps). Milestone 4c answers it from milestone 4b's 8,580 stored solves (`m4b_viewcov/results.json.gz`), with no new solve, on the same splits, disturbances, detectors and k ≥ 4 scoring.

- **Statistic.** W = dᵀ(J<sub>old</sub> + s²J<sub>new</sub>)⁻¹d on d = θ<sub>new</sub> − θ<sub>old</sub>, θ = (f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>), as in 4b; and the one-parameter z<sub>f</sub>² on f alone.
- **Four references for W at 1%.** (χ²) 11.34, as 4b. (F) a Hotelling-type reference with Satterthwaite degrees of freedom from the f variances, ν = (a + b)² / (a²/(m<sub>old</sub> − 1) + b²/(m<sub>new</sub> − 1)), a = J<sub>old,ff</sub>, b = s²J<sub>new,ff</sub>, m the delete-one solves that converged; threshold 3ν/(ν − 2) · F<sub>3, ν−2</sub>(0.99). (E) the other camera's empirical 99th percentile of W on unchanged splits (S0), same detector, k ≥ 4 pooled. (O, oracle) the same camera's own S0 99th percentile, k ≥ 4 pooled: false alarms ≈ 1% by construction, so it is an upper bound on the power any recalibration of the threshold can buy, not a usable test.
- **Two references for z<sub>f</sub>².** χ²<sub>1</sub> (6.63) and F<sub>1, ν</sub>(0.99).
- **H28 (F is safe).** J with the F reference: S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4, pooled) ≤ 1% on both detectors. Refuted if above 1% on either.
- **H29 (F is too strict).** J with F detects a ±4% focal change at k = 6 (pooled over sign and camera) in fewer than half as many splits as J with χ², on both detectors. Refuted if F keeps at least half on either. Milestone 4b found J already overstates the spread (rms z 0.46–0.84), and ν is 3–9 here, so F's threshold is several times χ²'s.
- **H30 (a transferred threshold travels).** J with E: S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4) ≤ 5% on each camera, on both detectors. Refuted if above 5% on any. Unlike milestone 4's borrowed κ, the jackknife already scales to each camera, so only the tail shape is borrowed.
- **H31 (the limit is information).** With the oracle threshold O, a ±2% focal change at k = 6 (pooled over sign and camera) is detected in fewer than 25% of splits on both detectors. Refuted if 25% or more on either.
- **H32 (testing f alone buys power).** z<sub>f</sub>² against χ²<sub>1</sub> detects a ±2% focal change at k = 6 in more splits than W against χ²<sub>3</sub>, on both detectors, with S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4, pooled) ≤ 3%. Refuted if either part fails on either detector.
- **Reported, unscored.** The full power table (±0.5–4% focal, 2–10 px principal point, the swap) for every reference; harmful adoptions under milestone 4's rule with each reference; the threshold values (median ν, F and E thresholds).
- **Caveat carried over.** The splits reuse 13 views, so the 95% Wilson intervals are too narrow, and an empirical 99th percentile from 180 statistics rests on about two of them.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 4b result: a jackknife over views makes the update test quiet with no borrowed factor (1.7–2.5% false alarms), but it cannot see a 2% focal change; SB corners lift its swap detection from 30% to 75%

- **Runs.** 2,145 distinct view sets per camera and detector (old and new sets plus every leave-one-view-out subset needed for the jackknife), 8,580 solves, 4 not converged (all 3-view sets; a jackknife drops a failed delete-one solve). The other camera's nested κ recomputed with H8's formula: 11×11 corners 2.67 (left) / 1.89 (right), as milestone 3; SB corners 3.47 / 2.74. All scored on k ≥ 4 (360 decisions per detector and covariance); 95% Wilson intervals.
- **H23 held.** Reported covariance (R), SB corners: S0 false alarms 57.8% (53–63), against 74.2% (69–78) with 11×11 corners. Better corners cut false alarms by a fifth, not to the nominal 1%.
- **H24 held.** Sandwich clustered by view (V), with G/(G − 1): 37.8% (11×11) and 36.1% (SB). 4–7 clusters per set are too few for a sandwich.
- **H25 held.** Jackknife by view (J): 2.5% (1.3–4.7; left 2.8%, right 2.2%) on 11×11 corners and 1.7% (0.8–3.6; left 0.6%, right 2.8%) on SB, with no factor taken from anywhere. The per-parameter rms z on S0 is 0.46–0.84, so J overstates the spread on average; that it still fires above 1% says the tails are heavier than χ²<sub>3</sub>.
- **H26 held.** The real unit swap (k ≥ 4, both directions): J 30.3% (11×11) and 74.7% (SB), against the transferred-κ test (T) at 41.7% and 93.1%. R and V detect it in 92–100%, but at their false-alarm rates that is not a test.
- **H27 held.** Harmful adoptions with J, all scenarios, k ≥ 4: 0.47% (11×11) and 1.54% (SB) of decisions. With the gate also on the jackknife σ<sub>f</sub> (unscored), 0.04% / 0.13%, but the rule then adopts in only 0.8–1.0% of decisions (13% with the reported-σ gate on SB).
- **Unscored: power with J, 6 new views.** ±1% focal change detected in 2–4% of splits (both detectors); ±2% in 7–12%; +4% in 46% / 49%, −4% in 41% / 62%. A 10 px principal-point shift: 6% / 12%. With SB corners, T keeps false alarms at 0.6% pooled (left 1.1%, right 0.0%) and detects ±4% in 70–88% and the swap in 93%: on the cleaner corners the borrowed factor no longer over-alarms on the left camera (11.7% with 11×11 corners).
- **Reading.** Five of five predictions held. An honest covariance from the views themselves exists and needs no outside calibration of the test, but the honest answer is that 4–6 views of this rig cannot tell a 2% focal change from the set-to-set offset. The corner localiser matters as much as the covariance: SB corners take the jackknife test from missing most real swaps to catching three in four.
- **Caveat.** As in milestone 4, the splits reuse 13 views, so the intervals are too narrow; J needs k + 7 extra solves per decision (about 1–3 s each here).

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 4b planned: does a view-level covariance (jackknife or clustered sandwich) make the update test honest, and do SB corners change it?

Written before any milestone-4b solve was run. Milestone 4 left the χ² update test firing on 72.5% of unchanged splits with the reported covariance, and a scalar κ borrowed from the other camera (H19) under- or over-corrected per camera (10.8% left, 2.1% right). Milestone 3 found the leave-one-view-out jackknife σ of f conservative (rms z 0.65–0.70) where the reported σ is overconfident (≈ 2×). Here the scalar κ is replaced by a covariance each calibration computes from its own views, and the whole test is rerun on milestone 6's `findChessboardCornersSB` corners.

- **Same splits.** Milestone 4's 60 splits per camera and k ∈ {3, 4, 5, 6} (old set of 7 views, new set of k from the other 6, seeds 4000 + 100k + i), the same disturbances S0–S3, the same rule (χ²<sub>3</sub> on f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub> at 1%, adopt iff detected and reported σ<sub>f,new</sub>/f ≤ 0.5%), for two detectors: `cornerSubPix` 11×11 (milestones 1–5) and SB.
- **Four covariances for Σ<sub>old</sub> + Σ<sub>new</sub>.** (R) reported (κ = 1); (T) reported × the other camera's nested rms z for that detector (milestone 3 for 11×11; milestone 6's SB subsets for SB, computed with H8's formula); (V) the 3×3 sandwich clustered by view, with the small-sample factor G/(G − 1); (J) the 3×3 leave-one-view-out jackknife, (m − 1)/m Σ(θ<sub>i</sub> − θ̄)(θ<sub>i</sub> − θ̄)ᵀ, each delete-one set re-solved cold from reference views 0 and n // 2. J needs k ≥ 4 (a 2-view delete-one set is not identifiable), so J's hypotheses are scored on k ≥ 4; V and R also on k ≥ 4 for the comparison. Under S1/S2 every covariance is scaled by s² as in milestone 4.
- **H23 (better corners do not fix the false alarms).** With R and SB corners, S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4, pooled) ≥ 50%. Refuted if below 50%. Milestone 6 found SB corners shrink σ more than the error.
- **H24 (few clusters bias the sandwich down).** With V, S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4, pooled) > 5% on both detectors. Refuted if ≤ 5% on either.
- **H25 (the jackknife makes the test honest).** With J, S0 false alarms (k ≥ 4) ≤ 3% pooled and ≤ 5% on each camera, on both detectors. Refuted otherwise.
- **H26 (the jackknife pays in power).** With J, the real unit swap S3 (k ≥ 4, pooled over directions) is detected in fewer splits than with T, on both detectors. Refuted if J detects at least as many on either detector.
- **H27 (no harmful adoption with J).** With J, over all scenarios with k ≥ 4, adoptions whose f error exceeds the old calibration's by more than 0.5% of f ≤ 2% of decisions, on both detectors. Refuted if more on either.
- **Reported, unscored.** The full power table (focal changes ±0.5–4%, principal-point shifts 2–10 px) for each covariance and detector; the gate applied with the jackknife σ<sub>f</sub> instead of the reported one; the per-parameter rms z on S0.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 4 result: the update test cries wolf on the raw covariance and goes deaf when inflated; each view set carries its own offset

- **Runs.** 908 distinct view sets (old sets of 7, new sets of 3–6, all-view), all converged. The disturbance transform was checked by re-solving disturbed synthetic corners (match to 1e-4 px, `tests/test_selfcal.py`).
- **H18 held.** Unchanged camera, κ = 1: the 3-parameter test fires on 72.5% of splits (95% 68–76; left 68%, right 77%); the f-only test on 22–30%.
- **H19 refuted.** With the other camera's factor (1.89 left, 2.67 right): 6.5% pooled (4.6–9.0), 10.8% left, 2.1% right.
- **H20 refuted.** The real unit swap, k ≥ 4: detected in 77% (new views from the left camera) and 30% (from the right). The f-only test almost never sees it (0–5%): the swap shows in the principal point.
- **H21 refuted.** A 1% focal change, k ≥ 5: 11.7% (−1%) and 10.0% (+1%). 4% is detected in 62–75% at k = 5–6.
- **H22 refuted.** Harmful adoptions (new f error 0.5% of f worse than the old) 3.5% of all decisions (3.2–3.9); 5.6% with κ = 1, 1.4% with the transferred factor. Flag rate 37% / 7%.
- **Unscored diagnostic: why.** Old-vs-new rms z for (f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>): 2.45 / 2.35 / 2.94 (left), 2.33 / 4.56 / 2.51 (right). The right camera's c<sub>x</sub> stays at 4.3–4.8 from 3 to 6 new views: an offset per view set, not noise. The in-sample factor for exactly 1% false alarms is 2.69 / 4.80 (3-parameter) and 2.24 / 2.69 (f only); at it, a 2% focal change with 6 views is detected in 32–38% (left) and 0–2% (right) of splits, 4% in 90–97% and 52–72%.
- **Caveat.** 60 splits of 13 views reuse views heavily, so the intervals, which assume independent splits, are too narrow. Corners are milestones 1–5's 11×11 `cornerSubPix`; the next step reruns on SB corners.

## 2026-11-12 · Milestone 4 planned: the accept/reject test on disjoint views, a real camera swap and exact disturbances

Written before any milestone-4 experiment was run. Proposal milestone 4: turn milestones 1–3 into the decision a robot makes, and measure its false-alarm and false-adopt rates.

- **Old and new from disjoint views.** Milestones 2–3 scored subsets against an all-view solution that contains them (hence the nested variance). A deployed camera's old calibration comes from other images, so here each camera's 13 views are split: an "old" set of 7 views, and a "new" set of k ∈ {3, 4, 5, 6} views drawn from the other 6. 60 splits per camera and k (fixed seeds), both sets solved with the milestone-2 solver (k1 model). The two estimates are then independent apart from the shared board, and the test covariance is Σ<sub>old</sub> + Σ<sub>new</sub> (full 3×3 over f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>).
- **Changes.** (S0) none. (S1) focal change: the new set's corners scaled about the image centre by s = 1 ± 0.5, 1, 2, 4%. (S2) principal-point shift of 2, 5, 10 px in u. Both are exact transforms of the pinhole-plus-k1 model, so the new solution is the transformed undisturbed one (checked by re-solving in a test) and the real detector noise is kept. (S3) a real change: old from one camera, new from the other (the stereo pair is two units of the same model; all-view f differs by 0.67%, c<sub>x</sub> by 17 px, c<sub>y</sub> by 10 px). Truth: the camera's all-view target-free solution, transformed for S1/S2, the other camera's for S3.
- **The rule.** χ² = Δᵀ(κ²(Σ<sub>old</sub> + Σ<sub>new</sub>))⁻¹Δ on (f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>). Change detected iff χ² > 11.34 (χ²<sub>3</sub>, 1%). Detected and σ<sub>f,new</sub>/f ≤ 0.5% (milestone 2's gate): adopt the new calibration. Detected and imprecise: flag for a target recalibration. Not detected: keep the old one. κ = 1 (reported covariance) and κ = the other camera's milestone-3 nested rms z (1.89 for the left camera, 2.67 for the right), so no factor is fitted on the camera it is scored on.
- **H18 (false alarms).** On S0 with κ = 1 the test fires on more than 5% of splits (nominal 1%). Refuted if ≤ 5%.
- **H19 (the transferred κ).** On S0 with transferred κ, false alarms ≤ 3% pooled and ≤ 5% on each camera. Refuted otherwise.
- **H20 (the real swap).** With transferred κ, S3 is detected in ≥ 90% of splits with k ≥ 4, in both directions. Refuted if below on either.
- **H21 (power for a 1% focal change).** With transferred κ, s = 1.01 and 0.99 are detected in ≥ 80% of splits with k ≥ 5. Refuted below. The power curve over all s, shifts and k is reported.
- **H22 (no harmful adoption).** Over all scenarios, k and both κ, the rule adopts an update whose f error against truth exceeds the old calibration's by more than 0.5% of f in ≤ 2% of splits. Refuted if more. Also reported: the flag rate, and how often the decision leaves an f error ≥ 1%.
- **Ablation, unscored.** The f-only test (1 degree of freedom) and the 4-parameter test with k1.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 6 result: the one-axis pattern was the corner localiser; it also made most of milestone 5's c<sub>y</sub> shift; better corners make σ less honest

- **Localiser.** Corners from `cornerSubPix` with an 11×11 half-window (milestones 1–5) against a 5×5 window and `findChessboardCornersSB` (`CALIB_CB_ACCURACY`), matched one-to-one in all 26 images. Interior median gap 0.07 px (11 vs 5), but 26 corners differ by > 1 px (max 6.4 px), every one on the outer ring of the grid, where the 23 px box runs off the pattern. SB vs 11×11: 52 over 1 px, max 7.7, all on the ring; SB vs 5×5: max 1.7 px.
- **H14 refuted.** SB along-column correlation 0.32 (left) / 0.56 (right), not < 0.2. The *one-directional* structure goes (along-row correlation rises to 0.23–0.34; per-(view, column) variance share 0.65 / 0.51 → 0.41 / 0.43, null 0.17), leaving a smoother 2-D correlation no localiser removes.
- **H15 held.** 91% / 78% of the per-(view, column) mean residual is perpendicular to the column, with the old corners (57–67% with the new ones).
- **H16 refuted.** (view, column)-clustered sandwich, original corners, random 4–12 views against all-view Zhang: rms z 2.44 / 1.72 (reported σ 2.21 / 1.63). Synthetic check (in the tests): with pure per-column offsets it rises above the reported σ but still covers only part of the true spread (4.3 against 5.8 px; reported 3.6).
- **H17 refuted, opposite direction.** Nested rms z with SB corners 3.12 (95% 2.88–3.37), 5×5 3.45, against 2.28 with the old corners; median |z| 1.97 / 1.70 / 1.40. σ halves (median 1.44 → 0.87 / 0.73 px) while the error against each detector's own all-view fit falls only from 0.24% to 0.23% / 0.15%. Fewer subsets drop from the nested test with the new corners (7–11 vs 58).
- **Unplanned, corrects milestone 5.** The flat-board c<sub>y</sub> shift is 8.7 px (old corners), 4.5 px (5×5) and 1.2 px, 1.8σ (SB) on the left camera; 5.3 / 3.8 / 1.4 px on the right. The bend survives every detector (flatness rms 0.0042–0.0058 against 0.0077; left/right height correlation 0.89–0.90). Milestone 5's "5–10 px" was mostly misplaced outer corners, which a free-board fit absorbs as bend.
- **Unplanned.** The localiser alone moves flat-board Zhang's f by 4.8 px (left) and 7.2 px (right), 0.9–1.3%, and the target-free f by 1.8 / 4.2 px, while every fit reports σ ≤ 1.2 px. No ground truth says which localiser is right.
- **Deviation.** The SB matching tolerance was first 3 px and failed on images with 4–8 px outliers (which were the finding); raised to 10 px (half the smallest corner spacing), with the one-to-one check kept. All 1,680 subset solves ran after that; 1,679 converged.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 6 planned: where does the column-wise residual correlation come from, and does a better corner detector or a column-clustered σ fix the 2×?

Written before any milestone-6 experiment was run (only the corner spacing and the detection rate of the second detector were checked: 25–41 px between neighbours, and `findChessboardCornersSB` finds the board in 26 of 26 images).

- **What milestone 5 left.** In the target-free k1 residuals on all 13 views, corners that are neighbours along a board column correlate at 0.56 / 0.44 (left / right), and corners two apart at 0.73 / 0.65, while neighbours along a row are at about 0. A correlation that rises with distance looks like an offset shared by each column of each image, not a local detector error. Free 3-D points absorb any fixed print error of the board, so whatever is left must change from image to image.
- **Descriptive, not a test.** The share of residual variance explained by a per-(view, board column) mean and by a per-(view, board row) mean, against the null for independent noise (1/6 and 1/9); and how much of the column-mean residual lies perpendicular to the column's line in the image, against along it.
- **H14 (the detector is the cause).** Detect the same corners with a different localiser, OpenCV's `findChessboardCornersSB` with `CALIB_CB_ACCURACY` (Duda and Frese, 2018), and, as an ablation, `cornerSubPix` with a 5×5 half-window instead of 11×11. Predicted: with SB corners the adjacent-along-column correlation of the all-view target-free residuals falls below 0.2 on both cameras. Refuted if it stays at 0.2 or more on either, which puts the cause upstream of the localiser (the image or the board's mounting).
- **H15 (the column offsets are sideways).** The per-(view, column) mean residual is predicted to lie mostly perpendicular to the column's image direction: ≥ 70% of its energy on both cameras (isotropic would be 50%). A sideways shift of a whole column points at the localisation of that column's edge line; a shift along it does not.
- **H16 (a column-clustered σ).** The sandwich σ of f clustered by (view, board column) — 9k clusters, which keeps the within-column correlation and drops only the cross-column part of the view clustering — on milestone 2's random 4–12-view subsets, original corners, scored against all-view Zhang as in milestone 3. Predicted to lie in [0.7, 1.4] on each camera. Refuted otherwise. Also reported, unscored: the (view, row) clustering and the nested test against the target-free all-view solution.
- **H17 (a better detector shrinks the 2×).** Re-solve milestone 2's 560 subsets (same views and reference views) with SB corners and the k1 model, and score the nested self-consistency test of H8 against the SB all-view target-free solution. Predicted: rms z ≤ 1.5 (against 2.28 with `cornerSubPix`). Refuted if > 1.5. Also reported: the all-view intrinsics with each detector, and whether c<sub>y</sub> and f move.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5 result: the bent board moves c<sub>y</sub> by 5–9 px; adding k2 does not make σ honest

- **Released-object Zhang is target-free BA.** `calibrateCameraROExtended` and our target-free solver return the same f, c<sub>y</sub>, σ, board shape (height correlation 1.000) and residuals to 1e-6 px on both cameras and both lens models. With the corners free, the only extra information the board gives is its metric scale, which carries none about the intrinsics. So H11's test (gap < 2σ) **held trivially** (gap 4e-6σ), and the evidence for the bend is the flat-vs-released comparison: the only difference between the two Zhang fits is the flat, evenly spaced board, and it moves c<sub>y</sub> by −8.7 px (left) and −5.3 px (right) and f by +0.79 / +1.28 px (k1 model).
- **H12 held, more strongly than predicted.** With k1 + k2, nested self-consistency rms z is 3.01 (95% 2.05–4.04; left 3.85, right 2.04), against 2.28 with k1 alone. Fragile: two left-camera subsets of 4–5 views (z −28, −23) carry it, where σ<sub>sub</sub> barely exceeds σ<sub>all</sub>; without the top five |z| it is 2.00 (k1: 1.94), median |z| 1.31 (k1: 1.40). Either way k2 is not the cause. All 560 solves converged (at most 180 evaluations); k2 = 0.143 ± 0.018 (left) and 0.091 ± 0.007 (right), clearly non-zero.
- **H13 held.** Adjacent-corner residual correlation with k1 + k2: 0.31 (left) and 0.24 (right); flat-board Zhang 0.36 / 0.32. The structure is one-directional: along the board's columns 0.44–0.56 (0.76 at two corners apart), along its rows −0.02 to 0.02. A lens term would act radially, not along one board axis; the cause (print, detector, the board's mounting) is not established.
- **Unscored.** Against the released-object reference, rms z is 1.66 (k1) and 1.79 (k2): adding k2 shrinks σ<sub>f</sub> by a median 5% and leaves the median error unchanged (0.24%).
- **Correction to milestone 1.** The reported reprojection "0.42 vs 0.26 px" compared OpenCV's per-point rms (√(u² + v²)) with our per-coordinate rms. On one definition, per coordinate: flat Zhang 0.30 / 0.34 px, target-free 0.26 / 0.29 px, 13–15% lower, not about 40%. Fixed in the README and card.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 5 planned: does the bent board explain the principal-point gap, and does an unmodelled k2 explain the 2× σ?

Written before any milestone-5 experiment was run. Proposal milestone 5 (unmodelled lens terms on real data), taken before 3b and 4 because it needs no new data and milestones 1 and 3 left two named suspects untested: the bent board (for the c<sub>y</sub> gap) and the k1-only lens (for the overconfidence).

- **Bend-aware reference.** Zhang with a released object (`cv2.calibrateCameraROExtended`, Strobl and Hirzinger 2011): the board's 3-D corners are refined with the camera, two corners fixed (OpenCV's `iFixedPoint` = 8, the last corner of the first row). Same model (f, c<sub>x</sub>, c<sub>y</sub>, k1; k2 as ablation), all 13 views.
- **H11 (the bend explains c<sub>y</sub>).** With the released-object reference, |c<sub>y,RO</sub> − c<sub>y,target-free</sub>| < 2σ on both cameras (σ: both solvers' σ in quadrature), against 5.3σ and 2.9σ with the flat board. Refuted if ≥ 2σ on either camera.

The nested self-consistency rms z of milestone 3 (H8; against the k1+k2 all-view solution) stays > 1.5. Refuted if ≤ 1.5, which would make the unmodelled k2 the main cause.
- **H13 (structured residuals survive k2).** On the all-view fits, the correlation of the residual vectors of horizontally and vertically adjacent corners in the same image is ≥ 0.1 with k1 and k2 (correlated detector error, not lens model). Refuted if it falls below 0.1 with k2.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 3 result: the overconfidence is real; the jackknife σ is honest but a worse gate

- **Re-solves identical.** All 560 subsets converged with the same f as milestone 2; 3,473 of 3,480 leave-one-view-out solves converged on tolerance. Median 2.7 s per subset with its jackknife.
- **H8 held.** Against the target-free all-view f, nested variance: rms z 2.28 (95% 1.93–2.73; left 2.67, right 1.89; 58 of 360 subsets dropped with σ_sub ≤ σ_all). The 2× was not the Zhang reference. Jackknife σ in the same test: 0.85.
- **H9 refuted, on the safe side.** Jackknife rms z 0.703 (left) and 0.651 (right; 95% 0.56–0.74), 2σ coverage 97% / 99%; near-critical 4–6 views 1.01 (reported σ 2.34). By size: 0.33 at 4 views (σ_jack a median 15× the reported σ), 0.55–0.94 from 5 to 11. The view-clustered sandwich: 1.76 / 1.59, and 2.23 at 4–6 views as predicted; corner-clustered and HC0 sandwiches are no better than the reported σ.
- **H10 refuted.** AUC for good (≤ 1%) against bad (> 2%) updates, 4–12 views: reported σ 0.91, jackknife 0.75 (difference −0.15, 95% −0.25 to −0.07), sandwich by view 0.76, by corner 0.87, HC0 0.89. Only 22 bad updates.
- **H4 second test refuted.** σ_jack/f ≤ 0.5% rejects 22/22 bad but accepts 19% of good (0% near-critical). Transfer: no σ's fitted threshold reaches 90/90 on the other camera; the reported σ comes closest (76/79 and 91/88).
- **Reading.** The jackknife's upward bias (Efron–Stein) is largest where leave-one-out leaves 3 views. Honesty and ranking power pull apart: next gate ranks by reported σ and spread, and vetoes by the jackknife.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 3 planned: is the 2× overconfidence real, and does a cluster-robust σ fix it?

Written before any milestone-3 experiment was run. The proposal's milestone 3 (natural features on a second data set) moves to 3b; this step answers the question milestone 2 left open, on the same 560 subsets, with no new data.

- **Why σ could be too small.** The Gauss–Newton σ assumes independent, equal-variance pixel residuals and a correct model. Corner-detector errors are correlated within an image (blur, lighting, the detector's shared sub-pixel window) and the k1-only lens leaves structured residuals. Both make the reported σ too small by a factor that differs between subsets, which a single inflation factor cannot follow (milestone 2's cross-camera κ).
- **Robust σ, two estimators, no fitted constants.** (a) The cluster-robust sandwich, clustered by view: var f = Σ_views ((J b)·r)² over each view's residuals, with b = (JᵀJ)⁻¹e_f, times the small-sample factor G/(G − 1). (b) The leave-one-view-out jackknife: re-solve the subset k times without one view each (cold start, same solver), var f = (k − 1)/k Σ (f₍ᵢ₎ − f̄)². Ablation: the sandwich clustered by corner (54 clusters) and unclustered (HC0).
- **H8 (the reference is not the cause).** Zhang's all-view f is itself 0.8 / 1.3 px from the target-free all-view f, so part of milestone 2's z could be reference mismatch. Self-consistency test against the target-free all-view solution, with the nested-estimator variance: z_n = (f_sub − f_all) / √(σ_sub² − σ_all²), random subsets of 4–12 views (subsets with σ_sub ≤ σ_all are counted and dropped). Predicted: rms z_n > 1.5 (the overconfidence is real). Refuted if ≤ 1.5.
- **H9 (robust σ is honest).** With σ from the jackknife, rms z against all-view Zhang over random 4–12-view subsets lies in [0.7, 1.4] on each camera, with no fitted factor. Scored separately for the view-clustered sandwich; predicted to hold for the jackknife and to fail (rms z > 1.4) for the sandwich at 4–6 views, where few clusters bias it down.
- **H10 (robust σ ranks better).** As a gate statistic for good (|Δf|/f ≤ 1%) against bad (> 2%) updates, pooled over all subsets of 4–12 views and both cameras, the jackknife σ/f has a higher ROC AUC than the reported σ/f. Refuted if it is equal or lower (paired bootstrap over subsets, 95% interval of the AUC difference).
- **H4, second test.** Accept iff σ_jack / f ≤ 0.5% (the same threshold as milestone 2); target 90/90. Also scored on the near-critical subsets alone. And the transfer test from milestone 1's next steps: the threshold on each σ that maximises min(accept good, reject bad) on one camera, scored on the other.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 2 result: geometry predicts the error, σ stays 2× small, and Zhang's σ is far worse near the critical set

- **Solver fixed.** The cap was not only the gauge: holding view `ref`'s pose fixed left the scale free (the initialisation puts that camera at the world origin, so its translation is zero), and scipy's `trf`/`lsmr` crawled even once the scale was pinned too (corner 0's depth). Levenberg–Marquardt on a dense analytic Jacobian (checked against finite differences, max error 1e-6 relative) converges in 21–23 evaluations on all 13 views and in at most 177 on any subset: **560 of 560 solves converged on tolerance**. All-view f moved by 0.0004 px (534.8239 / 538.4302).
- **H2 rerun held.** Same 360 subsets of 4–12 views: rms z 1.945 (95% 1.75–2.12) against milestone 1's 1.944; median f change 0.002 px, largest 0.66 px. The cap was not the cause of the 2× overconfidence.
- **H6 held.** Spearman ρ between view spread and |Δf|/f: −0.46 (left, n 240) and −0.58 (right, n 240), p < 1e-13. The reported σ also tracks the error (ρ +0.39 / +0.45).
- **H7 refuted.** On the 160 near-critical subsets (spread 7–36°) rms z is 2.41 (95% 2.09–2.81); 20% have |z| > 3. Unplanned but striking: Zhang's σ on the same subsets is far less honest, rms z 5.83, and its median error is larger than the target-free one at 4–6 views (1.50 / 1.15 / 1.08% against 1.09 / 0.99 / 0.82%).
- **H4 first test refuted.** The 0.5% precision gate rejected 92% (56 of 61) of bad updates (> 2% off) but accepted only 84% (340 of 407) of good ones (≤ 1%). The five bad accepts were 2.0–2.9% off with σ/f 0.45–0.48%, just under the gate. Doubling σ (unplanned) rejects every bad update but accepts only 37% of good ones.
- **Unplanned: σ inflation does not transfer cleanly between cameras.** κ = rms z fitted on left (2.21) gives right-camera rms z 0.74 (over-cautious); κ from right (1.63) gives left rms z 1.35 and 2σ coverage 85% (random) and 82% (near-critical). Median error / σ by σ bin is 1.0–1.8, so the overconfidence sits in the tails more than in the typical subset.
- **No rotation-only subsets.** The milestone plan named rotation-only motions; this data set has a moving board and no such views, so near-critical here means near-parallel board planes only.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 2 planned: does the view geometry predict the error, and can σ gate an update?

Written before any milestone-2 experiment was run.

- **Solver fix first.** Milestone 1's solves all stopped on the 1,000-evaluation cap. Fix the gauge by holding view 0's pose (rotation and translation) constant: that removes all 7 similarity directions (a fixed non-zero translation pins the scale), so the normal matrix is invertible and the intrinsics' covariance is the plain inverse, equal to the gauge-invariant pseudo-inverse. Raise the cap and record convergence status for every solve.
- **H2 rerun (was the cap an artefact?).** Rerun milestone 1's 400 random subsets (same seeds) with the fixed solver. Predicted: rms z for 4–12 views stays within ±0.2 of 1.94 (the all-view f already agreed across starts to 0.001 px). Refuted if it moves by more than 0.2.
- **View spread.** For each view, the board normal in the camera frame from the all-view Zhang poses. A subset's spread is the largest angle between two of its normals. All parallel normals is the planar critical case (Sturm and Maybank 1999): f is then unidentifiable.
- **Near-critical subsets.** For sizes 3, 4, 5 and 6, enumerate every subset of the 13 views and keep the 20 with the smallest spread, per camera (160 solves), alongside the 400 random ones.
- **H6 (geometry predicts error).** Pooled over random and near-critical subsets of 4–12 views, Spearman ρ between spread and |Δf|/f (against all-view Zhang) is ≤ −0.3 on each camera. Refuted if ρ > −0.3 on either.
- **H7 (σ flags near-critical subsets).** On the near-critical subsets, rms z ≤ 2, i.e. σ grows as fast as the error. Refuted if rms z > 2.
- **H4, first test (a precision gate).** Accept an update iff σ_f / f ≤ 0.5%. A good update has |Δf|/f ≤ 1%, a bad one > 2% (between is not scored). Pooled over all subsets and both cameras, the gate should reject ≥ 90% of bad updates and accept ≥ 90% of good ones. Refuted if either rate is below 90%. The proposal's χ² test against an old calibration needs a disturbed camera, which this data set does not have; that test waits for milestone 4.

- **H1 held.** All 13 views: target-free f 534.82 / 538.43 px against Zhang's 535.62 / 539.71 (left / right), gaps −0.15% / −0.24%, under 1σ; the same f from 0.7× and 1.3× starts.
- **H2 held narrowly.** Subsets of 4–12 views (360 solves): rms z 1.94, 95% bootstrap 1.75–2.12. Ideal is 1, so σ is about twice too small; worst at 3–7 views (2.1–2.8). Zhang's own σ against the same reference: 2.16. Not planned: subsets of 3 views (rms z 2.79, worst error 20%).

Target-free reprojection rms 0.26 / 0.29 px against Zhang's 0.42 / 0.49. The principal point's c_y differs by 8.7 px (5.3σ) and 5.2 px (2.9σ); the bend is the likely cause, not yet tested.

- **Problem found.** Every solve stopped on the 1,000-evaluation cap (the similarity gauge slows trust-region). For all-view fits f agrees across starts to 0.001 px; subsets were not checked. Milestone 2 should fix the gauge and use an analytic Jacobian.

## 2026-11-11 · Milestone 1 planned: target-free self-calibration against Zhang on a real stereo pair

Written before any experiment was run.

- **Data.** OpenCV's `samples/data` stereo calibration set, `left01..14.jpg` and `right01..14.jpg` (index 10 absent in both), 640×480, a 9×6-inner-corner checkerboard, Apache-2.0. Two real cameras, 13 views each.
- **Reference.** Zhang calibration with the board's known geometry (`cv2.calibrateCamera`, model fx = fy = f, cx, cy, k1, k2) on all 13 views of each camera; interval by bootstrap over views.
- **Target-free solver.** The same detected corners, but the 54 board points become free 3-D unknowns (no planarity, no spacing): initial focal guessed 30% wrong, two-view initialisation from the homography between images (no board metric), PnP for the rest, then bundle adjustment over poses, points and intrinsics (f, cx, cy, k1). Covariance from the Gauss–Newton normal matrix with the gauge fixed, scaled by the residual variance.
- **H1.** On all 13 views, target-free f lies within 2% of Zhang's f for both cameras. Refuted if either is further.
- **H2 (the solver knows when it is wrong).** Over random view subsets of size 4 to 12, the z-score (target-free f − all-view Zhang f) / σ_f(target-free) has rms ≤ 2. Refuted if rms > 2, i.e. the covariance is overconfident.
- **H3 (price of no target).** Target-free σ_f is at least twice Zhang's on the same views, and the ratio falls as views are added. Refuted if the ratio is below 2 at 13 views.
